# Gateway routing and fallback

The gateway is magpie's local LLM endpoint (`127.0.0.1:3425` by default). It
serves Chat Completions, Responses, Anthropic Messages and Gemini. It finds
the provider, key or account and model for each request. When the upstream
doesn't speak the agent's API, the gateway translates. When an upstream
fails before any of the reply is sent, the gateway moves to the next
candidate.

This page covers how a request is routed. Plugins that change requests and
replies are in [Gateway middleware](gateway-middleware.md). Subscriptions
served by a plugin are in [Provider and plugin ownership](provider-plugins.md).

WorkBuddy and CodeBuddy Code's `X-Conversation-ID` is a native session header
in `sessionHeaders` (`sessionOf`, `nativeSessionOf`, `conversationID` in
[`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go)). It keeps their requests
apart even when two chats have the same first message. The same identity is
used for affinity, usage and the Routing page, and is preserved when passed
to a remote magpie, not copied to an ordinary vendor. Existing header
precedence is unchanged. Affinity previously kept under an inferred first
message digest is not migrated to a newly recognised native id, so the first
request after upgrading can choose an account anew. Historical usage is not
backfilled; see [Gateway Session Aggregation](gateway-sessions.md).

## Responsibilities and sources of truth

| Part | Responsibility | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Address and caller | `DefaultAddr`. The local token `magpie` and the per-agent `magpie-<agent>` (`TokenFor`). `agentOf` tells which agent asked, first by token, then by User-Agent. A Codex turn that names itself Claude Code is Codex's: OpenAI's Codex plugin for Claude Code (/codex:rescue, /codex:review) runs `codex app-server` with clientInfo "Claude Code", which Codex sends as its originator and User-Agent, and only Codex's turn headers (`x-codex-window-id`, `x-codex-turn-metadata`) tell it apart from Claude Code's own requests (`codexForClaudeCode`); an app that runs Codex as its own engine under its own name (Lody) stays that app. VS Code's Agents window runs `codex app-server` as `vscode_agent_host`, its originator and User-Agent: its calls are counted under VS Code (`vscodeAgentHostUA` in [`vscode.go`](../../internal/agent/vscode.go)), and its turns are Codex's for every Codex rule (`fromCodex`, `hostedCodex`), so one of Codex's own models asked there goes on Codex's sign-in (`codexOwnOffMagpie`) rather than 404; only Codex's own names (`codexNamed`) are kept as Codex's shape for a test asked as Codex (`TestVSCodeAgentHostIsCodex`, `TestVSCodeAgentHostOwnModelGoesOnSignIn`, `TestVSCodeAgentHostNotCodexTestShape`). Claude Science, whose User-Agent is the Anthropic SDK's, is told by its client's `X-Anthropic-Surface: operon` (`isClaudeScience`). It is listed every model as `claude-magpie-<number>` (`scienceModels`; its picker keeps only a `claude-` id whose name isn't a lower-case slug), and a Claude id magpie doesn't serve (its default before a pick, its haiku side tasks) goes to the model its last turn with tools was on, else the first it is listed (`scienceTurn`, kept in `claude-science.model`). | [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go) |
| WSL under NAT | On Windows, while the gateway listens on loopback alone, `serveWSL` also serves it, in plain HTTP, at each address Windows has on a WSL network (`WSLNets`: the `vEthernet (WSL…)` adapters, looked for every 10s, since WSL's VM brings its adapter up and down). That is where a distro in NAT networking reaches Windows, and what `place` writes into its agents (zaidu259434 on X: Codex in WSL failed with sharing off). `lanGuard` takes a request there as this computer's (`fromWSL`) only when it arrives at that address, from inside that network, with the WSL key: `WSLKey`, a `wsl-` secret in magpie's folder (`wsl-key`, 0600), optionally followed by `-<agent>`. It becomes a loopback request carrying `magpie` or `magpie-<agent>` (`asFromLoopback`). Anything else there, a Docker Desktop container on the same network included, is judged as another machine's. Windows' firewall still decides whether WSL gets in: WSL's network is a Public one. | [`wsl.go`](../../internal/gateway/wsl.go) |
| TLS | `--tls-cert`/`--tls-key` (`MAGPIE_TLS_CERT`, `MAGPIE_TLS_KEY`) make `ListenAndServe`, `Relisten` (both through `Server.listen`) and `magpie web`'s page (`ServeTLS`) wrap the listener in `tlsListener`. A connection from another computer is `tls.Server` at once, so its plain HTTP gets net/http's 400. One from this computer (`fromThisComputer`: loopback, or a peer address equal to the local one) is sniffed on its own goroutine, 10s at most (`sniffWait`), and 0x16 makes it TLS, so `URL()`, the agents, MCP servers, `ServedBy` and the healthcheck keep `http://127.0.0.1`. `certFiles.get` reloads the pair when either file's mtime or size changes; a pair that doesn't load keeps the one served (logged once per change). `tlsConfig` refuses half a pair or files that don't load before binding. HTTP/1.1 only over TLS (hijack, Codex's websocket). `Scheme()` gives the scheme of `LANURLs`, a scheme-less `MAGPIE_PUBLIC_URL` and the page's network links. Host and Origin checks (`foreignPage`, `rebound`) don't depend on the scheme. | [`tls.go`](../../internal/gateway/tls.go), [`lan.go`](../../internal/gateway/lan.go) |
| HTTP surface | `Handler` routes `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/responses`, `/v1/messages` (each also without `/v1`), `/v1/models`, Gemini's `/v1beta/models/…`, the Codex backend path, `/v1/codex/models` (Codex's catalog shape for a Codex elsewhere with a gateway key, `codexCatalog`, kept to the key's models by `keyAllowed`; #1281), Ollama's `GET /api/version`, `GET /api/tags` and `POST /api/show` (`ollama.go`, for a client that takes a custom endpoint only as an Ollama server: Visual Studio's and VS Code's Copilot, wani on Discord; the list is `catalogFor`'s, so a key's models alone, `/api/show` tells `capabilities` (`completion`, `tools`, `vision` from `Images`, `thinking` from `Thinks`) and `model_info` with `general.architecture` `magpie`, `general.basename`, `magpie.context_length` (32768, VS Code's own default, when the window isn't known, since VS Code would take a 0) and the `general.parameter_count` and `magpie.embedding_length` the ollama CLI before 0.7 reads unchecked; a name ending `:latest`, as the ollama CLI asks, is the bare id unless a model is really called that (`ollamaName`); the client then chats on `/v1/chat/completions`, or on Ollama's own `POST /api/chat` and `POST /api/generate` (`ollama_chat.go`, Zed's Ollama provider, super_lalalalala on Discord), which `ollamaChatBody` turns into a `/v1/chat/completions` body handed to the same `handle(provider.Chat)` on a clone of the request, so they are routed, held to a key's models and counted as it is: `messages` with `images` (base64, its type sniffed, as data-URL parts; a tool message's in a user message after it), an assistant's `thinking` as `reasoning_content` and its `tool_calls` given ids where Ollama sent none, a tool result matched to its call by `tool_call_id`, else `tool_name`, else in order (one with no call is a 400), `tools`, `format` (`json` or a schema), `think` (a level is `reasoning_effort`), and `options`' `temperature`, `top_p`, `seed`, `stop`, the two penalties and a positive `num_predict` as `max_tokens` (`num_ctx`, `top_k` and the rest of Ollama's own are dropped); `ollamaWriter` turns the reply into Ollama's NDJSON lines (streamed unless `stream:false`, then one object), tool calls with their `arguments` as an object and an `id`, and a last `done` line with `done_reason` and the token counts, and an error into `{"error":"…"}` with the status `/v1` would give; a request with no messages or prompt is answered `done_reason:"load"`, as Ollama does to warm a model), `/mcp/{name}`, `/mcp/magpie/web-search`, `/mcp/magpie/vision` and `/mcp/magpie/image` with its `/mcp/magpie/image/files/{name}` (magpie's own web search, image recognition and image generation as MCP servers, below) and the `/v1/magpie/*` status endpoints. `/v1/magpie/heap` (`heapProfile`, [`heap.go`](../../internal/gateway/heap.go), for `magpie heap`) is held tighter than the rest by `heapRefused` in the handler itself, so it holds where `Handler` is served without `lanGuard` too: only a loopback caller that isn't proxied (`local`), carries no browser mark (`fromBrowser`) and names a loopback `Host` (`loopbackHost`) gets the profile; a gateway key never lets anything else in. `/v1/magpie/cpu` (`cpuProfile`, [`cpu.go`](../../internal/gateway/cpu.go), for `magpie cpu`) records a CPU profile for `?seconds=` (30, from 5 to 120) and is held to the same callers by `cpuRefused`, which shares `profileRefused` with `heapRefused`; one profile records at a time (`cpuRecording`), a second is answered 409, and a caller that hangs up stops it. `/v1/magpie/goroutines` (`goroutineDump`, [`goroutines.go`](../../internal/gateway/goroutines.go), for `magpie goroutines`, #3362) answers every goroutine's stack and wait as text (`runtime/pprof`'s `goroutine` profile at debug 2) to the same callers, through `goroutinesRefused`, and is answered ahead of everything below (`dumpFirst`, around `Handler`'s wrappers and around `lanGuard` in `Front`), since it is asked for while magpie hangs: the allow list reads settings.json under its lock, and `holdCatalog` takes `provider.Hold`'s and `filememo`'s, so a stuck holder of any of them would hold the dump too; a caller `goroutinesRefused` lets in is one those let in anyway, and any other goes through them as before (`TestGoroutineDumpAnswersWhileRequestsWait`). `/v1/alpha/search` (also without `/v1`, and on the Codex backend path for a magpie model) is Codex's web.run search (`alphaSearch`, `codex_search.go`): a ChatGPT account's model goes to the ChatGPT backend's `/alpha/search` on the account's sign-in, the next account asked on 401, 403, 429 or 5xx; any other model's `search_query` is searched by `webSearch`, in the mode its `settings.external_web_access` says (#5018; a ChatGPT account then searches by the same backend search, see [Which providers search for another model](#which-providers-search-for-another-model)), and when nothing answers the reason is a 200 with `output`, since Codex ends the turn on any failed web.run (anton on Discord). A WebSocket upgrade on `/v1/responses`, `/responses` or the Codex backend's `/responses` is Responses over a WebSocket, as Codex speaks it (below, [A Responses WebSocket](#a-responses-websocket)); one on another Codex backend path is answered 426, and a plain GET on `/v1/responses` 405. Every route is wrapped in `counted`, `corsGuard`, `callerGuard`, `withCaller` and `keyLimited`; `/v1/messages`, `/messages` and `/v1/messages/count_tokens` in `keepsSignIn` too. `corsGuard` (#1051) serves CORS only to the origins listed in Settings › Network › Web pages (`settings.CORSOrigins`, cleaned to `scheme://host[:port]` by `settings.CleanOrigins`, which refuses a wildcard). A browser extension's own pages, popup and service worker are listed the same way by their origin, which is the browser's scheme and the extension's id: `chrome-extension://<id>` (Chromium, the id 32 letters a–p), `moz-extension://<uuid>` (Firefox, the Internal UUID about:debugging shows, not the add-on id) and `safari-web-extension://<uuid>` (Safari, which picks a new UUID on each launch, so a listing lasts until Safari restarts); the id must have that browser's shape, cased any way, and nothing else is taken — no bare scheme, no wildcard, no path (SkyAerope on Discord: an extension without host permissions is held to CORS like a page). A content script's fetch carries the page's origin, not the extension's. A listed extension is treated exactly as a listed page, below; one not listed passes as before (other schemes, below) with no CORS headers. For a listed origin it answers the preflight (204) and adds `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, and every other request from it needs an enabled gateway key (`access.Authenticate`), even on loopback, or gets a 401. A browser sends a preflight without credentials, so a listed origin's preflight from another computer is let past `lanGuard`'s key check to be answered here (#4931, ggbdpq: a page on another machine got 401 to its preflight and never sent the keyed request); `lanGuard` hands on only an `OPTIONS` with `Access-Control-Request-Method` whose origin is listed (`corsAllowed`). Any other origin's preflight still meets the key check: `corsGuard` passes some unlisted origins on (loopback names, the request's own origin, other schemes), and Codex's backend relay would serve their `OPTIONS` on the user's sign-in (`TestCORSPreflightFromLANReachesNothing`). It takes the key from the caller `lanGuard` recorded too, since on the running server `lanGuard` has already put magpie's own token in the key's place. Any other web page is refused 403 on every route (`foreignPage`): on loopback a call needs no key and the body's Content-Type isn't checked, so a page the user visits could otherwise send a "simple" `text/plain` POST and spend their subscriptions blind. Only an `http`/`https` Origin is judged, and one on this computer (`localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `[::1]`, any port) or the request's own origin (its host and port equal to `Host`, such as a page on the gateway's LAN address) passes, as does usemagpie.ai on `/v1/magpie/omarchy`. Other schemes (`app://`, `file://`, `tauri://`, `vscode-webview://`, `chrome-extension://`) come from desktop apps, webviews and extensions that don't enforce CORS, and pass keyless as before; `null` is not one of them, since any web page sends it from a sandboxed frame and an opened file sends it too, so a call with it needs an enabled gateway key as a listed origin's does, and is answered 403 without one (no CORS headers either way, so a frame or a local page still reads no reply). A page at a subdomain of localhost (`localhostSubdomain`, #2256) needs a key the same way: Chrome and Firefox resolve `*.localhost` to loopback themselves, but Safari and other WebKit views ask the system resolver, which before macOS 26 asks the network's DNS (WebKit bug 160504), so a resolver that answers serves such a page from anywhere. `tauri.localhost` and `wails.localhost` stay keyless on Windows only, where Tauri's and Wails' webviews serve themselves at those names; on macOS and Linux those apps use `tauri://` and `wails://`. A request with no Origin (every agent's) passes through with no CORS headers, as before. A page whose own name resolves to 127.0.0.1 (DNS rebinding) would count as same-origin here, so `lanGuard` refuses it first (`rebound`, 403): a loopback request carrying a browser's marks (`Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-Site`/`Sec-Fetch-Mode`, a `Mozilla/` user agent) whose `Host` isn't this computer's (an IP, `localhost` but not a subdomain of it, which WebKit asks the DNS for (#2256), a container runtime's name for the host such as `host.docker.internal`, `MAGPIE_PUBLIC_URL`'s or `MAGPIE_ADDR`'s host, the machine's own name) needs an enabled gateway key. Agents send none of those marks and pass under any name. Before any of that, `lanGuard` lets in one request without a key from anywhere: `POST /_magpie/claude-mcp/<token>` with the token of a Claude subscription run now live (`ownCallback`, see [the Claude subscription bridge](claude-subscription-bridge.md)), the run's MCP helper calling back to a `MAGPIE_ADDR` that isn't loopback (#1546). Next, before the key or `rebound` is looked at, the allow list (lc on Discord, magpie in Docker on a public host): with `MAGPIE_ALLOW_IPS` or Settings › Network › Allowed addresses (`settings.AllowIPs`, this computer's own, never brought by a sync or restore: see [sync and backup](sync-backup.md#what-stays-on-each-computer); the variable wins and the page then only shows it) naming IPs and CIDR ranges, a request whose connection (`RemoteAddr`, IPv4-mapped IPv6 unmapped, zone dropped) is outside them is refused 403 naming its address, even with an enabled gateway key (`refuseNotAllowed`). Loopback and this computer's own interface addresses always pass. A forwarding header is never taken for the address, so a proxy or tunnel on this computer passes (it arrives from loopback) and its clients are limited at the proxy. An entry that doesn't parse lets nothing in and is logged once; the list stays on. Settings' list is read with `settings.LoadChecked`: a settings.json that can't be read, or doesn't parse with no last good generation, is unknown, never "no list", so the list read last in this process stays in force (`lastAllow`), and with none read yet only this computer passes, the 403 saying settings.json couldn't be read; logged once. No file, or an empty one, is no list. The Settings page's saves read with `LoadChecked` too and refuse such a file, as `settings.Save` does. `magpie web`'s server is wrapped in the same check (`AllowGuard`), and its port hands the gateway's client routes (`ClientPath`) to `Server.Front`, the same lanGuard-over-Handler the gateway's listener serves, each marked by `FromPagePort` so `local` is false for it (`viaPagePort`; `lanGuard` refuses it 403 while magpie isn't shared, saying to call the gateway's own address, and asks a gateway key otherwise): a raw TCP forward or a proxy with no forwarding header reaches that port from loopback, so no request there counts as an agent here (`TestClientPathCoversHandler` fails when a route Handler adds is in neither `ClientPath` nor its list of routes that stay on the gateway's port). Settings refuses a list that would leave out the address the page saving it is open from (`KeepsOut`); the page names that address, which behind Docker or a proxy is the proxy's, and offers to add it to the refused list (`allowSays`, Mathresearcher on Discord). | [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go), [`allow.go`](../../internal/gateway/allow.go), [`internal/settings/allowips.go`](../../internal/settings/allowips.go), [`cors.go`](../../internal/gateway/cors.go), [`codex_catalog.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_catalog.go), [`ollama.go`](../../internal/gateway/ollama.go), [`ollama_chat.go`](../../internal/gateway/ollama_chat.go), [`internal/settings/origins.go`](../../internal/settings/origins.go) |
| Model resolution | `serve` resolves the model the agent asked for: Claude tier stand-ins, a routing group (`provider.GroupFor`: its id, the model's id however a vendor spells it, else the one group whose name it is, unless a provider serves a model by that id; the user's own groups only while the `groups` plugin is on, [the plugin runtime](plugin-runtime.md#gateway-extension-points-p3-router-strategy)), auto stand-ins, the agent's own stand-in (`StandIn`: Codex's config.toml `model` when magpie is its provider — in a WSL distro, the distro's own, of the ones running now — for a name it sends that magpie doesn't serve, such as its auto-review's `codex-auto-review`), itself taken through `GroupFor` and auto stand-ins so a config naming a group bare reaches it, then `provider.Resolve`. A Codex memory call or auto-review (`x-openai-subagent: guardian`, or `codex-auto-review`) that still names no served model goes to the model Codex's last turn was answered on (`codexMemoryStandIn`, per agent and gateway key; a memory call first waits beside the turn it was started with, through `codexLeads`, see the side turns below), so a Codex on magpie through another provider table, a profile or another computer isn't answered 404 (Adam on Discord, `TestCodexAutoReviewReachesItsTurnsModel`). A bare model id several providers serve (MOMO on Discord: `glm-5.3` under opencode-go and a6api) is never refused: with found groups on (the default) `GroupFor` takes it to the group magpie found for it (`auto-<slug>`), its members in the Providers order, routed and failed over as any group, a conversation staying with the member that answered it while its cache is worth it (affinity); with them off, `Resolve` takes the first provider in the Providers order that exposes it, with no fallback. A provider the user keeps out of found groups (Routing's "Leave out a provider", `provider.SetAutoGroupSkip`, `autoGroupSkip` in providers.json; chenxiang0010 on Discord) is in none of them, so a model one other provider serves is no group; the groups the user made or changed keep it. A request still naming a found group that took away goes to its model from a provider not kept out (`AutoStandIn`), and an agent set to it is moved there (`agent.Reseat`). magpie writes only `provider/model` ids into agents' configs (`magpieModels`), so a bare id is one the user wrote. `/v1/models` lists bare ids only when the user asks for them ([Model ids without the provider](#model-ids-without-the-provider)). Every reply says who answered in `X-Magpie-Provider` and `X-Magpie-Model` (`noteMember`), and the usage log and the Routing page keep it. A model id with `~` and no slash that resolves as nothing else is the one served id (a provider's model or a group) whose slashes it spells as `~` (`unflat`, `provider.Unflat`; MiniMax Code's spelling, #1387), taken back in `validateModel` before key limits, middleware and usage see it; two ids spelled alike are neither. A Claude model id whose version is dashed (`relay/claude-opus-4-6-us`, with or without `:level` and `[1m]`) that is not served as spelled is the one served id it dashes (`provider.ClaudeUndashed`; the spelling magpie gives Claude Code, which reads a dotted version as Claude Opus 4), taken back in `validateModel` and `unprefixed` the same way. An unknown model gets a 404 naming why: a switched-off provider, a disabled group or an empty group. Otherwise it names up to three ids nearest to what was asked (`provider.Closest`: words of a model's display name count before words of its id, and a provider's id or name only alongside them, so "Zen-Space-Bunny" is told `opencode-zen-free/space-bunny-free`, MOMO on Discord), then the ways a model can be named, then every id (`unknownModel`; chat and embeddings). | [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go), [`internal/provider/group.go`](../../internal/provider/group.go), [`internal/provider/flat.go`](../../internal/provider/flat.go), [`internal/provider/claudedash.go`](../../internal/provider/claudedash.go) |
| A found group's two ids | A group magpie found is listed (`/v1/models`, the catalog, the lists written into agents, the pickers, the CLI and TUI) as `auto/<model>`: the model's id as a vendor spells it, dots kept and with no vendor prefix (`auto/claude-opus-5.5`; `provider.Group.Ref`, `spelledIn`), so a client that looks the part after the slash up on models.dev finds it (ZeroClover on X). The spelling is the one models.dev gives the model most (`catalog.Spelling`, its `normal` index: `glm-5.3-flash` whether the members serve `glm-5-3-flash`, Ark's dated id or `glm-5.3-flash`), so it no longer follows the order the providers were added in; for a model models.dev doesn't know, the first member's spelling it lists, else the first member's. The group's name comes from the same choice (`autoGroups`): the name models.dev gives that spelling (`catalog.NameOf`), else a vendor's own name for it, else the spelling itself; they were picked apart, a name of `glm-5-3-flash` beside `auto/glm-5.3-flash` (XYenon on Discord, `TestFoundGroupIDAndNameAreOneSpelling`). A found group the user changed keeps the name they saved. Every spelling it was listed by before is still the group (`CanonicalRef`), and `PublicRef` takes one to the spelling now. Its old id `group/auto-<slug>` is served forever. Inside magpie the group keeps that one id: `validateModel` takes `auto/<model>` (with `:level` or `[1m]`) back to it with `provider.CanonicalRef` before key limits, routing, affinity and the usage log see the request, and the request body is rewritten to it, so both ids count as one group. A gateway key naming either id, `group/*` or `auto/*` passes for both (`allows` in `key_models.go`). `SaveGroup` stores a member, rule or classifier picked as `auto/<model>` by the old id. An agent field magpie set to `group/auto-<slug>`, or to an `auto/<model>` spelt otherwise than the group is listed now, moves to `auto/<model>` on the next `SyncCatalog` (`agent.renameFoundGroups`, `TestFoundGroupMovedOffAnOldSpelling`), but only while its value is still what applied.json says magpie wrote; a value the user set or changed stays, and is still served. A pick of the group by its old id written now (`Agent.Apply`) is written as `auto/<model>`. An agent's hidden, picked and ordered models saved by the old id hold for the new one (`PublicRef` in `visible.go`), and a bare model id still reaches its found group (`bareTarget`). Claude Code is given a dotted Claude version dashed, so it may send `auto/claude-sonnet-4-5`: that is the same group, and `Reseat` still moves an agent off it once the group is gone (`samePick`). `auto` is no provider id: `Save` and `Rename` refuse it for a new provider, and a plugin's `auto` is `auto-plugin` (`PluginID`). A provider of the user's saved as `auto` before keeps its models: while it exists, `auto/<x>` is its model and found groups list as `group/auto-<slug>` (`autoRefsOn`). Hand-made groups stay `group/<id>`. | [`internal/provider/autoref.go`](../../internal/provider/autoref.go), [`key_models.go`](../../internal/gateway/key_models.go), [`internal/agent/autoref.go`](../../internal/agent/autoref.go) |
| A model's own id at a provider | A model the user lists by an id of theirs at a provider (a signed-in account's and a plugin's too; settings' `ModelIDs`, `magpie model id <provider/model> <id>`, the editor's "Listed as"; [Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md#runtime-path)) is that id everywhere the gateway names it: `/v1/models` and the lists written into agents (`Available`), `Resolve`, the routing groups and the group magpie finds for it (`auto/<id>`, with every other provider serving that id), a gateway key's models, `X-Magpie-Model` and the usage log's `model`. Only the upstream request differs: every place that sends a wire name reads `settings.Wires()`, which is `ModelWires` with each `ModelIDs` entry turned round (`"<pid>/<id>"` → the vendor's own id, wrapped by a `<pid>/*` wire), so the body, a Vertex path and the usage log's endpoint carry the vendor's id; an explicit `ModelWires` entry for that id wins. The vendor's reply names its own id, which the usage log, recent calls and the Routing page keep as `served`; the agent gets the id it asked for back in the reply, in every protocol, streamed or whole (`memberWriter` in [`member.go`](../../internal/gateway/member.go), `replyNamingOf`: the reply's `model`, Responses' `response.model`, Anthropic's `message.model`, Gemini's `modelVersion`; issennknight on Discord, whose agent warned that `deepseek-v4.1-flash` came back as `deepseek-v4.1-flash-1017`). The same holds for a group or a `provider/model` id the vendor knows by another name. A reply whose vendor sent the wire name as asked keeps the vendor's name (a dated `-20261017` included). Settings' `replyModel` (Settings › Models › Replies › Model name in replies) picks for every request: `""` the model asked for, `member` the group's member, `vendor` the vendor's own name, as before the asked-for name, each for every agent, Claude Code, Claude Desktop and Codex included (ZoeCats on Discord: in v0.1.2760 `member` still gave those three the model asked for; `TestPluginReplyNamesMember` holds it on a plugin too). A file from before it with `memberModel` on reads as `member`, and `memberModel` is written beside `replyModel` for an older magpie (`normal` in settings.go; `Merge` in settingsapi takes an older page's `memberModel` only when its switch moved). A request's `X-Magpie-Response-Model` (`asked`, `member` or `vendor`) overrides the setting for that request, for every agent (`TestReplyNamesAskedModel`, `TestGeminiReplyNamesAskedModel`, `TestReplyNamesMember`, `TestReplyModelFoldsMemberModel`, `TestReplyModelSaves`). A request still naming `provider/<own id>` (an agent's file magpie didn't rewrite) resolves and goes out as it is. A key held to either id passes for both (`modelAllowed` checks the ref, then `OwnModelID`, then `ListedModelID`); a group member passes the same way. An id is given to a model of the provider's chat list or its picks; the image, video and embeddings routes send no wire name, so they are not served under one (`TestLocalModelIDOnEveryRoute`). At an account or a plugin the account's own say of the model is read by the vendor's id: a plugin's API and fetch, Claude Code's `--model`, Antigravity's variant, and which accounts list it and at what levels (`TestLocalModelIDAtAPlugin`, `TestLocalModelIDAtAClaudeAccount`, `TestLocalModelIDAtAnAntigravityAccount`). | [`internal/provider/localid.go`](../../internal/provider/localid.go), [`internal/settings/settings.go`](../../internal/settings/settings.go), [`key_models.go`](../../internal/gateway/key_models.go) |
| Candidates | `plan` lists one provider's candidates: one per key or account on, then each `Fallback` model, with resting ones last (`restLast`). A fallback the user unchecked in its own provider's model list is skipped, as a group's member is (`provider.ResolveRoute`, #3911; `fallback_unpicked_test.go`), and so is it where fallbacks are read for careful accounts, native compaction, redaction and Codex's own turns. A fallback can be a routing group (`group/<id>`, 01huadalang on Discord): `planPaused` hands its members to `planLevel` as a request to the group would (`planFallbackGroup`), in the group's own routing and sink, its pause rules applied in `serve`, and its trace rows marked `Fallback` with the group in `Via`. Its turn rules (`ruleFor`, which may ask a classifier) are not applied: the plan is made before the primary is tried, and a classifier call on every request the primary answers costs too much. Each provider/model is tried once a round: a member that is the primary, an earlier fallback or another fallback group's is left out where it came second, so a group holding this provider is no loop (groups have no fallbacks of their own, and a member's are not followed). A group that is gone is passed over. Every place that reads fallbacks (careful accounts, native compaction, redaction, Codex's own turns, Claude's review) reads a group's members through `provider.FallbackModels`. `fallback_group_test.go`. `planGroup` and `planLevel` weigh a group's members together. A nested group plans by its own routing (#576); one left on the default (Smart) in a group that pools (Smart, In turn, Least used, Use before expiry, Free first, Speed first, a plugin's) takes the outer group's, as a provider left on the default does (`provider.Group.Within`, set on `Member.Via`, so the trace's `Subs` and the Routing page read the routing that applies, #3847). In a group In order, By weight or Manual it stays Smart. `TestFreeFirstReachesAGroupInItLeftOnTheDefault`. So does a member whose provider routes other than Smart (`ownRouting`): weighed as one by its first not resting (`firstAwake`), tried whole in its own order; `leastHeld` leaves such a group's order alone too. A provider on Smart, or routed as the group is, is pooled with the group's other keys and accounts. `group_own_routing_test.go`. A member's own fallbacks are not the group's. | [`fallback.go`](../../internal/gateway/fallback.go) |
| Order of keys and accounts | `Routing`: smart (""), `order`, `rotate`, `usage` (least used, with an `usageHalfLife` of 1h), `pace`, `weight`, `free` (free first), `fast` (speed first), `drain` (until used up) or, a group's only, `cheap` (cheapest first) or `expiring` (free quota by expiry). `weighRouted` reads each candidate into a `routes.Seat` and asks the strategy serving its routing (`routes.Find`, the router strategy point of [the plugin runtime](plugin-runtime.md#gateway-extension-points-p3-router-strategy)) for the order: Smart is `routes.Smart`, the core's; the others are `routes.Builtin`, registered by the built-in plugin `rotation`. With it off a routing no strategy serves is tried in its listed order, and `routingOf` treats it as `order` wherever the core asks about a routing (a turn's mark, `goesRound`, `sinks`, a rotate group's sessions, a group's heads). Smart, `usage` and `pace` weigh a subscription by its allowance (`allowances`), and a key by its own usage windows when magpie reads them (`keyAllowance`: a sub2api key given a 5-hour, day or 7-day limit, or a key bought as a plan, such as Kimi Code's or a GLM Coding Plan's); any other key has none. Among accounts a plugin reads as prepaid wallets (a window's `balance`, #3950), Smart, `usage` and `pace` put the one with more left first where they would otherwise tie, only within one provider and unit (`routes.Seat.Left`/`LeftIn`, see [provider plugins](provider-plugins.md#a-wallets-balance)): each kind is ordered in the places its own seats take, so a group weighing several providers' accounts as one pool orders each provider's wallets too (#4095, `TestGroupWalletsGoByWhatTheyHaveLeft`). `free` weighs by what a model costs there and nothing else: only a strategy with `routes.Strategy.ByFree` set (`routes.FreeFirst` is the one) has `weighRouted` fill `routes.Seat.Free`, from `provider.FreeModel` — the user's word that the whole provider costs them nothing (`Provider.CountFree`: the editor's Free for me, `magpie provider set <id> free=yes`; jiabubu on Discord, ModelScope's free daily quota), else the vendor's own `catalog.Model.Free` word, else a list price of $0 on every count — so a pool routed any other way never reads a model list for it, and one routed `free` spends the members whose model costs nothing before those that spend the allowance or credits, each tier in its listed order (nothing free is left behind a paid one, and a pool with no free model behaves as `order`). A group routed `free` weighs its members whole, each over its own accounts or keys as its provider routes them. Whatever a group's routing, the Routing page tags a member `provider.FreeModel` counts free "free", on the group's card and beside its provider in the editor (`groupsState`'s `memberInfo[].free`, jiabubu on Discord, `TestGroupMemberSaysFree`, `group-member-free.test.cjs`). `fast` weighs by speed lately and nothing else: only a strategy with `routes.Strategy.BySpeed` set (`routes.Fastest`) has `weighRouted` fill `routes.Seat.TTFT` and `TPS`, from `speedOf` ([`speed.go`](../../internal/gateway/speed.go)): per `restID` (account or key, and model), a decayed average (half-life 20m) of a streamed answer's ms to its first content and of its output tokens a second as `usage.DecodeOf` counts them, written once as an answer ends (`noteTrySpeed`, beside `servedCandidate`) and read with one map lookup per candidate, never from the usage history; past an hour since its last answer a seat counts as not measured, the records are capped at 4096, and a restart forgets them. `routes.Fastest` ranks the awake by their first token plus 200 tokens at their pace (a seat with no pace at the measured seats' middle pace), keeps seats within a tenth of their band's fastest in listed order, and of a pool's requests with seats both measured and not, gives every fifth to one not measured (round the unmeasured, `probes`, so one is never starved); resting seats go after the awake, used-up ones (`UsedShare`) after those, and rests and failover are as for any routing. The trace's rows carry `lateTtft`/`lateTps` for the Routing page's story ("answered fastest lately — first token in 0.8s"). A non-streamed reply tells nothing (no first token), so a pool that never streams stays in listed order bar the probes. `cheap` (a group's only: `provider.normalize` drops it from a provider, whose keys and accounts all bill one price, and the CLI refuses it there) weighs by price now and nothing else: only a strategy with `routes.Strategy.ByPrice` set (`routes.Cheapest`) has `weighRouted` fill `routes.Seat.Priced`, `Cost` and `CacheCost`, from `provider.Provider.PriceAt` at `priceClock()` — the price Usage would count a call made then (the user's `ModelPrices`, else list or maker price, at the price rate and `RateAt`'s window, a `FreeModel` at 0), doubled in its vendor's peak hours by `catalog.Price.In` (`PeakOf`: DeepSeek's API, Command Code's and OpenCode Go's DeepSeek models, each at DeepSeek's hours; Ollama Cloud's deepseek-v4.1-flash and deepseek-v4-pro, dated tags too, at its own 12:00–18:00 UTC weekdays, `catalog.PeakOllama`). `routes.Cheapest` sorts stably by a million in plus a million out, a tie (within a millionth) by cache read, and unpriced seats after every priced one in listed order; rests and failover are as for any routing. The trace's rows carry `price` (`input`, `output`, `cacheRead`, `peak`) for the Routing page's story ("costs least on Ollama now — $0.150 in, $0.600 out a million tokens"). `TestCheapestFirstGroupFollowsPeakHours` holds the clock at a peak and an off-peak hour, and `TestCheapestFirstGroupFollowsOllamaOffPeak` at each edge of Ollama Cloud's hours, a Saturday, and in far zones. A group routed `expiring` (`provider.FreeExpiring`, `routes.FreeExpiring`; wowbbs on Discord, five check-in accounts of WorkBuddy, MiniMax, Trae and Qoder in one group) spends free quota before paid, the soonest lost first. Its strategy sets `ByFree` and `ByPlan`, so `weighRouted` fills `Seat.Free` (the model is free there, as for `free`) and `Seat.FreePlan` (`provider.FreePlan` of the account's `Plan`: free, trial, 免费, 试用, 体验, 體驗). It orders three tiers: the free ones with quota left, by `routes.Expires` (the earliest of a window's renewal and a credit's `RunsOut`, `Lapse` included) truncated to the hour, a stable tie in listed order and an unknown expiry after every known one; then the paid ones as `Smart` orders them among themselves; then the used up and resting, by when they come back. A paid account takes a request only when no free one has quota left, and failover walks the same tiers. The trace's rows carry `free` and `expires` for the Routing page's story ("its free quota is lost soonest, in 1d 2h"). It is a group's only: `provider.normalize` keeps it off a provider, and `magpie provider set … routing=expiring` is refused like `cheap`. Nothing changes for a group not routed `expiring`. `TestGroupSpendsFreeQuotaSoonestToExpireFirst` (gateway), `TestFreeExpiringSpendsSoonestFreeFirst` (routes) and routing-free-expiring.test.cjs hold it. A plugin whose usage doesn't say when free credits lapse (Trae, Qoder's add-on pools) is ordered after the ones that do. A group routed `weight` (#1910) takes its members, not their keys, by smooth weighted round robin: `planLevel` makes each model member (its keys ordered by its own provider's routing) and each nested group one unit, and `weightedUnits` puts the round's pick (`routes.Round` over `group/<id>`, keyed by the member's spelling, `Group.Weights`, 1 if unset) first and the rest in member order for failover; a unit whose head rests leaves the round, while one whose accounts are only near their limit (Smart's 90% band, not resting) keeps its share, the band ordering only its own accounts (`TestWeightedGroupGivesALowGroupItsShare`; Creat.wu on Discord read the Routing page's "kept for when the others can't" as the member passed over, so the page now tells a member behind another by a group's weights or order as such, `memberSplit` in the routing plugin's `client.js`), and a kept conversation moves the round's charge to the member that kept it (`wrrSeat`, `routes.MoveRound`). `weight` on a provider spreads its keys by each key's `Weight` and a subscription's accounts by each account's (`AccountWeights`, rubbzha on X): `keyWeight` gives a seat its `Weight`, 1 when none is set, and `routes.Weighted` takes them by smooth weighted round robin, a resting one leaving the round and a kept conversation staying as Stays says (`TestWeightedKeys`, `TestAccountWeightSharesRequests`). A lone key or account is weighed against nobody, but what it has left is still read into the trace (`weighRouted`), so the Routing page shows it as it does for several (#1016); all but used up, it keeps its conversation rather than being said to move (`affine`). `drain` (dzxx0496 on Discord: free accounts one after another, the first not taking the requests back once it renews) stays on one seat per pool (`routes.Drain`, which remembers the seat's `Key` per pool) until it is used up — a known window at 100% (`provider.SpentShareOf`, as In order) or resting out of quota or credit (`routes.Seat.Spent`, filled by `spentRest` from `restingOf`'s `failQuota`/`failCredit`) — then goes on to the next not used up in listed order and stays there, wrapping round; the others follow it in listed order for failover. A rate limit or a failure only rests the seat, tried last meanwhile, and the pool comes back to it; with every seat used up it stays put, and a seat that leaves the pool leaves it on the first not used up. A group routed `drain` pools its members' accounts and keys under `group/<id>`, as Smart does. A restart forgets where each pool was, and it starts again at the first not used up (`TestDrainKeepsTheOneThatTookOver`). A failure rests a candidate (`failure`, `failureOf`). | [`routing.go`](../../internal/gateway/routing.go), [`weighted.go`](../../internal/gateway/weighted.go), [`internal/routes/`](../../internal/routes), [`internal/provider/routing.go`](../../internal/provider/routing.go) |
| Limits | Each key or account (a candidate's `who()`) has at most `MaxConcurrency` requests out at once (its own `AccountConcurrency` over it); one more waits in its lane's queue, bounded by `QueueLimit` and `QueueWait` and turned away with a 429 past either (`lanes.take`). One with no limit still counts its requests out in a lane of `Limit` 0, which never waits, so `Lanes` (`/v1/magpie/concurrency`, the GUI's `/api/lanes`) says how many run there too and the Providers page's pill shows it (chentuoyan on Discord, `TestLanesCountWithNoLimit`); a limit set while they are out counts them. `MaxRPM` (coeo91 on Discord: OpenRouter's free models take 20 a minute) bounds how many requests it sends the vendor in any rolling 60 seconds (`rpms`), counting each for a second more (`rpmSlack`), since the vendor counts a request when it arrives and the first on a new connection arrives later by its dial: one more waits for room in the minute, in the order they came, after it has its slot and right before it is sent. A wait that would be longer than 2 minutes (or the provider's `QueueWait`, when shorter: `rpmWait`) is turned away at once with a 429 whose `Retry-After` is when the minute has room; nothing rests for it. An agent gone while it waits gives its place back. Every request that reaches the vendor counts: each try of the candidate loop (a retry, the next key, a fallback) waits in `serve`, and the requests a try makes on the way and the gateway's own asks (count_tokens, System One's decisions, embeddings, drawing, Kimi's search) are counted by `rpmTransport` on `s.client` (`metered`, `paidFor`). count_tokens never waits: a candidate with no room is skipped, and the local estimate answers. A full lane or a minute with no room gives way to a ready key or account of the provider or the group (`laneMate`, `ready`) that isn't resting: its lane is free only because nothing is sent there. A request already waiting in a lane doesn't stay bound to it: whenever a slot is given back in any lane or a rest is lifted (`lanes.freed`, signalled by the releaser, `Unrest` and `UnrestAll`), and when the soonest rest among the others runs out (at most every `laneRecheck`), it goes to the first key or account after it in the planned order that has a free slot with no queue, minute room, isn't resting and isn't a reserve now (`waitMate`, `keepAtOnce` re-derived over the provider's candidates, `errMoved`). So one that took a seat while those before it rested, then became a reserve once they were back, doesn't keep requests waiting behind its own long turns (01huadalang on Discord: three ZCode accounts at three at once, the first two back from a 405 while the waiting ones stayed on the third until its requests were cut by hand; `TestWaitingRequestGoesToAccountBackFromRest` under Smart and In turn, with Stays on and off, `TestWaitingRequestTakesSlotGivenBackElsewhere` under every routing). Strict stays leaves no other candidate, so the request waits for its own. A fallback is never asked for want of a slot or a minute (`TestFullMemberGivesWay`). A plugin's account sends through its own transport, so only its tries are counted, not a plugin's own extra requests. Set in the provider editor (Concurrency, Requests per minute, Queue size, Queue wait) or with `magpie provider account-concurrency`, `queue` and `rpm`; a subscription's are kept with its picks, and every account of it keeps them, those on beside the first as well (`withPicks`, called by `All` and `AlsoOn`; #1899: a second WorkBuddy AI account had none, and queued 62s with the wait set to 1s). | [`concurrency.go`](../../internal/gateway/concurrency.go), [`rpm.go`](../../internal/gateway/rpm.go), [`internal/provider/concurrency.go`](../../internal/provider/concurrency.go) |
| Accounts at once | A provider's `MaxAccounts` (01huadalang on Discord: three ZCode accounts used at once got the exit address refused with a 405, two didn't) is how many of its keys or accounts take requests at the same time: `provider.AtOnce()` is the user's own value, else what its plugin declares (`maxAccounts` on the auth hook or in package.json's `magpie`, read by host.js `atOnceOf`; zcode says 2), else no limit; the user's 0 is no limit over the plugin's. `perKeyBarred` keeps the first N in the user's own order (`rank`, so a dragged list counts), preferring ones not resting, and hands the rest to `keepAtOnce`'s reserves: they go after everything else as asides with `candidate.reserve` set (`Weighed.Reserve`), so whatever the routing mode, a reserve is asked only once the kept ones failed or rest, and a kept one that rests gives its seat to the first reserve on the next request. Three guards keep a reserve from being reached for anything else: `laneMate` never spills a full lane onto one, a full queue (`laneErr`) drops the provider's reserves before passing on (`dropReserves`), so the request is turned away with the 429 instead, and session affinity keeps no conversation on a reserve once the kept ones can take it (`affine`'s "reserve"; Strict still holds). Leak known: a failure that rests nobody (a 5xx on one try) still passes that one request to the reserve, as it does to any next candidate. Set in the provider editor's Advanced (Accounts at once, beside Concurrency) or with `magpie provider at-once`; Routing's row says "in reserve · {n} at once" (`TestAtOnceKeepsFirstInUserOrder`, `TestAtOnceFullLaneWaitsForTheKept`, `TestAtOnceReserveTakesOverAndGivesBack`, `provider-at-once.test.cjs`). | [`fallback.go`](../../internal/gateway/fallback.go), [`concurrency.go`](../../internal/gateway/concurrency.go), [`internal/provider/concurrency.go`](../../internal/provider/concurrency.go) |
| Sink | With `Sink` on, a key or account that answers 429 while it still has quota goes to the back, behind every one not rate limited since. Held in memory until restart, or until its rest is lifted by hand (Try now, `Unrest` calls `unsink`, `UnrestAll` `unsinkAll` for the key and each of its models), which puts it back in its place (`TestTryNowPutsASunkAccountBack`, `TestUnrestAllUnsinksTheModels`). Another magpie's model rate limited there, whose rest is the model's alone (`remoteModelRest`), sinks alone too (`restID`), and `sunkAt` reads a candidate's model before its key, so the remote's other models keep their place (#2167, ggbdpq; `TestRemoteMagpieRateLimitSinksTheModel`, `TestMagpieAddedByHandRateLimitSinksTheModel`). A group in a group sinks by its own Sink, within its own order (`sinkOwn`, #2255), and the group asked for then sinks the whole by its own; `TestNestedGroupSinksItsOwnOrder`. | [`sink.go`](../../internal/gateway/sink.go) |
| Image generation | `/v1/images/generations` and `/v1/images/edits` are translated to the selected model's image API. OpenRouter image-only models use its `/images` endpoint and send reference images as `input_references`; OpenRouter models that return images through chat stay on Chat Completions. Volcengine Ark (`isArk`: an `ark.*.volces.com` base, the preset's plans or one added by hand) is asked at `/images/generations` only, as its doc (https://www.volcengine.com/docs/82379/1541523) takes it (`drawArk`): `watermark: false`, since it marks every image otherwise; reference images as JSON `image` (a URL or data URL, an array for several), never a multipart edit; no `n`, so one request per image (`eachDrawing`); no ratio as `size`, so a ratio its doc lists is sent as that ratio's 2K size and any other is a 400 that says to put it in the prompt; no mask, no quality (JL on Discord, `TestArkDrawsWithoutWatermarkOncePerImage`). DashScope (`dashScope`: Qwen's, Bailian's and Qwen Cloud's presets, or one of their hosts) draws its Qwen-Image and Wan 2.7 image models (`dashScopeDraws`) on its own API, `<host>/api/v1/services/aigc/multimodal-generation/generation` (`drawDashScope`), as docs.qwencloud.com gives it: the prompt as the user message's `text` part after an edit's images as `image` parts (a URL or data URL), `n`, `size` as `W*H` (a ratio is a 400 that says to give WxH), `watermark: false`, no mask; the images are read from every `output.choices[].message.content[].image` (`readDashScopeAnswer`), and a refusal says its `message` and `code`. A Token Plan's host serves no OpenAI images API, so the drawing went on to chat completions and "drew nothing" (judi0713 on Discord, `TestDashScopeDrawsOnItsOwnAPI`). A routing group named for a drawing or a video (`drawingGroup`: `group/<id>`, or a bare id the group goes by that resolved to one of its members) is tried member by member in the order its routing plans for a chat (`drawingTries` over `planGroup`), each member over its own keys or accounts (`drawOnAccounts`, `startVideo`); the next member is asked while one fails as a chat's would move on (`retryable`: its vendor 5xx, out of quota, the model not served), never for a refusal of the request itself, and the call's `Fallback` names those tried first (#2005, tianxia3111: a group of two relays answered the first one's 502). A group with chat models beside its image or video ones is tried on those alone (`drawingTries`' `makes`: `drawsModel`, or `servesKind` for video), so a drawing never goes to a chat member, on its provider's images API or as a chat asked for an image (changqiu4890 on Discord: a group of three relay chat models, a DeepSeek one and the relay's gpt-image-2.5 drew on its first chat model; `TestMixedGroupDrawsOnItsImageMemberOnly`, `TestMixedGroupMakesVideoOnItsVideoMemberOnly`). Codex's image tool draws on the routing group its turn asked for when that group has an image member (`codexDrawingProvider` gives the turn's group, `GroupDraws`, then `resolveDrawing`), and only otherwise on the provider that served the turn or Settings' pick (`TestCodexDrawingTakesItsGroupsImageMember`). Pause rules and a gateway key's group hold apply as for a chat. A failed drawing rests no member and no key. An account out of quota for the image model (`failure` reads `failQuota`) rests that model on it alone (`restDrawer`, keyed by `restID`), until the reset its refusal names (`resetsAt`: Antigravity's "Your quota will reset after 3h19m30s.") or `quotaRest`, so the next drawing asks the accounts that still have it first (`restLast`), and the account's text models stay in. Antigravity also tells a model's quota used up as a 200 whose stream is the error; `drawCodeAssist` gives such an error the status its words stand for (`saidStatus`: quota or rate 429, credit 402, else 502), as the images API's 200 with no image does, so a spent account hands the drawing to the next one as its 429 does (01huadalang on Discord: a 502 ended the drawing on the spent account; `TestAntigravityDrawingOutOfQuotaInsideTheReplyGoesOn`, `TestAntigravityDrawingQuotaRestsTheImageModel`). `TestImageGroupMovesOnToTheNextMember`, `TestVideoGroupMovesOnToTheNextMember`. Such a group may be Settings → Images → Image generation's pick (7up6666 on X): the list offers each routing group with a member among its provider's `Drawers` (`GroupDraws`), so a request naming no model, the Magpie Image MCP's included, draws on the group; a picked group none of whose members draws any more is `DrawerMissing` (`drawerMissing`), the row says so and Automatic draws in its place. `TestImageGroupPickedInSettingsDraws`, `TestImageGenOffersDrawingGroups`. A group's Add a model picker offers each provider's image models, `/api/groups`' `drawers` (`gateway.Drawers` less hidden ones, apart from the chat `models`), said as such and with no reasoning level (jzw690 on X); such a member draws whether or not its provider's picks name it, as picks are of chat models (`provider.FindDrawingGroup`, used by `GroupDraws`, `resolveDrawing` and the image group's turns), and the group is no model agents are offered. A group named on `/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/rerank`, `/v1/audio/speech`, `/v1/audio/transcriptions` (and translations) or `/v1/videos` keeps the same way a member its provider's chat list doesn't have (`mediaMembers`: `provider.FindGroupAlso` with `offChatList`), and is tried from its first such member (`mediaGroupFirst`), so a hand-made group of embedding or speech models of providers with picks is served, not a 404 (XYenon on Discord); a chat model the user unticked stays out. `TestMediaGroupOfProvidersWithChatPicks`, `TestVideoGroupOfProvidersWithChatPicks`. The same models need no group made by hand (#5339): a fetched list keeps its models made for another API, each marked with it (`catalog.Model.Serves`, from the id: `catalog.ServesOf`; `LiveServing`), out of the chat models (`catalog.Chat`), so agents' lists, gateway keys and Routing's chat pickers never offer them, and a moderation model is still dropped. A model of one kind that more than one provider serves under exactly the same id (no other case, no other version) is a group of that kind made per request on its route (`provider.KindGroup`, `mediaMembers` and `servesKind` in [`media_group.go`](../../internal/gateway/media_group.go)): asked by the bare id there, or by `group/auto-<kind>:<id>` (`KindGroupID`; a chat model's found group's id has no `:`), never on another kind's route, its members in the Providers order, none switched off and none the model is disabled on. It is in no list of groups (`Groups`), so no agent, key or picker offers it. Video is the kind `video` (`Videomakers`); images keep their own groups. A list saved before this is fetched again once a run, schedule or none (`refreshDueLists`, `catalog.LiveKinded`). Another magpie's list (`X-Magpie-Drawers`) is not marked; its models are found by their id (`ServesOf`). `TestKindGroupsServeEachAPIByRoute`, `TestVideoKindGroupByTheModelsID`, `TestKindGroupHoldsTheExactModel`, `TestKindlessListFetchedOnce`, `TestFetchKeepsModelsOfOtherAPIs`. `TestImageGroupOfUnpickedImageModelsDraws`, `TestGroupEditorOffersImageModels`, `group-pick-drawers.test.cjs`. An account's own list of models (#474) never bars a ChatGPT account's GPT Image, a Grok subscription's Grok Imagine or a WorkBuddy plan's image models (`listsCantName`): the list is picked from the editor's chat models, which they are never among, so a list kept for some chat models had every drawing refused as "set not to be served by any account" (heyiwei5 on X). A key's list, picked from models that include its image ones, still bars them. `TestCodexDrawsPastAccountModelLists`. A drawing is given Settings → Images → Image generation timeout (`imageTimeout`, 1 to 60 minutes, 5 when unset; neighbads on X: a slow image endpoint needed more) as its context's deadline (`drawTimeout`, `drawWithin`), and so is downloading an image answered as a link (`imageLongest`, never under `ImageLongest`). A drawing's request goes through a transport with no `ResponseHeaderTimeout` (`drawsApart`), since an images API sends its headers only once it has drawn, and every other request keeps `upstreamHeaderWait` (10 minutes). A video's submit, status and download calls keep 5 minutes each (`videoTimeout`). The `magpie mcp image` process, which may not read these settings (WSL), waits on a call up to `callLongest` (two hours and a minute), so it never cuts one the gateway still waits on. `TestLongImageTimeoutLetsASlowVendorFinish`, `TestImageTimeoutSettingReachesTheVendor`, `TestOnlyDrawingsWaitPastTheHeaderWait`. | [`draw.go`](../../internal/gateway/draw.go), [`draw_volcengine.go`](../../internal/gateway/draw_volcengine.go), [`draw_dashscope.go`](../../internal/gateway/draw_dashscope.go), [`video.go`](../../internal/gateway/video.go), [`catalog.go`](../../internal/catalog/catalog.go) |
| Affinity | A conversation stays with the key or account that answered it, so the vendor's prompt cache is read again. Modes: auto (""), `session`, `turn`, `off`, `strict`. Stored in `affinity.json` next to `providers.json`. A conversation is kept by its id (`affinityID`, in `stickKey` and `ruleKey`): the agent's session header, else the caller's `X-Magpie-Session` (#5242: requests that each named a session of their own, and opened alike, were taken for one conversation and kept together; `TestMagpieSessionIsTheConversation`), else (`conversationID`, which is what Kilo and a plugin are sent as the conversation, so `X-Magpie-Session` never goes upstream) a hash of how the conversation opens, which every later turn repeats: its first user message, or, when the agent wrote that one alone (`userWords`: an `<environment_context>` block, Reasonix's `<session-context …>`, an instruction file), the user messages from it through the first that holds the user's own words, an image, a file or a recording (`conversationOpening`, `userOpens`). A system or developer message between them is passed over; a reply, a call or a tool's result ends the opening, and with none of the user's before it the first message is hashed alone. One message is hashed as the request has it; several are hashed without their cache marks and with a content of one text part read as that text (`writeOpening`), since an agent moves its mark on to each turn's newest message, some spelling a marked message as one part and an unmarked one as a string (`TestCacheMarkMovingOnKeepsTheID`). Before, the first message was always hashed alone, and every conversation an agent opened on the same message in one folder was one: kept under `session` on the account that answered the first, and never moved on by rotate (keeyangyy, #3938, `TestConversationsOnOneEnvironmentMessageAreApart`, `TestReasonixConversationsInOneWorkspaceAreApart`, `TestNewConversationsInOneFolderAreRoutedApart`). A conversation whose first user message is the user's own keeps the id it had, which the Context page and the Routing page's requests find dsh's sessions by (`dshConvs`, `convOwners`, `convSessions`): dsh's pi-ai routes send no session header. A Claude Code subagent's is kept apart from its lead's by `x-claude-code-agent-id` (`stickKey`): Claude Code sends the session's id with its subagents' requests too, and a subagent's answer took the lead's stick over, so the lead's next turn was judged by the subagent's cache and moved to another account, where its whole conversation was written again (slkassinthehood on X, `TestClaudeConversationKeepsItsAccountsCache`). Behind a remote magpie, the key or account is the remote's key and the provider there that serves the model, its id's first part (`remoteUpstream`, `candidate.vendor`, `sameVendor`): the remote is one key here, but each of its providers keeps its own cache and seals, so a conversation is kept, held strictly, told its seals were refused and given its native compaction's reader by that pair, as the same group on the remote does (fremontdango on Discord: a group of one model from two of the remote's providers kept a conversation on the second as the same account when the first was gone, and a strict one failed over to it; `TestRemoteMagpieStrictKeepsTheRemotesProvider`, `TestRemoteMagpieOtherProviderIsNotTheSameAccount`). A remote's group is one vendor by its whole id, its own routing deciding inside it; a model named without a provider is the key alone. Lanes and requests a minute still go by the key here, the limits the user set on it. | [`affinity.go`](../../internal/gateway/affinity.go) |
| One try | `attempt` sends one candidate the request. It goes through the proxy of the account or key it uses, else the provider's (`p.Via`, `ProxyChoice`: `AccountProxies` holds an account's by its name, a key's by its `KeyID`). A request magpie makes for one provider while serving another's (a web search, an image description, a classifier call) takes its own provider's proxy or the global one, never the outer one: `netproxy.With` replaces a choice the context already names, "" too, and `webSearch` starts from none, so the search APIs follow the global proxy (#1522). A provider of the user's set not to check its certificate (`InsecureTLS`, #2383) has it skipped for its own base URLs' hosts alone, through `p.Via` ([Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md)). Every request to one vendor's host shares one HTTP/2 connection (`upstreamTransport`); one with no frame for 20s is pinged, and dropped when no answer comes within 10s (`netproxy.HTTP2Health`, on `http.DefaultTransport` too), so a connection gone silent without closing (a proxy's way out died, a NAT forgot the flow) fails the requests on it, which move on as a 502 does, and the next request dials a new one. A live vendor answers a ping whatever its streams are doing, so a long wait for a first byte is not cut by it (#2843: a group's requests hung with no first byte until a restart; `TestGroupMovesOnFromSilentHTTP2Connection`). A large body (64KB or more, or of no stated length) is pinged too, as a silent connection takes a body that fits its send buffer, but its ping's answer is queued behind the body's own DATA, so on a slow uplink a working connection loses it (#3909: a ~680KB turn on a 2-10KB/s uplink failed every try). Such a body whose connection was dropped for a lost ping is sent once more, through `GetBody`, on a new connection that is not pinged and is given up on when a write moves no bytes for a minute (`uploadTransport`); for 30 minutes after, large bodies go there at once (`drawsApart.slow`; `TestUploadStillUnderwayIsNotCutByHealthPing`, `TestSlowUplinkSkipsTheLostTry`, `TestLargeBodyOnSilentConnectionIsSentAgain`). A subscription with its own client is served specially, such as Claude through the genuine Claude Code. The request is relayed as is (`passthrough`) when the model is usable on the agent's API, except that a Claude model where the provider has an Anthropic URL is translated to Messages from Chat or Responses too (`OnMessages`: OpenCode speaks Chat for every model, and a relay drops cache_control there), unless the user set the model's API or the vendor's list names its APIs. An account that only streams (`Account.Stream`: a plugin's, and most subscriptions magpie signs in to) has a request that asked for one body translated, which magpie always asks streamed, except a plugin's on Responses (`gathers`): that is relayed all the same, asked with `stream:true`, and the client given the `response` of the stream's `response.completed` (or `response.incomplete`) as one JSON body (`gathered` in [`gathered.go`](../../internal/gateway/gathered.go); its `output` filled from the stream's `response.output_item.done` items, or else its text, when that event's is empty). So the tools the vendor runs itself, and its response's `tools` and usage details, reach the client (plugins#112: a translation dropped Grok's `x_search`, and Grok answered without searching; `TestGrokPluginNonStreamKeepsHostedTools`). A stream that fails or ends before that event is a 502 (`TestGrokPluginNonStreamEndedShort`), never an empty 200. A Messages request is still translated, its stream having no event that holds the whole reply, and so is a subscription magpie signs in to itself (the ChatGPT backend's, Qoder's): its own code fits its requests. A request offering web search to a provider that doesn't search by itself is translated too, so magpie can search for it (`search.go`; a provider that searches for some of its models only, Command Code's Provider API, lists them in `searchModels` by API, and none count under its `x-cmd-zdr` header, #1590), but on the agent's own API where the provider serves it and no `ResponsesFirst`/`MessagesFirst` rule prefers another: Codex offers web_search on every turn, and each went out as Chat (#997). Otherwise it is translated (`translate`) to the first API `usable` gives: Chat, except Responses for OpenAI's models where their maker serves them (`ResponsesFirst`), Responses for every model at ModelScope, whose Chat API reports neither the tokens its cache answered nor reasoning while its Responses API reports both (`ResponsesTellsCache`, #1578; a 404 there sends the model to Chat, and only there), and Anthropic's Messages for a Claude model where the provider has an Anthropic URL (`MessagesFirst`, which keeps its cache breakpoints, #997). An API that answers the model is not served there (`wrongEndpoint`, `catalog.WrongEndpoint`: the English wordings and AgentRouter's Chinese one, #3766, read escaped too; a model's test reads them as well, see [Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md)) is remembered in `unfit` until magpie restarts. A Chat API that refuses function tools with reasoning, with no other API to take them to, is asked once more with `reasoning_effort` `none` (#176), unless `none` is known refused (`turnedAway`); `none` refused naming the levels, the tools refusal is the answer, not a retry at the lowest level (#2670). When every API has turned the model away, the API the user picked for it (`ModelAPI`) is asked again, and without a pick each API the provider has a URL for (`Speaks`), Chat first. `Provider.Native`, the API agents with a per-model API (Pi, omp, droid) are wired to ask on, follows the same order. A custom provider with a Gemini URL (#1346) has Gemini last among the APIs it speaks: a Gemini client's request is relayed there, and any other is translated to it when it has no other, or when the user set Gemini as the model's API (`SetModelAPI` takes chat, responses, anthropic or gemini, each only where the provider has its URL, from the editor's Save or `magpie model api`; a model set to Gemini has no `Native`, so those agents ask it on their own API and it is translated). Upstream it is always asked `models/{model}:streamGenerateContent?alt=sse` under the base (`upstreamPath`), the model only in the path (`forwardOnce` drops the body's), so a Gemini client that asked for one JSON body is translated, as for Factory's generate route. It goes to the first API `usable` gives, Chat where the provider has a Chat URL too, except one that asks an image's size or shape (`generationConfig.imageConfig`), which only a Gemini API takes: that one is translated on the Gemini URL (#3820, `TestGeminiImageConfigGoesToTheGeminiAPIBesideChat`). | [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go) |
| Trace | The Routing view's record of each decision, written where the decision is made: candidates, rests, which try answered. `Route.Folder` is the folder the agent says it works in (`workDir`), its last two levels only (`shortFolder`, e.g. `workspace/magpie`), never the full path; a request that says none (a title, a topic check) takes its session's as said last (`trace.sessionFolder`), one without a session id has none (#1068). It is kept in the routing history on disk for as long as the route is (`history.go`: 30 days, 200 MB). The GUI shows it in By session's headings, a request row's tooltip, the stage's agent working in more than one folder, and the tray panel's agent tooltip. | [`trace.go`](../../internal/gateway/trace.go) A request outside the chat path gets a route of its own too ([`media_route.go`](../../internal/gateway/media_route.go), `sideRoute`): an image (`images`, each account `drawOnAccounts` asks a try), a video's start (`videosCreate`; its polls and download are the same video and add none) and an embedding, rerank, transcription or speech (`retrieve`). Each try's key or account is put in `Order` so the page finds its seat, the route's `Kind` stays empty, and the one usage row names it in `RouteID` (zzzkl3994 on Discord: a video the magpie-image MCP made was in Usage's requests and not on Routing; `TestVideoIsOnRoutingOnce`, `TestImageIsOnRouting`, `TestEmbeddingAndRerankAreOnRouting`). |
| Prompt | What each request's prompt held, part by part: the agent's system prompt, tool definitions (an MCP server's tools as one item), instruction files and skills, files its tools read, their other output, and the conversation. `inspectPrompt` reads it from the body as the agent sent it, before translation; `calibrated` scales the estimate to the prompt the vendor counted and sets the window of the model that answered. The route keeps names and sizes only, never the text, so the routing history on disk holds none. For the card's text (masaka on Discord), the trace keeps the last `promptBodies` (10) request bodies in memory only (at most 32 MiB each, 64 MiB together), masked as the vendor got them; for a model let have it unmasked, the body is kept as it went and masked when `PromptText` first reads it, so the request itself never waits on masking (#3973). `PromptText` reads a part's items and their text from that body again (`prompt_text.go`), served to the GUI as `/api/context/text`. Once a body is dropped, or magpie quits, the GUI reads the request archive's copy when the route names one (`Route.Archive`; wangbinyq on Discord): `ArchivedPromptText` parses it the same way and masks it as the body in memory is (`maskedCopy`), and the reply beside it comes from `ReplyBody` (the Recent calls' reply) or the archive's, read into pieces by `ReplyPieces` and masked alike (`prompt_reply.go`, served as `/api/context/reply`). A body or reply that can't be masked shows nothing, and one the archive cut short isn't parsed (`TestArchivedPromptTextIsMasked`, `TestReplyPiecesAreMasked`, `TestContextTextFromTheArchive`). It goes on the trace as `Route.Prompt`, with `Route.Conv` naming a conversation that has no session id. The GUI's `/api/context` scores each agent from it (`internal/gui/context.go`). A session's line and its compactions are its own turns': a call with a `Kind`, a Claude Code subagent's (`Route.Sub`, from `x-claude-code-agent-id`), and, in a session whose turns carry tool definitions, a request without any (auto mode's classifier, the title, the quota check) are left out, so a classifier's 40K after a 100K turn is no compaction (`toolSessions`, `TestContextSideCallsAreNoCompaction`). | [`prompt.go`](../../internal/gateway/prompt.go) |
| Vertex AI | A [Google Vertex AI provider](providers-accounts.md#responsibilities-and-sources-of-truth) speaks Gemini for its Gemini models, chat completions for its partner models, and Anthropic's Messages API for Claude. Claude (`provider.VertexClaude`: `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-4-5@20250929`) is relayed or translated as at any Anthropic vendor, and `forwardOnce`, after every other change to the body, moves the request to where Anthropic's Vertex SDK sends it (`provider.VertexClaudeRequest`): `<base>/publishers/anthropic/models/<model>:streamRawPredict` when the body streams, `:rawPredict` when it doesn't, the model out of the body; count_tokens to `count-tokens:rawPredict` with the model kept (`countTokens` counts at a provider whose `Base(Anthropic)` is set); `anthropic_version` `vertex-2023-10-16` added unless the body has one; Claude Code's `?beta=true` never sent. `thinkingOffAsTaken` and `samplingAsTaken` run before, so they read the model from the body. Tool search's beta goes as `tool-search-tool-2025-10-19`, the name Claude Code gives it for Vertex as for Bedrock (`bedrockBetas`); Bedrock's left-out betas aren't left out for it. Gemini's effort rules (`vertexEffort`) don't touch it (`TestVertexClaudeModel`). A partner model (`provider.VertexPartner`: a `publisher/model` id such as `deepseek-ai/deepseek-v4.1-flash-maas`) goes to Google's OpenAI-compatible `<base>/endpoints/openapi/chat/completions` (`Base(Chat)`, `ListedAPIs`), signed with the same token, its full id in the body. It is relayed or translated there as any chat completions vendor is, and none of the Gemini rules below (`vertexEffort`, `vertexThinking`, signatures) touch it (`TestVertexPartnerModel`). A Gemini model's request goes to `<base>/publishers/google/models/<model>:streamGenerateContent?alt=sse` (`provider.VertexPath`, `upstreamPath`), the model named as the user's upstream name for it says (`UpstreamNameIn`, which the usage log's endpoint names too), with no `model` or `stream` field in its body (`passthrough`, `forwardOnce`, `geminiRequest`). A Gemini client's request is relayed as it came, its own `thinkingConfig`, `safetySettings` and the rest kept: streamed there, asked whole to `:generateContent` (`provider.VertexWholePath`), which answers one JSON body (Factory's rule of translating a whole request, as its route only streams, isn't Vertex AI's). Every other API's is translated (`buildFor`, `buildVertex`) and its stream read by `codeAssistDecoder`, which gives thought parts as reasoning, never as the answer's text. Effort: `vertexEffort` fits it to the model's levels, reasoning turned off going as `minimal` fitted the same way, and holds a model whose levels aren't known to `low`…`high`. `vertexThinking` sends Gemini 3 a `thinkingLevel` (`includeThoughts` false with reasoning off) and 2.5 a `thinkingBudget` in the model's range (`vertexBudgets`): turned off, the budget that stops its thinking, or Pro's least, as Pro can't stop. Thought signatures are every Gemini path's (#1445): a call's signature reaches the client in its id (`signedID`) and goes back on the call it came on, while a call with none in a step Vertex AI signed goes without; a step with nothing to give back has every call sent `skip_thought_signature_validator`. A 400 `Invalid thought signature.` (or AI Studio's `Corrupted thought signature.`, `signatureRefused`) is asked again with every call sent so (`withoutSignatures`), for that request only, at any upstream Gemini's signatures reach (`signaturesReach`), relayed too; it rests no account and lets a group go on (`restAfterMarked`, `shapeRefused`). A 400 refusing `minimal` (`geminiRefusedLevel`) is asked again at the model's lowest other level (`onEffort`), and `markUnfit(offRefused)` keeps that level until magpie restarts; that retry is the translated path's. Vertex AI's own Google Search grounding isn't used: `searchHosts` names only `generativelanguage.googleapis.com` for Gemini, so magpie searches for it (`search.go`). | [`vertex.go`](../../internal/gateway/vertex.go), [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go), [`codeassist.go`](../../internal/gateway/codeassist.go), [`gemini.go`](../../internal/gateway/gemini.go), [`gemini_signature.go`](../../internal/gateway/gemini_signature.go), [`search.go`](../../internal/gateway/search.go) |
| Bedrock Mantle | A request on chat completions or Responses to an [Amazon Bedrock Mantle provider](providers-accounts.md#responsibilities-and-sources-of-truth) goes to the prefix that serves its model (`mantleRoutes`, `provider.MantleBase` in `forwardOnce`): `/openai/v1` for OpenAI's closed GPTs and xAI's Grok, `/v1` for the rest. A model Mantle refuses there with "model `x` isn't supported on this route" (a maker the rule doesn't know yet) is asked once more at the other prefix, and when it is taken there that prefix is remembered for the model in `unfit` (`mantleRouteKey`) until the gateway restarts. A model refused at both gets the first refusal and nothing is remembered (`mantleReroute`). Messages go to `/anthropic` alone. | [`mantle.go`](../../internal/gateway/mantle.go), [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go) |

## Codex titles, descriptions, summaries and predictions

Codex's title and description requests are independent hidden turns. The
settings `CodexTitles` and `CodexDescriptions` select their models separately;
an empty setting preserves the requested model. On a Magpie model, both
paths wrap plain model text in the object Codex requested. Titles use
`{"title": ...}`; descriptions use `{"description": ...}` with nonempty text
and no invented maximum length. Each has its own Off (`off`): magpie answers
the request itself with a finished reply holding no message, sends nothing
upstream and records it as answered here (`sentNowhere`); the Codex app reads
that as no title or no description (its side-turn helper's "empty" result,
not an error) and keeps the thread's as it was. Title Off does not turn
descriptions off, nor the other way round (cybercaius on Discord asked for
the description's).

The Codex app asks for titles, descriptions and one-line thread summaries
(`thread_summary`, after each message) on a model of its own (`gpt-6-luna`),
whatever the conversation is on, and composer predictions
(`composer_predictions`) on the thread's model. An unset setting ("Same as
the session" in the picker) sends these on the session's model when it is
one of magpie's (`codexSideTo` with `Server.codexSideSession`): the model the
conversation's own turns last went on (`codexLeads`: its parent's, the
thread it was forked from for a prediction, its own), else the last turn by
the gateway key, else, before any turn
has come through magpie, `agent.CodexSideModel` (Codex's config `model`, when
it is magpie's and magpie is not Codex's provider). So a conversation on a
third-party model spends none of the ChatGPT account's allowance (atom_Ray on
X), whether the model was picked in Codex's config or in the app (cybercaius
on Discord, `TestCodexAppSideTurnsFollowTheSession`). Codex writes a new
conversation's title on a temporary thread with no link to it, started as
the first message is sent, so it can arrive before that conversation's
first turn, when the key's last turn is the previous conversation's, on the
model the user just switched from. A call tied to no seen thread whose
key's last turn is older than 3s (`codexSideFresh`) waits, up to 5s
(`codexSideWait`) or its cancellation, for the next turn and takes its
model (`codexLeads.sideOf`, `TestCodexTitleBeforeTheFirstTurnFollowsTheSwitch`);
a later rename suggestion with no turn beside it waits the 5s. Codex
starts its memories with each turn of a conversation that has the user's
input (codex-rs `start_memories_startup_task`; extraction under that
conversation's thread, consolidation on a thread of its own), so a memory
call waits the same way even when tied to a seen thread (`sideOf`'s
`beside`): one that beats the first turn after a switch, in a new
conversation or one under way, goes on the model switched to, through the
ChatGPT backend (`codexMemorySession`) and with magpie as Codex's provider
(`codexMemoryStandIn`, which reads `codexLeads` before its last-turn
record), cybercaius on Discord
(`TestCodexSideCallsBeforeTheFirstTurnFollowTheSwitch`,
`TestCodexMemoryOfASwitchedConversationFollowsTheSwitch`,
`TestCodexMemoryStandInFollowsTheSwitch`). A call tied to a seen thread
and not started with a turn (a prediction, a known thread's title) takes
that thread's last model at once. Codex
