asks a title with no reasoning effort on any model but its own title
model, and its auto-review at a model's default, which it has none of for
magpie's models; a model that needs an effort answered those with 400
(cybercaius on Discord). A title, description, summary, prediction,
auto-review or memory call on one of magpie's models whose levels are
known, asking no effort or an off one the model hasn't, is asked at its
lowest on level (`codexSideEffort`, `TestCodexSideCallAskedAtAnEffortTheModelTakes`).
Wherever the levels aren't known, serve asks any request again once at
its lowest (low) when the upstream turns no effort or none away as needing
one, matched on the error body (`effortRequired`, on Chat and Responses,
forwarded or translated; `TestEffortRequiredRefusalAskedAgainAtLow`), or
turns none or minimal away in `alwaysThinks`' words, which name no levels
(`needsReasoning`: Kimi Code's K3.1 Pro on Chat, #3739,
`TestKimiChatThinkingOffRefused`), then at the model's lowest from the start; any
other 400 goes back as it came (`TestOtherBadRequestNotAskedAgainAtLow`). A session on Codex's
own model sends them to ChatGPT as Codex sent them, whatever the config says
(`TestCodexAppSideTurnsStayWithAnOwnModelSession`); so does a session model
magpie can't serve now (turned off, gone, held from the caller's key). Summaries use
`{"summary": ...}`, with any other string the schema requires
(`compactSummary`) filled and cut to its length (`summaryJSON`).

`codexTitlesTo`, `codexDescriptionsTo`, `codexSummariesTo`, `codexTitle`,
`codexDescription` and `codexSummary` in
[`codex_titles.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_titles.go) own this selection
and response wrapping. Both the Responses API and Codex backend entrypoints
use it. Usage and Routing retain the original purpose; unusable description
answers are recorded as description failures. These helpers do not decrypt
foreign sealed history or repair ChatGPT's response protection.

Codex's composer predictions (#1970), its guess at the user's next message,
are another hidden turn on a fork of the thread, sent only through the
ChatGPT sign-in, with `thread_source` `composer_predictions`. Each one reads
the whole conversation again. `CodexPredictions` selects where they go: empty
sends them as Codex asked, `off` answers `{"suggestion":null}` from magpie
and sends nothing (`sentNowhere`, as title Off mode), and a model id sends
them to that model. `codexPredictionsTo` and `codexPrediction` own this, at
the same two entrypoints. A model's answer reaches Codex only as the
`{"suggestion": string|null}` object it asked for (`predictionJSON`): plain
text is never taken as a suggestion, as Codex would put it in the user's
composer as their next message, so it gives no prediction and the call is
recorded as a prediction failure (`noPrediction`).

## Runtime path

1. `handle(proto)` reads the body and the model (`requestBody`, `requestModel`), then calls `serveAgent`. Every route that reads an agent's body (each API, count_tokens, Gemini's, embeddings, images, videos, System One, the Codex backend path) reads it through `readBoundedRequestBody` in [`request_bounds.go`](../../internal/gateway/request_bounds.go). It decodes a `Content-Encoding` of `gzip` or `zstd` (`decodedBody`; Codex sends zstd to a custom provider's `/v1/responses` too, #1223) and drops the header, so the provider is sent plain JSON, with one exception: a ChatGPT account's turn (POST `CodexBase`/responses, from the accounts loop in `forwardOnce` or relayed by `codexUpstreamOn`) is compressed again with zstd and a window of at most 2 MiB, as Codex's own client sends it (`provider.CodexCompressed`; openai/codex `responses_request_compression` and `http-client/src/request.rs`). It is compressed once the body is final, and again on each try. `/responses/compact`, an API key's requests and every other provider stay plain. Sent plain, a 1.1 MB text-only turn was answered 503 (ylorn on X). The plain body is what magpie keeps and shows, and `codexDropped` judges a reset by the bytes that went on the wire. `codexDropped` also reads Envoy's 507 "exceeded request buffer limit while retrying upstream" (the backend failed and the front couldn't retry a body that large) as that reset, at any size, told as a 503 so magpie, Codex and pi ask again and nobody rests (#2034). The size limit (`defaultBodyLimit`, 512 MB) holds for the decoded bytes (413), and any other encoding is refused 415. It was 128 MB until a long Codex session that had read many images went past it decoded, its inline screenshots resent each turn, and got the 413 on all five of Codex's retries (#1422, `TestImageHeavyCodexTurnIsRead`). Codex's own path (`codexBackend`, the relay, its WebSocket, and `keyLimited` on it) reads to `codexBodyLimit` instead: a sixteenth of the machine's memory (`memLimit`, else `proc.MemTotal`), 512 MB at least and 2 GB at most, since Codex keeps every image it generated in the conversation as base64 and over HTTP resends it whole each turn; a session that generated and revised images went past 512 MB while Codex signed in to OpenAI went on (dallascao on Discord). One of Codex's own models is relayed at about 2 to 5 times the body in Go heap at its peak (1000 MB took ~12s and ~2 GB of heap past the body); a turn for a magpie model is translated at several times that and is still held to 512 MB once its model is read, its 413 saying Codex's own models take more (`TestCodexGeneratedImagesPastOldLimit`). magpie's own 413 says it is magpie's gateway limit, with the size, and that the provider was not asked (`overLimit`); `writeError` adds to any 413 it isn't holding that this is a byte limit, not the model's context, and to `/compact` or start a new conversation (`TestOverLimitSaysWhoseAndWhatToDo`). Every shape but Gemini's types a 413 `request_too_large`, Anthropic's own type, which pi's overflow table knows: pi compacts and tries once more rather than stopping on an OpenAI-shaped `api_error` (@wilsonxuuuu on X, `TestPayloadTooLargeSaysRequestTooLargeToPi`). `keyLimited` reads a limited key's compressed body the same way, so its reservation is the decoded size. `serveAgent` runs the [middleware](gateway-middleware.md) chain, then `serve`.
2. `serve` redacts secrets in the request (`redacted`; `redact.MaskJSON` leaves ids alone, and in any text a backend's id (a UUID, 32 hex digits or more, or a digest of 16 to 31 hex digits in one case with letters and digits both, a cache key or a short commit, which went as a key before: _kaichen on X, 10-28; `digestShaped`), or an element token cua-driver hands the model for computer use (`s0000002a:14`, #3782), goes as written: no rule, model label or value known from before masks a piece of one (a phone number at the end of `…-a13800138000`, a card number across a UUID's groups), and a whole one is masked only by the user's own words and rules or as a credential, by the password, API key and token rules or a secret rule whose field is named for one (`api_key=`, `refresh_token`), never one named for an id, a cursor, a request or a digest (`secret_id`, `next_page_token`, `Idempotency-Key`, `secret_hash`), nor cua-driver's own `element_token` (any other name with `element` in it, `element_api_key`, is a credential's as before), a bare `key` (not after api, access, secret, private, signing or license: `keyPhrase`), a field named for a cache, commit, object, blob, file, content, revision, partition, shard, bucket, dedup, lookup, row or sort key, a serial number, or what the model takes a UUID or an element token for, nor an element token known from before (one the model labelled in prose a turn earlier) (`noIDs`, [`internal/redact/ids.go`](../../internal/redact/ids.go)); and a tool's name and its MCP server's (`namespace`, `server_label`, `server_name`, #3143); the request's tool definitions and output schemas, found by their place in each protocol (top-level `tools`, `tool_choice`, `response_format`, `text`, `generationConfig`, `stream_options`, the client's ids in `client_metadata` and the like, and the tools of a Responses `additional_tools` or `tool_search_output` item), go as written save their descriptions, so a word the user masks can't break a grammar or an enum (#1911); and what the vendor sealed with a signature, a thinking block, Gemini's thoughts or Responses' encrypted reasoning, goes back as the vendor wrote it: only the values masked elsewhere in the request, and secrets masked before, are put back as their placeholders; a value masked once, at least 8 characters long, is masked again wherever a request has it, as an exact match whatever words are around it, so what the vendor wrote about a placeholder doesn't go back with the value in it, `redact/known.go`; a text masked for a vendor before, such as a system prompt or a turn already sent, is masked as it was then on the same route (the providers the request can reach: its model's, its fallbacks', or a group's members'; `maskedFor`, `masking.WithRoute`, `redact.Options.Route`), so neither a value a rule first finds later in a conversation nor a name the local model reads in a tool's output only after its first send (the default model's 2s wait, `pii.Partial`; vvrfxyz on X, dsh's cache hits) changes the prompt the vendor has cached and rewrites all of it to the cache; such a late find is masked in new text (`learnLate`), and another route, never sent the text, gets it masked with what is known and found now, `sentSpans`, `TestModelReadingLateKeepsTheSentPrefix`, `TestMaskedPrefixKeptOnlyOnItsRoute`), resolves the model to a provider or a group, and refuses what can't be served. The reply has the values put back on its way to the agent (`redact.Writer`, a stream event by event, a whole body at its end, before the writer outside it that names the member, `memberWriter`, writes what it holds: the other way round a whole reply with a value masked in its request went out a 200 with an empty body, #4129, `TestMaskedWholeReplyNamesMember`): a placeholder the model wrote as it was given, or as models rewrite one (spaces inside the braces, a brace or both dropped, another case), is the value again when what is left names one magpie made, and one split across events is held until it is whole (`redact.Restore`, `partialTail`, [`internal/redact/redact.go`](../../internal/redact/redact.go)). Every reply is read so, a request that masked nothing too: a vendor holding the conversation (Codex's compacted history, a server-side session) answers with placeholders sent turns before, and those came back as written (yapechan123 on Discord, a table of `{{PERSON_…}}、{{PERSON_…}}`; `TestReplyToAnUnmaskedTurnIsRestored`). Every string of an event is read, a tool call's `name`, `type` or `data` inside its arguments too, but for signatures, `data:` URLs and opaque blobs (`sealedText`); a held tail is kept per string, so a Responses event's random `obfuscation` never takes the hold from its `delta`; a whole body with a placeholder in no braces or one is restored as with two (`heldIn`); an audio, image or other binary reply goes through as it comes (`binaryType`). Past 200,000 values the half used longest ago is let go (`makeRoom`, `touch`; before, a random half, so a value just masked could be gone when its reply came). Values are held in memory only, so a placeholder from before a restart that only the vendor still holds comes back as written. A request whose provider and fallbacks, or whose group's models, all skip redaction (`unredactedRoute`, `provider.SkipsRedaction`: set *Send requests unmasked*, `Unredacted`, and has an address: the user's switch for each provider whatever its address, AcMilan on Discord, `http://llm.corp.lan:8000`; nothing is looked up to decide, `TestSkipsRedaction`, `TestLANNameUnredactedProvider`. A signed-in subscription and a plugin's provider take it too, kept with the account's model picks (`withPicks`; isee1_1 on Discord: a translation plugin as a provider); another magpie never does, as it masks on its own side. The editor shows it for every provider but another magpie, and, while it is on, a caution under it naming an address not on this computer or the local network, or, for a subscription or a plugin's provider, its name (`unmaskRow`, `awayHost` in app.js; a name of the user's own DNS is asked about, only while it is on, through `POST /api/local-hosts`, `provider.LocalHost`, which takes a name every address of which, looked up in 400ms and kept a minute, is loopback, private or link-local, `netproxy.LocalName`; no request asks it)) goes to them as the agent wrote it and isn't masked on its way, since a request is masked only once its route is known (#3973: masking a body whose masked copy was thrown away took up to minutes of magpie's own time). The log, recent calls and the request archive keep it as it went (the archive's bodies have their secrets taken out, `redact.ScrubJSON`, as every call's do), whether the archive is on or off; an observer that wants the bodies (the OTLP export, a JS plugin's) is handed it masked, masked once when it is first handed it after a try (`Call.maskIn`, `laterMasked`), never before the request is sent. A request turned away before it is routed is logged masked (`maskedCopy`). count_tokens and embeddings follow the same rule. A request masking would change can go to such a model instead (jessyoungyu on X: 「{{百度}}这家公司怎么样」 can't be answered from a placeholder): with Settings › Privacy's *Instead of masking, send to* set (`RedactRouteTo`, `POST /api/settings/redact-route`, which takes only a model `MaskRoute` passes: `unredactedRoute` for its provider and fallbacks, or its group), an agent's turn that masking would change (`maskHits.n` > 0: a warn-only hit doesn't count, and a held-back one stays held back) goes as written to that model, the model asked for left alone, in `serve` after the request is masked (`maskRouteFor` in [`redact.go`](../../internal/gateway/redact.go); the masked copy is thrown away, so streaming and every API go as for any route). If it fails, the request fails: its fallbacks are all unmasked ones by `unredactedRoute`, and nothing is masked and sent to the model asked for. It is judged again on each request, so a route that has since got a vendor's address, a fallback elsewhere, was turned off, or isn't one of a gateway key's models is ignored and the request is masked as before. Only an agent's own turn is moved (`agentTurn`, set in `serveAgent` and cleared by `ownAsk`): a title, a search stand-in, a picture described, System One and Jev stay masked. count_tokens is counted on the route (`countTokens`), and a turn of Codex's own model on its ChatGPT sign-in goes there too (`codexMaskRoute` in `codexBackend`; masked once to decide, only while it is set). Such a turn, and ChatGPT's web search for it (`alphaSearchOn`), goes as written when the Codex subscription it is on is set to go unmasked (`codexRouteUnmasked`; `TestCodexSignInUnmaskedSwitch`); a plugin's provider is asked through `serve` (`TestPluginProviderUnmaskedSwitch`). Speech and video requests are masked whatever the switch says (`maskTextHits`). Unset, as by default, nothing changes. Tests: `TestMaskedRequestGoesToTheModelSet`, `TestCodexSigninTurnGoesToTheModelSet`, `TestMaskRouteOnlyForTheAgentsTurn`, `TestMaskRouteTakesOnlyUnmaskedRoutes` ([`mask_route_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/mask_route_test.go)) and `TestRedactRouteSetting` in internal/gui. What a routing group's Jev is told of the turn (the message, the user's message before it and the reply to it) is masked all the same unless Jev's own provider skips redaction too (`maskedState` in `askJev`, [`decide.go`](../../internal/gateway/decide.go)): a remote Jev is none of the group's members. The masking is the built-in plugin `redact`'s (`gwmw.Masker`, [Plugin runtime](plugin-runtime.md)); when the settings ask for masking and no masker serves, or it fails, the request is a 503 and nothing is sent (`redacted`, `gwmw.Masking`). A request masking swapped a value in (`maskHits.n`) is told, on each try, that the placeholders are used as written and filled in before anything runs (`placeholderNote`, [`placeholder_note.go`](../../internal/gateway/placeholder_note.go); treeMan on Discord: weaker models refused to search with one, asked to verify it, or rewrote it as an example). The note names neither magpie nor a vendor or client. It goes where placeholders first appear, after what is there, so it is new text the first time and the same on every later turn: in the instructions when they or the tools hold one (the last block of Anthropic's `system`, the end of Chat's first system or developer message, a part after Gemini's `systemInstruction`'s), else on the earliest message that holds one (a text block or part after its content; on Responses a developer message after that item and the tool outputs that follow it). Responses `instructions` (Codex's base_instructions) is never touched. A conversation's values are masked again each turn and a value found later is masked only in new text, so the note never lands in text the vendor has cached (`TestMaskedConversationKeepsItsCachedPrefix` goes red with the note in the system prompt). It is not added for a Claude subscription (the bridge puts the caller's instructions in the run's first message, which Anthropic classifies), for a ChatGPT account (Codex's own turns, `codexUpstreamOn`, never pass here either), or for a Responses turn with `previous_response_id`, where it would pile up in the stored conversation (`notesPlaceholders`; `TestPlaceholderNotePlaces`, `TestPlaceholderNoteNotOnClientPaths`, `TestMaskedRequestTold`, `TestPlaceholderNoteIsNeutral`). count_tokens counts without it. Inside a tool call's arguments (Anthropic's `tool_use` input, Gemini's `functionCall` args) and Gemini's `functionResponse` response, whose keys are the tool's, a field named for a secret (`SecretName`: `api_key`, `db_pass`, `Authorization`, not `sort_key` or `public_key`) has a value of 8 characters or more masked by its name whatever its shape, with secret masking on (#2946, `namedSecret`); a key anywhere else is the protocol's, `mcp_servers[].authorization_token` included, and never masks by its name (`TestMaskJSONSecretNamesOnlyInToolArgs`).
   - With *Mask personal data* on, each kind in `redact.Categories` ([`internal/redact/more.go`](../../internal/redact/more.go)) is masked unless the user turned it off (`redactKinds` in settings, `Options.Kinds`). On by default: US SSNs, passport numbers, IBANs that pass mod 97, birthdays next to a word that says so (a date over a year ago: one in the last year or ahead is the day an agent runs on, `dateOK`); the word must be right before the date, so a log line's or a file name's date is none, and a time beside the date doesn't make it a log's: a date of birth stored as a time (`1990-05-11T16:00:00.000Z`) is one (`TestBirthdayLogDates`; #3878), MAC addresses, and serial numbers next to S/N or the like. Eleven digits from 13 to 19 are a mainland mobile's form and an id's too, so they are a phone only with +86 or 86 before them, as the value of a field whose name says phone (phone, mobile, tel, cell, contact, 手机, 电话, a field named `number`), or in prose with no id's word right before them (order, id, no., #, 订单号, 编号, 单号, 流水号); under order_id or uid, after 订单号, or with a letter or digit against them they go as written (`phoneAt` in [`internal/redact/phone.go`](../../internal/redact/phone.go); _kaichen on X, 10-28). Off by default: the user name in a home folder, user@host in a prompt or ssh command, public IPv4 and IPv6 addresses (never private, loopback, link-local, CGNAT or documentation ranges) and cloud bucket names, since agents send them in nearly every request and the model must copy the placeholder exactly inside a path or URL. When the user name is on, it goes as a made-up name of 8 lowercase letters, the same for that name every turn and put back in replies like any placeholder (`standIn` in [`shape.go`](../../internal/redact/shape.go)), not as `{{USERNAME_…}}`: with mimo-v2.6-flash a model read `C:\Users\{{…}}` as a temporary folder and set out to delete it (eerttreeeert on X, 10-28). A user name found once is masked again as known (8 bytes or more) only where it stands as the user's name: a path's part, `name@host`, a git author, `ls -l`'s owner, `uid=501(name)`, prose, a quoted string; not where code has the same word as a name of its own, joined to another part (`developer_dir`, `self.developer`, `developer.go`), declared, assigned, called, a parameter or a JSON key (`nameAlone`). A `User:` line's unquoted value made of words one of which says it holds a user (`current_user`, `UserInfo`, `loginName`) is a variable's or a type's name, not a login (`notLineIdent`); a quoted one is masked (`TestUserNameInCodeIsNoUserName`, `TestUserLineInCodeIsNoUserName`; Tomyu_2034 on X). A public IPv4 or IPv6 address goes the same way as a name that says what it is, `public-ip-` and eight lowercase letters (`public-ip-kvxqmrtz`), the same one for that address while magpie runs and never another address's, put back only where it stands as a whole name, in a streamed tool call's arguments too (`ipStandIn`, `restoreAddr`; `TestIPGoesAsAPublicIPName`). Told `{{IP_…}}`, models asked the user for the real address or searched `~/.ssh` for it instead of running ssh (0x0_air on X, 10-28). Told an address from the documentation ranges (192.0.2.0/24 and the like, used from 10-28 to 10-31), they ran ssh but told the user, when asked why it failed, that it was an example address and not their server's, and a 192.0.2. one read as a LAN address (akic404 on Discord, 10-31). A name with no dot reaches nobody if the vendor tries it itself. An EVM address (`0x` and 40 hex digits) is public, so the local model's finds leave it as written unless a key's name is right before it; a 64-digit private key is still masked (`evmRe` in `modelFits`; `TestEVMAddressGoesAsWritten`). Decimal digits alone that the local model calls a secret are a count, a time or an id unless the name right before them ends in a key's, a token's, a password's or a PIN's word (`api_key=`, `PIN:`, `密码是`): Claude Code's `<total_tokens>14998720 tokens left</total_tokens>`, `"total_tokens": …` and `max_tokens: …` go as written (`numberNamedSecret` in `modelFits`; `TestTokenCountIsNoSecret`, steven_4422 on Discord). Context rules run only near their words (`rule.matches`). What reads as an address but ends in a file's extension that no domain has (`…@2x.jpg`, `strings@base.json`; `fileExt` in `realEmail`), or a scale before an extension that is a domain too (`icon@2x.zip`, `intro@1.5x.mov`; `scaleExt`), is a file's name, for the rule and the local model alike (#3029). A domain needs a dot and labels a host can have, so a disassembly's symbols go as written for both too: `system@plt`, `puts@got.plt`, `memcpy@GLIBC_2.14`, `foo@@GLIBC_2.2.5`, and `system@got` (`.got` is a top-level domain; `mailHost` in `realEmail`, `TestSymbolIsNoEmail`; ch35tnut_ on X, 10-28). `ops@files.zip` is still an address. The Settings rows come from `REDACT_KINDS` in app.js, which `TestRedactKindsAreRedacts` holds to the same ids and defaults.
   - With *Find with a local model* on (`masking.ModelWanted`: on by default since 10-28, `settings.RedactModel` the user turning it on and `RedactModelOff` off, this computer's own, kept by `KeepOwn`; a user with a model downloaded or picked and neither flag turned it off before the default, and `RedactModelAuto` marks the default's own download), a local model finds what no rule can, a name or an address in free text, under the same switches: a name, address, phone, email or account number is personal data, a secret a secret (`modelKinds`, `modelSpans` in [`internal/redact/model.go`](../../internal/redact/model.go)). `MaskJSON` asks it once per request, for every text it would mask, each once (`withModel`), but for what the agent defines (its tools' and schemas' descriptions, under `defKeys`), which the rules alone mask, as with the model off, after the rest of the request so a value the model found in a message is masked there too as known: those were 52,577 of Claude Code 2.1.296's 67,532 bytes the model read, and a 4-core server's first request waited out the gateway's 2 minutes (niuniu_8964 on X; 11.5s cold at 4 procs on a Ryzen AI Max+, 2.6s without them, and on three real Claude Code requests the model masked nothing there the rules didn't); its spans join the rules' with a rule's kind kept where both find a value, and ids a vendor or tool made (`req_…`, `toolu_…`) are never taken, nor a snake_case word as a name (Codex's `sequential_cutoff`, #3144), nor one word as a name where what is around it says a thing and nothing says a person: an app's name in a list of names alone, in a list of apps, or beside a thing's word (using, switched to, the … app, vs, 这个软件), so Privacy Filter's Cicada and Mimi in a chat about choosing apps go as written, while a full name, a Chinese name and a first name beside a relation, a greeting, said or he/she are masked as before, and a text of one first name alone goes as written by design (`thingNamed` in [`thingname.go`](../../internal/redact/thingname.go); ylorn. on Discord, whose follow-up questions had the names as written while the history had them as placeholders, so the vendor answered about another app), nor as a secret what names a thing: a UUID, a digest with its algorithm's name, a file's path (`namesThing`; the model takes all three for secrets). A package's name goes as written, for the model and the rules alike (dniray on Discord, v0.1.1917): the module a line of code loads (`from ' drizzle-orm '`, `require('jsonwebtoken')`, `from x import`, `use x::`, with an editor's line number or a diff's mark before it; `moduleAt`, `codeHead`), a manifest's dependency pinned at a version (`"drizzle-orm": "^0.36.4"`, TOML's `x = "9"`; `dependencyAt`), a folder under node_modules or site-packages, the module an error could not find (`Cannot find module 'x'`, `No module named 'x'`; `missingModuleAt`), a package an install names (`pnpm add x`; `installedAt`) or pins with @ (`x@0.36.4`; `pinnedAt`), all in `packageAt`; a value known from an earlier mask (`knownSpans`) goes as written there too, since once the model took the name for a secret anywhere in a request it was masked on the import line as known (dniray, v0.1.1965), a requirement's name before its pinned version (`django-rest-passwordreset==1.4.0`; `pinnedAfter`), a lock file's integrity hash (`sha512-…`; `sriRe` in `namesThing`), a version as the value of a field named for a secret (`"js-tokens": "^4.0.0-beta.1"`; `versionValue` in `fieldAt` and `keyAt`), and a package pinned at its version as user@host has it (`js-tokens@4.0.0:` in pnpm-lock.yaml; `notPackagePin`); `TestPackageNamesGoAsWritten` and `TestModelPackageNamesNotMasked` hold them. A field named for a secret and set to nothing takes nothing from the field after it: the SECRET rules let a space stand after `=` or `:` (`password = hunter2` in a config), and `env -i UI_PASSWORD= UI_ADDR=127.0.0.1:39117 ./syncer` went with `UI_ADDR=127.0.0.1` as UI_PASSWORD's value (mx_albert on Discord); a value that is an assignment of its own after a space (`UI_ADDR=`, `addr=`, `--listen=`) is the next field, read with its own name, so a token there still masks (`fieldMatches`, `nextField` in `internal/redact/fieldvalue.go`; `TestEmptyFieldTakesNotTheNext`, `TestSecretBesideEmptyFieldMasks`). The model misses many Chinese names (张伟 after 给, 陈建国 after 客户, however low its threshold) and runs a name it finds on into the words after it (「王芳，她下周一」), so with Privacy Filter a Chinese name is also found by its surname and a word around it that says a person is meant (客户, 我叫, 给…发, …先生, …说, joined by 和 to another name; 和 or 与 says so only after a person, 吴敏和郑浩 but not 判断和边界, 发 or 打 only as the verb, 发了个 but not 关键发现, and a sentence starts after punctuation, not after a space after code, `gh pr view` 易触发; xuanwo on X; 一下 only after a verb that asks a person, 问一下张伟 but not 改一下文案, a word any subject takes after it, 已经, 明天 or 他, only with a person's cue before it, and 说明 is no 说; zeewell on X), and the model's are cut at punctuation and titles ([`zhname.go`](../../internal/redact/zhname.go)); a surname with nothing around it (王国的, 张开的) is left, and so is a common word that starts with one (`zhWords`: 文案, 文档, 方案; 毛利, 毛重, 毛巾, since 毛利 is a margin far more often than the Japanese surname, so 毛利低 goes as written: _kaichen on X, 10-28; the model still finds 毛利兰, and neither model nor rule masks 毛利小五郎), the model's own name too when it is that word whole. A name the model or the cues take is also no name when it is an ordinary word (`zhWordShaped`, `zhWordNotName`, the one check both go through): a colour (黄绿色, 红色, any that ends in 色 or is colour characters alone: hh05928822 on X after v0.1.1850), a word that starts with a surname from `zhWords` or `zhDict` (about 1,550 two-character words from jieba's dictionary, [`zhdict.txt`](../../internal/redact/zhdict.txt), its header saying how they were chosen; 边界, 应该, 尚可), or such a word with what makes a longer word after it (关键词, 关键字: wangjunxiao128 on X; 应用层, 毛利率), unless a role before it or a title after it says a person is meant (客户方圆, 江山老师), where a name that is also a word is masked. The surnames stay (黄 starts 黄绿色 and 黄晓明), and 毛利兰, 关羽 and 黄晓明 are still masked. With AstrLink Guard only a nickname is (小王, 老李, `zhNick`; `redact.Options.NamesByModel`): Guard at 0.8 finds the full names those cues did in zhname_test's sentences (张伟, 陈建国, 欧阳娜娜…), and misses the nicknames, 孙经理, and all but the surname of 王老板 and 邓总; the cues took words for names (关键发现; xuanwo on X). The model is the user's pick (`settings.RedactModelName`, `pii.Choose`; the app's menu beside the switch, `pmPick`): AstrLink Guard (QuantumNous/astrlink-guard at a pinned revision, Apache-2.0, a 39 MB int8 Multilingual-MiniLM token classifier for Chinese and English), run in magpie's own process on the CPU by [`internal/pii/guard`](../../internal/pii/guard) (an ONNX reader, the XLM-R tokenizer and int8 kernels in Go, NEON/SDOT on arm64 and SSE2 on amd64; its labels named as Privacy Filter's, `guardLabels`); a text over one window, 510 tokens, is read in windows that overlap by 128, and on the CPU as many windows at once as it has cores, half of `GOMAXPROCS` and 4 at most (`guard.Cores`: on every core a long history unread kept a 32-core Linux box at 13 cores' worth for as long as requests came, xinvxueyuan and shensmart on Discord, 10-28): one window's matmuls split over the cores left them idle between, and a 4-core server's first Claude Code request waited minutes (niuniu_8964 on X; 22,488 tokens took 23.9s at 4 procs, 17.9s at once, and 48.8s against 30.5s at 2), and on a Mac through CoreML on the Neural Engine or the GPU ([`coreml.go`](../../internal/pii/guard/coreml.go), [`internal/pii/guard/coreml`](../../internal/pii/guard/coreml); yyhhyyyyyy on X; Settings › Privacy names which, from CoreML's own compute plan of the compiled model, `MLComputePlan` on macOS 14.4 and later, the device most of its program's estimated cost is on (`coreml.Plan`, `Model.Backend`, `Status.Via` and `Device`): it said "on the CPU" under CoreML, yyhhyyyyyy on Discord, 10-27; an M5 Pro's plan puts all of it on the GPU; after a CoreML read fails it says the CPU and why until one doesn't): the same network from the same int8 weights in float16, written from the downloaded model into a program made by `coreml_gen.py` and compiled once into the model's folder, 512 tokens in about 12 ms where the CPU takes 400, its probabilities within .07 of the publisher's fp32 model where the CPU's are within .13 (the CPU quantizes activations as the int8 model does, CoreML doesn't), so a span near the threshold can fall the other side (a go.mod module path is a secret at .81 on CoreML, .79 on the CPU, .785 in fp32); a window under 16 tokens (`coremlMin`), a Mac before macOS 13 or any failure goes to the CPU, loaded on the first request and let go after 10 minutes with none; or OpenAI's Privacy Filter. With no pick it is AstrLink Guard, the default; a user who had Privacy Filter on before there was a choice keeps it only while AstrLink Guard downloads, which the first masked request starts and which then saves AstrLink Guard as the pick (`masking.picked`, `toGuard`; an hour between tries after a failure). Measured for that default (#3578): on 234 segments of real code and config it found 2 spans where Privacy Filter found 100 (38 of them go.sum hashes after redact's own checks), and with the rules it found 42 of 42 test values to Privacy Filter's 41. Each model has its own download, cache file and turn (`backend`). AstrLink Guard's turn has `guard.Cores()` places (`guardTurn`), so that many requests read at once, and they share those cores: every window, of any request, takes one of `guard.Cores()` tokens before it reads and more only while they are free (`guard.take`), so requests together never read on more cores than one did (the 4 promised xinvxueyuan and shensmart above); the model loads once (`guardLoading`), the idle timer lets go only when no place is held (`guardIdle`), and a text one request is reading another waits for rather than reading it too (`backend.reading`). Before, one request read at a time, and an agent's parallel requests queued behind it (yyhhyyyyyy on Discord, 10-28, who asked for 16 at once). Measured at 1, 2, 4 and 8 requests at once (a Ryzen AI Max+ 395 under WSL, 4 cores): 512-byte requests 4.2, 6.1, 8.1 and 8.4 a second where one at a time was 4.2 whatever came, on 1.9 to 3.9 cores, memory flat at about 150 MB; 4 KB requests already read their 3 windows on 3.6 cores alone, so they stay at 0.6 a second (Windows on the same box: 7.5 to 12.1 a second at 1 to 8, 4 KB 0.7 to 0.8, on at most 3.8 cores). Each request on cores of its own would read 20 a second at 8, on 12 cores, and 4 KB at 8 took 20 cores and 521 MB (953 MB on an M5 Pro), so the bound is the cores, not the count, and there is no setting for it. Through CoreML the GPU is the bound: on an M5 Pro 4 KB requests read 23, 20, 25 and 25 a second at 1, 2, 4 and 8, a model per request no faster, so CoreML reads one window at a time, and only the windows under `coremlMin` or after a failure use the CPU's tokens. Both find at a floor of 0.5 (`pii.Floor`) and give each span its score (AstrLink Guard: the least of its tokens' probabilities, rounded to a float32 so a cached span reads back the same; Privacy Filter: pf-serve's own), and masking keeps those at the model's threshold or over (`pii.Threshold` in `masking.model`): the user's for that model (`settings.RedactModelScore`, this computer's own; Settings → Privacy's Confidence threshold, `pmScore`, 0.5 to 0.95), else the model's own (`MinScore`: Privacy Filter 0.5, AstrLink Guard 0.8, its publisher's). A value known from before follows the same threshold: each value masked remembers the highest score anything found it with (a rule's counts as sure), and one only the model found, under the threshold now, is no longer masked again as known (`noteScores`, `knownSpans` in [`known.go`](../../internal/redact/known.go); before, a raised slider changed nothing for a value masked once: _kaichen on X, 10-28). The defaults stay because on the eval a higher one mostly loses values the checks after the model would have kept (Privacy Filter at 0.85 lost a Chinese name; AstrLink Guard at 0.9 a phone in a tool result), while the false alarms it would drop are mostly dropped by those checks already (Yayoi_no_yume on X). Measured again on 2000 segments of real agent traffic (Codex and Claude Code sessions: prompts, tool arguments and results, a quarter with Chinese) and 30 more labelled cases: AstrLink Guard at 0.88 rather than 0.8 drops 3 false alarms (a model id, a sandbox path word, a token counter) and lets a colleague's name and a signed name through; Privacy Filter at 0.88 rather than 0.5 drops 11 (timestamps in paths, log lines, a plugin id) and lets 6 real names and a phone through (4 of the names in the real traffic). Lowering Guard to 0.5 finds 2 more names in the real traffic and 3 more labelled values, for 10 more false alarms (9 of them Claude Code's `<total_tokens>` counter). Those false alarms are shapes a check can turn away; a threshold can't without losing real values. Privacy Filter is a q8 GGUF (1.6 GB), run by `pf-serve` ([`native/privacy-filter`](../../native/privacy-filter), built from privacy-filter.cpp on ggml and released under `pf-v*` tags; on a GPU it holds the model once, in the device's memory, #3590; every pf-serve keeps the engine's own MoE chunk, `PF_MOE_CHUNK` at the window's 4096, which an RX 470 through Vulkan read 6-9% faster than 2048 in interleaved runs and Metal ~6% faster, and inherits a value the user set): Settings → Privacy's On downloads both (`pii.Download`, each checked against its pinned sha256, through magpie's proxy (`netproxy.Func`, as its other requests; hh0592821 on Discord, whose download went direct before), from hf-mirror first in China (`source.chinaFirst`) and from hf-mirror after Hugging Face everywhere else, the switch off too (`source.modelMirror`), so a poisoned or blocked huggingface.co doesn't stop it; hf-mirror serves AstrLink Guard's files itself and sends Privacy Filter's back to Hugging Face) and saves the setting only once they are on disk (`/api/privacy-model`, [`privacymodel.go`](../../internal/gui/privacymodel.go)). `pii.Find` starts `pf-serve` on the first request, in the model's folder and given the model's file name, never its path (a Windows C program's argv is in the ANSI code page and ggml reads it as UTF-8, so a user folder named in Chinese failed every load with the path in mojibake, AIMetaPhD on X; a pf-serve error that names the file is shown with the whole path) (Metal, Vulkan, else the CPU; one that dies on the GPU, doesn't load there in 40s (`gpuLoadTimeout`, well inside the gateway's 2 minutes), or doesn't answer in time there twice in a row, is started on the CPU, and Settings says why; a request it didn't answer in time once is tried on the GPU again, since a slow request on a weak GPU is no lost GPU (#3682). The GPU is tried again 10 minutes later, doubling to 2 hours while it keeps failing, with no restart (`gpuRetryAfter`), and one that answers is the model's again), joins short texts into 12 KB chunks and maps the spans back, cuts a text over 12 KB into pieces of 12 KB at most that overlap by 1 KB (`pieces`: after a line's end, else a space, of those in the piece's second half the one whose 48 bytes before it hash least (`landmark`), so a line edited early in a text leaves the pieces after it as they were, else at a rune's start), each kept by its own hash, their spans put back at the text's offsets and merged where pieces overlap (`merge`: one found twice is one, one cut by a piece's end is whole), and a value of 8 bytes or more found in a text is found wherever else it is in it, as sure as its surest find (`spread`: the model scored an address on 400 lines alike 0.9 on some and 0.5 on most, wangwu25685333 on X), and asks pf-serve a chunk at a time (#3682: a 480 KB text whole took an RX 470/580 90s and kept nothing when cut off), keeps the last 20000 answers by hash, on disk too (`found-<model>.bin` beside the model: hashes, offsets, labels and each span's score, never text; a `pfound01` file from before scores is read with every span sure, so it masks as it did; written through temp-and-rename, a bad read taken as empty), so a restart doesn't ask the model about every history again (#3682), and ends it after 10 minutes with no request. Its requests take turns on its one pipe (`turn`), and a request reads the cache again when its turn comes; a request waits for its turn only while its context lives (`pii.FindContext`, through `gwmw.ContextTeller`), so one whose client went away leaves the queue (the gateway's 2 minutes leave masking's time out, #3682); the chunk pf-serve is on is finished and kept, so a request sent again asks only about what is left, and between chunks the turn is given up and taken again, by the request waiting with the least left to read (`takeTurn`, `queue`), so a short request goes before the rest of every long one (wangwu25685333 on X: a 5 KB request behind two long ones on 4 cores waited 8.3s in the order they came, 4.5s now). The status (`pii.Now`), `pii.Stop` and the idle timer never wait for a load or a request: `srv.mu` is held only to read or set the state, and `Stop` ends a load or a request under way at once. A request held before routing while it waits for the model is answered 504 saying so, and how to send without it (`unroutedMsg`); one of an older tag (`oldTags`, magpie updated since the download) runs until the pinned tag's is fetched (`upgrade`, on its start), which is then started on the next request and the old deleted ([`internal/pii`](../../internal/pii)). A failure names what ended `pf-serve`: magpie's own end of a request it didn't answer in time, else its exit status or signal and its last stderr lines past ggml's startup chatter (`died`, `lastSaid`). After one, requests are held back at once with that failure, not after a load each, until the model is tried again 30s later, doubling to 10 minutes while it keeps failing (`heldBack`, `retryAfter`; lazyeo on Discord, an M2 whose every request waited out the 504). Until the model is on disk the rules mask alone, and its download starts (`masking.fetch`, an hour between tries; the default's, done, saved by `settings.UseDefaultRedactModel` unless the user picked or turned it off meanwhile; cancelling it is turning it off). A model the user turned on that fails (didn't start, isn't on disk, held back after a failure) panics, so the request is held back with the 503 above rather than sent with the rules' masking alone (`masking.model`, strict); the default's finds nothing then, and the rules mask alone. A model that is only slow is waited for at most 2 seconds from the request's masking start when on by default (`masking.defaultWait`) and 15 seconds when the user turned it on (`masking.strictWait`): its turn, its load and its reading. Past that, or when the request's context ends first, `pii.FindContext` answers with what was known of the request's texts by then (its cache: the history read on earlier turns, and the chunks read meanwhile) and a `*pii.Partial` saying how many it hadn't read; `masking.model` masks those with the model's finds and the rest with the rules alone, logging it at most once a minute (`notePartial`), and the request goes. Nothing the rules mask goes as written. Before, the user's model was waited for with no bound and any end of the wait held the request back whole, its known finds thrown away: in a burst of requests with text the model hadn't read, xRayySummers on X (0.1.1877, Linux) saw masking take 1.2s to 290s in one minute and each request it ended was "masking failed, so nothing was sent". Reproduced on 8 cores of a Linux CPU: AstrLink Guard reads about 6 KB a second there (a 125 KB history 20.8s), and six requests at once with 67 to 145 KB of new tool results each took 62 to 110s, all ending near the last, since the turn goes round between chunks; with the bound each went at 15.0s with the history's finds and the model's for 1 to 4 of its 9 texts. `masking.find` gives a model that doesn't stop for its context 100ms to answer with what it knew (`handBack`) before giving up on it. AstrLink Guard reads on the CPU, so a long Codex history it hasn't seen took minutes there, and every request queued behind it showed masking with no bound (shensmart on Discord, 10-28, magpie in Docker). The wait is given up even where `Find` doesn't stop for its context. A request that stops waiting leaves the chunks after the one being read (`backend.find`), so it doesn't hold the turn; that chunk's answers are kept for the next request. Each model reads a request's unread texts newest first, the last text and a long one's last piece before the rest (`backend.missing`), since an agent's history is oldest first and what a request adds is at its end; AstrLink Guard joins them into chunks of 4 KB, not Privacy Filter's 12 KB (`backend.chunk`, `guardChunk`), so the chunk a request leaves the model finishing past its wait is about a second on 4 cores. The default's request is `pii.Bounded`: what one went without the model's reading is remembered by hash (`backend.sent`), and a request after it reads what it adds first and that backlog `backlogCap` (4 KB) at most, nothing found for the rest, so a history resumed or never read is read a little a request rather than on every core for each one. Measured on a 2 MB, 200-turn Codex history never read, 30 requests 3s apart (`TestLiveHistoryCost`, gated on `MAGPIE_GUARD_FILES`): on a 32-core Linux box 33 to 60 CPU seconds a request before, 23 minutes of CPU over the session's 108s (13 cores busy), 6 to 10 after, 4 minutes over 90s (2.7 cores), and on an M5 Pro's CoreML 2.2 CPU seconds a request for the first three before, the first alone after; nothing runs on after the last request either way. A request the user turned the model on for still reads all of it, waited for `strictWait`.
   - With *Mask secrets* on, the user's own masking rules (`redactRules`, `redact.Rule`: a placeholder name and a prefix or an RE2 pattern, [`internal/redact/rules.go`](../../internal/redact/rules.go)) run before magpie's. Settings → Privacy adds them, removes them, and edits one in place (`POST /api/settings/redact-rule` with the rule's index and its current value, `editRedactRule` in [`internal/gui/api.go`](../../internal/gui/api.go)). Every rule is checked by `redact.CheckRules` when settings are saved; an edit that doesn't compile, matches the empty string, repeats another rule or names a rule no longer at that index is refused with the rules left as they were. The masker reads the rules from settings on each call (`options` in [`internal/masking`](../../internal/masking/masking.go)), so a change applies to the next request.
   - The user's own masked words (`redactWords`, `Options.Words`; Settings → Privacy's *Masked words*) go as `{{TERM_…}}` whatever else is on, and come back as written. They match as written, inside longer words too, unless the user asks: *Masked words in any case* (`redactWordsAnyCase`, `WordsAnyCase`) matches in any capitals by Unicode simple folding, and *Whole words only* (`redactWordsWhole`, `WordsWhole`; acmilan_ on Discord) skips a match with a letter, digit or `_` beside it, and for a number a `.` and digit beside it, so `ABC` masks `name='ABC'` but not `XABCD`, and `123456` not `91234567`, `0.123456` or `123456.78` (`wholeWord` in [`redact.go`](../../internal/redact/redact.go)). Chinese and Japanese letters count as no letter there (`wordRune`): a word of theirs is found inside their text, and `ABC` in 用ABC做 is whole. Both are off by default; a page from before them that saves without the field keeps the user's choice (`api.go`). `TestWordsWhole`, `TestWordsWholeJSON`, `TestCountTokensRedacted`. A word that is a date (`1990-05-12`, `1990/5/12`, `1990年5月12日`, `May 12, 1990`; eight digits alone are not one) matches that calendar date in every common form instead (`dateSpans` in [`dateword.go`](../../internal/redact/dateword.go); JackyCcao on X, who masks their own birthday with birthdays off): `-` `/` `.` `、` or a space between, 年月日 or 년월일, with or without leading zeros, `19900512`, the month's English name, and day-first or month-first only where the day is over 12 or equals the month (`13/05/1990`, never `05/12/1990`, which is December 5th too; such a word matches only as written). A match never has a digit, or a dot and a digit, beside it, whatever *Whole words only* says, so `1990.5.12.1` and `11990-05-12` are not that date. `TestDateWordEveryForm`, `TestDateWordNotAnotherDate`, `TestDateWordDayMonthOrder`.
   - With *Keep a key's form* on (`settings.RedactShape`, `Options.Shape`, under *Mask secrets*; Charles Cao on X), a secret of 16 characters or more, all letters, digits, `-`, `_` or `.`, goes as a made-up key of its form instead of a placeholder: its prefix kept (`sk-proj-`, `ghp_`, `AKIA`, up to four letters and their separator; `keyHead`), the same length and separators, and each other character a random one of its kind (a hex key's any hex digit of its case), drawn from an HMAC of the value under the placeholder key, so one key goes as the same made-up one every turn and a vendor's cache still matches (`shapeOf`, `shaped` in [`internal/redact/shape.go`](../../internal/redact/shape.go)). Personal data, the user's words and anything shorter or with other characters go as placeholders still. A made-up key is kept in the masker's memory with the placeholders, so it is recorded, re-masked in what a vendor sealed, and put back wherever the reply has it, before the placeholders (`restoreShapes`); a stream holds the end of an event that may be the start of one (`shapeTail`, after the last whole one), and a made-up key the vendor sends back unrestored isn't masked again (`outsideShapes`). `TestShapeKeepsForm`, `TestShapeJSON`, `TestShapeStream`. Insights › Masking and Settings' *Try it* mark a made-up key where they mark a placeholder, matching the text against the request's own values only, so a key of the same form the user wrote isn't marked (`maskMatches` in `plugins/redact/page.js`; #3599, masking-records and mask-actions GUI tests).
   - What is done with a value found is the user's per kind (#1977, `redactActions` in settings, `Options.Actions`, set by `POST /api/settings/redact-actions`; a kind left out is masked). `redact.Warn` sends it as written: the masker tells it, with a placeholder no one remembers, and it isn't counted as masked. `redact.Block` masks it as any other, and the gateway holds the request back with a 400 that names the kind and the value's hint, never the value (`blocked`, `maskBlocked` in [`redact.go`](../../internal/gateway/redact.go)); nothing is sent and the record is kept as `Blocked` (`noteBlocked`). A value on the *Never mask* list (`redactAllow`, `Options.Allow`, `POST /api/settings/redact-allow`) goes as written when it is the whole value found, in any case, and isn't told; a longer value it is part of is masked. Settings → Privacy's *Try it* posts a text to `POST /api/masking/preview` ([`masking.go`](../../internal/gui/masking.go)), which masks it as a request would be (`redact.Preview`) and remembers nothing: its placeholders stand for nothing afterwards, its values aren't masked later as seen before, and it returns only the masked text and each hit's kind, finder, action, placeholder and hint. The page keeps the text in no storage.
   - Each request that had anything masked is recorded as it goes to the vendor (`noteMasked` in [`masked.go`](../../internal/gateway/masked.go), from chat, the Codex backend, search, images, video, System One, embeddings and audio; count_tokens is masked and not recorded). The masker says what it swapped through `gwmw.Teller` (`JSONHits`, `TextHits`: each placeholder's kind, its finder, `redact.ByRule`, `ByYours`, `ByWord`, `ByModel` or `ByKnown`, and a hint of its first characters and its length), and [`internal/maskrec`](../../internal/maskrec/maskrec.go) keeps one line per request in a file per day under `~/.config/magpie/masking`, 30 days and 100 MB in all: the agent (the one usage records, `callerOf`, so a request another magpie passed on is named by the agent it came from, not as `magpie`; linkaww on Discord), session, provider, model, and each placeholder with its kind, finder, action and hint, never the value. A chat, an embedding or a video whose group can pass it to another member is held in memory until the request is over (`noteMaskedUntil`, `maskrec.Hold`), and its line names the provider and model the last try went to, not the first planned, which a failover may never have reached; the list sorts each day's lines by key, since a long request's line is written after later ones. The text around each placeholder as the vendor saw it is kept in memory only, for the last 400 records, and what a placeholder stood for only in the masker's own memory (`Teller.Value`); after a restart neither is there. A recent call (`Call.Masked`) and the route trace (`Route.Masked`) carry the counts and the record's key, which the Gateway and Routing pages' badge opens in Settings → Privacy (`/api/masking/*`, [`internal/gui/masking.go`](../../internal/gui/masking.go)). A masker without `Teller`, a plugin's, masks as before and records nothing. Under a test binary testenv isolated nothing is written (`maskrec.inTest`). The page's Clear records… (after a confirm) posts `/api/masking/clear`, and `maskrec.Clear` removes every day's file whole under the store's lock, with the in-memory text around placeholders; a day it can't remove stays listed and is named in the error. What the masker holds for each placeholder stays, so a placeholder still in an agent's context is put back in its answers.
   - `DecidesModel` gives a 400 (`decisionModelRefused`; a decision group, `decisionGroupRefused`), and count_tokens gives the same 400 before the vendor is asked. For OpenRouter, a variant of a model its decisions list names (`inception/mercury-decide:free`, picked by hand) is a decision model too (`openRouterVariant`): OpenRouter lists the model once, and answered such a variant 400 "is a decisions model" on chat/completions (coeo91 on Discord).
   - A gateway key held to some models (`keyHolds`, #882) gets a 403 for any other model. A routing group passes only through `groupAllowed`, which every route asks (the model lists' `entryAllowed`, `serve`, `countHeld`, embeddings, images, videos, decision groups, a subagent's and masking's route): a group of the user's own only when the key names it, whatever its members (kautism on Discord), a found group (`foundGroup`), or any group for a key that names accounts and no model (`Identity.AccountModels`), when the key may use every member; a bare name that is both a group of the user's the key may not use and a model it may use is that model for the key (`keyGroupFor`; `TestGatewayKeyGivenAModelIsNotGivenItsGroup`). A decision model is one: `serveSystemOne` and the decision list another magpie asks for (`DecidersHeader`) hold the key to it, while a group the key names asks its own decider in `askJev` with no key check (`TestGatewayKeyHeldToAGroupKeepsItsDecider`); the key editor offers the decision models (`GET /api/caller-keys/models`, `decides`, `TestCallerKeyModelsOfferDeciders`, `gateway-key-deciders.test.cjs`).
   - A model the user disabled (#1867) gets a 403 for every caller (`idOff`), before anything is sent; see [Disabled models](#disabled-models).
   - Sealed tasks (#619) are checked here. A Codex subagent's task its lead's `spawn_agent` sealed goes only to a ChatGPT account, or back to the Responses provider that answered the lead (`leadProvider`, a relay of the ChatGPT backend such as Sub2API, #1109), relayed as it came even when web search is offered. A lead's turn carrying its subagent's sealed reply (`send_message`) goes back to the Responses provider that answered the lead's own conversation (`leadProvider` of the request's `conversationID`, #1237); otherwise it is refused before anyone is asked (`sealedReader`, `sealedTaskError`). A sealed message (`Message Type: MESSAGE`, not a task) that nobody asked can open is left out instead, an `input_text` note naming its sender in its place (`withoutSealedMessages`, #1613): a lead on a chat provider whose subagent is on one of Codex's own models goes on, as the subagent's final result still reaches it as text. Only a sealed task (`NEW_TASK`, or an item with neither header) is refused. Who answered is first what affinity remembers; when affinity has let it go (a day since, a restart past the 512 conversations `affinity.json` keeps, or the lead's last reply broken off, `unanswered`), the usage log's answers to the lead's thread and the request's own conversation in the last 30 days count too (`loggedAnswers`, #1367), and `leadFirst` reads the lead's key or account from them. A refused or failed call and a title answer nothing, and Codex's own sign-in is told of as the ChatGPT backend. The refusal names who sealed the task when it is known, and says how to go on with it. It also says what is sealed (`sealedTaskItself`, #1855): the task itself, `spawn_agent`'s `message`, which the backend returns sealed (`encrypted_function_args`) and Codex hands the subagent as it came, so `fork_turns="none"`, which only leaves the lead's turns out, doesn't help; nothing in it can be left out for the subagent to go on.
   - A model set for Codex's subagents (`settings.CodexSubagentModel`, the Agents page's Codex "subagent model", willz on Discord) is put on a Codex subagent's request (`x-openai-subagent` read as `kind:collab_spawn`) before this check, whatever model its lead's `spawn_agent` asked for (`codexSubagentPick` in [`codex_subagent.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_subagent.go)): in `codexBackend` for one of Codex's own bare models, which then goes through `serveAgent`, and in `serve` for any other. Any model magpie serves can be set (`provider.SetCodexSubagentModel`, #1633): a task handed as text (a lead magpie served on a chat provider, or multi-agent V1) is read by any model. A request carrying a task the ChatGPT backend sealed (`sealedTaskIn`: a sealed agent message that isn't a `MESSAGE`, which `withoutSealedMessages` leaves out) is put only on a ChatGPT account's model; set to another, the subagent stays on the model asked for, which the sealed gate below handles as with nothing set. When magpie doesn't serve the model set now, or a gateway key held to some models may not use it, the subagent goes on the model asked for too. The route trace keeps both models and the reason it stayed (`Route.Subagent`), and the Routing page's `kindWhy` says it. Set to `lead` (`provider.CodexSubagentLead`, "Same as the lead"), the model is the one the lead's own turns last went on (`codexLeads.lookup`: the parent thread's, else the session's, else the gateway key's last turn), with `SubagentPick.Lead`; `codexBackend` then picks for any model asked, a magpie model too, and a lead on one of Codex's own models takes the subagent to the ChatGPT sign-in on it (in `serve` such a lead leaves the request as asked). With no lead turn seen, the request goes as asked. The lead's turns, titles and every other kind are never moved.
   - With no model set for them in Codex's config (`[memories] extract_model`/`consolidation_model`, read by `agent.CodexSideSet`; a config it can't read counts as set), a Codex memory call on one of Codex's own bare models, made in a conversation whose own turns go on one of magpie's models, follows that model instead of going to the ChatGPT sign-in (`codexFollowTo` in [`codex_follow.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_follow.go), cybercaius on Discord). The model is the parent thread's last own turn (`x-codex-parent-thread-id` or the turn metadata's parent), else its own thread's, else the last one by the gateway key (`codexLeads`, at most 512 threads), else, before any turn has come through magpie (Codex consolidates its memories as it starts), Codex's config `model` when it is one of magpie's (`servedSession`, `TestCodexMemoriesBeforeAnyTurnFollowTheConfig`). A memory call is read after the turn it was started with (`codexMemorySession`, above). A model magpie can't resolve now or one a gateway key may not use leaves the call as it came. A subagent never follows: Codex starts a spawn that names no model on its lead's (codex-rs `child_config.rs`), so a bare model in a subagent's request under a lead on magpie's was named (spawn_agent's `model`, `[agents] default_subagent_model` or a role's config), and with no model set for Codex's subagents in magpie it goes on that model (`TestCodexSubagentKeepsTheModelItWasNamed`, mengxiao on Discord); only `settings.CodexSubagentModel` moves it (`codexSubagentPick`). Codex's auto-review follows the session only when `settings.CodexAutoReview` is `session` (`settings.CodexAutoReviewSession`): `codexcat` then has each of magpie's entries name itself as `auto_review_model_override`, and Codex's own entries keep OpenAI's, since codex-auto-review is free on a paid ChatGPT sign-in (#1593). On a Free plan it isn't (cybercaius measured it on Discord), so with the setting unset and Codex's live sign-in on the `free` plan (`codexcat.AutoReviewModel`, the plan from `provider.CodexSignInPlan`), it counts as `session` (`TestCodexAutoReviewOnAFreePlan`).
   - A model set for Claude Desktop's subagents (`settings.DesktopSubagentModel`, the Agents page's Claude Desktop "subagent model") is put on a subagent's turn in Desktop's Code tab or Cowork after `desktopTurn`, the tiers and the groups, whatever model its lead or its tier asked for (`desktopSubagentPick` in [`desktop_subagent.go`](../../internal/gateway/desktop_subagent.go)). Desktop runs its Claude Code with `CLAUDE_CODE_PROVIDER_MANAGED_BY_HOST=1`, under which Claude Code ignores the `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` magpie writes into its settings (see [agent wiring](agent-wiring.md)). A subagent's request is one with `x-claude-code-agent-id` (`subagentOf`) from `claude-desktop`, and only one that offers tools is its turn: Claude Code's tool-less helper calls inside a subagent, on the haiku tier's model, stay where they are, and so do the lead's turns, terminal Claude Code's and every other agent's. The model set must be served by its exact id (`provider.DesktopSubagentUnserved`: `Resolve` takes any model name under a provider that is on), enabled, and allowed to a gateway key held to some models (`groupAllowed` for a group); otherwise the subagent goes on the model asked for and the route trace says why (`Route.Subagent`, said on the Routing page by `subagentWhy`, its `Kind` staying empty). `Route.Model` and the ledger's `Requested` keep the model the subagent asked for. `count_tokens` counts on the model set the same way (`countTokens`). The setting is read on each request, so a change needs no restart of Desktop, and a provider renamed since it was set is read by its new id (`RenamedRef`). A Desktop on another computer that reaches magpie with a named gateway key is read as Claude Code (`agentOf`), so none of Desktop's routing, this included, applies to it. A subagent's turn, moved or not, is never kept as the session's model in `claude-desktop.model` (`desktopTurn`'s `bySubagent`): a subagent on the opus tier's model took the session's place until the lead's next turn, and Desktop's titles asked for meanwhile went to it.
   - Desktop's subagent effort (`settings.DesktopSubagentEffort`, `desktopSubagentEffort` in [`desktop_subagent.go`](../../internal/gateway/desktop_subagent.go)) applies to the same subagent tool turns, independently of model replacement. Empty leaves requests unchanged. Set, it takes the place of a request model's effort suffix before the existing attempt-level effort fitting: a member fixed at an effort wins, then this pick fitted within group/model levels, then the turn's or request's effort. `withFixedEffort` can enable reasoning when it was disabled, or disable it with `none` on a model that supports that level; a reasonless model is still sent no reasoning fields. `countTokens` applies the pick after restoring any unmasked body. `Route.Effort` keeps the incoming effort, `Route.SubagentEffort` the configured pick, and each try and usage record the level actually sent; the Routing story explains the configured pick even when no model was replaced. A nonempty model pick already classifies the turn, so the effort check reuses it; empty effort costs only a held-settings read. The next request takes a changed or cleared effort without a Desktop restart.
   - Every Claude Code subagent's turn, Desktop's or a terminal's, moved or not, keeps its `x-claude-code-agent-id` in `Route.SubagentID` (`subagentTurnOf` in [`desktop_subagent.go`](../../internal/gateway/desktop_subagent.go)): a request with the id that offers tools and names no kind, the turn the two settings above take. Claude Code 2.1.295 sends the id from any agent context but the main one; its `x-claude-code-request-class` and `x-claude-code-agent-type` hints go only to a first-party base URL or with `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS`, so magpie doesn't get them. A Desktop pick or effort that found the turn is trusted, so the body isn't decoded again, and a request without the id isn't decoded at all. The Routing page tags it as Codex's subagents are (`tagKind` in [`routing.js`](../../internal/gui/assets/routing.js)): in the request list, the story, the replay and the tray panel, with the reason `kindWhy` gives Codex's. `Kind` stays empty, so its purpose stays unmarked in the Purpose filter, as in the ledger and on the Usage page. The ledger's `Subagent` keeps the same id, and also marks a subagent's tool-less helper calls, which aren't tagged. A route recorded before this has no id and no tag.
   - A request a stand-in of magpie's put on another model than the agent named keeps that model and the rule in `Route.Moved` (`ModelMove{To, Why}` in [`trace.go`](../../internal/gateway/trace.go), set by `move` in `serve`): Claude Desktop's moves (`desktopMove` in [`desktop.go`](../../internal/gateway/desktop.go): `desktop-title`, `desktop-check`, `desktop-gone`, each with a `-first` form when it went to the first model listed for want of a chat turn or a menu pick, `desktop-tier`, `desktop-beside`, `desktop-aside`), `classifier`, `claude-tier`, `stand-in` (the agent's config), `codex-turn` (`codexMemoryStandIn`) and `privacy` (`maskRouteFor`). A subagent's move says its own (`Route.Subagent`), and a group or `Resolve` names no move. The Routing page tags such a row Title or Stand-in (`movedTag`, `movedWhy` in the routing plugin's client.js), in the list, the replay and the tray panel, and says why in the row's title and once in the story; a kind's tag wins, and its story still says the move, but for Desktop's connection check (`connection_check`), whose own story says where it went (`movedSays`). Neo on Discord read Desktop's title on `chatflowsai/claude-opus-5-5` (the only Claude-looking id Desktop is shown, so its own pick) served on the chat's `agentrouter/gpt-6.1-sol` as the model swapped unasked (`TestClaudeDesktopMoveSaysWhy`, `routing-moved-why.test.cjs`). Codex backend rewrites done before `serve` (`codexFollowTo`, `codexMaskRoute`) show the moved model as asked and have no `Moved`.
   - A Chat or Responses client's effort spelled `off` (`reasoning_effort`, or `reasoning.effort`, any case) is read at the top of `serve` as magpie's own off level, `none` (`offAsNone`, plugins#334), and a model asked `<model>:off` as `<model>:none` (`askedAt`): every check and route below reads `none`, a model with an off level is sent its own spelling of it, and one without goes at its least level as `none` asked does (`fitEffort`). `off` itself is never sent on. `saysOff` looks for it without parsing the body, which only a body naming it pays for.
   - A level a vendor names in words of its own (`catalog.EffortAliases`: Trae's `light` and `extra_high`, which Our Free Model's plugin lists) is the level it is (`EffortLevel`): `effortOf` reads it as `low` or `xhigh`, and `NearestEffort` places a model's own words by it, so `low` asked of a model listing `light` is sent as `light`, and its `light` as it is (nianshou5 on Discord; `TestNearestEffortTakesVendorWords`, `TestPluginThinkingOff`). dsh and Pi offer such a model's levels as theirs, sent in its words (`modelEffort` in [`agents.go`](../../internal/agent/agents.go); `TestDshOffersTraeNamedLevels`).
   - Rules and image inputs are handled here too.
   - A rule is decided as a user's turn begins and held for the rest of it (`ruleFor`, [`rules.go`](../../internal/gateway/rules.go)). What counts as the user's own words is one helper, `userWords`/`userSaid` in [`prompt.go`](../../internal/gateway/prompt.go), which the turn count (`turnIn`), the classifier's question (`userText`), the conversation's key (`firstWords`, in `ruleKey` and the affinity scope), the conversation's id where no session header names it (`userOpens`, in `conversationID`), Copilot's Auto (`lastUserText`) and the prompt breakdown's turns (`userSays`) all go by. It drops `<system-reminder …>` blocks (a `source=` attribute too) and any text that begins as an agent writes for the model alone: Codex's `<environment_context>`, `<user_instructions>`, `<turn_aborted>`, `<user_shell_command>`, `<subagent_notification>` and `# AGENTS.md instructions for …`; Claude Code's and ZCode's `<task-notification>`, `<subagent-notification>`, agent lists ("Available agent types …", "New agent types are now available …"), TodoWrite and task-tool nudges and rewind notices; ZCode's `<plugin_reference>`; the Codex app's `<in-app-browser-context …>`; Reasonix's `<session-context …>`, its snapshot of the workspace (keeyangyy, #3938). The Codex app's context before `## My request (for Codex):` is dropped and what follows it kept (the `injected` list beside `agentsFor`). A user message with nothing of the user's in it (and no image or file) begins no turn: after an answer or a tool round it carries the turn on (`within`), so the turn's rule and stick hold and the classifier isn't asked; right after the user's words it leaves their turn as it began. The classifier is asked about the last user message that has words of the user's, only those words (`userText`: past 4000 runes, its first 3000 and last 1000). It is told what came before it too (`turnBefore`, @HirotaNagai on X: a short follow-up said alone, 继续 or fix that, came out the hardest kind): the user's message before it, the same way and cut to 1000 runes (`priorCap`: the first 750 and the last 250), and the assistant's last reply between the two, its text parts alone and their last 1000 runes. A tool call's arguments, a tool's result, thinking, a search's hits and the system prompt are never in either, and a reply of only tool calls is none. Jev gets them in its `state` as `previous_user_message` and `previous_assistant_reply` beside `message`, `previous_message_kind` and `previous_message_reasoning`, each only when there is one, and its intent, level and effort instructions name the ones there are (`jevState`, `priorAsk` in [`decide.go`](../../internal/gateway/decide.go)); a classifier asked in words gets them in `<previous_user_message>` and `<previous_assistant_reply>` before `<message>` (`writePrior`). Both come from the request the rules parsed, so Privacy masks them as it masks the message, and the answer kept for a message is kept for it with what came before (`classify`'s key). `TestTurnBeforeEachProtocol`, `TestTurnBeforeCuts`, `TestJevIsToldWhatCameBefore`, `TestChatClassifierIsToldWhatCameBefore`, `TestClassifyKeepsAnAnswerPerTurnBefore`, `TestJevSeesTheTurnBefore`. `firstWords` hashes the words of the first user message that has any, so an instruction file or notice the agent changes beside them doesn't move the conversation to a new key and lose its stick mid-turn (#1826: ZCode's TodoWrite nudge classified as a new, harder turn, and three keys for one ZCode session). `TestTurnInSkipsWhatTheAgentPutIn`, `TestUserTextSkipsWhatTheAgentPutIn`, `TestFirstWordsAreWhatTheUserSaid`, `TestIntentHoldsPastWhatTheAgentPutIn`.
   - A rule's members go first (`ruleOver`, `applyRule`): the members it names, in order, then those the matching rules after it name (`ruleOrder`), each not resting, then the group's others to fail over to. A member that is a group in the group is one member here: its models keep the order the group's own routing planned them, where ranking each by the group's listed order pinned a rotate group's first member on every request the rule put it first (#2407, `TestRuleNamingARotateGroupKeepsItsTurns`). That holds within the turn too, at the group's rule and at each nested group's (`nestedRules`): the one the turn stays on (affinity) stays first while it is of the rule's member that goes first, and the member's others follow it ahead of the group's others, so a failure fails over within the member the rule chose before going outside it. One the turn was kept on outside that member, which took over when the member failed, isn't held there: once one of the member is out of its rest, the turn's next request goes back to it (`Affinity.Why` `rule`). Before #1946 (XcodeFish) a held rule's order wasn't applied to a kept turn at all: a coding-quality member failing mid-turn failed over to coding-fast, the parent's first, and the turn stayed there. `TestHeldRuleFailsOverWithinItsMember`. Two groups in the group may share a model (the same provider, model and effort): it is asked once a request, where the walk met it first, and `provider.Member.Also` keeps its later places, each with its own path, `At` (its place in the walk) and Fast. A rule naming the later group has the model as that group does (`Member.In`, `membersNamed`), in that group's own order, and the nested rules below it, Fast and the first token's wait go by that group's way down (`homed`, `homeCands`). Before #2361 (davisjiahao) such a rule found none of the shared models, read unready, and the group ahead of it answered. A pause rule naming the later group still leaves a shared model to the group ahead of it. `rule_shared_test.go`.
   - A classifier's timeout is global (`Settings.ClassifyTimeout`, JSON `classifyTimeout`, seconds, in [`settings.go`](../../internal/settings/settings.go)). Missing or 0 keeps the 8-second default; saving accepts 1–60 seconds or 0 and rejects negative values or values above 60. `Settings.ClassifyWait` bounds a hand-edited value above 60 to 60 seconds and uses the default for a value below 1. `classifyTimeout()` reads it for each call: both `askChat` in [`classify.go`](../../internal/gateway/classify.go) and `askJev` in [`decide.go`](../../internal/gateway/decide.go) give the classifier that time, including its provider's failover. A request shares one such wait across every routing group it goes through (`Server.askWaited`, from the first classifier asked): a nested ruled group asked with less left gets only that, one asked once it is spent goes by its order at once (`waitSpent`, "spent by the routing group above"), and a classifier or decision key cut short that way is not rested (`cutShort`). Each nested level had a whole wait of its own before, so three ruled levels whose classifiers didn't answer held a request past the unrouted watch's 2 minutes, a 504 (heatonz on Discord, v0.1.2215; `TestNestedGroupsShareTheClassifierWait`, `TestDecisionKeyCutShortIsNotRested`). Settings → Models → Replies → *Intent classifier timeout* (`renderReplies` in [`app.js`](../../internal/gui/assets/app.js)) offers 8, 15, 30 and 60 seconds and shows a custom valid value as its own choice. A change applies to the next classifier call, with no restart; the existing fallback, 30-second cooldown (`classifyRest`) and 10-minute answer cache (`classifyKeep`) still apply.
   - A classifier that can't say which kind a turn is (an error, no answer within `classifyTimeout()`, an answer that isn't a number, or resting `classifyRest` after a failure) leaves a turn whose conversation had an intent, still one of this turn's, with that intent (`Classified.Fallback` is `previous`, its `Error` kept), so the turn goes where the turn before went rather than to the group's first member; with no intent before, no rule with an intent matches, as before. The Routing page says the turn kept it (`classWhy`). The turn's decision itself (`turnRules`) is kept in memory only: after a restart a turn under way waits for the next one and the stick holds it meanwhile. `TestIntentClassifierFailsKeepsThePreviousIntent`, `routing-intent-fallback.test.cjs`.
   - A group's classifier is asked only for that group's own turns: a request whose model is the group (`group/<id>`, its bare name, or a stand-in an agent's config names it by: Claude Code's tiers, Codex's bare name), or a group in it that its rules or order put first (`nestedRules`). A request for another model or group asks it nothing. The Routing page's card says why the classifier couldn't say the last time *that group* asked it (`GroupClassifierFailure`, kept by `classifiedFor` in `ruleFor`): the classifier's own words and time, resting or not, until it answers that group, and nothing once the group's classifier changed. Keyed by the classifier model alone, a group nothing reached showed another group's failure on a classifier they share (jjt on Discord). A classifier on a subscription out of its quota is not asked of the vendor again until the reset: out of quota, its account rests until the quota comes back (`restAfter`: the time the refusal names, else when the account's window renews), and the classifier's call meanwhile gets that refusal back from magpie, at most once each `classifyRest`. `TestClassifierFailureIsTheGroupsOwn`.
   - The classifier's requests are magpie's own (#1839). Asked in words, they go out with `RouterAgent`'s User-Agent (`magpie-router/1`), so their records' agent is magpie and `callKind` marks them `routing`; a decision model's (Jev, System One) record is written with `Kind: "routing"` too. They go to the same key or account as any other request and count in its totals, while `usage.Group.Own` (`Record.ByMagpie`: agent `magpie` or one of its `magpie-` User-Agents, with search's and image description's) says how many of an account's, key's or model's calls, tokens and cost were magpie's own. A classifier set to a subscription's model (`modelRef.Sub`, or a group whose first member is one) asks that subscription as every turn begins, twice when it picks both intent and effort in words; the Routing page says so beside it (`classifierSub` in `plugins/routing/client.js`). `TestIntentRoutesATurn`, `TestJevPicksTheIntent`, `TestSummaryCountsMagpiesOwnCallsApart`, `TestByMagpie`, `TestServedRefSaysASubscription`, `own-requests.test.cjs`.
   - A rule may name several members (`Rule.More` after `Rule.Use`, `more` in the group's JSON, `use=a,b` typed; monkeyin_92 on X: one rule, several models, not a rule each). They go first in that order, ahead of the later matching rules' members (`ThenUses` leaves out every one the rule names) and the group's others: when the first fails or rests the rule's next takes the request, and the hit says so (`RuleHit.More`; `Instead` is that next one with `Unready` false, where an `Instead` from a later rule comes with `Unready` true). `Uses`, `SetUses`, `MapUses`, `Without` and `Names` in [`grouprule.go`](../../internal/provider/grouprule.go) are how every reader goes through them: `cleanRules` (each must be the group's, each named once, a pause names one), the turn's hold, the tool rounds and compaction (`ruleFor`, the rule's first that takes the summary), `nestedRules` and `ruleLed` (the one of them a nested group's turn stays on), `ruleEffort` (the rule's `useEffort` asked of each), `ruledEntry` (what a group takes is what every one of them takes), a model's rename and removal (a rule goes with its last), `ShareGroup` and its import (one this magpie hasn't is left out of the rule), the CLI's `groups rules` and the TUI. The editor draws a chip per member with a + for the next and an × on each while there is more than one (`rUses`/`setUses` in [`plugins/routing/client.js`](../../internal/gui/assets/plugins/routing/client.js)). A rule of one member writes no `more`, so a group saved before it reads and saves as it was.
