A magpie from before it reads `use` alone: its requests go to the first, and the others are plain members in the group's order; if it saves the group, `more` is dropped. `TestRuleSeveralModelsInOrder`, `TestCleanRulesSeveralModels`, `routing-rule-models.test.cjs`.
   - The editor's rules are checked top first, so their order is theirs to set: a rule's number is its handle (`ruleHandle`, `.rt-rhandle`), dragged (`dragRows`, as the members are) or moved a place with Alt+↑/↓, the rules renumbered at once; the grip beside it shows on hover only, and Move up and down stay under its ⋯ (`moveRule`). Saved as the group's `rules` in their new order. `routing-rule-drag.test.cjs`.
   - A rule can set the reasoning its member is asked for (`Rule.UseEffort`, `useEffort` in the group's JSON, `use-effort=low` typed; #2633, dengtongcai: an intent picks the model and the effort as a pair). It is an output like `Use`, not a condition like `Effort`. `ruleFor` copies it onto the hit (`RuleHit.UseEffort`) from the rule that put a member first, outer or nested; `ruleOver` keeps that rule's members, and `ruleEffort` gives each candidate of them the level fitted to its model's own (`fitLevel`), which `withFixedEffort` writes into the request's field for its protocol (Chat's `reasoning_effort`, Responses' `reasoning.effort`, Anthropic's thinking budget or `output_config.effort`) even when the agent asked none. A member fixed at an effort keeps its own; otherwise the rule's beats the model's suffix, the turn's pick and the agent's pick, and isn't bounded by the group's levels. A model whose levels aren't known (`Efforts` empty) is asked as the agent asked, as with the auto verdict. The members the group fails over to are asked it too (#3664): the effort is the turn's decision, so when the rule's own member is turned away the member that takes the request over is asked the same level, fitted to its model, rather than the agent's. A rule that names the candidate goes before one it only fails over from, and a nested group's rule decides for that group's members only: when the whole nested group fails, the outer group's member outside it is asked as the agent asked, or by the outer group's rule (`TestIntentEffortSurvivesTheGroupsFailover`, `TestNestedRuleEffortStaysInItsGroup`). A Gemini-protocol client's request is left as it came, as for every effort magpie sets. `cleanRules` refuses a value outside `provider.Efforts` and a pause with one. The try records it (`Try.RuleEffort`), and the Routing page says the rule set it. `rule_effort_test.go`, `grouprule_useeffort_test.go`, `routing-rule-effort.test.cjs`.
   - A rule by folder (`Rule.Folders`, `folders=~/work/acme,~/oss`; Cario0x on X: routing by the project being worked on) matches when the folder the agent works in is one of them or inside one (`provider.FolderUnder`, [`rulefolder.go`](../../internal/provider/rulefolder.go)): at a path's boundary, so `/a/b` is not under `/a/bc`; `~` is the home folder; a Windows drive is one with its WSL mount (`C:\work` is `/mnt/c/work`) and a `\\wsl$\<distro>` or `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>` share is the distro's own path; case doesn't count on macOS and Windows, nor ever for a drive. A rule outside folders (`Rule.ExceptFolders`, `except-folders=~/big-task`; #1606, Ti800) matches when the folder is in none of them, and an agent that says none isn't known to be in one, so it matches; a rule may have both (`Rule.ByFolder`, `inFolders`). The folder is read from the request only when a rule of the group has folders or except-folders (`workDir` in [`workdir.go`](../../internal/gateway/workdir.go)), in each agent's own words, taken from captured requests: Claude Code's ` - Primary working directory: <path>` (its `<`/`>` as entities; ` (was <old>)` after a change dropped), Codex's `<environment_context>` `<cwd>`, Pi's `<cwd>` or `Current working directory:` in the system prompt, and OpenCode's `Working directory:` inside its `<env>` block. The caller's own layer — the system prompt and its `{role: "system"}` messages — is read whole, a `claude --bare` run's `CWD: <path>` among the forms; a user turn counts only for the forms a caller states in one (`<cwd>`, OpenCode's `<env>`, Claude Code's `<system-reminder>`, which says a folder it moved to): a working-directory line a user message quotes is no caller's word (#4940). Never the model's words or a tool's result, and the last said wins. An agent that says none has no folder, and no `folders=` rule matches it (unknown, never empty). The same reading tells a plugin's fetch where its agent works ([provider-plugins](provider-plugins.md#where-the-agent-works-for-a-plugins-fetch)). A pause rule may hold by folder too (below). The trace's `RuleHit.Folder` is the folder read, and the Routing page tells it beside the rule's `folder …` condition. `TestFolderUnder`, `TestRuleMatchesByFolder`, `TestParseRuleFolders`, `TestWorkDirOfEachAgent`, `TestRuleByFolder`, `TestRuleMatchesExceptFolders`, `routing-folder.test.cjs`. Rules by folder are never in a shared group (`ShareGroup` drops every `ByFolder` rule).
   - A rule by agent (`Rule.Agents`, `agents=codex,sillytavern`) matches the agent the gateway names the request's (`agentOf` in [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go): its `magpie-<name>` key, else its User-Agent), in lower case (`cleanRules`, `MatchesBesidesIntent`, `Pauses`). A client magpie doesn't know is named by its User-Agent's product name, before the first `/` or space (`usage.AgentOf`), so a rule can hold SillyTavern or one's own app (raikyou on Discord). The editor's agent picker lists the agents magpie knows, then those `/api/usage?period=30d` heard from that it doesn't (not `other`, nor `magpie`), and takes a name typed in its filter as the gateway reads a User-Agent (`uaName` in [`plugins/routing/client.js`](../../internal/gui/assets/plugins/routing/client.js), through the picker's `field.typed`): "MyApp/2.0 (Linux)" is `myapp`. `routing-rule-agent-typed.test.cjs`.
   - A pause rule (`Rule.Pause`, `pause=<model>`; John on Discord: a model of the group paused in its vendor's peak hours) leaves the model it names out of the group while it holds (`provider.PausedOut`, on `ruleClock`): the group's own rules and each nested group's on the way down to a member, a group named taking all its models, and a model named taking it wherever a group in the group has it (#625). It runs before planning, on every request rather than once a turn, so the model is not first, not a failover, and not kept by affinity, and comes back as soon as the hours end. It holds by hours, days, agents and folders only (`cleanRules` refuses another condition), and is not a send-to rule: `Matches`, `MatchRule`, `ThenUses`, `Ruled` and the compaction rules leave it out. A send-to rule naming a paused model finds it unready and the group routes as without it. The trace keeps what was paused (`GroupRef.Paused`), and the Routing page says so. Every model paused answers 503 (`pausedError`) naming each rule. Embeddings go by the same pauses; manual groups have no rules (`Live`), so none pause there. A pause by folder (#1606, Ti800: a subscription's quota kept for the long task in one folder, `pause=<model> except-folders=~/big-task`) reads the folder only when such a pause is on the way (`provider.PausesByFolder`, then `workDir`): outside its except-folders, which includes a request that says no folder (a subagent's or a small model's that doesn't), the model is left out; with `folders=`, only requests known to be in them. Embeddings and System One's decision calls have no folder. `Paused.Folder`/`ByFolder` carry what was read, and the Routing page says the folder or that none was said. `rules_pause_test.go`, `TestPauseByFolder`, `TestPauseByFolderRule`, `TestParsePauseByFolder`, `routing-pause-folder.test.cjs`.
   - A rule's hours and days (`Rule.Time`, `provider.TimeWindow`) are read on `ruleClock` in the zone they were set in (`TimeWindow.Zone`, an IANA name; gaubeebangeel on X: magpie on a cloud server in UTC, its user in Beijing, the hours 8 off). The GUI saves the browser's zone when the hours or days are changed in the editor and leaves a rule's zone alone otherwise; the CLI (`magpie group rule add … time=09:00-18:00@Asia/Shanghai` or `tz=`) and the TUI save the one typed, or this computer's (`provider.LocalZone`: `$TZ`, `time.Local`, the `/etc/localtime` link) when it has an IANA name. A window with no zone, every one saved before zones were kept, stays on magpie's own clock. A zone magpie doesn't know is refused on save (`TimeWindow.clean`), and one in a file edited by hand makes the window never hold (`zoneOf`): the rule is as if absent, never always on and never hours off. Zones come from Go's embedded tzdata, so Windows knows them too. `/api/groups` gives magpie's clock (`clock`: its zone and offset in seconds), and when it differs from the hours' zone the Routing page tells the zone beside them, on the group's rules and in the editor (`zoneNote` in [`plugins/routing/client.js`](../../internal/gui/assets/plugins/routing/client.js)): the zone's name, or the server's offset for hours with none. `TestRuleTimeWindowInItsZone`, `TestRuleTimeWindowInItsZoneAtTurnStart`, `TestRuleTimeWindowUnknownZone`, `TestParseRuleTimeZone`, `TestGroupRuleTimeZoneCmd`, `routing-time-zone.test.cjs`.
3. Candidates come from `planGroup` for a group or `plan` for a provider. They are filtered by `sealedReaders` and `allowedCandidates`. An account past its share is held out of the candidates (`capHeld`): a usage cap's share, each window at its own where the account gives it one (`AccountWindowCaps`, willz on Discord: 50% of the five hours, 40% of the week) and at the account's cap otherwise, or 100% of a window for a Codex account set not to spend its credits (`provider.HoldCaps`, `WindowCaps.Of`, `CodexNoCredits`). A ChatGPT account's call on `codex-auto-review`, which OpenAI counts in no window, is held by no usage cap, only by credits it may not spend (`freeOnChatGPT`, #1593, `TestAccountCapLetsAutoReviewThrough`); a Free plan's call is capped as any other (`TestAccountCapHoldsAutoReviewOnAFreePlan`). The account is held while any window is past its own share, and `cappedError` and the trace name the fullest such window's share and cap. When every candidate is held and one held for its credits spends its resets by itself, `autoReset` spends a reset on it and it is tried (`planned.held`). A Codex account let spend its credits is never held or refused for its week, so before a try goes to one whose week its last reading says is used up and that spends a reset when its week runs out, `resetBeforeCredits` spends one first, one a week at most, in routing and in the relay (`resetBeforeCreditsOn`); a reset is free and credits cost money (tongzhenbao on X, `TestCodexResetBeforeCredits`). Otherwise a capped or barred set returns `cappedError` or `barredError`. A provider with Stop at the caps on (`CapStop`, plugins#349) answers that 429 instead of going on to its fallbacks when every primary account is held (`plan`, `TestCapStopSkipsTheFallbacks`); off, the default, the fallbacks take it as before. A careful provider (`CapCareful`, plugins#349, off by default, `cap_careful.go`) has `readCareful` read a capped account's usage again before planning when its reading is over 15s old within 5 points of a cap, or over 5m old at all, waiting at most 10s and asking a failed account again at most every 15s; `capHold` then holds an account whose usage was never read, whose last read failed or whose reading is still that old (`carefulDoubt`, `Weighed.Unread` in the trace), as one at its cap is: unknown is not spent (`TestCarefulHoldsWhatItCantTell`, `TestCarefulReadsAgainNearTheCap`, `TestCarefulEveryAccountUnread`, `TestCarefulOffByDefault`). Planning goes by the last reading, so turns sent together were each planned on the same one: an account a point under its cap took all of them and went past it by what they used between them (dyzdyz010 on Discord: capped at 90%, used to 100%). In serve's walk an account within 5 points of a usage cap (`nearCapOf`, `nearCapPoints`, `cap_near.go`) takes one turn at a time (`Server.nearCap`, its turn held until the try ends): a turn that finds it busy goes to the next candidate first, the account moved behind them once (`nearBehind`); the last candidate waits. Once its turn comes `capHeld` judges it again, by what the reply before it left (Codex's `codex.rate_limits`, Claude's `rate_limit_event`), or, when that reply said nothing of the account's usage, by a reading taken again then (`nearCapAgain`, at most `carefulWait`); held, it is passed over, and when it was the last the agent gets `cappedError`'s 429. The cap is passed by one turn at most, as a gateway key's limit is, Claude Code accounts as Codex's (`TestNearCapTakesOneTurnAtATime`, `TestNearCapWaitsThenHolds`, `TestNearCapReadsAgainAfterASilentReply`, `TestFarFromCapTurnsGoTogether`, `TestNearCapClaudeAccountsTakeOneTurnEach`). A conversation kept on its account by Stays: Strict goes on past the cap as `strictKeep` keeps it, and takes no turn there (`TestStrictAffinityGoesPastTheCap`). Then `affine` moves the conversation's last answerer first, unless it is resting or nearly used up. An `X-Magpie-Account` header (`AccountHeader`) pins the request to one account: only that account is tried, and when it can't answer the caller is told why. A header value is ASCII on the wire and fetch refuses one that isn't, so an account whose user isn't ASCII (北冥, plugins#615) is named percent-encoded; `pinnedName` decodes it, the value as written matches too, and the trace and the call's record name it decoded (`TestAccountPinnedPercentEncoded`).
4. The candidate loop calls `attempt` on each candidate in turn, through a `holdWriter`. The writer holds the reply until it is clearly passing. A candidate that fails before anything is sent rests (`failure`): out of credit for 30 minutes (a key longer each time it is refused so again once its rest has ended: 30 minutes, 2 hours, 8 hours, a day, 3 days, then a week (`creditLadder`, `creditBackoff`), the same rung while a rest still runs, a new run after "Try now" (`Unrest`) or an answer; each such rest, its rung and when it ends, is kept in `rests.json` beside `providers.json` (written through a renamed temp file whenever one is set or dropped, `saveRests`) and read once as the gateway starts to listen (`loadRests`), so a restart rests out what is left and climbs on from the rung: an ended rest keeps its rung there as in memory, unless it ended over `restsKeep` (35 days) ago, a key no longer in `providers.json` is let go, and a file or `providers.json` that can't be read or parsed adds no rest (PaulT on Discord, `TestKeyCreditRestOutlivesRestart`, `TestKeyCreditRestFileCorrupt`); a refusal that names its reset rests till then, and one that says it can pass soon (a `Retry-After`, OpenRouter's in-flight budget or "can only afford", a "try again in 30 seconds", `creditSoon`) rests 30 minutes or less and doesn't climb; a remote magpie's model rests 30 minutes as before: PaulT on Discord, `TestKeyOutOfCreditRestsLongerEachTime`; a ChatGPT account until the reset its refusal names, else until its full window renews, else 5 hours, `chatgptCreditRest`, and a reading finding its window renewed has it back: PaulT on Discord, `TestChatGPTOutOfCreditsRestsLong`), out of quota until reset, rate limited until retry-after, otherwise a minute and longer on repeats: doubled each time, up to `longestRetry` (10 minutes) for a failure and `longestRateRest` (30 minutes) for a rate limit back as soon as its rest is over, or up to the provider's own `restCap` in minutes (`Provider.RestCapOr`, at most `MaxQuotaRest`; `magpie provider rest-cap <id> 4h`, #2008: a free key down for hours was asked every 10 minutes). A time the vendor names stands over it as before (`TestOwnRestCap`). An error `failure` reads as out of credit is `retryable` at any 4xx, so the next account or member is asked and this one rests as credit; before, a 400 or 422 counted only by `quotaWords`, and SuperMagic's 400 「您的可用积分已不足…通过「充值」通道补充积分」 went to the agent with another account of 6,467 points unasked, the empty one never rested (#4095, mycf_711; `TestCredit400MovesToTheNextAccount`). A shape refusal that also says so (`creditWords` in `shapeRefused`) is the credit it says. The failure count is kept per key or account (`restKey`), and any of its models' answers clears it (`served`). Each model's own count (`restID`) is kept beside it and cleared only by that model's answer (`servedCandidate`): once it is past the account's, a sibling model answered since it last did, so the failure is the model's and it climbs its own ladder and rests alone, its siblings still served (#2241, weizsw: an Antigravity seat's gemini-3.5-flash-lite rested a minute 41 times of 43 while gemini-3.8-flash kept answering; `TestModelLadderClimbsPastSiblingAnswers`). Every model failing with no answer between still climbs the account's ladder and rests the account, and a candidate naming no model has the account's count alone. `rest-cap <id> off` (`provider.RestCapOff`, `restCap: -1`) is for a relay or account pool whose unknown errors come now and then and pass by the next request (Kismet Alan on Discord): a failure that says nothing of why (`failOther`: a 5xx, a dropped connection) then rests nobody and isn't counted, its trace rest `by: "off"` with no time left. A 401 or 403, a model not served, an account's window full, a rate limit (magpie's own 30-minute cap), quota, credit and a restriction still rest it as before (`TestRestCapOff`). A provider the user added can be set never to rest for a limit instead (`provider.LimitRestOff`, `limitRestOff` in providers.json; the editor's Advanced fold, "Set it aside when out of quota or rate limited", ticked by default): a refusal `restAfterMarked` reads as out of quota, out of credit or rate limited, and any 429, then rests nothing, isn't counted or sunk, and is `by: "off"`; `restDrawer` rests no image model of it. The next request is sent to it as usual. It is for a relay whose limits pass in a moment (zoecats on Discord: a local aggregator said "quota exhausted" while it switched upstreams, and magpie answered itself for 30 minutes; `TestLimitRestOffKeepsAsking`, `TestLimitRestOffRestsNothingForALimit`). A 401, a model not served and a plain failure still rest it. The shortest cap otherwise is a minute, the first rest; `rest-cap <id> 30s` is turned away and names `off`. A 400, 403, 404 or 422 that says the model isn't served (`unservedWords`) and says nothing of quota, credit or a rate limit rests only that key's or account's model (`modelRefused`, keyed by `restID`, its backoff counted apart): its other models are still served first in order (#1235, SenseNova's 403 "model is not available in the current token plan"). A Google account's Code Assist (the Antigravity and Gemini CLI built-ins) answering 404 `NOT_FOUND` ("Requested entity was not found.") rests its model the same way, as its endpoint is the same for every model (`codeAssistModelMissing`; Packing1 on Discord: Argon, listed for a Google AI Pro account, answered 404 and rested the whole account; `TestCodeAssistMissingModelRestsTheModel`). A refusal for want of quota, credit or rate, for an account its vendor restricted, or for a verify it asks of its account there, from another computer's magpie (a Remote magpie provider, LAN share) rests only the model it was about (`remoteModelRest`, keyed by `restID`, in `restAfterMarked` and `rateRest`): that magpie serves each of its models from its own providers and rests them itself, so one out of quota there says nothing of the others (bigsteven on Discord: a 429 for one model rested every model on the remote for the 3 hours it named, so a group never moved on to the remote's other models; `TestRemoteMagpieQuotaRestsTheModel`, `TestRemoteMagpieRestrictedRestsTheModel`). Such a rest out of quota or credit (`Rest.there`) still puts the model behind the others, but is never given again without asking (`quotaHeld` passes it by): that magpie holds its own rests and answers from them at once, and what it serves under a name may have changed there since (zaidu259434 on X: a routing group there moved off a spent Open Design, and this magpie gave Open Design's 402 again until the time it named, never asking; `TestRemoteMagpieQuotaIsAskedNotReplayed`, `TestHandAddedMagpieQuotaIsAskedNotReplayed`). Another magpie added by hand, as an OpenAI-compatible provider of the user's own name rather than the preset, is read as one by its reply: a magpie names the provider that answered or held it (`X-Magpie-Provider`, `providerHeader`; `noteMember`, also on a `quotaHeld` or `verifyHeld` answer), as no vendor does, and `keepRetry` notes it as `answeredThereHeader`, since this magpie writes its own provider into the same header of its reply and the note is kept from the client (lagrangepoint1813 on Discord: "Remote Magpie: Claude Code: You've hit your session limit" answered every model of it, from `quotaHeld`, for the five hours it named; `TestAnotherMagpieAddedByHandRestsTheModel`, asked there and held there). Its key refused, the computer unreachable, or the key's own limit there (`keyLimited`'s 429, which carries `X-Magpie-Limit-Reset`, `remoteKeyLimitHeader`), still rests it whole: #2077's reporter had a Claude Code session limit on the other magpie refuse its Qoder models here (`TestRemoteMagpieRestsTheModel`, two gateways over HTTP; `TestRemoteMagpieKeyLimitRestsTheKey`). The held try keeps that marker only as a note of magpie's own (`keepRetry` sets `keyLimitThereHeader`, which `holdWriter` never passes on), so the rest is whole however the 429 came, and the marker never reaches the agent or a magpie this one answers, which would read it as this magpie's own key limit (#2791, ggbdpq; `TestRemoteMagpieKeyLimitRestsTheRemote`, `TestRemoteMagpieKeyLimitMarkerStaysHere`). A 400, 404, 410 or 422 that says the model is retired (`retiredWords`: OpenCode Zen's 410 `ModelDeprecated` "Model exo-free has been deprecated.", whose own model list still names it; MOMO on Discord) rests only that model the same way, is retryable so a group's next member or another provider of it is asked, and is remembered (`provider.Retire`, `retired.json` beside `providers.json`): the model leaves that provider's entries in the catalog (`buildEntries`), so /v1/models, every agent's model list and the found groups go without it, until a request on it is answered again (`Unretire` in `servedCandidate`) or a week has passed. A refresh of the vendor's list doesn't bring it back early, since that list is where it still is. A `provider/model` request for it is still sent. A 400 or 422 that says this upstream's API can't take the request's shape (`shapeWords`: xAI's 422 "unknown item type" (#350), OpenAI's "Unknown parameter", and a limit of a model's own on tool schemas, OpenCode Go's 400 "JSON schema exceeds the maximum nesting depth of 10 levels" on muse-spark-1.3-contributor, #1870), or that it is served on another API than the one asked (`wrongEndpoint`, once the member has tried every API it has a URL for: #2670, a relay with a Chat URL alone refusing tools with reasoning and `none` too), rests nobody: the next candidate is asked once (`shapeRefused`, `failShape`), and the last one's error reaches the agent. A request missing what every API requires ("field required") isn't one, nor is a 200 that mentions it. A model asking for an earlier reply's reasoning the history doesn't have (`reasoningBackRefused` in `thinking_back.go`: Command Code's Beam answering "Missing required parameter: 'messages[1].reasoning_content'" to a second turn, DeepSeek's and Kimi's words for their tool turns) is one too, after any refill `refuseThinkingBack` makes: the request is whole, and another member's model may not ask for it (EDwinpzy on Discord, `TestGroupFailsOverAMissingReasoning`); a missing `messages` or `model` still stops (`TestMissingReasoningIsNotABadRequest`). Command Code's Provider API turning a request away with nothing but a trace id (`unsaidWords`: `{"message":"invalid request error trace_id: <hex>","type":"invalid_request_error"}`, a 400, or a 422 before) is handled the same way: it turns away a request whose history and asked-for output together are over a limit below the model's listed context (the reporter's A/B: one ~748K-token agent conversation answered 200 with `max_completion_tokens` 64000 and this 400 with 384000, the model's listed max output), which another member's model may take, and the key is fine (#2313, `TestGroupFailsOverCommandCodesBare400`). Its overflow, which names the model's context length before the trace id, and an error that names a `param`, aren't one. A relay's own cap on one request's input that says so (`tooLongRe`: B.ai's 400 "Input token exceed the limit", on a 380K request under the model's 1M window; #2445, worksyang) is taken as an overflow (`overflowed`), which no word list of `retryable` matched: a group's next member that may hold the request is asked (`withRoom`, `failOverflow`), nobody rests, another account of the same model isn't asked, and with no member left the agent is told the conversation is too long, so it compacts. The conversation remembers the refusal (`noteOverflow` in [`affinity.go`](../../internal/gateway/affinity.go), by its stick key and the member's provider and model, with the request's size): its next request at least as large asks that member after the others (`behindOverflow`, after `roomy`, so after a rule that named it), behind and not out, until the member answers one (`forgetOverflow`) or the conversation shrinks below the refused size. Another conversation, and a 413 byte cap, are not affected (#3453, `TestOverflowRefusedOnceIsAskedLast`). A rate limit worded with input tokens (`paceWords`) isn't one, and a plain bad request is still asked of nobody else (`TestRelayInputCapGoesToTheNextMember`). Chinese that names the input rather than the context is one too (`tooLongRe`'s third line: a group member's 400 「内容太长了，请精简输入或开启新对话」, z.ai's 「Prompt 超长」, iFlytek Spark's 「…字数太多，需要精简输入」, 「输入长度超过限制」); before, it was a plain failure that rested the member and asked no other (#4420, MYCF711; `TestChineseInputTooLongGoesToTheNextMember`). Each names what was sent beside its length, so a field's or the reply's length isn't one, and Chinese pace words (速率, 频繁, 并发, 每分钟…, in `paceWords`) beside them make it a rate limit, not a size (`TestChineseNotTooLong`). A 400 or 422 whose message speaks only of length, without naming what is long (`lengthWords` in `fallback.go`: 太长, 过长, 精简, 开启新对话, "too long", "too large", "shorten"), with no pace word and no part of the request named (`partWords`: a picture, a file, the reply, a field), is the request too long for that member once the request is past half its window (`saysLength`, `lengthMargin` 0.5 in `tooLongUnsaid`), not the 1.2 margin a refusal that says nothing needs: a vendor whose own ceiling is under the window it declares answered 「内容太长了…」 at ~730K of a declared 1M (#4420, MYCF711). It then goes as an unsaid overflow goes: to a member with room, nobody resting, else to the agent as "prompt is too long" (`TestLengthWordsPastHalfTheWindowGoToTheNextMember`). Under half the window the same words are left as the vendor's error. A subscription account that fails any other way with a window full, by magpie's latest reading of its windows, rests until that window renews. A 401 or 403 is the exception: one that names a quota is out of quota already (`usedUpWords`), and one that doesn't is about the key or the request, so it rests on the backoff ladder whatever the reading says (plugins#243, zygs1083-dotcom: a Factory account read at 98% sat out its week over one plain 403; `TestPlain403DoesNotRestUntilTheWindow`). A plain 403 (`plainForbidden`: no quota, credit, rate, verification, restriction or model not served) from a second key or account of the same provider, model and reasoning in one request is the request refused, not each account: neither rests, the first one's rest and counted failure are taken back (`forbiddenAlike`, `unforbid`), and the provider's other mates are asked last (`matesLast`). Before, that one request walked every account of the provider into backoff (`TestPlain403OnTwoMatesRestsNeither`). One account's plain 403 alone still rests it. A 500, 502 or 503 that says nothing of why (`plainUnavailable`: no quota, credit, rate, overload, proxy down, ChatGPT reset or protection refusal, and some words) is read the same way when a second account of the same provider, model and reasoning gives it in the same words and status, ids left out (`refusalWords`), and the provider answered another request within `answeringLately` (5 minutes, `answeredLately`, noted in `servedCandidate`): neither rests, and the last one left isn't asked again after a moment (`sameRefusal`). Before, one agent's request an upstream always refused (mycf_711 on Discord: 127 of a provider's 128 503s were "AIError AI服务异常", for an empty tool schema) walked every account into backoff and the other agents found them all resting. One account's 503 alone, 503s in other words, and alike 503s from a provider that answered nothing lately (an outage) still rest as before, so an outage is backed off once 5 minutes have passed since its last answer (`TestPlain5xxOnTwoMatesRestsNeither`). Both quota rests and window rests are held to `longestQuota` (8 days), so a reset time read wrong (a year off) doesn't bench the account for that long. The next candidate is then tried. A failed provider waits `fallbackCooldown` (1 minute) at the back. An overload is no failure of the account's (`vendorOverloaded`: a 529, the ChatGPT backend's `server_is_overloaded` and Anthropic's `overloaded_error` among them, or a 5xx whose words say overloaded, as Gemini's 503 does): its model rests on that account `overloadRest` (30 seconds), or as long as a Retry-After asks up to `overloadRestMost` (a minute), every time alike, and no failure is counted on the backoff ladder (celestial_eagle_99230 on Discord: eight Codex accounts each rested up to the 10-minute cap for ChatGPT's overload, "failed 5 times in a row", leaving nothing to answer). A WorkBuddy account the vendor restricted (#1849: 403 `{"code":11140,"msg":"request illegal","displayMsg":{"en":"Your account has been restricted due to unusual activity",…}}`) rests `restrictedRest` (2 hours) as `restricted` (`failRestricted`), not the minutes of an unknown failure: only WorkBuddy lifting the restriction, after the user's appeal, brings it back. Both shapes are known: the built-in's `wbExplain` and the plugin's `explained()` (0.1.17, the mover's `min`) end the error with `provider.WBRestrictedHint` (`restrictedSaid`), and the vendor's own body, its code 11140 or its words (`provider.WBRestricted`), counts only from a WorkBuddy candidate (`restrictedRefused`, `isWorkBuddy`), as another vendor's 11140 is its own. Bare "request illegal" isn't matched. A vendor's risk control reads the request as well as the account, so a restricted account (`failRestricted`, ZCode's 3012 too) walls its provider off for the rest of the request (`riskRefused`, `elsewhere`; `busyNow` and `roomy`'s left-out list too): none of the provider's other accounts is sent it, a group's other provider is asked, and with none left the agent is told and the account rests, the last one left too. Before, one request was sent on from account to account and each was restricted in turn (_teamtaylor_ on Discord; `TestRiskRefusalNotSentToTheProvidersOtherAccounts`, `TestRiskRefusalAloneIsToldAndRests`). The next request still goes to the provider's next account. A remote magpie's restriction walls off nothing (`remoteModelRest`). WorkBuddy's 11128 channel refusal stays `promptRefused` and rests nobody. The Routing page names it "account restricted by the vendor", says the appeal is the way back, and gives the account's row a button that lifts the rest (`/api/gateway/unrest`) once the vendor has. Every other rest a key or account takes (quota, rate limit, retry-after, a failure's backoff) can be lifted by hand the same way (#1869): "Try now" on its row on the Routing page and beside its rest note in the provider's key list. It ends the rest, and with Sink on puts the key back from the back of the order; the backoff count stays, and a refusal after it rests the key again the usual way. Every lift before its time (Try now, `UnrestAll` for a replaced key, `renewed` for windows that renewed) is noted by its rest key (`restingUntil.lifted`) and handed to the Routing page in the trace (`TraceState.Lifted`, `lifted` in `/api/gateway/trace`), and a lift wakes a page waiting on the trace though no route changed (`trace.lifted`). The page stops showing a rest or a sink found before its lift, wherever the lift was asked for (zoecats on Discord: Try now in the provider's editor left Routing at 429 until a restart; `TestTryNowToldToTheRoutingPage`, `routing-lifted-elsewhere.test.cjs`). With another magpie serving the gateway, Try now lifts nothing (#3555). The vendor's safety filter refusing a reply rests nobody, whether it refuses the turn before any of the reply is said or once some of it has been said (`refused`, `refusedAfter`). So does one said in a stream a client is answered whole from (a Codex account is streamed upstream for a `stream:false` Responses, Chat or Messages request): each decoder gives the refusal's error event status 400 and its code (`filterRefused`), a whole reply it cut is answered the refusal rather than its words so far, and Anthropic's error shape, which has no code, carries it in `refusedHeader`; before, it was answered 502 `api_error`, asked again, and each account rested (#3274). `restAfterMarked` itself rests nobody, and counts no failure, for a 400 whose error object `policyRefusal` reads as a refusal, by its code, never by words a body may echo of the request. A refusal after the reasoning went through as it came (a vendor whose reasoning is never held, a trusted DeepSeek member, a hold that timed out) but before a word of the answer or a call is handed over (`refusalHandover`, `refusal_handover.go`): when the agent streams Anthropic Messages, Chat Completions or Responses, another candidate is left and `refusalAsksAgain` allows one more (`handsOver`), what passes is read event by event (`passOver`); the refusal's event and everything after it are not sent, an Anthropic block still open gets its `content_block_stop`, the try fails as a refusal with nothing said (`failRefused`, nobody rests, `matesFirst`), and the next try writes through `spliceAfter`, so its stream is the same reply: Anthropic's `message_start` is dropped and its block indexes go after those sent; Responses' `response.created`/`in_progress`/`queued` are dropped, `output_index` goes after the items sent and `sequence_number` goes on from the last one sent; Chat chunks go as they come. Once text or a call has gone, a refusal is the agent's as before (`refusalAfter`). Without another candidate nothing changes. A Gemini stream is not handed over, the reasoning item of a Responses stream handed over gets no `output_item.done`, and the next reply's `response.completed` lists only its own output (#4604). Zhipu's `finish_reason` `"sensitive"` and error 1301 (敏感), DashScope's `data_inspection_failed` and Volcengine Ark's `*SensitiveContentDetected` are refusals (`filterReasons`, `sensitiveCode`). Its note on the try (and in the error the agent gets when nobody is left) is the vendor's reason, with Anthropic's `stop_details` category and explanation when it gave them (`refusedDetail`, #1768: "safety filter: refusal (category cyber)"); none is made up when it gave none. `stop_details` is carried through the IR (`Event.StopDetails`), so a Claude subscription run through the claude CLI and a translated Anthropic route keep it for an Anthropic client too. How many more candidates a refusal before any of the reply is asked of is Settings' `RefusalRetries` (`refusalAsksAgain`, counted per request in the candidate loop; a group's other members count too): unset, every one left (#248); 0, none (#1925, DD on Discord). A Claude subscription account's refusal of a conversation of `retellMost` (100K) tokens or more leaves the subscription's other accounts out (`tooBigToRetell`, `otherThanClaudeAccounts`): each would be a new Claude Code told the whole conversation, all of it written to that account's cache again, for a refusal about the content it most likely gives too (Aisiverto on X: a 900K conversation refused and told again cost a Pro account most of its 5 hours). The size is the larger of what the account counted and `estimate`; other members are still asked, and a smaller conversation still goes to the next account (`TestClaudeSubscriptionBigRefusalNotToldAgain`, `TestClaudeSubscriptionRefusalMovesToNextAccount`). When nobody is left, an agent on Anthropic's Messages API gets the held refusal itself, the vendor's 200 with `stop_reason` `refusal` and its `stop_details`, streamed or whole (`holdWriter.handBack`), since that is what Claude Code switches to its fallback model on; a 400 is only an API error to it. Codex and Chat clients still get `failTo`'s 400 (`refusedStatus`), which they don't ask again for. `TestRefusalRetries*` in [`refusal_retries_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/refusal_retries_test.go) cover keys, a group and the Claude subscription. The ChatGPT backend's 502 "response protection is unavailable" is about the request's content, not the account: the same history fails the same on each of its accounts and models. It rests nobody, isn't tried again after a moment, and isn't asked of the provider's other accounts or models; another provider left in a group is asked once (`protectionRefused`, `elsewhere`). One cause is known (#1270): a history that replays the backend's own `web_search_call` in a request that doesn't declare the `web_search` tool is refused so, whatever the account, model or turn (a plain turn, a thread's description, a compaction, Codex's own text compaction for a custom provider with `tools: []`). Every request to the ChatGPT backend with such a history therefore declares cached-only search (`{"type":"web_search","external_web_access":false}`, `provider.DeclareSearch`): an account's in `codexBody`, Codex's own sign-in relayed in `codexUpstreamOn`. It goes in the tools, or for Responses Lite (its header, `reasoning.context` `all_turns`, or an `additional_tools` item already there), which refuses a top-level `web_search`, in the `additional_tools` item. A request that offered no tools gets `tool_choice` `none` so the model can't search; one with a forced choice keeps it. A history without a search, or a request that declares search already, is relayed unchanged; Codex's own `web.run` (a function in the `web` namespace, which Codex offers in place of the hosted tool on a Responses Lite model or with its standalone web search on, and runs through `/alpha/search`) counts as declaring it, since the backend refuses a request carrying both `web.run` and the hosted `web_search` (Savin on Discord, Codex 0.163.0-alpha; `TestSearchedConversationUnderWebRun`). siwc, API-key requests to openai.com and the legacy `/responses/compact` are left as they were. The ChatGPT backend also turns a whole request away when one tool name in it, a call's in the input or a tool's in `tools`, is outside `^[a-zA-Z0-9_.-]+\z` (400 "Invalid 'input[83].name': string does not match pattern"; wing3556 on Discord, on a compaction): a call another provider's model wrote with a space, a colon or non-ASCII in its name, kept in the history beside its "no such tool", failed every later turn on Codex. Every request to it sends each such name as `provider.CodexToolName` makes it (each refused byte `_`, cut to 55, `_` and 8 hex of its sha256, the same on every request), a valid one unchanged and a body with none byte for byte (`CodexToolNames`): an account's in `codexBody`, Codex's own sign-in's in `codexUpstreamOn` (`/responses/compact` too). A tool the agent declared under such a name is called back by its own: a translated reply through `decoderFor` (`codexNamesBack`), a relayed Responses reply through `nsTidy.back` (`codexRenamedIn`; `TestCodexToolNamesTheBackendTakes`). Namespaces, the OpenAI API, Azure and Copilot are not covered (#4875). Command Code turning a model away for its Zero Data Retention setting (its plugin's switch, 0.1.14, which sends `x-cmd-zdr: 1`; magpie-community/plugins#246) is a refusal of that request's setting, not of the model or the account: the Provider API's 422 with `"code":"cmd_zdr_no_providers"` and the 400 the plugin makes of Go's error line with `"type":"cmd_zdr_no_providers"` (`zdrCoded`, matched as the error's code or type, never by its words, whose "…a different model.","type":"invalid_request_error" read as a model unserved and rested the model on the account). `keepRetry` notes it beside the error (`zdrHeader`), since the error the agent gets may keep no code (Anthropic's shape, or the 400's numeric code). It rests nobody, anywhere (`restAfterMarked` returns `by: "off"`), so the model answers as soon as the switch is off; it isn't asked of the provider's other accounts at that model, which carry the same header (`notMates`), nor again after a moment; another member, the provider's other models among them, is asked once, and with none left the agent gets Command Code's words (`TestCommandCodeZDRRefusalRestsNothing`, `TestZDRRefusalNeverRests`). Another vendor's refusal in the same words, without the code, still rests the model (`TestZDRRefusalIsCommandCodesCodeOnly`).
5. The last candidate's error reaches the agent, with one exception for a group (#1418, `patience.go`), which a plain provider shares once its same-one retries are spent (below). When every member has failed in a way that passes (`busy`, its statuses `busyStatus`: 408, 500, 502, 503, 504, 529, or Cloudflare's 520 to 524, which a relay behind it answers with now and then (`TestCloudflareErrorIsTriedAgain`; the same-one retries of `passing` read the same list), that isn't a proxy, a quota, response protection, or an upstream that answered a stream request with a whole reply, which fails the same however often it is asked (`didNotStream`; the same-one retries of `passing` leave it alone too, `TestStreamlessMemberIsAskedOnce`), or a 429 that is a short rate limit, not a plan or credit, nor an account's token wall: a vendor code in `wallCodes`, Raccoon's `user_model_rate_limited`, which rests as a rate limit but is asked again neither here nor by the same-one retries, as each ask draws on the bucket it waits for; plugins#512, `TestTokenWallFailsFast`), and nothing of the reply has been sent, the members that failed so are asked again in turn after a pause (`replan`). The pause is `retryPause` doubled each round up to `replanLongest()` (30 times `retryPause`, 30s), and never shorter than the soonest Retry-After; a member whose Retry-After ends past the pause is asked after the others. This goes on while the group's `Patience` lasts. A member of a group in the group uses the innermost that sets `Patience` (`innerPatience`, #2410), as FirstToken (#2246) and Sink (#2255) do; unset falls back to the group asked for, and an inner `PatienceOff` skips replan for that member (`TestNestedGroupPatienceThroughOuter`, `TestNestedGroupPatienceInnerOff`, `TestNestedGroupPatienceFallsBackToOuter`). `replanRounds` (12) is only a backstop that never comes first: the pauses alone add up to `PatienceLongest` in 11 rounds (with 6, a group set to 240s gave up after 90s of pauses, real-jacket on #1418; `TestPatienceIsUsedUp`). The budget is the group's `Patience`, counted from the first time every member had failed, when the agent would have had the error (`waitFrom`; `Group.Waits`: 0 is `PatienceDefault`, 60s; -1, `PatienceOff`, turns it off; at most `PatienceLongest`, 240s). A pause that would end past the budget isn't taken, and the agent gets the error; a member the vendor said is back within a second past its budget (read in whole seconds) is asked as the budget ends (`TestReporterResetFitsTheDefaultPatience`). The last member out of its quota or credit (not `busy`), after an earlier member failed in a way that passes, has those earlier ones asked again the same way, and rests as they did (`lastSpent`): before, the agent had the relay's moment of 503 while the relay would answer a moment on (`TestGroupRelayBlipDoesNotEndOnSpentLast`). While another member is busy too, the last one isn't first tried again alone (`passing`): the members take turns, and once the budget has begun, the same-one retries aren't taken either. While the budget lasts, the last candidate's failure is held (`newHoldWriter`), and a streaming agent is kept alive through the pause with SSE comments (`pauseAlive`). The try is traced with `Again` (the pause), `Replan` (how many are asked again) and `Patience` (the budget in seconds). The group editor's "Every member busy" row sets it. A plain provider (a `provider/model` request, its accounts and fallbacks the candidates) first takes the same-one retries `passing` gives the last one left: `lastRetries` for a busy vendor, `rateRetries` for a rate limit, each pause `retryPause<<n` or a Retry-After of at most `longestPause` (8s). Past those, or for a Retry-After longer than `longestPause`, it is replanned the same way, its accounts taking turns as members do, for the provider's own `Patience` (`Provider.Patience`, the same values as a group's, set with `magpie provider set <id> patience=2m|off|default`; the provider editor shows no row for it and its save keeps it, `TestProviderSaveKeepsPatience`), unset `singlePatience` (`PatienceDefault`, 60s), counted from when the agent would have had the error, so it works with no setting and the user's value wins either way (`TestSingleProviderOwnPatienceWins`; ChatGPT's reset of a large request, `codexReset`, is left out: Codex is told it at its 503 with what may help, as `codexBusy` leaves it). A provider's own Patience is for a request to it alone: a group it is in, set or unset, keeps the group's (zoecats on Discord: a local relay's short 429 went straight to Claude Code once the few retries were spent). So a 429 whose Retry-After is 20s is waited out, the stream kept alive past `keepHeldAfter`; one of an hour, a quota, credit or plan limit, a 401 or 403, a refusal, response protection's 502 and `didNotStream` are not `busy` or don't fit the budget, and reach the agent at once as the vendor said them. Its tries are traced with `Again` alone, no `Replan` or `Patience`, so the Routing page tells it as the last one left asked again rather than as a group's members (`TestSingleProviderWaitsOutAShortRateLimit`, `TestSingleProviderPassesALongLimitThrough`, `TestSingleProviderAgentGoneInTheWait` in [`single_busy_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/single_busy_test.go)). Codex's own one-account relay (`codexUpstreamOn`, below) doesn't join it: its request is relayed unchanged outside the candidate loop, and the backend's 429 is always the plan's usage limit (`codexBusy` never retries one), so the short rate limit this waits out doesn't arise there; its overloads keep their `lastRetries` and then the subscription's fallbacks. An agent that goes away in either pause (the same-one retry's or a group's replan) ends the request there with 499: nobody is asked again, and the try whose pause it was is marked `Gone` (#1894, heng91: a relay down while its proxy was off, the agent's window closed). The Routing page says the agent canceled the request before the retry and lists it as canceled, rather than ending its story on "tried again in 2s", a retry that never came (`TestAgentGoneInTheRetryPause`, `routing-agent-gone.test.cjs`). A try that answered is recorded (`served`, `answered`) for routing, affinity and usage. A try is added to the route's `Tries` only as it is asked, so a later try is the record that the gateway went on from this one, and the Routing page tells what came of a try from that, never from where it stood in the order (#2281, xishuchkui-code: an Antigravity 502 empty reply that went on to the next account was told as "an error another account wouldn't fix, so Claude Code gets it"). The one case the record can't show is the last try left out of its allowance after an earlier failure that wasn't one, where the agent is told the earlier error: that try is marked `Earlier` (`routing-went-on.test.cjs`).

## Constraints and failure behavior

- A fallback happens only while none of the reply has been sent. An agent never gets half a reply from one upstream and the rest from another.
- A refusal that comes as an error (#2400, Jichao). An error whose own `message` is, whole, `The request was rejected because it was considered high risk` is a refusal with no code beside it (`highRiskRefusal` in `policyRefusal`): in an HTTP error body or a stream's error event, never in an answer's text, which may quote it. The vendor that sends it isn't known; it says the request was turned away, so it goes to the next candidate under `RefusalRetries` like any refusal and rests nobody. A refusal error event before any content ends the try at once (`eventRefusalError`, `stop` as for any error), so an upstream that keeps the stream open doesn't hold the next candidate back; a refusal's stop or finish marker is still read to the end for the usage and terminator after it. After content, every write is read for a late refusal (`refusalAfter`), so one whose `event: error` name and data come in separate writes is still noted, and nothing is replayed. `TestHighRisk*`, `TestRefusalErrorDoesNotWaitForEOF` in [`refusal_error_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/refusal_error_test.go).
- An error's own status beats the words (#4855). A provider that already sent its 200 can only say what went wrong in an in-stream error frame — a plugin's runtime watching a clock, aborting at 285s and emitting `{"error":{…,"code":"upstream_timeout","status":504}}` — and the frame is relayed to the client as it came. The status it names on a member of the error object (`namedStatusOf`, a number 400–599, `status` first, Gemini's relays' `code`; a string code is not one) is what the failure fails and rests as, before `streamStatus` guesses one from the message's words, wherever the frame is read: a held stream's error event (`errOf` in `streamEvent`), a whole 200 body that is only an error (`errorReply`), a carried error's words (`carriedStatus`), and a reply already streaming, whose turn rests at the status the sniffer noted on the try (`namedStatusHeader`, kept from the client; `failedNamed`/`restAfterMarked`) — so a terse frame naming 429 rests as a rate limit, not `other` at a word-guessed 502, and the rest says "Gateway Timeout", not "OK". With no such member, a status the words name as one, "(HTTP 503)" or "（HTTP 503）" (`textStatus`, `httpSaid`), counts too, for a 429 or a 5xx only, since a 4xx a message quotes may be someone else's ("the image link answered (HTTP 404)"): wps-lingxi's AuditFailed and AIError frames name theirs that way (mycf_711). A reply already streaming when its frame came is booked at that status as well, in Routing and Usage, though the client's 200 went out. Words magpie wrote itself (`magpieSaid`: the loop guard's `loopGuardSaid`, `emptyReply`, `reloopedError`) and words saying the request is too long name no status, whatever members they carry: a LAN peer's loop guard frame carries 502 in its Gemini shape, and is no failure of the provider, so it neither rests the route nor is booked as one (`lateRests`). An error naming no status reads exactly as before. `TestRelayStreamErrorFrameSaysItsStatus`, `TestRelayStreamErrorFrameWithoutStatusAsBefore`, `TestRelayStreamErrorFrameNamesItsStatusInWords`, `TestNamedStatusOf` in [`stream_frame_status_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/stream_frame_status_test.go).
- A request is one usage record, counted once by how it ended (小一 on Discord: Codex on credits showed "5,482 · 1,094 failed" while rows said "2 tries/3 tries" and had been answered). The final record carries `tries` when the route took more than one (`rec.Tries`, from the trace's `Tries`). A try a vendor refused or turned away (`hw.refused`, `hw.turnedAway`) still writes its own record, for what it billed and why it failed, marked `handed_on`; so does any other try that failed, billed something and handed the request on, to another account or member or the same one again (`failedRow` in `serve`, written once the next try begins): a Claude subscription run that failed after Anthropic's `message_start` had written the conversation to that account's cache, and the next account wrote it again, while the Usage page counted the second alone (hhxk233 and Kayrainforest on X; `relay` keeps the reply start's usage on a failure, `TestClaudeSubscriptionFailedTryKeepsItsTokens`). A try that failed before anything was billed has no record; `usage.Totals.count` skips such a record for calls and failures. A request failed only when the agent got an error (`LostRequest`); one answered after a failed try is counted beside it as answered on a retry (`Totals.Retried`, `RetriedRequest`, the `retried` filter). Logs written before `handed_on` are read the same way: the log index marks a failed, non-rejected record followed by another of the same route id as handed on (`routeRows` in [`log_index.go`](../../internal/usage/log_index.go)). Counts by account or route (`vias`, window holds) still count every try. A turn relayed on Codex's own ChatGPT sign-in is recorded as that codex account (`codexServed` in [`codex_backend.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_backend.go)), so the account card's window counts it; the Routing view still names the seat OpenAI, an API-key call stays `openai` with no account, as does one whose `chatgpt-account-id` isn't the account Codex's auth.json here is signed in to (a Codex in WSL or under another `CODEX_HOME`), and a row already written without an account is left as it is. Those relay rows are still not a lead's answerer (`loggedAnswers`, #1367); a row an account answered, its host `host as email`, still is.
- A failed call is in `app.log` too, not only the Routing page and the usage ledger (wjh66 on Discord: Codex's "error decoding response body" after "reconnecting 5/5", and the log had nothing of the turn). `record` writes each call whose status is 400 or more, or that carries an error, as one line (`failedCallLine`: agent, model, provider, status, time, the error to 300 characters); the same failure again within a minute is counted into the next line, not written (`failedLogEvery`), so a client asking in a loop can't fill the log. A Codex reply relayed from the ChatGPT backend that ends before its last event also says which side ended it (`codexEndedEarly` in [`codex_backend.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_backend.go)): the read of OpenAI's stream that failed, with its error, or a clean end; and the client gone, a write to it that failed, or the failure passed on as `response.failed`, with the bytes passed on. reqwest, Codex's HTTP client, says "error decoding response body" for any read of a body whose connection broke, so only this line tells the legs apart. A gateway that ends with requests still in flight logs that they were cut (`srv.Shutdown`'s error; on Windows there is no handover, so an update or quit cuts a stream after `shortDrain`) (`TestCodexReplyBrokenOffLogged`, `TestCodexClientGoneLogged`, `TestFailedCallLineOncePerMinute`).
- Each record keeps the service tier three ways (#1784): `tier_asked` is what the client's body asked (`bodyTier`; Claude's speed fast reads `fast`), `tier_sent` is what that try's last request went out with, noted in `forwardOnce` after every rewrite (fast picks, Ultrafast to priority on a plan without it) onto the per-attempt `wireAt`, and `tier_served` is only what the reply said it ran at (`upstreamTier`, read by the sniffer and the translated readers), empty when it says nothing; from the ChatGPT backend (CodexBase's host) its `auto` and `default` are empty too, since it says them whether priority ran or not (`servedTierAt`, applied in `attempt` and `codexUpstreamOn`). The route's tries carry `tier` and `tier_served` the same way. A bridge that doesn't go through `forwardOnce` (the Claude subscription, Cursor's built-in) leaves `tier_sent` empty. Code: [`tier_record.go`](../../internal/gateway/tier_record.go).
- An agent's fast pick (`settings.FastPicks`, the bolt in its picker, #954) may also be in `settings.UltraPicks` (#1954), offered by the GUI only where one of Codex's accounts lists Ultrafast on the model (`provider.CanUltra`, `UltraModels`). `fastPick` marks its candidates `ultra`; a try of one goes with `service_tier: "ultrafast"` on Chat and Responses, and an agent on Messages carries it in the context (`withUltra`) to `forwardTranslated`, which sets `Request.Ultrafast` for the ChatGPT backend. Either way the same check as a client's own Ultrafast ask applies, now on Chat as well as Responses: an account whose list doesn't offer it is put after a mate that does (`tierMate`), or, with none left, sent `priority`. A Chat body's own `service_tier: "ultrafast"` is parsed as Ultrafast too (`chat.go`). `TestUltraPickSentUltrafast`.
- A usage record carries its prompt's cache beside `in` and `out` (#1839): `cache_read` (tokens read from the vendor's cache) and `cache_write` (cache creation, Anthropic's `cache_creation_input_tokens`; `cache_write_1h` the part written for an hour). `in` holds neither. Both are written on every record, 0 too, so a prompt that missed the cache reads as that. A record from before then has neither and reads as 0. Every route fills them: Anthropic's messages, Chat (OpenAI's `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, DeepSeek's `prompt_cache_hit_tokens`), Responses (`input_tokens_details.cached_tokens`) and Gemini (`cachedContentTokenCount`), passed through or translated, whole or streamed, a plugin's fetch, and the Claude subscription bridge. System One's decision calls report no cache. `TestUsageRecordsCacheTokensOnEveryRoute`, `TestPluginUsageRecordsCacheTokens`, `TestClaudeSubscriptionUsageKeepsCacheTokens`, `TestRecordAlwaysCarriesCacheTokens`.
- What the vendor read from its cache reaches the agent in the names its SDK reads, not only the record. A relayed Chat reply whose usage tells the cache in another vendor's names (a relay's Anthropic `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens`, DeepSeek's `prompt_cache_hit_tokens` or Moonshot's top-level `cached_tokens` beside a 0 in `prompt_tokens_details`) is told in OpenAI's too, streamed (`chatTidy`) and whole (`chatWhole`), by `openAIUsage` in [`chattidy.go`](../../internal/gateway/chattidy.go): `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (and `cache_write_tokens`), with `prompt_tokens` and `total_tokens` made to hold the cached part where the vendor left it out. Every other field stays, and a usage already in OpenAI's names, or with no cache, goes byte for byte; one in OpenAI's names alone isn't even decoded (`mayMendUsage`). pi-ai, which DeepSeek Harness and Pi use, reads `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` first, a 0 there too, and maps no Anthropic names at all, so such a relay showed DSH 缓存命中 0% while magpie had counted the hit (B3TT3R on Discord asked; DeepSeek's own API sends both names and was already read right). Relayed Responses and Messages replies, translated replies and a plugin's need nothing: their usage is written in the agent's own names. `TestAgentReadsCacheTokensOnEveryRoute`, `TestPluginAgentReadsCacheTokens`, `TestOpenAIUsageLeavesOpenAIsAlone`, `TestMayMendUsageOnlyOtherNames` in [`usage_cache_agent_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/usage_cache_agent_test.go).
- A request whose key is a Claude sign-in (`claudeSignIn`: `Authorization: Bearer sk-ant-oat…`, Claude Code signed in to claude.ai with magpie as its base URL) is never told a 401, nor a 403 saying "OAuth token has been revoked". magpie never passes that sign-in on, so such an answer is a provider refusing magpie's credential, or one of magpie's own Claude accounts lapsing; told it, Claude Code renews its own sign-in before each of its retries (ten for one request) and then asks to `/login`. Any error whose message mentions x-api-key, whatever its status, Claude Code tells as "Not logged in · Please run /login" too. It is told as a 502 in magpie's words, an `api_error` saying "<provider> refused magpie's credential (HTTP 401): …" with x-api-key said as "API key", and any other error such a request is told keeps its status with x-api-key said as "API key", as does a stream's error after its 200 (`keepsSignIn`, `claude_signin.go`). Recent calls and the usage log keep the provider's status and message. Any other 403, and a request with magpie's key or an API key, is answered as before. The sign-in's beta (`oauth-…` in `anthropic-beta`) isn't passed on either (`askedBetas`), so a provider gets the request as Claude Code with magpie's key sends it; a provider's own `header.anthropic-beta` still goes.
- A 400 that names betas a provider doesn't take, in Bedrock's words ("Unexpected value(s) `x` for the `anthropic-beta` header", `unexpectedBetas`) or Copilot's ("unsupported beta header(s): x, y", `unsupportedBetas`), is asked again at once without them, and they are left out for that provider from then on (`refuseBetas`, `markUnfit`). Copilot turns away `tool-search-tool-2025-10-19`, which Claude Code in Claude Desktop's Code tab asks with tool search on, and answers the same request without it (bigcat1800, `TestCopilotUnsupportedBetasLeftOut`).
- A 2xx that can't be an API's answer is the 502 it stands for (`notAnAPIReply`, #1012): a web page (`text/html`, or a body that begins as one: a sign-in page, Cloudflare's challenge), a body with nothing in it, or one sent as JSON that begins neither as JSON nor as an event stream. A reply whose first bytes are an event stream's (`event:`, `data:`, a `:` comment) is read as one under any Content-Type it names (`eventStream`, `eventsStart`): before, a stream under another type (`application/octet-stream`, say) was held whole and reached the agent only at its end, and one under `application/json` was a 502 (uk.jent on Discord: MiniMax Code to Grok Build showed each thinking block "Thought for 0.0s"; `TestPluginThinkingStreamsAsItComes`). Every upstream request goes through it (`forwardOnce`, and the built-in clients' own: the ChatGPT backend's `/responses`), so it fails over, rests and is logged as a 502 does, its reason naming the page's title.
- An error status whose body holds no message of its own says what it was (`provider.Unsaid`, through `APIError` and the plan-quota and drawing readers): a web page by its title as plain words, a firewall's block page (`EdgeBlocked`: Alibaba Cloud's, Cloudflare's "you have been blocked", "used Cloudflare to restrict access" and `error code: 1005`–`1020`) with `BlockedHint` after it, and an empty body under an HTTP status as refused with no details, often a firewall or proxy in front of the API (`NoDetails`; an empty `response.failed` event, whose fallback is no status, still reads "upstream stream failed"). A bare "403 Forbidden" told a relay's user nothing (maralica on Discord). A JSON error still reads as its message. `TestAPIErrorUnsaid`, `TestRelayRefusalSaysWhatItWas`. Only the first bytes are peeked, which a stream waits for anyway, and a compressed body is left as it came.
- A 400 that is no error at all, from OpenCode's gateway (`openCodeBareRefusal` in `forwardOnce`, #3740), is the 502 it stands for: OpenCode Go answered s-leeq's requests `400 {"model":"deepseek-v4.1-flash"}`, the model's name and nothing else, with nothing in or out, and the same request was answered some seconds later (13s to 25s in their timelines). A body any error field names goes on as it came (the missing `x-opencode-session` refusal among it), and so does a body that isn't a JSON object, or one over 64KB; a body is never cut. Read as the 502, a group's next member is asked, and a lone provider is asked again within its patience, which outlasts that window, where the 400 ended the turn at once. The failure is tied to the request (30 of 214 requests with ~246K tokens of tools, none of 420 with under 1K), so nobody rests for it (`restAfterMarked`, `openCodeBare`): the key goes on serving the requests it serves. Only OpenCode's hosts are read this way; every other vendor's bare 400 stays as it came. (`TestOpenCodeGoBare400AskedAgain`, `TestOpenCodeGoBare400ToldAs502`, `TestOpenCodeGoBare400RestsNobody`, `TestOpenCodeGoError400Untouched`)
- A 2xx from Zhipu or Z.ai holding the platform's refusal envelope (`{"code":1000,"msg":…,"success":false}`, which their Responses API at `/api/v1` answers a refused key) is the refusal it stands for (`provider.ZhipuEnveloped` in `forwardOnce`): 401 for a key refused, 429 for a limit or no balance, as Zhipu's error codes give, so it fails over and rests as those do and the agent is told why. `TestZhipuResponsesRefusalIsRefusal`.
- A request refused for its bytes (`byteCapRefusal` in [`body_cap.go`](../../internal/gateway/body_cap.go): a 413, or words such as Command Code's 400 "Request body is 40.1 MB, over the 32 MB limit for one request (33554432 bytes)", EDwinpzy on Discord; never a 401, 403, 429 or a context overflow) is asked again in `forward` with its oldest history images left out (`refitOverCap`), up to three times. The cap is the smallest size the refusal names below what was sent, else three quarters of what was sent, and it is kept per provider id for as long as magpie runs (`bodyCaps`), so the next request near it is fitted in `forwardOnce` before it goes (`withinCap`). A request is fitted to three quarters of the cap. Each left-out image becomes a text part, "[An earlier image was left out here to keep this request under its size limit.]", which names neither magpie nor the vendor. The latest user message's images (`latestUser`: not a tool result, not Codex's compaction prompt) and every text, tool output included, stay; the rest of the body keeps its bytes. When leaving out every earlier image can't bring it under, or it is refused at every size, the agent gets a 413 `request_too_large` naming the size, the cap and what was kept (`overCap`), so Codex is told `context_length_exceeded` and its compaction goes on to the text-only ask (`summaryTooLarge`, `plainCompact`) instead of sending the same body into the compaction guard's hold. Every route reaches it, magpie's own (compaction, titles, the classifier, search, vision) included, since they all go through `forward`.
- The vendor's reply headers a provider passes on (`ReplyHeaders` and the default request ids, see [providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md), #4145) reach the agent from the reply it is given alone. Every request that can become the agent's reply goes out through `forward` (relayed, translated, a plugin's fetch, a signed-in account) or `retrieveAs`/`retrieveOpen` (embeddings, audio), and each tells its reply's passed headers to the try that asked through the try's context (`withReplySink`, `heardReply` in [`reply_headers.go`](../../internal/gateway/reply_headers.go)). The sink is the try's `holdWriter` (`heardUpstream`), so a try that fails and is let go takes its vendor's headers with it, a fallback's reply carries the provider that answered's, and a failure `release`d to the agent carries its own vendor's. A reply to another provider (a side model, a searcher) is not heard: the sink takes its own provider's alone. Within a try the last reply replaces the earlier ones (an ask again without a refused beta, a Mantle reroute), and once the try has its status nothing more is heard (a cut reply's continuation). count_tokens writes them straight (`passReplyHeaders`), and embeddings through `heardHeaders`. A stream whose headers `keepAlive` sent before the vendor answered (past `keepHeldAfter`) has none: they can't follow a status already sent. Not carried: images, video and the Codex backend relay, which copies its reply's headers whole already. `TestReplyHeadersPassedOn`, `TestReplyHeadersOfTheReplyGiven`, `TestReplyHeadersOnCountTokens`, `TestReplyHeadersOnEmbeddings`.
- The agent's request headers a provider passes on (`PassHeaders`, see [providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md); 3jRzfF52Qx on X) go with every try `forwardOnce` makes, so chat, responses, messages, count_tokens, a group's members, fallbacks, signed-in accounts and a plugin's fetch all take them: `forwardOnce` puts the agent's headers in the try's context under the try's provider id (`provider.WithClientHeaders`), and `Provider.Sign` passes the ones that provider lists and no other's. Not carried: embeddings, images, video and the Codex backend relay, which don't go through `forwardOnce`. `TestPassHeadersReachTheProvider`, `TestPassHeadersOnlyToTheirProvider`.
- A streamed request is never left silent while its vendor says nothing. `holdWriter.watch` runs beside each try: past `keepHeldAfter` (3s) it sends a held try's agent the stream's 200 and SSE comments (`keepAlive`), one every `keepHeldEvery` (5s), none of the try, so another candidate may still answer in the same stream. It was 15s, and a proxy between the agent and magpie that hangs up on a request with nothing back for about 10s ended every turn slower to its first event: Codex on Windows sends 127.0.0.1 through a Clash-style app's system proxy, `127.*` in its bypass list or not, and its turns ended 499 (mrten_95615 on Discord; `TestHeldStreamHeardBeforeAProxyCuts`). A failure before it is still a status; once the reply passes, it puts a comment between two of its lines after `keepaliveEvery` of quiet. It stops after `keepaliveLongest` (5 minutes) with nothing from the vendor. A translated stream (`streamTranslated`) whose upstream has begun its reply and then says nothing at all, not even a comment (a relay that sends the rest of a long reply in one burst at its end), is kept alive in the client's own protocol by a ticker beside its reader (`keepThroughSilence`): every `keepaliveEvery` it sends the client's keepalive (`enc.keepalive`: a ping for Anthropic, `response.in_progress` for Responses, a comment for Chat, nothing for Gemini), which also counts as the try heard from, so the held stream's comments go on too; it stops once the upstream has been silent for `translatedQuietLongest` (30 minutes), past the 10 minutes `upstreamHeaderWait` gives a vendor to begin and long enough for a long reasoning turn sent whole at its end, after which the client's own timeout ends the wait (youyo.p on Discord: before, Claude Code heard only comments, which its event watchdog doesn't count, and gave up 300s on; `TestTranslatedSilenceKeptAliveForABound`). An upstream that has answered its headers but sent no byte yet is still waited for under the held stream's comments alone (`notAnAPIReply` reads its first bytes before the reply is translated). An Anthropic client is sent a ping at most once each `pingEvery` (1 minute), comments between, and none before `message_start`: Claude Code's watchdog counts only 30 pings in a row as life, and a ping on every upstream keepalive spent them in the first minute; the SDKs' `MessageStream` fails on any event before `message_start` (`anthropicEncoder.keepalive`, `TestTranslatedStreamKeepsClientAlive`). A try waiting for its key's or account's slot or for room in its minute (`lanes.take`, `rpms.wait`) is kept alive the same way (`keepQueued`), for as long as it waits; once the 200 is out, the try is held, and its failure or its queue turning it away reaches the agent as the stream's error (`failTo`), not a status (coeo91 on Discord). A wait shorter than `keepHeldAfter` sends nothing, so its 429 keeps its `Retry-After`. A proxy in front such as Cloudflare (125s) otherwise ends the request (#947). Gemini streams get no comments (#934). A vendor that then ignores `stream:true` and answers 200 with its reply whole has it sent as the agent's stream (`wholeAsStream`: Chat, Responses, Anthropic), not as the stream's error, while a reply under a type that says neither JSON nor a stream is held as a stream when its first bytes are one (`sseStart`), read event by event like one that says so, so an error among its frames still goes to the next candidate (#1418: it went through at once); a Chat stream a vendor ends with `finish_reason:"error"` — WorkBuddy's and a DeepSeek harness's word for a turn it failed, not finished (#2471) — is the failure the word says, held for the group's next candidate, and nobody rests, as an empty reply's (`TestFinishReasonErrorFailsOver`); a 200 that is no reply of the protocol's fails as a translated reply that didn't stream does (502, "… did not stream"), for the next candidate to answer, and the last one's reaches the agent as the stream's error. A held stream that has sent nothing but the frames before a reply (`response.created`, `response.in_progress`, `codex.rate_limits`, pings, an empty part begun, and any event `streamEvent` doesn't know to carry text, reasoning, a call or a reply's end (`replyEvent`), or an item not known that isn't a call or a tool's (`streamPart.said`): `eventLead`; Aliceonly on #1418: an unknown event was taken for content and let a frames-only 200 through, and its `server_is_overloaded` reached Codex with five candidates left) stays held as long as its agent is kept alive, up to `holdLead` (4 minutes, under the 300s Codex waits for an event; `keptQuiet`), so a `server_is_overloaded` that comes after it is a failure the next candidate answers (#1418: let through at 15s, the ChatGPT backend's failure 36s in ended Codex's turn with sub2api, next in the group, never asked). A stream whose agent can't be kept alive (Gemini's) is let through after `holdLongest` (15s), as before. Codex counts only events toward that 300s, not comments, so the bound is the request's, not each try's: once Codex on Responses has heard no event for `agentQuietMost` (4 minutes) since the request began or its last event (`keptAlive.tooQuiet`), the try in hand is let through (`scan`, `keepQuiet`), and no group replan or same-one retry is taken whose pause would end past it (fadenoob on Discord: held tries and replan pauses added up past 300s and Codex reconnected on "idle timeout waiting for SSE"). A translated reply whose upstream stream stops before it said its reply had — an Anthropic one with no stop_reason and no message_stop, a Responses one with no `response.completed`, the same two a relayed one is told by (`sniff.whole`; a Chat stream may end without `[DONE]` and be whole), a Gemini or Code Assist one whose last chunk has no candidate's `finishReason` (`geminiEnds`, #1643: `usageMetadata` is no end, Vertex AI sends it on every chunk; a chunk with no candidate at all is the empty reply's to answer, #667), and a relayed Gemini one told the same way (#1644; it ends in Gemini's own `{"error":{"code":502,…,"status":"UNAVAILABLE"}}` data line from `streamFailure`, `TestGeminiRelayEndedShort`; whatever a relayed stream gets appended, that error or a filled-in `finish_reason`, begins after a blank line, two newlines when the upstream's last write stopped inside a line, so it is an event of its own rather than more of the half-written one, and the half event itself is dropped: a relayed stream holds the line the upstream never ended back, puts it out when the reply ends whole or is gathered (`TestGrokPluginNonStreamLastLineUnended`), and throws it away when a failure is appended in its place, for no client can parse it — pi read "malformed server-sent event JSON" off a stream Z.ai reset mid-line, and died before the error after it; a line whose data parses (its JSON whole, or `[DONE]`) is no half event and goes out before the failure (#2446, #2580, `TestPassthroughStreamCutMidLine`, `TestChatStreamCutMidLineLeavesNoHalfEvent`, `TestChatStreamCutAfterWholeEventKeepsIt`, `TestGeminiCompatFinishAfterPartialLine`), and a Chat one from Gemini's OpenAI-compatible API (`geminiCompat`) with no `finish_reason`, relayed or translated (`saysItsEnd`; Linka on Discord: the upstream hung up between a whole function call and its `finish_reason`, and Pi threw the call away as "Stream ended without finish_reason"; that API counts the usage only once the generation is done, so when the last chunk carried it only the `finish_reason` was lost and it is filled in, `tool_calls` after a call and `stop` otherwise, as a relayed chunk before `[DONE]` or a translated reply's end, and the reply counts as whole (`chatTail`); only one with no usage at its end is a cut, `TestGeminiCompatRelayEndedShort`, `TestGeminiCompatTranslatedEndedShort`) — ends the turn with "the reply ended before it was complete" (`errEndedShort`, `TestResponsesUpstreamEndedShort`, `TestGeminiUpstreamEndedShort`), asked again in place where the upstream goes on from a prefill (`continuation.go`). only one with no usage at its end is a cut, `TestGeminiCompatRelayEndedShort`, `TestGeminiCompatTranslatedEndedShort`), and a Chat one from Command Code's API (`commandCodeHost`), whose slow starts a killed worker ends with no closing chunk at all, relayed (esp32gallery on #2471: 20 of 665 requests in a day, every ledger row a 200 with no stop and no error while the client read "Stream ended without finish_reason"; `TestCommandCodeRelayEndedShort`, `TestCommandCodeTaillessFailsOver`) — ends the turn with "the reply ended before it was complete" (`errEndedShort`, `TestResponsesUpstreamEndedShort`, `TestGeminiUpstreamEndedShort`), asked again in place where the upstream goes on from a prefill (`continuation.go`). A client that asked for no stream is told the same 502 of such a cut (`translate`, `TestNonStreamEndedShort`, `TestGeminiNonStreamEndedShort`), rather than handed what came as its whole reply. A translated reply from an Anthropic or Responses upstream that ended but said nothing (no text, call, search or image) and counted no token at all is no reply either, whatever its end says (`repliesCounted`, #2086: an Anthropic-compatible proxy's 200 ended a Codex turn as `response.completed` with no output, no usage and no served model, and Codex didn't ask again). It is the empty reply's to answer (#667): asked again in place, then failed with "an empty reply", streamed or not, for every client (`TestUncountedEmptyReplyFails`, `TestUncountedEmptyReplyFailsEveryClient`). Claude's own empty `end_turn` counts its prompt and stays a whole reply (`TestWellFormedStreamsStillComplete`). A relayed one is read the same way once: a Responses stream whose `response.completed` carries no output and counted no token is no reply either, failed before it is let through, held for the group's next candidate, and nobody rests (#2471, MurmuredVelpro: WorkBuddy AI's account ended Codex's turn completed with `output: []` and all-zero usage three times running, the healthy members never asked; `TestEmptyCompletedFailsOver`). A reply passed through in its own protocol isn't read this way, but for that completed.
- A vendor's error keeps its `code` and `param` on its way to an OpenAI-shaped client (`writeVendorError`), where `writeError` used to set both to null: a status error relayed or translated, and a request refused inside a Responses stream.
