A Responses stream's `error` event or failed response whose error is of type `invalid_request_error` is the request at fault (`requestRefused`, read by its type, not its words): its `Event` carries status 400 and the vendor's error (`Event.Said`), so a client answered whole gets the vendor's 400 with its code and param (the whole-translated path wrote 502 `api_error`), a reply cut by it isn't resumed (`continuation.again`), and a translated stream's error says the vendor's type, code and param (`saidError`), so `holdWriter` reads it as the 400 it is. Before, the hold read magpie's `api_error` as a 502: the same request was sent three times, then to the other accounts, and every one rested (magpie-releases#18: the ChatGPT backend answers `access_programs` naming a program the workspace hasn't got with a 200 stream whose error is `access_program_not_enabled`). Asked once, nobody rests ([`codex_access_programs_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_access_programs_test.go)).
- An error that reaches the agent in a stream already begun can't carry its status, so `streamError` says it in the words (`saysPassing`, #2372, zminds): for a status that may pass (408, 429, 5xx) whose text names none of the words a text-judging agent retries on (Pi decides by its `errorMessage` alone), it appends `(<status>: you can retry your request)`; a request's own fault (400, 401, 404 …) and a text that already says so are left as they were. `TestStreamErrorSaysItPasses` holds Pi's own lists; `TestGroupLastFailureInStreamSaysItPasses` runs a group whose last member fails after the keepalive. A 413 byte cap in that stream says `request_too_large` in the error text, and Chat and Anthropic name that type, so Pi compacts; Gemini keeps its own status shape (`TestStreamedByteCapSaysRequestTooLargeToPi`, `TestStreamErrorByteCapInEveryShape`). Responses' `response.failed` code for it is `context_length_exceeded`, the one code Codex compacts on: with `server_error` it sent the same body five more times ("reconnecting 5/5"), each refused, and never compacted (celestial_eagle_99230 on Discord, an nginx 413 page in front of a DeepSeek model; `TestCodexByteCapInStreamCompacts`). An overload told so (a 529, or a 5xx whose words say overloaded, `saidOverloaded`) is `server_is_overloaded` there, as the ChatGPT backend says one in a stream: Codex shows the model at capacity and stops, where `server_error` had it reconnect five times, each turn asked again by magpie, and end on the last error alone (wjh66 on Discord; `TestCodexKeptAliveOverloadSaysServerIsOverloaded`). Codex reads a 413, or a 400 `context_length_exceeded`, told by HTTP status as an unexpected status or an invalid request and compacts on neither, so when Codex (`fromCodex`) asked for a stream, a byte cap or an overflow is told in a stream even before one began (`keptAlive.compactsInStream`, `failTo`; `TestCodexByteCapBeforeStreamCompacts`). Another client keeps its status (`TestByteCapBeforeStreamStaysStatusForOthers`), and so does magpie's own summary ask for a compaction (`keptAlive.whole`, set for `wantsWhole`), which is never sent a 200 or a keepalive either: `codexCompact` reads a 413 as its cue to ask the conversation as text and then compact without a model, and told in a stream it failed 502 instead (#4862, `TestCompactionOverByteCapEveryTime`, `TestCompactionOverByteCapSlowFitting`). Nobody rests for either. Wherever the protocol says a model with the error (Chat's first chunk, Anthropic's `message_start`, Responses' `response.created` and the events after it), it is the model the agent asked for, as `serve` has it, an effort on it kept (`keptAlive.model`; on Codex's own sign-in, the one Codex wrote) (#3728, jilen: with `"model":""` fx took Chat's first chunk for a broken one and stopped there; `TestStreamErrorNamesTheModel`, `TestKeptAliveChatErrorNamesTheModel`, `TestKeptAliveChatOnCodexErrorNamesTheModel`, `TestKeptAliveCodexErrorNamesTheModel`). An error after a reply that already went, a loop cut's, still starts a message of its own after the vendor's, under a new id and no model (#3792).
- Claude's, GPT's, Gemini's and DeepSeek's reasoning is held (`refusesAfterThinking`, up to `holdThinking`, 4 minutes) so that a refusal after it goes to the next candidate unseen (#248). It is held only while one could: on the last candidate a refusal isn't asked again, so their reasoning streams as it comes there, and a refusal after it reaches the agent as the vendor's own. Held there, a lone relay's Claude or GPT showed Claude Code its thinking all at once with the text, and Codex none until then (iTianbao on X). Gemini's streams there too: a reply that only reasoned is asked again in place by `streamTranslated`, the reasoning the agent has kept and the next try's after it (#667), so holding it only kept Codex on a Gemini model with nothing at all, not even `response.created`, for the whole of its thinking (LuckymanJC on Discord; `TestThinkingStreamsWithNoOneElse`'s gemini cases). DeepSeek's is held since plugins #540: deepseek-v4.1-flash on WorkBuddy reasoned, then ended `content_filter` with nothing said (the WorkBuddy plugin takes the filter's canned "Sorry, I can't respond to this question." out of such a reply), and with its reasoning already through, the group's next member was never asked (`TestDeepSeekRefusalAfterThinkingFailsOver`). Where a refusal after the reasoning can still go to the next candidate in the same stream (`handsOver`: the agent asked for a Chat, Anthropic or Responses stream and another candidate is left; [`refusal_handover.go`](../../internal/gateway/refusal_handover.go)), a member whose thinking has gone on to an answer `thoughtCleanMost` (3) times running has its reasoning as it comes (`holdsThinking`), and a refusal after it, nothing yet said, is handed over, the next candidate's stream following as the same reply: DeepSeek's whether or not it can be handed over (#4512), Claude's, GPT's and Gemini's only where it can, so their refusal never reaches the agent while another could answer. Held whatever the member answered, a group of one model's accounts showed Claude Code its Claude's thinking only with its text, all at once, and never its `thinking with <effort> effort` (#4166). A retryable error after the reasoning, nothing yet said (Anthropic's `overloaded_error` mid-stream), is handed over the same way (`passOver`), its status and words failing the try as a held one's would, so trusting a member doesn't cost the group its failover; one the next candidate wouldn't be asked for (a request at fault) is the agent's (`TestTrustedThinkingFailsOverOnEveryPath`: Claude, GPT and Gemini members, refused, overloaded or invalid, to Claude Code, a Chat client and Codex; `TestClaudeTrustedThinkingErrorFailsOver`). A refusal takes the trust back (`thoughtRefused`), and the member's next reasoning is held again. `TestDeepSeekThinkingStreamsOnceTrusted`, `TestDeepSeekRefusalHoldsATrustedMemberAgain`, `TestClaudeThinkingStreamsOnceTrusted` and `TestClaudeTrustedThinkingStreamsThroughGroup` in [`thinking_streams_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/thinking_streams_test.go). Any other model's reasoning (GLM's, Kimi's…) is never held.
- A streamed reply stuck in a loop is ended, not let run to its output limit (#1359, Moody-Sin: WorkBuddy's DeepSeek v4.1 flash reasoned round "Now." "Writing." "Go." "Let me write." "OK." until max tokens while dsh and Pi waited). `holdWriter.Write` feeds every streamed try, passed through or translated, a key's, a built-in subscription's or a plugin's (a moved WorkBuddy's fetch in the plugin host, `TestLoopEndsWorkBuddysReply`), in each of the agent's protocols, to a `loopGuard` ([`loop_guard.go`](../../internal/gateway/loop_guard.go)). It cuts the reasoning and the text, each apart, into units, its lines and sentences (a list's `1.` or a decimal is no sentence's end), compared without case or a closing stop. A unit with no word in it (a letter run with no digit: not a brace, a rule, `0x00`, a hash or a row of numbers) isn't counted. When the last `loopWindow` (256) counted units of either are among no more than `loopDistinct` (24) different ones, the try is cut (`cutLoop`): what came with that write goes out, then, after a blank line (two newlines when that write stopped inside a line, `holdWriter.atLine`, #2446), the protocol's stream error (`streamError`, 502) naming the loop and quoting its units, and the vendor's request is cancelled. It falls back to nobody and rests nobody (`failLoop`); its usage record's error type is `reply_loop` (`usage.LoopErrType`), and Usage counts them per model (`Totals.Loops`), as "{n} looped" beside the model's failures in the Overview's rows and the Requests ranking (#1858, `TestLoopsCountedPerModel`, `usage-loops.test.cjs`). A reply magpie translates into the agent's protocol (`streamTranslated`) whose *reasoning* loops before it has said a text or a call, and isn't a continuation, is asked again once in place instead (`loopReasks`, through `holdWriter.onLoop`; cdredfox on X, WorkBuddy AI's DeepSeek v4 flash): the agent keeps the reasoning it has, the next try's follows it in the same stream, as an empty reply's re-ask does (#667), and only a loop on that try ends the reply with the error (`TestReasoningLoopIsAskedAgain`). A reply passed through in the agent's own protocol is asked again once too, at the fallback loop (`holdWriter.mayReloop`, `letGoLoop`; #2436, lqisgod: pi's Chat stream to WorkBuddy was cut at once): when nothing of the try has reached the agent yet, in any protocol, its held bytes are dropped; when its reasoning is already passing, only a Chat stream goes on, written up to the end of the tripping event, with the next try's chunks after it. An Anthropic or Responses stream already passing still ends with the error, since a second `message_start` or `response.created` can't follow. Each re-ask, in place or at the fallback loop, counts in the usage record's tries. A loop in the text, or after a text or call, is ended at once (`TestLoopAfterTextIsNotAskedAgain`). A cycle need not be exact. Code, tables, lists, logs, CSV and base64 differ line to line or have no words, and don't trip it (`TestLoopGuardLeavesOrdinaryRepliesAlone` feeds magpie's own sources and docs through it). A reply sent whole (no stream, or Gemini without `alt=sse`) isn't read. Settings › Models › Replies › *Stop looping replies* (`noLoopGuard`) turns it off.
- A conversation going round one tool call is answered by magpie and sent nowhere (audie on Discord: Codex Desktop on WorkBuddy's deepseek-v4-pro read the same file 308 times in half an hour, and 340 times that evening, 537 credits). `stoppedToolLoop` ([`tool_loop.go`](../../internal/gateway/tool_loop.go)) runs in `serveAgent` before hooks, middleware and routing, and in the Codex backend's passthrough on Codex's own sign-in. It reads the request's own history, so it is per conversation and keeps no state: when the last `toolLoopRepeats` (8) turns are each the same calls (name and arguments, keys in any order, call ids ignored) answered with the same results (with Codex's `Wall time`, `Chunk ID` and `duration_seconds` taken out, `runNoise`) and nothing else from the user, the turn is answered in the agent's own protocol, streamed or whole (`writeLocalText` through `relayStatus`), with a notice naming the call. Any different result, or a word of the user's, starts the count again; the notice is a turn without calls, so the user's next word goes on. A body with fewer than 8 tool results isn't parsed (`mayLoop`), and only the newest 64 items of a long one are (`tailOf`, gjson), ~1ms for a 1.5MB turn (`BenchmarkToolLoopCheck`). The turn is recorded as Codex's title turns are (`answeredHere`): the Routing trace as "Tool loop stopped", and a usage row with provider `magpie`, rejected, its session and an error starting `tool loop:`. A poll that gets the same answer 8 times in a row is stopped too. Settings › Models › Replies › *Stop looping replies* (`noLoopGuard`) turns it off with the reply guard. Verification: `go test -tags nogui ./internal/gateway -run 'TestToolLoop'`.
- A Chat request's earlier turns carry the assistant's thinking back in the spelling its own client used: `reasoning_content` (DeepSeek's, which OpenCode and most clients send), `reasoning` (OpenRouter's) or `reasoning_details` (OpenRouter's structured one). A request translated to a non-Chat upstream reads whichever of the three the turn has, in that order, as the turn's thinking (`messageReasoning`, `reasoningText`, read by `parseChat` in [chat.go](../../internal/gateway/chat.go)); the passthrough has read all three since `mistralThinking` (#494), which was the one place that did. Before that the parse layer read `reasoning_content` alone, so a tool-calling session whose client spelled it another way went on from its second turn without the model's own reasoning.
- The order of the steps a request's accounts go through, how much the accounts' own order (*Make first*) counts under each routing, and how often an allowance is read are described for users in [How magpie picks an account](../reference.md#how-magpie-picks-an-account). Smart compares renewals window by window, biggest first, each truncated to the hour (`weighRouted`), so the order decides only when every window falls in the same hour. A window's renewal is its reset, or, when what it has left runs out sooner or it has no reset, that moment (`Limit.RunsOut`, from `QuotaWindow.RunsOutAt`; `Allowance.Renewal`): the WorkBuddy plugin (0.1.14) sends it on its Credits window as the soonest end of a package with credits left (cdredfox on X). When the renewals tie to the hour, Smart goes by that moment once more (`Allowance.Lapse`, `Seat.Lapse`), so of accounts renewing together (WorkBuddy's free accounts all reset at the month's end, plugin 0.1.35) the one whose credits left lapse first, after that end, goes first, and one telling none goes after (zhaocai_83020 on Discord). Only Smart reads it; Use before expiry, the 90%/98% tiers and rests go by the window's share and reset as before. A row on the Routing page says the share used (the fullest window's, `Allowance.For`) with when that same window renews (`Allowance.Fullest`, the trace's `usedRenews`), not the biggest window's: a Factory account 32% into its week and 16% into its month reads "32% used · renews in 3 days" while Smart still ranks it by its month (#1778). A route from before `usedRenews` says when only for an account of one window. Affinity keeps a conversation on an account at 90–97%: only `SpentShareOf` (98%, 100% In order) stops it (`affine`), while the 90% tier (`lowShare`) orders only conversations nobody has answered.
- A turn's end is told to the app for the "Task done alert" notification (itieniu on X, `turndone.go`). The trace follows each agent session's turn (`turns`, keyed by agent and session): a request is of the turn unless it has a `Kind` or is a Claude Code subagent's (`agentTurnOf`), and its reply is scanned on the way to the agent for a tool call in any wire format (`toolScan`, a JSON value such as `"type":"tool_use"` or `"finish_reason":"tool_calls"`, never a key or text). The turn is done when no request of it is going, the last reply was whole, under 400 and called no tool, and nothing more came for `turnQuiet` (4s); a session idle past `turnStale` (30m) starts over. Only a turn that ran at least `settings.TurnDoneAlert` seconds is told (`TurnDone`, set by the GUI; nil under `magpie serve`, so nothing is followed). The ChatGPT backend path (`codexBackend`) is followed the same way. `TestTurnDoneSaidAtTheTurnsEnd`, `TestTurnDoneCodexBackend`, `TestToolScan`, `turn-done.test.cjs`.
- A request's masking record is told to the app for the "Masking alert" notification (Must2333 on X, `OnMasked` in [`masked.go`](../../internal/gateway/masked.go), set by the GUI; nil under `magpie serve`), after `maskrec.Hold` and with its text around placeholders (`Snips`, `Back`) taken out. [`mask_alert.go`](../../internal/gui/mask_alert.go) keeps the records while `settings.MaskAlert` is on (off until turned on, in Settings → Usage, hidden with masking) and says only the values a record masked for the first time (`maskrec.Fresh`, [`first.go`](../../internal/maskrec/first.go): each placeholder by the key of the first record that had it, read from memory and the days kept, so a conversation's history sent again each turn, or after a restart, is not news; a value sent as written is not one masked). A burst is gathered for `maskGather` (3s) and said as one, and one is said at most every `maskGap` (1 minute), what came in between said after it. It says each kind with its count and up to two agents, never a value nor its hint; a click opens the Masking page on the latest record (`clickView`, `masking&key=`). A held-back request (`noteBlocked`) is not said; a value whose records have all aged out (30 days) is new again. `TestFreshOnlyTheFirstTime`, `TestMaskAlertFirstTimeOnly`, `TestMaskAlertNeverSaysTheValue`, `TestMaskAlertCoalesces`, `mask-alert.test.cjs`.
- A resting candidate is tried last (`restLast`). Of the resting candidates, a request asks only the one whose rest ends soonest, the first in order on a tie (`probeOne`, #1799). The others aren't asked by that request, so a member that is down isn't asked by every request its rest was meant to spare it from, and a group that is down whole takes one upstream request per request, not one per member. Every ready candidate is still asked in its order. A lone candidate is tried even while it rests, because there is no other. The trace still lists every member, the resting ones with their rest. Only rests are read, and a failure about the request's content or its model rests no member (a model refusal rests only `restID`), so neither leaves a member out. The same applies in `count_tokens`, embeddings and rerank, image accounts and a group's System One decision models. A Codex reset (`autoReset`) still looks over every candidate. This is like AxonHub's error-aware load balancing: the rest and its backoff are the penalty that decays, and the member back soonest is the one asked to show it is back. Keys out of their allowance aren't asked even so, not even the one back soonest or a lone one (#1772: four Ollama Cloud keys out for the week, each asked again and refused on every request). The refusal given again says when they are asked again on this magpie's clock with its offset (`heldClock`: "20:12 UTC+8", "Oct 16 09:00 UTC+8"), as do the gateway's other held errors (a capped window, a spent searcher or describer): its reader may be a Remote magpie's user on another clock. When every candidate left is a key (not a subscription account, whose resets `autoReset` may spend) resting out of quota or credit after a 429 or 402, none is asked. The first one's refusal is given again (`quotaHeld`, its words kept in the rest's `said`), saying when it is asked again. A key whose rest magpie guessed the length of, the vendor and the user naming no time (`creditRest`, or `quotaRest` with no `Provider.OwnQuotaRest`), is held so only once it was refused so twice in a row (`Rest.first`): a single refusal may be a moment's, and the next request, the agent's retry first, asks it again (zoecats on Discord: a local aggregator said 429 `insufficient_quota` "quota exhausted" for the moment it switched upstreams, and every request was answered from its rest of 15 or 30 minutes, which only the provider's "Rests" switch, `LimitRestOff`, let through before). Held, it is asked again a minute on (`Rest.askAt`, `fallbackCooldown`), once, and held again if refused (`Rest.asksAt`); a time the vendor named, or the user set, holds it from the first refusal to then. The rest itself stays as long as before, so the order still passes it over for a candidate that is ready. The last key left rests too when it is refused so, as the keys before it do, and so does the last account left (a Codex or other subscription account; `quotaHeld` still holds keys alone, and `autoReset` lifts the rest of an account it spends a reset of): before, an account last in an in-order group, out of its quota, was never rested, so every request a relay ahead of it failed for a moment went on to it and ended on its 429 (dbydd on Discord; `TestGroupSpentLastAccountRests`). Any 429 that reaches the agent without a vendor `Retry-After`, while every candidate tried is resting, carries one: the seconds until the first of them is asked again (`soonestBack`, by `asksAt`), none after a key's first refusal. `TestRetryAfterALimitIsAsked`, `TestHeldLimitAskedAgainInAMinute`. A quota refusal with no word of when it is back rests `quotaRest` (15 minutes), or the provider's own `quotaRest` in minutes (`Provider.QuotaRestFor`, at most `MaxQuotaRest`, 8 days; `magpie provider quota-rest <id> 7d`), or until the time of day the user said its allowance comes back (`QuotaResetAt`, `Provider.OwnQuotaRest`; `magpie provider quota-rest <id> until 08:00`, or `until 00:00+08:00` on the vendor's clock; #2008, a free tier's "today's free-model token quota"). Whichever of the two was set last counts. A time the vendor names stands over it, and so does its `Retry-After`, unless that was more than `longestWait` (an hour) and so cut to it while the user's own rest is longer: then the user's stands (`TestQuotaRestsUntilTheTimeOfDaySet`). A key listed in `keys` as well as in `key` is one candidate (`KeysOn`), not asked twice. `TestAllRestingAnswersFromRest`, `TestOwnQuotaRest`, `TestQuotaHeldOnlyForKeysOutOfAllowance`, `TestFirstKeyListedTwiceAskedOnce`.
- A model its plugin reports busier than the user's *Busy models last* (`settings.BusyLoad`, 1 to 9999 (`BusyLoadMost`-1), past 100 for a vendor that queues, its Custom… field taking any of them, off by default; axelburks on Discord) goes behind the ready candidates, ahead of the resting ones (`restLast`, `busyOf`), and its trace row has `busy` with the load. The load comes from the plugin through `plugin.ModelLoad` (see [provider plugins](provider-plugins.md#a-models-load)); one not reported in the last 5 minutes is unknown and keeps its place. A lone candidate is asked whatever its load. A conversation it answered moves, at its next turn, to the ready one that leads (`affine`'s Why `busy`, `busyBehind`); within a turn it stays. A turn going round (`after`), a new conversation spread (`readyForNew`) or In turn (`leastHeld`) never lands on it past a ready one. When every candidate is busy, nothing moves.
- A key resting after an outage that names no time is asked again in the background (`rest_probe.go`, heatonz on Discord). Behind ready keys, `probeOne` never asks it before its rest ends, though its vendor may be back in a minute. `restAfterMarked` marks the rest `Probed` when it is a key's own (not an account's or a plugin's, not one model's `restID`), `failOther` by `backoff` after a 5xx, a 408 or no connection, with no Retry-After or reset header, at a provider whose `List` asks over the network (`Provider.ListAsks`), and long enough for a first ask. `keepProbingRests`, started in `ListenAndServe`, then asks the vendor's model list with that key through its proxy (`Provider.List`, which saves nothing) 1, 2, 5 and then every 10 minutes into the rest, while it is the same rest. When the list answers, the rest is lifted as Try now lifts it (`liftedLocked`, `unsink`, the trace's lift). Not asked: rate limits (a list answers while the chat endpoint still refuses, and lifting one early would undo Sink), quota, credit, a Retry-After or reset, a refused key (401, 403, 400), restrictions and verification, each of which ends on its own time or the vendor's. A key a probe lifted that fails again within 30 minutes isn't asked again until it answers a request (`probeMissed`, `probeAnswered` in `served`): the list answered for the vendor, not for its chat. Only the loop's own `Server` asks (`restProbes.asking`), so a test's `Server` never reaches the network. The cost is one list per resting key per step, never one per request. The key list (`KeyRest.Probed`) and Routing's words for the try (`restHow`) add "checked in the background, back as soon as it answers". `TestRestProbeLiftsAKeyBackUp`, `TestRestProbeLeavesOtherRests`, `TestRestProbeOnlyByARunningLoop`, `rest-probed.test.cjs`.
- A provider whose keys are on different plans (`KeyAccount.Plan`, `PlansMixed`; yckonnnn on X) sends each key to its plan's endpoints: every key path takes its copy through `WithKey`, which moves it there (`atKeyPlan`), on the first try and on a failover alike. `perKeyBarred` leaves out a key whose plan's list lacks the model (`PlanServing`: a pay-as-you-go model id at the Agent Plan's endpoint only answers 404), not shown in the trace; only when no key's plan has it are those keys asked. Embeddings and drawing go through the same `perKeyBarred` and `WithKey`; a video started on a mixed provider names its key in the task id (`videoStart.onKey`, `keyMark`) so its poll goes to that key's plan. `TestKeyPlansEachToItsOwnEndpoint`.
- A subscription's account whose own model list lacks the model (`Account.Lists`: an Antigravity account's `fetchAvailableModels`, a plugin account's `models`) isn't planned while another of its accounts lists it (`perKeyBarred`'s `left`, `planned.unlisted`, the Routing page's "unlisted, skipped"). It is the next asked when every account of the subscription that lists the model has been asked and one refused it as a model it doesn't serve (`unservedTry`: `modelRefused`, or Code Assist's 404 NOT_FOUND, `codeAssistModelMissing`), before any later member or fallback (`unlistedNext`, `askUnlisted` moves its row from the route's left to its order after the one that refused). A list is fetched once and can be stale: 01huadalang's Gemini 4 was served by one Antigravity account of several, and the one whose list named it refused it with a 404 the agent got while the other could answer. The one that refused rests for that model alone. Not for another refusal (a 403, a quota), a request pinned to an account, a native compaction sealed to one, or a conversation kept strictly on one; a gateway key held to some accounts narrows the unlisted ones too (`allowedCandidates`). `TestUnlistedAccountAskedAfterModelRefused`, `TestUnlistedAccountNotAskedForOtherRefusals`.
- A member whose model is known to take less than the request isn't asked it (`roomy` in [`fallback.go`](../../internal/gateway/fallback.go), in `serve` after the rules, affinity, the image filter and `pinTo`, before `probeOne`; dgqyushen on Discord: OpenCode Zen's free DeepSeek V4.1 Flash, 200K, first in a group and a 1M subscription of it second). It is a check before sending, not a retry: the request's tokens are estimated as the overflow fall-over does (`estimate`, bytes/4 over text, tool arguments and schemas), and a candidate whose window (`windowOf`: the provider's `Contexts`, the catalog, `ContextOf`) is known and no larger is left out of the candidates and of the trace's order, and listed in the trace's `left` with `window` set to that window. Asked, such a member only refuses, and a refusal that doesn't say why, for a request short of `tooLongUnsaid`'s 1.2 margin, reached the agent as the vendor's error while the next member had room; it also spent a request against the member's limits. A conversation kept on a member by affinity moves once it outgrows it. Nothing is left out when its window isn't known, when the body doesn't parse, when only one model is among the candidates, or when no candidate holds the request: then the first is asked, and its overflow reaches the agent as one, to compact (`withRoom`, `failOverflow` and `tooLongUnsaid` stay for the window the estimate misses, which counts low on code and high on CJK text). A body shorter than four bytes per token of the least window isn't parsed. `count_tokens` (a count on the group's first member, not a send) and System One's decision models (short decision text) don't go through it. Those left out are the last resort: when the last candidate with room fails out of its allowance or credit (`failQuota`/`failCredit`), nothing before it having failed otherwise (`other`), and no account is pinned, the ones left out that aren't resting are asked after all, their rows moved from the trace's `left` to its order, and the one that failed rests as one before the last does. Its overflow tells the agent to compact, where the 429 only told it to wait for an allowance hours away (Kai on Discord: both Claude accounts spent until the next day, the Claude provider's Fallback a Codex model of 272K under a longer conversation, never asked). Another failure (a busy vendor, a bad key) still reaches the agent as it was, the small one not asked. The Routing page keeps a row for a member left out and not asked, saying the request is longer than its window and that it was passed over, and its why list says so (#3749; it used to show it only by its absence from the order). `TestMemberTooSmallIsNotAsked`, `TestMemberTooSmallIsTheLastResort`, `TestClaudeAccountsSpentFallBackToCodex`, `TestConversationOutgrowingItsMemberMoves`, `TestRoomyLeavesWhatItCantJudge`.
- Routing decides who goes first only when nobody has answered the conversation yet. Affinity keeps the answerer while its cache is worth it. Auto mode keeps it across turns while the last reply read from the cache and wrote to it at least `cacheWorth` (1024) tokens between them (the usage's `CacheRead` plus `CacheWrite`, passed to `answered`) and the cache isn't cold (`cacheCold`, 5 minutes; for a ChatGPT account `chatGPTCold`, an hour, as measured on a real account (a thread left 7 to 60 minutes read back from cache all it had sent before), since the ChatGPT backend keeps a Codex conversation cached far longer and a move sends the whole context to the other account uncached; on DeepSeek's own API (`api.deepseek.com`) `deepseekCold`, an hour, since DeepSeek clears an unused prefix cache after hours and a Claude Code conversation idle 7 and 26 minutes read 98–99% back, so a second DeepSeek key no longer takes it over after five minutes: `coldAfter`, which a group's rules read too for `keepsEffort`). Past that window it stays while the vendor said it keeps at least `cacheWorth` tokens of the conversation for an hour (`hourCache`, #2302): a reply with Anthropic's 1-hour writes (`cache_creation.ephemeral_1h_input_tokens`, the usage's `CacheWrite1h`, as Claude Code asks of a subscription) keeps what it read plus those for `cacheHour`, a later reply on the same account and model that reads all of it starts the hour again, and one that reads less (moved, compacted) drops it. The stick keeps it on disk too (`cacheHour`, `cacheHourAt`), and a group's rules keep the turn's effort the same hour (`keepsEffort`). Vendors that report no 1-hour write are unchanged. `TestAffinityKeepsAnHourCachePastFiveMinutes`, `TestHourCacheAfter`, `TestDeepSeekConversationStaysPastFiveMinutes`. What was written counts: a first turn reads nothing and writes the whole prompt (Anthropic's `cache_creation_input_tokens`), and the second turn stays to read it rather than moving as `no-cache` and building it again elsewhere (vilicvane on Discord). The stick keeps both, on disk too (`affinity.json`'s `cacheWrite`, absent in a file from before and read as 0, so such a stick is judged by its read alone), and the trace's `Affinity.CacheWrite` lets the Routing page say what was read and what was written. A vendor that keeps a prompt cached without reporting it (OpenAI's automatic prefix cache, Gemini) says neither of a turn that read none, so the prompt that turn was sent (the usage's `Input`) stands for the write and its second turn stays too; a reply that reports no usage at all counts as no cache, as before. `TestAffinityKeepsACacheJustWritten`, `TestAffinityKeepsACacheTheVendorKeptSilently`, `routing-affinity-cache-write.test.cjs`.
- A conversation kept where it is (`Affinity.Kept`: Auto with a warm cache, Session, or within a turn) whose key or account fails before anything is sent, in a way that passes (`busy`: an overloaded 503 or 529, another 5xx that says nothing of quota, a 408), and not as a rate limit (429), a quota, a refusal or a sign-in, is asked once more after `retryPause` (1s, or a Retry-After up to `longestPause`) without resting, before the request moves on (`keptAgain` in [`affinity.go`](../../internal/gateway/affinity.go), jiabubu on Discord: a channel that errs once and is back a moment later had the conversation moved off it at once, to be sent it all uncached elsewhere). Once a request; failing again, it rests and the next is asked as before. A conversation nobody has answered yet, or under Off, moves at once, as it has no cache to keep; Strict's lone candidate is the last one left, asked again as that is (`lastRetries`). The trace marks the try (`Try.Kept` with its `Again`), and the Routing page says it was asked again for what its vendor cached, not that nobody else was left. `TestKeptConversationAskedAgainBeforeItMoves`, `routing-kept-again.test.cjs`.
- Strict (`strict`, #1797, miniers) never moves a conversation to another key or account (`who`). Its stick is kept as session's is, and while it is fresh `affine` keeps only the candidates of that `who` (`strictOnly`): its other models stay, so a group's rules still pick among them, but no other account, key or provider is asked, and a resting or spent one is asked anyway. A failure there reaches the agent and rests the account as a non-lone candidate's would (`strictAlone`), so new conversations go elsewhere. A reply that breaks off after its 200 (#733) forgets the stick under every other mode; under strict it doesn't, so the agent's retry goes back to the same account, member or key, failing there while it fails and going on there once it answers (imwhatiam123 on Discord: a group of Qoder CN and WorkBuddy AI moved a session to WorkBuddy AI, the retry routed as a new conversation; `TestStrictGroupKeepsItsMemberPastABrokenReply`). Before planning, `strictKeep` takes the stuck account back from what holds new conversations off it: a usage cap (`pl.capped`, `capHeldOnly`), so the conversation goes on past it. An account held for credits it may not spend is answered 429 naming the account and Strict, and nothing reaches the vendor, rather than moved. An account removed, switched off, barred, or not allowed for the calling key is gone, and the conversation moves as under session. The trace says `strict` even with one candidate. `strict_affinity_test.go`, `stays-strict.test.cjs`.
- A provider's fallback is its emergency path, not a place a conversation stays (01huadalang on Discord). When the one that answered a conversation last is one of a provider's `Fallback` models (its trace row's `Fallback`) and the provider's own model stands first in the plan, ready, the next turn goes to the provider, whatever the fallback cached and in every mode but Strict (`backToMain` in `affine`, `Affinity.Why` `main`). "Ready" is what `restLast` says: the provider's rest over, which is when the rest `restAfter` set ends (a minute and backing off for a plain failure, the vendor's named reset or Retry-After for a quota or rate limit, `quotaRest` 15 minutes when nothing names a time), or earlier when its windows are read full no more (`renewed`) or a background probe finds it answering (`Rest.Probed`). While the provider rests, the fallback is ahead of it and keeps the conversation as any answerer does. Within a turn (the agent sending tool results back) the conversation stays on the fallback until the user speaks again: the turn's tool loop runs on reasoning the fallback sealed, which the provider can't read, and the provider is sent the whole prompt afresh at the next turn all the same. Strict is left as the user set it: it never moves a conversation, a fallback's included; the trace then names the provider's ready key or account (`Affinity.Main`) and the Routing page says the conversation stays on the fallback for Strict while new conversations go to the provider (`TestStrictKeepsTheFallbackAndSaysTheMainIsReady`, `routing-affinity-order.test.cjs`). A provider routed In turn goes round its own keys and accounts only: its next turn (`after`) and a new conversation (`leastHeld`) pass over a fallback while one of the provider's own leads (`standsIn`). Before, a fallback was one more seat in the round, so with one key every other turn went to the fallback, and a new conversation went to it as the one nobody was on, the provider ready (01huadalang again, 10-31; `TestRotatingProviderKeepsItsFallbackForEmergencies`, `TestFallbackGivesBackAcrossRoutings`). A routing group's members are never `Fallback`; a group gives a conversation back by its own rules (`order`, `back`, below), and a routing group named as a provider's fallback is a fallback here as a whole. Before this, Auto kept a conversation on the fallback by its cache for as long as each turn read it, and Session for the session. `TestFallbackGivesTheConversationBackOnceTheMainIsReady`. The Routing page says so ("{who}, a fallback, answered this conversation last, but the provider's own {first} is ready again…", `affWhy` in `plugins/routing/client.js`); before, `main` showed no reason at all.
- A routing group set to In order is tried in the order the user put its members in, and a later member keeps a conversation only until a member ahead of it is ready again (yyhhyyyyyy on Discord: an API key first and a Claude subscription second for Opus, 8 of 10 Claude Code requests on the subscription). One failure of the first member (a 429, a 5xx, a rest) sent a conversation to the second, and Auto affinity's `cache` kept it there at every turn after, since each turn read what the last had written. Now, with Stays left on Auto, a new turn goes back to the first ready member when it is another member than the one that answered last (`backToFirst` in `affine`, `Affinity.Why` `order`; another key or account of the same member, or a group in the group, keeps its own affinity). Within a turn it stays, on the reasoning the later member sealed, as with `main`. Stays: Session and Strict are the user's own and keep the conversation where it is. The Routing page says why it moved. `TestOrderedGroupGivesTheConversationBackToItsFirstMember`, `routing-affinity-order.test.cjs`.
- A group routed any other way (Smart, Least used first, By weight and the rest, In turn aside) gives back a conversation a later member took only as a stand-in (01huadalang on Discord, 11-01: Codex on two accounts first in a Smart group, a MiMo key second as the backup; after an outage of Codex's 5 hours, a session that had gone to MiMo stayed there by MiMo's cache once Codex was back, while new ones went to Codex). A member stood in when, as it answered, a member the group lists ahead of its own was resting (a 429, a 5xx: `restAfter` rests the one that failed before the try goes on) or known all but used up (`Used` at `SpentShareOf`): `standsInFor`, over the plan as it was for the request, by each candidate's `place` in the group's list (`placed` in `planGroup`; a group in the group is one member). The stick keeps it (`stick.stoodIn`, and `stoodIn` in `affinity.json`, absent from a file written before and read as no stand-in) while the same account and model answer on, so a stand-in that routing still puts first after the one ahead is back (Smart's low tier, a week at 92%) gives the conversation back once routing puts the one ahead first. At a new turn, with Stays on Auto, the conversation goes to the first ready member when that one is listed ahead of the stand-in's (`backFromStandIn`, `Affinity.Why` `back`); another key or account of the same member keeps it by its cache. A member routing put first while those ahead of it were ready and had room is no stand-in and keeps its cache as before, so peers of a Smart group don't trade conversations by renewal order. Within a turn it stays; Within a turn and Off route each new turn afresh anyway; Session and Strict keep it where it is; In turn's next turn goes round (`after`). Routing still decides who is first: Least used first and Smart put a key whose usage isn't told (counted unused) ahead of accounts at 90% or more of a window, so a conversation, and every new one, goes to such a backup while the subscription's accounts are that low (routing's own choice, not affinity's; In order keeps a backup last). `TestGroupBackupGivesTheConversationBack`, `TestGroupBackupStandingInWhileTheFirstIsBackAndBehind`, `TestGroupMemberPutFirstOnItsMeritsKeepsItsCache`, `TestGroupStandInStaysWithinATurn`, `TestGroupMemberGivesTheConversationBackOnceTheOneAheadIsBack`, `routing-affinity-order.test.cjs`.
- In turn (`rotate`), a conversation nobody has answered yet goes to the key, account or model the fewest other conversations of the provider's model or the group answered in the last `heldFor` (30 minutes) are on (`leastHeld`, #946). Ties, resting ones, a provider's fallback while the provider's own leads (`standsIn`) and a group with a group in it keep routing's order. With session affinity, two sessions of one group so stay on different models.
- Requests that come together spread (`burstSpread` in [`burst.go`](../../internal/gateway/burst.go), #5242: Moody-Sin and Tericerice sent four at once to three Antigravity accounts, In turn and By pace, and every one went to one account). Routing weighs what each seat served (`Tokens`) and its allowance, which move only once a reply ends, and a lane counts a request only once its try begins, so requests planned at the same moment were all planned on the same figures; In turn's next turn (`after`) reads a stick that moves only on an answer too. Once the plan is made (after affinity, rules, pinning, `roomy`, `behindOverflow`), `burstSpread` puts first, of the candidates alike to the first, the one with the fewest requests in flight (`inFlight`, by `candidate.who`), ties keeping routing's order, and counts the request there in the same hold of the lock; the count is given back when `serve` ends. Alike (`burstAlike`): ready as the first is (not resting, aside, reserved, barred, held, capped, a fallback standing in, credits-last or busy behind), the same fallback, fit, protocol, URL and `Via`, the rule's member when a group's rule picked one; In turn any of the round; By pace a pace within a tenth (`paceAlike`, routes.Pace's band), the same `Learns`, both with room (`burstTier` 0) and no wallet told apart (`sameBalance`); Least used within a point used, the same tier and known-ness. In a group, a provider routed its own way (`ownRouting`) is alike only to its own provider's keys, by its routing, and a group in the group to nothing. Smart, In order, By weight and the others keep their order. Not moved, only counted: a conversation kept (`Affinity.Kept`), Strict, an `X-Magpie-Account` pin, a sealed task's lead, a native compaction's reader, and a new conversation `leastHeld` spreads (In turn, `spreadsSessions`), which counts those in flight itself (`sticks.opening`). A plugin's provider and a remote magpie take the same path; the remote spreads over its own accounts, the sessions passed on to it (`passOnCaller`). Not done: putting a markedly slower account behind (speed is read only for Speed first, `speedOf`), as a first token's wait goes with the prompt's size and the vendor's queue more than with the account. `burst_spread_test.go`.
- Stays: Session or Strict under a routing that picks by what is left (Smart, Least used first, Use before expiry: `spreadsSessions`) spreads new conversations the same way (connor_yang on Discord: three sessions over three accounts landed on two). `leastHeld` with `ready` passes over one routing keeps new conversations off (`readyForNew`: resting, a key for another protocol, a provider's fallback while its own model leads, one used past `lowShare`), counts only leads (a key without `|agent:`), and leaves the turn marks alone. A subagent's first request goes to its lead's key or account while that one is `readyForNew` (`withLead`), so a session keeps one account, its subagents included. Held is the last `heldFor` (30 minutes): a session idle longer counts no more. In order, By weight, Free first, Speed first, Cheapest, Manual and a plugin's routing keep their order. An answered conversation is kept as before; the spread only decides where a new one starts. `session_spread_test.go`.
- A ChatGPT account that holds credits answers past its windows on them; the vendor spends them. That is the default. Set not to spend them (Usage card's *Use credits*, `magpie quota credits <account> off`), the account is held at 100% of a window till it renews, by magpie's latest reading of its windows (`provider.Allowances`, refreshed about every minute), so the other accounts, groups and fallbacks take the request. A cap set on the account (1–99%) takes precedence. Unlike a cap, the hold is at the vendor's own 100%, so an account allowed to spend its resets spends one there, both when every candidate is held and after the last one is refused. A reset made forgets the account's cached allowance (`renewedNow`), so neither hold outlives the windows it started again. One let spend its credits, and holding some by its last `/wham/usage` (`credits.has_credits` with a balance over 0, or `unlimited`; `QuotaWindow.Credits`, `Limit.Credits`, `Allowance.OnCredits`), is not out when a window fills (豆子 and Zhenzhen on Discord): ChatGPT may answer the turn that uses the window up 429 `usage_limit_reached` before it spends the credits, and the next turn on them. So that 429 is asked again at once, once a request, on the same account, before a reset is looked at, on the routed path (`creditsAgain`, `creditsAsked`, `Try.Credits`) and on Codex's own sign-in's relay (`relayOnCredits`), rather than ended for the agent, which ended a running session while a new one worked. Refused again, it rests `fallbackCooldown` (`Rest.By` `credits`) and is read again, not until its window renews, and its full windows rest it nowhere else (`full` is zero for it, `spendsCredits`). Smart, Pace and Least used put such an account first of the spent tier (`routes.Seat.Credits`, `spentBefore`): after those with allowance left, ahead of those used up with nothing to go on with (`Weighed.Credits`, told on the Routing page). Switching to credits early, at a few percent left, was weighed and not done: the vendor spends credits only once the allowance is used up, so it would only waste what is left. `TestCodexCreditsAskedAgainAtTheEdge`, `TestCodexCreditsNoLongRest`, `TestCodexCreditsFirstOfTheSpent`, `TestCreditsFirstOfTheSpent`, `routing-codex-credits-again.test.cjs`.
- A third way, set per account, spends the credits only after everything else (gokoshiki on Discord: a Codex subscription, then an OpenAI API key, then the subscription's credits): *Only after every other account, key and fallback* under the Usage card's *Use credits*, `magpie quota credits <account> last`, `settings.CodexCreditsLast` (`provider.CodexCreditsLast`, `SetCodexCreditsMode`; on or off clears it, and an account never in it keeps its place, so a setting saved before keeps its order). Such an account is `creditsLast` while it goes on on its credits (`spendsCredits`) and a window for the model is used up where *off* would hold it (`Allowance.CapHeld` at `WindowCaps{Credits: true}`; a call ChatGPT counts in no window, `freeOnChatGPT`, stays). `restLast` then puts it behind every candidate of the plan, the provider's fallbacks and groups included: ready, then resting, then the credits-last ones in their own order (`Weighed.CreditsLast`). Affinity doesn't keep a conversation on one while one not on its credits leads (`Affinity.Why` `credits`, `lastBehind`), within a turn too, and In turn's next pick and Smart/Pace/Least used's spreading of new sessions never bring one forward (`after`, `leastHeld`, `readyForNew`). Strict affinity keeps it, as a rule, an `X-Magpie-Account` pin and a sealed task's lead (`leadFirst`) still put theirs first. A reset is still spent before its credits when the try reaches it (`resetBeforeCredits`). Codex's own sign-in's relay has the one account and nothing to put first, so there it is as *on*. The Routing page tells each: behind the rest, first when every one is, and the conversation moved. `TestCodexCreditsLastAfterTheOthers`, `TestCodexCreditsLastAfterTheFallback`, `TestCodexCreditsLastNotKept`, `TestCodexCreditsLastMode`, `codex-credits-last.test.cjs`.
- A fourth, set per account, spends the credits before moving to another account (jojonas_xyz on X: the account ran out, magpie moved to another by itself, and no setting kept the first one's credits in use). *On* alone lets the vendor answer on credits, but Smart and Pace still put an account at `LowShare` (90%) or `SpentShare` (98%) behind every account with room (`tier`), Drain counts it used up at `SpentShareOf(Drain)`, and `NextLogin` moves Codex's own sign-in at the spent share, credits or not. *Before moving to another account* under *Use credits* (the Usage card, the menu bar panel, and the account's ⋯ in its provider's editor), `magpie quota credits <account> first`, `settings.CodexCreditsFirst` (`provider.CodexCreditsFirst`; one of first and last at most, on or off clears it) makes an account that spends credits (`spendsCredits`) a `routes.Seat.First`: Smart, Pace and Fastest weigh it at tier 0 (`seatTier`), Drain doesn't count it spent, affinity doesn't move a conversation off it as `spent`, and `readyForNew`/`standsInFor` treat it as having room (`Weighed.CreditsFirst`). `NextLogin` keeps Codex signed in to it while one of its windows says it holds credits (`spendsFirst`); with the credits gone it moves as before. The account magpie moved Codex off says where the setting is, on its *First again once it has room* tag. `TestCodexCreditsFirstKeepsItsPlace`, `TestCodexCreditsFirstDrainStays`, `TestCodexCreditsFirstMode`, `TestCodexStaysOnAnAccountSpendingCreditsFirst`, `codex-credits-first.test.cjs`.
- Different failures are classified separately: out of credit, out of quota, rate limit and other errors rest for different lengths. A fix must not merge them into one bucket (see #873).
- A refusal of where the request comes from (`regionWords`: Google's 400 "User location is not supported for the API use.", as Antigravity and Code Assist pass it on, and OpenAI's `unsupported_country_region_territory` / "Country, region, or territory not supported"; `regionRefused`) is the network's and the edge's the connection reached, not the account's. It rests no account and counts no failure (`restAfterMarked` returns `Rest.By` `off`, `Until` now), and the next try goes out on a new connection: `regionFresh` (in `upstreamTransport`, around each of its three transports, so under every request the gateway sends through `s.client`) reads the head of each failed reply, and when it matches it closes the transport's idle connections as the body closes and again before the next request (`pending`), so the HTTP/2 or keep-alive connection that drew the refusal isn't reused. The last candidate is asked again after `retryPause` (`passing`). Refused a second time for the same provider and model within a request (`regionAsked` in the serve loop), it is the place, not a connection: Google serves some models to no request from where the user is while the same account's other models answer (taoxiaoxin, #5182: Antigravity's gemini-3.8-flash refused, its Claude models answered). Then none of the provider's other accounts is asked that model (`notMates`), the last isn't asked again (`protected`), another provider or model left is, and the agent is told the vendor's words with `regionNote` (through `explained`, beside `Provider.Explain`): the vendor doesn't serve that model to requests from that region, it is no account's fault, other models may answer, and a proxy in a served region or another provider gets past it. So a refused model costs two asks a request, not one per account and retries. Before, every Antigravity account was asked on the one kept-alive connection, each refused the same and rested, and the agent got a 400, while a new connection answered 12 seconds on (#5182, ggdayup; `TestRegionRefusalDropsItsConnection`, `TestAntigravityRegionRefusalAsksTheNextOnANewConnection`, `TestAntigravityRegionRefusalAsksTheLastAgain`, `TestRegionRefusalRestsNoAccount`, `TestRegionRefusalOfTheModelEndsAfterOneMoreAsk`, `TestRegionNote`). Not covered: an HTTP/2 connection carrying another stream at that moment outlives the close until it is idle, and the close drops other vendors' idle connections too (one handshake each). A user whose region is refused gets the refusal after the one more ask, a moment later than before.
- One of Codex's own models on the backend path with no routing to do (`codexAccounts` false: one ChatGPT account, no usage cap, no key holds, nothing pinned, and the sign-in not known to be out of its allowance, `provider.CodexUsedUpKnown`, which routes the turn as `codex/<model>` as a restarted Codex's goes) is relayed as Codex sent it, on Codex's own sign-in, its bytes and headers unchanged and its reply streamed as it comes (`codexUpstreamOn` with no account): routing was built for more than one account, and that path also keeps the turn's native compaction with the backend. Before, nothing was asked again on it, so an overload went straight to Codex, which doesn't retry the backend's `server_is_overloaded` itself (夜松云 on Discord). Now a transient overload (`codexBusy`: what `busy` calls busy, a 529, a 503 saying overloaded, the backend's in-stream `response.failed` with `server_is_overloaded` (#1418); never a 429, a usage limit, an edge's drop of the connection (`edgeSaidDrop`) or a reset (`codexReset`)) is asked again of the same account, the same body and headers, up to `lastRetries` times after `retryPause<<n`, as the accounts loop asks its last account, and only before any of the reply has reached Codex. A stream is held for it through a `holdWriter` with its reasoning let through (`thinkingShown`), kept alive past `keepHeldAfter` across the tries (one `keptAlive`), so Codex sees the stream's headers and comments, never a failed try's frames. Nothing rests for an overload. Once the retries are spent, a turn whose codex subscription has fallbacks (`codexFallsBack`; never a compaction) goes on to them (`codexFallBack`: the turn served as `codex/<model>` with the account left out of the candidates, `fallbacksOnly`, and the same keepalive carried in `handedOnKey`); without fallbacks Codex gets the last try's failure as the backend said it. The account out of its allowance goes on to the same fallbacks at once, after the credits retry and the automatic reset are looked at (`codexAccountOut`: a quota, credit or rate refusal, as a status or as a stream's failure before any reply, and a 403 `usage_not_included`; never a 401, which Codex renews its sign-in on). Before that, Codex was handed the 429 with its fallbacks unused (adorable_piglet on Discord, `TestCodexOneAccountSpentFallsBack`); with none set it still is (`TestCodexOneAccountSpentNoFallbackRelayed`). Each try is a `HandedOn` usage record and a try in the trace. The reply's `x-codex-*` windows are kept as the sign-in's usage and a `usage_limit_reached` refusal has it read again at once (`heardCodexLimits`, `codexMayBeUsedUp`), as on the accounts path. A success is relayed as before, with nothing held when no retry or fallback is left (`TestCodexOneAccountOverloadAskedAgain`, `…StreamOverloadAskedAgain`, `…OverloadBounded`, `…OverloadFallsBack`, `…OverloadKeptAlive`, `…RefusalNotAskedAgain` in [`codex_one_account_overload_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_one_account_overload_test.go)).
- A subscription account whose sign-in its vendor revoked (`provider.SignInRevoked`: 401 `token_revoked` and the like, #1793) fails as `failAuth`: the next member is asked at once, nothing rests, and the account is marked (`noteSignIn` in [`signin_revoked.go`](../../internal/gateway/signin_revoked.go)). Its later requests aren't sent while it has that credential: `forwardOnce` answers them with a 401 of magpie's own (`revokedReply`), so they too pass on. Compaction on an account of magpie's (`compactOn`) picks a marked one last. A key's provider answering the same words rests as before.
- A ChatGPT 401 that says the token sent has expired and asks for a refresh (`provider.SignInExpired`, #2973) while its `exp` is ahead renews the account's sign-in once (`provider.NoteExpiredSignIn` from `noteSignIn`), keyed on the token sent, so the next request goes with a fresh one; nothing is marked, and the request fails over as any 401 does ([Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md), A token called expired).
- A sub2api key out of its own limit answers 429 `api key 7天限额已用完` (or `日限额`, `5小时限额`) as `rate_limit_exceeded`. It is out of quota (`plannedWords`), not rate limited: it rests till the window it filled renews when its windows as last read say so, else `quotaRest`, and its windows are read again (`staleKeyAllowance`). A reading that finds that window full no more (the limit raised in the relay's panel, or the key's usage reset) lifts the rest (`renewed`, told through `provider.OnRenewed`), as a Codex reset spent does an account's; the key's other rests stay. While it rests out of its windows, they are read again each time it is planned (`restingOf`, once a minute at most), so this holds where nothing weighs it by them: an ordered group. The relay out of accounts for everyone (503 `No available accounts`, or a later version's 429 `All available accounts are currently rate-limited`) says nothing of the key's windows: it backs off as any failure or rate limit does, never to the window. A key's windows count only its own spend, so other users draining the relay's accounts show up as those errors, not in the windows.
- A 429 that says in words it is back within `soonest` (`PatienceLongest`, 240s) is a rate limit (`failRate`), whatever plan words it has besides, unless it speaks of money (`creditWords`): `backSoon` reads `resetsAfter` (now with seconds and Chinese minutes, `soonWords`: "try again in 30 seconds", "请 1 分钟后重试") and `tryAgainIn`. A LAN aggregator's "Usage has exceeded the frequency limit. We will switch you to another model. Your usage limit for the original model will reset in 1 minutes." read as a plan used up ("usage limit") and reached the agent at once; now it is `busy`, rests the minute it says (`rateRest` reads `resetsAt`), and a lone provider waits it out within its patience (zoecats on Discord; `TestShortResetIsARateLimit`, `TestShortResetRestsWhatItSays`, `TestSingleProviderWaitsOutAFrequencyLimit`). One that says hours is still out of quota and not waited (`TestSingleProviderPassesALongLimitThrough`). "Frequency limit" is a rate word, as 频率 is.
- A 5xx is a server error, not quota, unless the vendor's own quota code says so (`quotaCodes`: `usage_limit_reached`, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, Claude Code's `usage limit reached|<unix>`). A relay's 500 "当前模型 gpt-5.5 负载已经达到上限，请稍后重试" names its load, not the key's allowance: it was read as out of quota by `usedUpWords` (上限), which rested the key till a reset and kept a group's Patience from waiting it out (#1418). The quota words (`usedUpWords`) still apply to a 4xx.
- A 429 that names in words when the account is back (WorkBuddy's "将在 2026-10-11 20:16:02 UTC+8重置" and "your usage will reset at 2026-10-11 12:37:53 UTC+8, …", #1669; Volcengine Ark's "You have exceeded the monthly usage quota. It will reset at 2026-10-25 23:59:59 +0800 CST.", #3251, #3276) is out of quota in either language (`resetsWhen`), and rests until that time (`resetsWritten`, read in the offset written). Only the reset phrasing right beside a whole date and time with its UTC/GMT offset, or its offset as Go prints one (four digits, then the zone's name, which isn't read), followed by 重置 or punctuation, is read, so a refusal cut short isn't read as UTC and another vendor's date in passing is no reset; a time past, not a real date, or further off than `longestSaid` isn't read: `longestWritten` (7 days), or `longestMonthly` (35 days) when the refusal says its allowance is a month's (`monthlyWords`: Ark's, Zhipu's 1310 "Weekly/Monthly Limit Exhausted"). Such a reset is the one rest out of quota held past `longestQuota`, to `longestMonthly`; a window read full or the user's own rest is still held to 8 days (`TestMonthlyWrittenResetRestsTheMonth`). Ark's windows as its AccessKey reads them (`volcPlanQuotas`) still reach only its usage card, not routing (#3251). Zhipu's 1308 and 1310 (Z.ai and bigmodel.cn, GLM Coding Plan: "Your limit will reset at 2026-10-15 20:12:13", "您的限额将在 … 重置") write the time with no zone; it is Beijing's, and is read as UTC+8 in those words only (`zhipuResets`, `resetsZhipu`), never when a digit, colon or zone of its own follows (Player on Discord: read by nothing, the key rested `quotaRest` and the refusal said "not asked again before 17:12" while Z.ai said 20:12; `TestZhipuRestsUntilItsReset`). One that points to the account's other models (`otherModels`: "切换其他模型", "switch to the other models") rests that model alone (`restID`). Out of credit or quota on a model that costs credits, where the subscription serves other models free (`catalog.Model.Free`: WorkBuddy's x0.00, Qoder's is_free, Cline's free models; `Provider.PaidBesideFree`), rests that model alone too: the free ones draw on no credits (Creat.wu on Discord, `TestOutOfCreditLeavesFreeModels`). A Credits window leaves the free models out the same way, so an account at 100% isn't ranked spent for them: the WorkBuddy plugin's lists them as `notModels` (0.1.23), and the built-in's scopes itself by the provider's last fetched list (`wbSpareFree`; with no list read, every model counts) (plugins#138, hheei: an account at 560 / 560 was still served deepseek-v4.1-flash; `TestWorkBuddyCreditsSpareFreeModels`). A free model the vendor refuses all the same (WorkBuddy refuses even 0-rate models to an account with no package) rests the account on its own refusal. Cline's daily limit of one free model, 429 "Daily free limit reached on model deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash. Try again in 23h 59m" (the words Cline's own clients read it by, `isClineFreeModelLimitMessage`), rests that model alone (`clineFreeLimit`, `clineModelLimited`, keyed by `restID`) until the time it names (`clineBackIn`, `tryAgainIn`, held to `longestWritten`; `resets`), from a provider whose requests reach Cline's API (`ClinePinnable`: the ClinePass preset, one at cline.bot, the Cline plugin's). The key's other models, free or the plan's, are still served, and the model out of its day is answered with its refusal without asking Cline again (#1398, wuasakura: one free model's 429 sent the whole Cline account to the back; `TestClineFreeLimitRestsTheModel`, `TestClineFreeLimitLeavesOtherModels`). Another vendor's same words, and a Cline 429 that doesn't name the model's limit, rest the key as before. WorkBuddy's 429 14018 "Credits exhausted" is out of credit (`brokeWords`), not a rate limit, on the built-in's raw body and the plugin's `insufficient_quota` alike (`TestCreditsExhaustedIsOutOfCredit`). On the rate-limit branch, a reset time the refusal names (`resetsAt`, or `resetsHeader` as `keepRetry` noted it) is believed up to `longestQuota`, as out of quota believes it, and the Routing page says it rests until then (`resets`); a `Retry-After` or a rate-limit reset header is still held to `longestWait` (an hour) (`rateRest`). The same rest runs for a plugin's requests: the WorkBuddy plugin passes these 429s on as they come (`TestPluginWrittenResetRests`). A JSON error body that escapes what isn't ASCII as `\uXXXX` (Python's json.dumps does by default) has its strings decoded before any word list (`creditWords`, `rateWords`, `plannedWords`, `usedUpWords`, `resetsWhen`, …) is matched (`wordsOf`), so an escaped 余额不足 or 用量 reads as an unescaped one does.
- A 429 that names a limit per minute or second (`minuteWords`: per minute, per second, TPM, RPM, `PerMinute` in Gemini's quotaId, Azure's "token rate limit" and "call rate limit") is a rate limit even when it calls itself a quota, unless it also names a longer window or a plan's limit (`longWords`: hour, day, week, month, usage limit, 用量, 套餐) or money spent (`brokeWords`, `insufficient_quota`) (`minuteLimit`). Gemini's "You exceeded your current quota … PerMinute", Vertex's "Quota exceeded for …requests_per_minute…" and Azure's rate limit, whose link is aka.ms/oai/quotaincrease, each rested a key the quarter of an hour of a quota with balance left (monater.114514 on Discord); now they rest as `rateRest` does, a minute or the vendor's `Retry-After`. Words inside a link (`urlWords`) don't make a rate limit a plan used up. DashScope words its TPM/TPS limit as a quota too, `Allocated quota exceeded, please increase your quota limit.` or OpenAI's `insufficient_quota`; from a DashScope provider (`dashScope`) those are rate limits (`dashScopeRate`, in `failureOf`), while its "Free allocated quota exceeded." and a Coding Plan's hour/week/month stay out of quota, and OpenAI's `insufficient_quota` stays out of credit (`TestMinuteLimitIsNotQuota`, `TestMinuteLimitRestsAMinute`, `TestDashScopeAllocationQuotaIsRate`).
- Antigravity's 429 for a model's quota used up writes how long until it is back as Go prints a duration: "You have exhausted your capacity on this model. Your quota will reset after 3h19m30s." (`quotaResetAfter`, read by `resetsAfter` beside Factory's "resets in …", held to `longestWritten`). The account (or, as `pooled` says, that model on it) rests that long (`resets`), and the agent's Retry-After says it when no account is left. Unread, it rested `quotaRest`, so the account first in order was asked again, and refused, every 15 minutes while the next ticked account had its quota (Dani Ariyanto on Discord, 10-24; `TestAntigravityQuotaRestsUntilItsReset`). Its other 429, "Resource has been exhausted (e.g. check quota).", names no time and rests `quotaRest` as before.
- A ZCode plugin account's Start Plan trial entry (`<model>-Trial`) that the plugin refuses before sending anything rests that entry alone (`zcodeTrialRefused`, `restID`), not the account: a 400 "ZCode has no active limited quota for GLM-5.3-Trial" when no live plan holds it any more (the trial ended), and a 429 "ZCode's limited quota for glm-5.3 is exhausted or temporarily unavailable" when its bucket is spent (the plugin names the bucket, the entry less `-Trial`). Read as `failQuota` by their words, both rested `zcode@…`, and the account's GLM Coding Plan models were paused with the trial (#3661, orion-nebula-dev; after skygunner's e04a1b994, which covered the 400). Only the plugin's exact words naming the model asked for count: another model named, a model without `-Trial`, the words on another status, the account's own quota or key, and the same words from another vendor still rest the account (`TestZCodeTrialRefusalRestsTheTrialAlone`). An expired trial still kept in a group is asked first and refused locally, as the guessed quota rest is asked again a minute on; the Routing page strikes it as skipped, which the gateway doesn't do for a member no account lists (#3805).
- An account resting out of its window (a Claude account by the time Claude Code's refusal named, `resets`, or any by `window`) is back once magpie's reading of its windows (`provider.Allowances`) finds it full till sooner: the window used below `SpentShareOf`, or its reset passed, with no other window over the same models still full. The reading tells `provider.OnRenewed` as the account's `UsageAgent` and user, and `renewed` lifts that account's rests out of quota or by `window`; its rests for a rate limit, its credit, a verification or a failure stay, as a key's do, and so does every rest of other accounts still out of their windows. Planning reads every account candidate's allowance, ordered groups included, so this needs no step of its own in `restingOf`.
- Only an account is held at a usage cap (`capHeld`) or rested out of one model's pool (`pooled`); a key's windows order it and rest it, nothing more. A key is held only at its own spend cap (below).
- A key at its spend cap ([providers](providers-accounts.md), `provider.SpendCap`) is barred in `perKeyEvery` (`spendHeld`) with a `capHold` whose `spend` is set: it is passed over and the provider's other keys and its fallbacks take the request. Routing's row says what it spent of its cap (`Weighed.SpendCap`, `Spent`, `SpendPeriod`, `SpendSince`). With every candidate held, `cappedError` answers 429 naming each key, what it spent of its cap and when it is back (the next day or month, or not until a cap in all is raised), and the command that changes it, with `Retry-After` at the soonest; a cap in all gives none. A spend hold is not a usage cap: `capHeldOnly` leaves it out, so neither Stop at the caps (`CapStop`) nor Strict's `strictKeep` sends a request to a key past what it may spend. `TestKeySpendCapPassesTheKeyOver`.
- A rest of one model alone (keyed by `restID`: a pool, a model not served, a paid model beside free ones) names that model in the trace (`Rest.Model`). The Routing page shows it on that model's rows only: the account's other models' rows on the stage, and the account's row under Accounts and keys, don't read as resting. The account's row names the models resting on a line of their own under what it has left, and its Try now lifts their rests. A kept request from before `Rest.Model` has the model on the end of `Rest.Key`, which the page reads the same (`restModel` in `plugins/routing/client.js`, #1960, `routing-model-rest.test.cjs`).
- The Providers page shows a subscription account's rests in force on its row there too: `RestsUnder("<provider>@")` lists them, `providerJSON.accountRests` gives them by user in lower case (the account's own, and each model's, with `Rest.Model`, `Until`, `By` and the vendor's words, `Rest.Error`), and the row's line says which model, why and when it is back, the vendor's words beside it, with Try now lifting it by its key (`accountRestLines` in app.js). Before, only a key's rest showed there, and a WorkBuddy AI account whose model rested on a 429 "将在 … 重置" read as fine, its credits 82% left (maxleebeta on Discord; `TestRateLimitedModelListedUnderAccount`, `TestAccountRestsInProviderState`, `account-rest.test.cjs`).
- A group member let go for no first content within the group's `FirstToken` (`hw.slow` in [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go), 504 "no first token in Ns", `failSlow`) doesn't rest the first time: a long prompt is slow to start anywhere. Slow again on the same account's model with no answer from it in between, and within `slowForget` (30 minutes) of its last slow start, that model rests on that account (`restSlow` in [`routing.go`](../../internal/gateway/routing.go), keyed by `restID`, `Rest.Model` set): a minute from the second time (`slowRests`), doubling each time after, up to the provider's rest cap (`RestCapOr(longestRetry)`), and never for a provider whose cap is off. Its answer clears the count (`servedCandidate`). A request its client cancelled counts nothing, as the cancel is read before `hw.slow`. The walk has a total budget, `groupBudget` (540s, under the 600s a client waits): a member asked when little of it is left gets only what is left (`hw.budgeted`), and when it runs out no further member is asked, nothing rests, and the error names the budget (504 "no member of the group answered within its 540s budget", #5113, mycf_711: nine members at 60s took 660s and the client's hang-up was told as the last member's failure; `TestGroupWalkStopsAtItsBudget`). Before it, each new request asked the stalled member first again and waited out the whole `FirstToken` (小八 on Discord: a WorkBuddy account's kimi-k3-1 and glm-5.3 stalled 58 times on the vendor's per-model token limit, 15 times in 17 minutes in one session). The same model on another account and another model on the same account are asked as before (`TestSlowStartsRestTheModelOnItsAccount`, `TestCanceledSlowStartRestsNothing`; the Routing page's story, `routing-went-on.test.cjs`).
- A plugin's try that isn't the last candidate is sent with `x-magpie-fallback: 1` (`provider.FallbackHeader`, `withFallback`), so a plugin whose vendor queues the request (Qoder's 10605, CodeArts' `queue-status: queued`) answers a `busy` 503 with Retry-After and the queue's reason at once instead of waiting minutes in the queue, and the next is asked; the last candidate isn't told and waits as the vendor's client does ([provider-plugins](provider-plugins.md#whether-another-candidate-follows), `TestPluginToldAnotherFollows`).
- A 402 is out of credit, unless it speaks of a usage limit (`usedUpWords`) and nothing of money (`creditWords`, `insufficient_quota`): then it is out of quota, and rests only the model when the account's windows say its pool is the one used up (`pooled`). Factory answers Standard's pool used up with 402 "You've reached your weekly standard usage limit (resets in …). Switch to Droid Core …"; read as credit, it rested the account 30 minutes and its Droid Core models with it (#1979). Both the built-in's windows and the plugin's (Standard's `notModels`, Core's `models`) are held to it. As a quota rest it lasts until the time the refusal names (`resetsAfter`: "resets in 0h 19min", "resets in 2d 3h", held to `longestWritten`), else the window's end, else `quotaRest` or the provider's own `quota-rest`, and is lifted once a reading finds the window renewed (`renewed`), which a credit rest never was (`TestFactoryStandardOutLeavesCore`, `TestPaymentRequiredReadByItsWords`, `TestResetsInWords`). An account whose windows weren't read yet (a start, or a reading that failed) can't tell the model's pool from the account's, so it rests whole, and `narrowWhenRead` waits off the request for a reading (`AwaitAllowance`, up to `allowanceWait`) and moves the rest to the model alone (`restID`, the same end) when the reading says its pool is the one used up; no reading in time, or one that says the whole account, leaves the account's rest, as does a rest lifted or made again meanwhile (#2036; `TestFactoryStandardOutLeavesCore`'s unread case, `TestResetsInWords`). The request itself isn't held for the reading: it has failed over already, and the rest's scope only matters to later requests.
- Codex offers its image tool (the `image_gen` namespace, #870) to every magpie provider. When a provider other than a ChatGPT sign-in or openai.com answers a bare 400, `attempt` asks it once more without the tool (`image_tool.go`). This happens both passed through and translated. If that works, the provider is marked unfit for the tool (`tool:image_gen`) and isn't offered it again. If it doesn't, the tool is restored and the 400 stands (#949).
- A turn the agent stopped with its tool call still out is replayed by the client with the call and no result, and Google's APIs turn a history whose call turn goes unanswered away with 400, every later turn of the conversation with it. The Gemini build (`buildCodeAssistSent`, which serves a Google sign-in, Vertex AI and a custom provider's Gemini API alike) answers such a call with the synthetic result the chat build pairs an interrupted turn's call with (`pairToolMessages`, #1275), right after the calls (`pairFunctionTurns`, #1560): beside the answers the client sent there, or in a user turn of its own before what the user said, since a turn mixing a `functionResponse` with text is turned away too (#1533); a Claude model on Antigravity gets it in the one tool_result turn. A call with no id counts as answered by a response of its name. A second answer to one call is left out, with the images it carried, before the turns are built (`dropSecondAnswers`, #2262): Google refuses a call turn whose `functionResponse` count differs from its `functionCall` count, and the Chat and Messages builds already keep the first answer. The Responses build (`buildResponses`) leaves it out the same way. A call made again under the same id takes an answer of its own. A Gemini client that keeps no ids answers two calls of one function by name in their order, and each answer goes to its own call (`parseGemini`), not both to the latest. `TestGeminiDropsASecondAnswer`, `TestSecondAnswerDroppedWithItsImages`, `TestGeminiClientUnnamedCallsAnsweredInOrder`.
- A request built for a Messages upstream (`buildAnthropic`: a Chat, Responses or Gemini client translated) has its tool exchanges mended after its messages are built (`pairToolBlocks`, #1558, ggbdpq), since Anthropic answers 400 for a `tool_use` the next message doesn't answer and for a `tool_result` naming no call of the message before it. A call left unanswered gets the synthetic interrupted result, `is_error` set, first in the user message after it or in one of its own; a result that answers no call goes as a text block with what it said (its images as they came); a second answer to one call is dropped. A well-formed exchange goes as it was built. An Anthropic client relayed to a Messages upstream isn't mended. [`anthropic_tool_pairing_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/anthropic_tool_pairing_test.go).
- A Responses request bound for anything but a ChatGPT sign-in has its tool exchanges mended in `attempt`, before it is relayed or translated, plugins included ([`responses.go`](../../internal/gateway/responses.go)). An item inside a run of calls and results (after one of the run's results and before the last, or before any result when the model didn't write it) moves to the end of that run (`joinToolRuns`, #1752, Edward4226): Codex writes its `<image_resize_notice>` right after each result whose image it resized, so a batch of two calls had it between their results, and a strict upstream answered 400 (OpenCode Go/DeepSeek "No tool output found for tool call …", Volcengine's "must be followed by tool messages", #1822); a Chat translation answered the second call with a synthetic result. An item carrying a `call_id` never moves, the model's own reasoning between its calls stays, and its text between them (an assistant message, with the reasoning right before it) goes ahead of the run's first call, so the step's calls stand together: OpenCode Go opens a new assistant message at such text and answered the call before it "No tool output found for tool call …" (#2100). A call opens a run only when a result after it answers it. A result with no call id becomes a user message after its run (`orphanedToolOutputs`), and a call nothing answers gets a synthetic result at the end of its run (`pairToolItems`, #1341). `TestRelayedCodexImageBatchKeepsItsResultsTogether`, `TestJoinToolRunsOnlyMovesWhatBreaksARun`, `TestRelayedTextBetweenABatchsCallsIsAnswered`.
- A Chat client's own body relayed to a Chat upstream (`passthrough`) has its tool exchanges mended when they don't pair (`toolPairsOut` in [`chattidy.go`](../../internal/gateway/chattidy.go)), by the rule a translated one is built with (`pairToolMessages` in [`chat.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat.go)): Kimi's Chat API, like OpenAI's, turns the whole request away for an assistant's call the tool messages right after it don't answer ("an assistant message with 'tool_calls' must be followed by tool messages responding to each 'tool_call_id'…"), a user message between a call and its answer included, and for a tool message with no call before it ("tool_call_id  is not found"), both checked against api.kimi.com/coding on 2026-10-26. Qoder's chat forked at an earlier turn, its model switched to Kimi Code, was refused on every prompt (Paidaixinbao on X).
