A turn's calls split over consecutive assistant messages join into one first (`joinSplitChatCalls`); a call left unanswered gets the synthetic interrupted result; an answer to no call becomes the user's text, a parts content by its text parts. A body whose exchange pairs goes byte for byte, answers out of the calls' order included, which Kimi takes (`TestChatRelayPairsToolsForKimi`, `TestChatRelayPairedToolsUntouched`).
- A Chat request has every tool call that came with no id (`""`, null or none) given one in `attempt`, before it is relayed or translated, plugins included (`chatCallIDs` in [`chat_call_ids.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat_call_ids.go), #2611, RunMintOn). An upstream that sometimes streams a call without an id (OpenCode Zen's free DeepSeek) has the agent keep it as `""`, which that upstream takes back, while WorkBuddy AI turns a streamed request carrying one away (400, code 11133) and Anthropic takes no empty `tool_use` id. The id is made from the call's place in the request, its name and its arguments, never at random, so the next turn's history goes byte for byte and the upstream's prompt cache holds; the tool messages after the call that answer with no `tool_call_id` take the made ids in the calls' order. A body whose calls all have ids goes as it came. On the reply side, a relayed Chat stream's call whose first fragment has no id is given one, and a later fragment's empty id is left out (`chatTidy`); a whole reply's call is given one too (`chatWhole`). `TestEmptyCallIDRelayedWithOne`, `TestEmptyCallIDReachesPluginWithOne`, `TestEmptyCallIDTranslatedWithOne`, `TestStreamedCallGivenAnID` in [`chat_call_ids_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat_call_ids_test.go).
- A relayed Chat stream's tool call fragment with no `index` (none, or null) is given its call's (`streamCallIndexes` in [`chattidy.go`](../../internal/gateway/chattidy.go), #3386). Gemini's OpenAI-compatible API sends its calls with none, where the OpenAI contract has one on every fragment: the AI SDK's OpenAI provider (Cursor's Private Inference) turned the chunk away ("expected number, received undefined" at `tool_calls[0].index`), and OpenAI's node SDK's stream helper kept no call at all. A fragment with an id other than the open call's, or after the first in its delta, is the next call; any other goes on with the open one. So two calls in chunks of their own are 0 and 1, never both 0, which that helper merges into one call. A fragment with an index keeps it. `TestChatTidyGivesCallsAnIndex` in [`chattidy_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/chattidy_test.go), `TestGeminiCompatRelayedCallsHaveAnIndex` in [`gemini_compat_cut_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/gemini_compat_cut_test.go).
- A translated Chat stream's tool calls are read by `index`, not as one after another (`chatDecoder.toolCall` in [`chat.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat.go)). An upstream may send several calls' fragments by turns (index 0, 1, 0, 1…), the first of each with its id and name; read as a new call at each change of index, two calls reached Codex as six `function_call` items, the first two with `{}` and four with no name (mysterious_dove on Discord). The open call streams as it comes; a call that starts while the open one's arguments aren't yet whole JSON is held by its index and given whole, in index order, at `finish_reason` or `[DONE]`. Every translation from Chat (Responses, Messages, Gemini, a whole reply) reads the stream through it. `TestInterleavedToolCallsToResponses`, `TestInterleavedToolCallsToAnthropic`, `TestInterleavedToolCallsWhole`, `TestToolCallsNamedFirstThenArgued` in [`translate_stream_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/translate_stream_test.go).
- A relayed Chat reply whose choice made calls and ended `stop` ends `tool_calls`, streamed (`chatTidy`) and whole (`chatWhole`), as the translations' collector already ends it (`collector.finish`): told it stopped, a client that reads `finish_reason` ends its turn with the calls not run (喜欢一个人 on Discord, a relay in front of deepseek-v4.1-flash). Any other reason stays as it came. `TestChatCallsEndToolCalls` in [`chattidy_finish_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/chattidy_finish_test.go).
- A Chat reply's call whose function names nothing in a JSON string — no name, null, another kind — is dropped, its later fragments with it, and a finish of `tool_calls` with no call left after the drop ends `stop` (a reply that came with no calls keeps its finish), and a whole reply given as a stream numbers the calls it keeps with no gap: streamed (`chatTidy`), whole (`chatWhole`) and a whole reply given to the agent as the stream it asked for (`chatWholeEvents`), since a client that checks the shape of every frame refuses the whole reply for one — the turn not retryable, the call already in the history, the session dead, while the gateway's own log says the reply went (AricRedemption, #1475). A nameless call runs nowhere, so it is no call, as a written block naming no offered tool is; a name the client reads as a string, empty included, goes as it came. `TestNamelessCallStreamDropped`, `TestNameAbsentStreamDropped`, `TestNamelessCallBesideAGoodOne`, `TestNamelessCallWholeDropped`, `TestNamelessCallWholeAsStreamDropped`, `TestNamelessCallWithoutAnIDStaysDropped`, `TestNamelessCallBesideAGoodOneWholeAsStream`, `TestToolCallsFinishWithNoCallsStaysWhole` in [`chat_call_name_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat_call_name_test.go).
- Codex's model list from the backend path (`codexModels`) is the ChatGPT backend's for its sign-in and then magpie's (`provider.CodexListedBeside`): every model but a ChatGPT account's own the sign-in's list has already, so a model only another account on in magpie has (a Team or Pro one beside a Free sign-in) is listed by magpie's id, `codex/<slug>`, and routed to that account (bigfisheatsmall on X: Codex kept signed in to a Free account, the Team account's models in dsh and not in Codex; `TestCodexListHasAnotherAccountsModels`, `TestCodexListOneAccountNoneTwice`). Its ETag and every reply's `X-Models-Etag` tagged with `provider.CodexListTag` so a change has Codex ask again. A Codex there only for account failover, not connected (`CodexOwnOnly`, `agent.CodexOwnOnly`; #1385), gets the backend's alone, tagged with `provider.CodexOwnListTag`; neither tag contains the other, so connecting or disconnecting has Codex fetch the list again. The user's picks on the codex provider (`provider.CodexNativePicked`) narrow the backend's listed models. They keep its hidden ones (`visibility: "hide"`), which no picker shows. One of those is `codex-auto-review`: when the list lacks it and no reviewer is set, Codex's auto-review runs on the conversation's model at low effort (#1460, `TestCodexModelListPicksKeepHidden`). The account's own models stay in it when they are kept out of other agents' lists for routing groups, by the codex provider "Only through routing groups" (`Provider.Unlisted`) or a group that unlists its members (`Group.Unlists`): Codex's own catalog (`catalogOf("codex")`, behind `ListedFor`/`CatalogFor`) keeps `CodexOwn` entries, so the Agents page shows its Codex section, its picker offers them as magpie's, and the desk file and `spawnChoices` list them. #1602 dropped them here, and the picker then still offered them through the own-models switch while the Codex app had none (士奇 on Discord, 10-28). They are hidden from Codex through Codex's list (`provider.CodexNativeHidden`, `TestCodexModelListKeepsOwnForGroups`, `TestCodexOwnModelsListedOnlyThroughGroups`). Every other agent, and every client of `/v1/models` (one that names no agent is `other` or its User-Agent's name, never `""`), is given `Catalog()`: a group listing alone keeps a signed-in subscription's models out of its list too. 88e6fb9cf had kept them for every agent (`SubscriptionKept`), so for a group of subscription models the option changed no list fetched over HTTP (shaoyang. on Discord, 11-03; `TestGroupAloneKeepsSubscriptionModelsOutOfEveryAgentsList`, `TestGroupAloneKeepsSubscriptionModelsOffAgentsModelList`). The group stands in for them only for a caller given it: a gateway key that may use none of the groups listing a model alone is listed the model, when it may use it (`keyListed` over `provider.GroupStandIns`, on `/v1/models` and every list `catalogFor` feeds, `/v1/models/{id}`, the key's page, Codex's catalog and spawn choices), beside its provider's other models; a key given the group is given the group in its place. The Gateway page's key picker (`/api/caller-keys/models`) offers them too (luckymanjc on Discord, 11-05: a Remote magpie's key held to `codex/*` was listed GPT-6-Luna alone; `TestKeyNotGivenTheGroupIsListedItsMembers`, `TestCallerKeyModelsOfferGroupKept`). A backend answer that says the sign-in itself is revoked (`provider.SignInRevoked`, a 401 `token_revoked`; jjt on Discord, #4308) isn't passed through: the list is Codex's cached one (`codexcat.CacheEntries`) and magpie's, so the picker keeps magpie's models; any other refusal, a 401 `token_expired` among them, still is. The desk file does the same while the live list says revoked (`provider.CodexListRevoked`, `codexDesk.revoked`) (`TestCodexModelListRevokedSignIn`, `TestCodexDesktopCatalogRevokedSignIn`).
- The Codex app and its VS Code extension read only the first 100 of that list by priority, hidden entries counted: each asks its app-server for `model/list` with `limit: 100` and never for the next page (ChatGPT.app 26.1013, openai.chatgpt 26.51021; the CLI's `/model` lists every one). So `codexcat.FitOwn` ranks the list last of all: hidden entries after every listed one, and, with more than 100 listed (`codexcat.AppList`), Codex's own listed models (the backend's entries in `codexMerged`; a ChatGPT account's `codex/…` ones, `codexcat.ChatGPTOwn`, on the other two routes) and then the models Codex used in the last 30 days (`usage.CodexUsed`, most recent first, at most 100) brought into the first 100 in the order they stood, the rest kept in theirs. The user's own order (`codexcat.Order`, #855) goes first, so an order that names only magpie's models (set while Codex's list had no Codex section, or before OpenAI added a model) no longer puts every GPT model past the 100th (maiya on Discord); the user takes one out of the app by hiding it in Codex's list. The same holds for `/v1/codex/models` (with the models that key's Codex used alone, `usage.CodexUsedBy`, before the 1 MiB cut) and the `model_catalog_json` magpie writes (`codexcat.CatalogUsed`). `codexListTag` carries a tag of the used set (`codexcat.UsedTag`), so the ETag and `X-Models-Etag` change when Codex uses a model it hadn't; `usage.CodexUsed` is read again a minute on, or at once when `Append` writes a Codex call to a model it doesn't hold (`codexUsedNew`). `TestCodexModelListKeepsUsedModelsInTheAppsHundred`, `TestCodexCatalogKeepsTheKeysUsedModelsFirst`, `TestFitKeepsUsedModelsWhereTheCodexAppReads`, `TestCodexUsed`, `TestCodexModelListKeepsOwnModelsInTheAppsHundred`, `TestFitOwnKeepsCodexsOwnInTheAppsHundred` and `TestCatalogUsedKeepsChatGPTOwn` cover it (@ZhenLuoTTAWDTT on X).
- Reasoning state goes through magpie's translation as the vendor wrote it when the agent and the upstream speak the same API (#1445, XYenon): a non-streaming Messages request to a stream-only account, web search on the agent's own API, a plugin, failover. The IR carries it sealed (`Part.Sealed`, `SealedBy`; events `KThinkStart`, `KSealed`, `KTextSig` in [`ir.go`](../../internal/gateway/ir.go)), so each thinking block or reasoning item stays apart, whole or streamed, and a seal is written only for its own API (`sealedBy`). Anthropic's `redacted_thinking` goes back raw (to DeepSeek as a thinking block, below), and a thinking block signed with no text keeps its signature (`aBlock` still writes `"thinking": ""`, #1447), on Claude's subscription too. A Responses reasoning item keeps its id, summary and `encrypted_content`, and goes back to a Responses upstream as it came when it has no reasoning text (`sealedReasoning`; text, another vendor's, is still left out for OpenAI, #1411). Gemini's `thoughtSignature` stays on the call it signed and on text; a later call of a signed step goes without one, and only an unsigned step or Claude on Antigravity gets `skip_thought_signature_validator`. Another API's agent gets the call's signature in its id, as on chat (#687), with its length in the mark (`__ts<n>__`), so an id a client shortened carries no signature and the call goes as unsigned (#1536); it gets no one else's seal. The signature itself is kept in magpie's folder, `gemini-signatures/<key>`, named by its hash (`keepSignature` in [`gemini_sigstore.go`](../../internal/gateway/gemini_sigstore.go)), and the id carries only the key (`__tsk16__<key>`), so an id stays under 64 characters however long the step's signature (#2411: LobeHub's PostgreSQL index turned away a 3KB id). A key magpie no longer has, or whose file doesn't hash to it, reads as no signature; one not read for 90 days is removed. Where the folder can't be written, the id carries the signature as before. A signature goes back only to the provider that gave it (mimi001234: a group of Vertex AI and AI Studio moved on Vertex AI's 429, and AI Studio answered its signatures "Corrupted thought signature." on every try): the provider is noted beside it (`gemini-signatures/<key>.by`, `noteSigner`) where a reply is decoded (`decoderFor`, `signersNoted`) or relayed (`sigTidy`, a Gemini client's reply in `native` mode), and every request is fitted to its provider (`ownSignatures` in `forwardTranslated`, `chatCallSignatures` and `geminiBodySignatures` in `passthrough`): another provider's signature goes as `skip_thought_signature_validator` on a call and not at all on text. One of no known provider (an id from before, or a note that couldn't be written) goes as before, and the 400 retry above covers it. The provider is the provider id, not the account. `TestSignatureGoesOnlyToItsSigner`, `TestSignatureGoesOnlyToItsSignerTranslated`, `TestCorruptedSignatureTriedWithout`, `TestGeminiBodyKeepsOnlyItsOwn`. Across APIs, only Claude's reasoning is carried to a Responses client, below.
- A Responses upstream reads only the reasoning it sealed itself (`encrypted_content`). When one answers that it can't read the sealed reasoning or compaction it was sent (`foreignReasoning` in [`affinity.go`](../../internal/gateway/affinity.go): OpenAI's and Codex's "could not be verified", xAI's "Could not decrypt", Azure's "invalid encrypted summary", a relay's compaction-blob refusal), `serve` asks once more without it and remembers its hashes for that conversation (`stickKey`) and key or account (`candidate.vendor`, `refusedSeals`), so the conversation's later turns there go without it the first time; the try is `fail: foreign`. A Responses client's own items go first (`withoutReasoning`, `withoutRefused`), then OpenAI's compaction (`withoutCompaction`). A Chat, Messages or Gemini client holds a Responses upstream's sealed reasoning in its calls' ids instead (`<id>__rk<n>__<key>`, `carryReasoning` and `unreasonCalls` in [`reasoning_carry.go`](../../internal/gateway/reasoning_carry.go), 9da9bfabd), and every later turn of its history sends it again, so a conversation that moved to another Responses upstream or account (Grok to Codex after a 402, and back) was refused on that turn and every one after it (#4798, zane668, ZanderH-code). Such a refusal is asked again once too: `refuseCarried` remembers what the request's calls carried as refused by that key or account, and `translate` leaves those items out of this and every later request to it (`withoutRefusedCarry`, scoped to the try's provider through `withSealScope`). The calls, their arguments and results and the text go as the client wrote them, under the calls' own ids; the one that sealed an item, and every other key or account, is still given it. As for a Responses client, all the reasoning the request carried goes, the upstream's own earlier items among them; what it seals next goes along. `TestForeignSealAcrossGroupMembers`, `TestForeignCarriedReasoningAskedAgainWithout`.
- Claude signs a thinking block for the conversation and the account it was made in. When an Anthropic upstream answers 400 that a block's signature is invalid, "bound to a different conversation" or "not created in this conversation" (`boundRefusal`: a conversation moved between accounts or providers, or a relay that serves Claude from several accounts of its own; ferock on Discord, through Command Code's plugin), `forward` asks once more without the block it names (`messages.<m>.content.<b>`) and every thinking block after it, whose signatures cover it, and remembers those signatures for that provider and account (`refuseBoundThinking`, `withoutRefusedThinking` in [`thinking_bound.go`](../../internal/gateway/thinking_bound.go)), so the conversation's later turns go without them the first time. Every route to an Anthropic upstream (relayed, translated, a plugin's fetch, count_tokens) passes there. Blocks before it, other providers and accounts, and every other refusal (DeepSeek's wanting its thinking back among them) are left as they are. magpie doesn't send the `thinking-binding-controls` beta's `drop_block` for an agent. `TestBoundThinkingLeftOut`, `TestPluginBoundThinkingLeftOut`, `TestOtherRefusalKeepsThinking`.
- A thinking block DeepSeek's Anthropic endpoint made is signed with its reply's id, a UUID, which Claude refuses ("Invalid `signature` in `thinking` block"). A routing group whose conversation ran on DeepSeek and goes on to Claude (DeepSeek unticked; GgbondHi6930 on X) holds such blocks, so before any refusal, `forwardOnce` leaves them out of every request to an upstream serving Claude (api.anthropic.com, or a `claude-` model name; `withoutForeignThinking` in [`thinking_bound.go`](../../internal/gateway/thinking_bound.go)), and `refuseBoundThinking` reads a refusal's indices against that same body. Claude's own signatures (base64), unsigned blocks, redacted_thinking and other vendors' signatures go as they were; DeepSeek's endpoint and every other upstream get the body byte for byte. `TestDeepSeekThinkingThenClaude`, `TestDeepSeekThinkingThenClaudeResponses`, `TestWithoutForeignThinking`.
- Claude's reasoning goes to a Responses client (Codex) in an envelope and comes back as Claude wrote it (mysterious_dove_96933 on Discord). Anthropic wants a tool loop's next request to have the turn's thinking blocks, signed, and its `redacted_thinking` whole, or answers 400; Codex with `store: false` keeps only a reply's items, and of a reasoning item only `encrypted_content` is opaque. So the Anthropic decoders name a signature as Claude's (`KSig` with `sealAnthropic`, `Part.SignedBy`), and the Responses encoder and `renderResponses` give each thinking block a reasoning item whose `encrypted_content` is `magpie-claude1:` and the block's JSON in base64 (`claudeThinking`, [`internal/claudeseal`](../../internal/claudeseal/claudeseal.go)): its summary with it, an empty summary for a block signed with no text, and a `redacted_thinking` block as an item of its own. `parseResponses` reads such an item back into the block (`claudePart`), in order, so `anthropicBody` sends it as it was; an envelope that can't be read is its summary, as before. A Gemini thought's signature isn't named, and isn't sealed. The envelope goes to no Responses API: `passthrough` and `forwardOnce` take it out (`withoutClaudeSeals`: the item keeps its summary, one with none goes), `codexInputAs` leaves such an item out for Codex's own models as it does one with nothing sealed, and `codexUpstreamOn` takes it out before the ChatGPT backend; OpenAI's own `encrypted_content` is untouched. `redact.MaskJSON` keeps it whole (a reasoning item's seal), so the placeholders Claude was sent stay in what its signature covers. Claude's subscription names its signatures too, so Codex gets the same items, though its tool loop goes on inside Claude Code's own run, which keeps the thinking itself. `TestCodexToolLoopGivesClaudeItsThinkingBack`, `TestClaudeBlocksNeverGoToAResponsesAPI`, `TestGeminiSignatureIsNotSealedAsClaudes`, `TestMaskJSONKeepsClaudeBlocks`.
- A translated Responses reply gives Codex the reasoning items a Responses upstream sent, as it sent them — one that carries its text in it (content parts, another vendor's shape) made over into one magpie could have, its text a summary and nothing sealed, in a reply translated or relayed as it came, for the client to store and replay where OpenAI's rules hold even with magpie disconnected (#2855, the reporter's rollout: 25 reasoning items a DeepSeek phase had left, each with its text in it and a seal of the vendor's own, stuck every later turn on OpenAI with a 400 `array too long` nothing of magpie's could strip then); any other upstream's reasoning becomes items magpie made: an `rs_` id and a summary, nothing sealed. Codex hands them back on every turn. A request relayed as is to OpenAI's API or Azure OpenAI goes without them (`withoutBareReasoning`) unless `store` is true, because both look the id up among stored items and answer 400 "Item with id 'rs_…' not found" (#1008). Any other Responses upstream gets them, because a DeepSeek model wants its reasoning back (#388). When one answers 400 naming the input (`refusesInput`: `param` is `input`, or the error names an item by id), as a relay in front of OpenAI does with a message of its own, `passthrough` asks once more without them and, once that works, leaves them out for that provider and model from then on (`bareReasoningRefused`, #1044). Codex's compaction offers no tools, so it is relayed even when its turns were translated. The summary request magpie makes offers none either: `codexInputAs` sends `tools: []` and leaves out the `additional_tools` input item, where Codex's Responses Lite (the entry of a ChatGPT account's gpt-6 model, copied from Codex's own) puts every tool; left in, the model may call one in place of the summary (the likely cause of WuSix's codex/gpt-6-astra "the model wrote no summary" on Discord, not confirmed on a real reply), and a reply with no summary says what it held instead (`answeredWith`). A reply that called a tool instead (`calledTool`) is asked once more with the conversation as text (`plainCompact`), and compacted locally when that writes no summary either: a model deep in an exchange of calls goes on with it whatever it is offered (Lucy on Discord: DeepSeek through group/auto-deepseek-v4-1-flash answered reasoning and an `exec_command` call 3 times in a row; `TestCodexCompactToolCallAsksAgainAsText`, `TestCodexCompactToolCallEveryTimeCompactsLocally`, over Chat, Responses and Messages, a group and a single model). When a provider answers its summary request 404, 405 or 501 (`lacksEndpoint`), or 400 for an item it didn't find (`itemNotFound`, a relay in front of either), `codexCompact` asks once more with the conversation as text (`plainCompact`), then compacts locally (#866, #1008). So does a summary request refused for its size (`summaryTooLarge`): a 413 byte cap, or an overflow of the model's window. Codex's remote compaction trims only by its own token estimate against the catalog's window and asks nothing smaller after either, so the turn's `context_length_exceeded` led to a compaction refused the same way, every time, and the session could neither compact nor go on (celestial_eagle_99230 on Discord: DeepSeek's own API, 1M window, answered 413 "Failed to buffer the request body: length limit exceeded"). The text ask leaves images out, keeps the ends of a long output and the latest 400K characters. `TestCodexCompactTooLargeAsksSmaller`, `TestCodexCompactTooLargeCompactsLocally`, `TestCodexCompactOtherRefusalStays`. Any other failure goes back with its status, said as the compaction's with who answered it (`compactFailed`: "compaction: <provider> (<model>, <base URL>) answered the summary request 502 Bad Gateway: …"), the vendor's code, param and Retry-After kept. Not so the compaction Codex runs before a turn on another model, on the model the thread was on: a switch to a smaller window (`compaction.reason` `model_downshift` in the turn metadata) or to another compaction hash (`comp_hash_changed`), the only places Codex names either (`maybe_run_previous_model_inline_compact` in codex-rs `core/src/session/turn.rs`). When it fails, Codex asks the turn's own model instead only under its provider named "OpenAI" (`compact_model_fallback.rs`). Under magpie's provider, the one Codex has while its sign-in is used up (codex.out), the refusal was the turn's, and every turn of that thread asked the used-up account again, restarts or not, while a new thread worked (hiwise_aigc on X). Off `CodexPath`, `codexCompact` compacts such a failure locally (`previousModelCompaction`), whatever the failure, the compaction-failure hold included, and the thread goes on on the model it was switched to. On `CodexPath` the provider is Codex's own "OpenAI", which asks the turn's model itself, so the failure goes back as before. `TestCodexLeavingModelCompactsLocally`; Codex 0.162.1 in a sandbox sent the old model's `compaction_trigger` to `/v1` and failed the turn on a 429, and went on to the new model once the trigger was answered. A compaction relayed to OpenAI (`/responses/compact` on Codex's own model, as a `/responses` turn) that OpenAI answers with an error status and an empty body, which Codex shows as "unexpected status 502 Bad Gateway: Unknown error", or with a web page, is said in words naming the model, the status, and the account it went to (`codexSaidNothing`, outrageous_piglet on Discord); an error OpenAI says in JSON goes as it came. `TestCodexOwnCompactFailureIsSaid`, `TestCodexCompactFailureSaysCompaction`, `TestCodexCompactNoEndpointCompactsLocally`. So does the ChatGPT backend's 502 "response protection is unavailable" (`protectionRefused`): a long history refused so was answered as text. Codex also compacts remotely, a `compaction_trigger` on `/responses` that wants exactly one compaction item back, on any provider table named "OpenAI", as CC Switch names its relay tables. magpie points those at `/v1` while a magpie model is on, so `handle` makes the summary there too (`codexInput`, `codexCompact`), and magpie's summaries go back to the model as text (#1301). A summary is wanted only whole (`askWhole`, `holdWriter.wantWhole`): its stream is held to its end, not let through at its first words, so a reply cut short (`errEndedShort`), failed halfway (a 429 "Individual quota reached" after some of it) or ended `incomplete` (`endedIncomplete`, `incompleteReply`) fails over to the next candidate like a failure before any of it, and rests the account as that failure would; one that reached its output limit rests nobody. With nobody left, `codexCompact` answers 502 and never hands Codex the words before the cut as the summary (#2274, huangzuomin: Antigravity cutting each summary short) — but one whose stream merely broke off, with nothing failed or incomplete in it, is followed by magpie's own summary (#3526, Edward4226: Codex told a 502 asked the same huge summary over three times and the guard then held the conversation), as when no model can be asked at all. So is a translated summary serve found cut and answered 502 "the reply ended before it was complete" (`summaryEndedShort`).. Codex with context_management's experimental mode compacts in the background at every step while the conversation is over its threshold and drops a failed compaction without a word, so a conversation whose summaries keep failing is asked again every few seconds, each paying for the whole history (1169 compactions in 6 hours there). After `compactFailsMost` (3) failed summaries in a row of one conversation (`prompt_cache_key`) on one model, each within `compactHeldFor` (5 minutes) of the last, the next is answered at once with a 400 naming the last failure, which Codex doesn't ask again, and no model is asked until 5 minutes have passed (`compactFails` in [`codex_compact_guard.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_compact_guard.go)); a summary that comes clears the count, and a sign-in's 401 or 403 isn't counted. `TestCodexCompactCutSummaryFailsOver`, `TestCodexCompactCutSummaryIsNoSummary`, `TestCodexCompactKeepsFailingIsHeld`. A summary is waited for `compactWithin` (4 minutes) at most: Codex hears nothing while it is made and gives up on a stream silent for 300s, so a later summary, and a local one made after Codex left, reached nobody, and Codex sent the same history again, 300s each time (#5275, hayden4608: a ~3.1MB summary request still going up a ~10KB/s uplink at 299s). Past it the ask ends (`sideTimedOut`, logged as magpie's own limit, not the agent leaving) and magpie's own summary goes to Codex while it still waits. A compaction Codex left before it was answered writes nothing and counts as a failure (499), never clearing the count. `TestCodexCompactLateSummaryCompactsLocallyInTime`, `TestCodexCompactLeftBeforeAnsweredStillCounts`.
- An `output_text` annotation without the offsets OpenAI's schema wants of its kind (a `url_citation` or `container_file_citation` with no `start_index`/`end_index`, as Volcengine Ark's Responses endpoint writes its url citations) cannot be replayed where that schema holds: the ChatGPT backend refuses the whole request, every turn of the thread, with "Missing required parameter: 'input[n].content[m].annotations[k].start_index'" (#4921; `withoutOffsetlessCitations` in [`codex_backend.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_backend.go), the same leaving-out as a reasoning item's text, #1411). A whole reply streamed as the vendor's own events drops such an annotation in every place a client reads one (`dropOffsetlessCitations` beside `responsesWholeEvents` in [`whole_stream.go`](../../internal/gateway/whole_stream.go), the #2855 rule: what magpie hands a client must be something it could send again); a history that carries one goes to the ChatGPT backend, OpenAI's API and a relay that has turned one away without it, and a refusal naming the parameter is asked again without it (`refusesCitationOffsets`). The text, a citation with its offsets, and a `file_citation` or `file_path`, which OpenAI places by a single `index`, go as they came (`TestCitationsWithoutOffsetsAskedAgainWithout`, `TestWholeAsStreamDropsCitationsWithoutOffsets`, `TestCitationsWithOneIndexKept`).
- A reasoning item with its text in it (`content` parts: DeepSeek's Responses API writes `reasoning_text`, relayed to Codex as it came) is another vendor's. OpenAI's API and the ChatGPT backend take a reasoning item's `content` only empty, and refuse the whole request over one with 400 "Invalid 'input[n].content': array too long. Expected an array with maximum length 0" (`array_above_max_length`). Codex hands the item back on every later turn, so it never goes to them: for one of Codex's own models, `codexInput(body, false)` leaves it out (and a reasoning item with nothing sealed in it) on `/backend-api/codex`'s `/responses` and on `/responses/compact`, before Codex's own sign-in, its API key or a magpie Codex account (`compactOn`) is asked. Codex compacts a thread before its first turn back on its own model, and a refused compaction shows as that turn's error (jue on Discord). `passthrough` leaves it out for OpenAI, Azure OpenAI and a Codex account (`withoutReasoningText`), and for any other upstream once it has refused one (`refusesReasoningText`, `reasoningTextRefused`, #1411). OpenAI's own sealed reasoning (`encrypted_content`, no text) stays, and a DeepSeek model (`replaysReasoning`) still gets the text. `TestForeignReasoningNeverReachesOpenAI`, `TestReasoningTextLeftOutWhereRefused`, `TestReasoningTextNotSentToCodexAccount`.
- A conversation that moves to another key, account or vendor carries what the one before sealed (`encrypted_content`): its reasoning, and the sealed copy an answer's other items may carry: a web search's (`web_search_call`), and with Codex's output token replay on, each message's and call's (Codex keeps those for a provider named "OpenAI" only, and signed in it reaches magpie as that provider). `forVendor` takes the copies out for a vendor not OpenAI's, reasoning and compactions aside; between ChatGPT accounts, or a ChatGPT account and an OpenAI API key, all of it goes as it came. One that can't read it says so (`foreignReasoning`: OpenAI's `invalid_encrypted_content`, "could not be verified" of reasoning and "Encrypted content could not be decrypted or parsed." of a web search; xAI's "Could not decrypt"), as a 400 or as the stream's error, and `serve` asks it again without them (`withoutSeals`): a reasoning item goes whole, any other item keeps all but its `encrypted_content` (`withoutItemSeal`), and a compaction keeps its seal. Refused again, or with nothing else sealed, the compaction OpenAI sealed goes (`withoutCompaction`), unless native compaction is on (below). What was taken out is remembered by its hash for the conversation and the key or account that refused it, for `stickKeep` (`refused`, `refusedSeals`), and its later requests there leave it out up front (`withoutRefused`); what that one sealed itself still goes along, and a request that stays where its seals were made goes as it came. A refusal doesn't say whose seal it couldn't read, so every one goes, the asked one's own from an earlier stay in the conversation too, and all are remembered. It is the same whatever moved the conversation: a key or account resting, used up or rate limited, the next member of a group, or routing's own pick once its affinity went cold, on the backend path and on `/v1/responses` alike (#3887, hheei: after such a move a `web_search_call` another ChatGPT account of the group had sealed was refused, only the reasoning was left out, and every turn failed). Codex's own sign-in, relayed as it came (`codexUpstreamOn`), is asked again the same way when the backend answers its `/responses` with a 400 (`withoutUnreadable`, after the item the refusal names, if any); it keeps no record of what was refused, and a refusal in the stream reaches Codex as it came. `TestForeignSealAcrossGroupMembers`, `TestForeignSealGrokToCodexAccount`, `TestForeignSealedItemsMoveToAnotherAccount`, `TestForeignSealedItemsAfterAColdSwitch`, `TestForeignSealedItemsRelayedToAnotherSignIn`, `TestSealsTakenOutLeaveTheRest`.
- Native compaction (#1099, off by default): a provider with `nativeCompaction` on (`provider.Provider.CompactsOn`; the editor's Compaction row, `magpie provider set <id> compaction=native`) is asked Codex's `compaction_trigger` as Codex sent it, and the compaction item it seals goes back to Codex unchanged, not as a `magpie1:` summary. Only a wire that carries it can be set so (`CarriesCompaction`): a Responses API, a ChatGPT account, GitHub Copilot (its models asked on Responses; its Claude models keep magpie's summary). The decision is made where `handle` and `codexBackend` read the input (`nativeCompactionFor`, `codexInputAs`): a group compacts natively only when every member does. Such a request, and a later one carrying a sealed compaction to a provider set so or to the provider recorded as its writer, is relayed, never translated, which would drop the item (`keepsCompaction` in `attempt`); a provider that can't be asked it on Responses answers a 400 saying so. The trigger is kept only when a key or account the request would go over (the provider's and its fallbacks', or a group's members', held and capped ones too, not one whose own list lacks the model) can be asked it on Responses (`plannedCarrier`, read from `perKeyBarred` without weighing, so a provider routed in turn isn't moved along by the decision). When the vendor lists the model only to keys that speak no Responses there, a key set to Chat say, `codexCompact` gives magpie's summary through the listed key, as with native compaction off; before, the trigger was kept and refused 400 `carrierError` with no upstream asked (#2725, ggbdpq; `TestNativeCompactionTriggerSummarisedWhenNoListedKeyCarriesIt`, `TestNativeCompactionTriggerThroughTheListedKeyThatCarriesIt`, `TestNativeCompactionDecisionLeavesTheTurnsAlone`). The key or account that answered is recorded, whoever it is: a fallback or another member that answered the trigger sealed it whatever its own setting, and only it can read the seal (#2271, JasonBuildAI: a fallback's seal went unrecorded, and the next turn was answered 200 without the compacted history; `TestNativeCompactionFallbackSealStaysWithItsWriter`). It is recorded by the hash of what it sealed (`wroteCheckpoint`, `compactions.json` beside `affinity.json`, 0600, at most 1024 for 30 days). A later request carrying it goes only to that writer (`checkpointReaders`); a writer whose own list has since dropped the model is tried still, as the last resort an account whose list lacks the model is — it sealed the model's compaction, and no other can read it (`planned.unlisted`, `TestNativeCompactionWriterWhoseListLacksTheModel`, #2642: it was refused as "switched off, removed, or no longer serves" though it was on, and switching it on changed nothing). With the writer gone from the candidates, magpie answers 400 `checkpointError` with the reason its settings give (`checkpointGone`: the provider removed, the account signed out, its sign-in lapsed, paused while others are on, switched off, kept from the model in Names, prices & levels, the key off or gone, the model disabled, or the model or group asked for not going to it), what lets the conversation go on, and whether native compaction is on for the writer's provider now, and never sends it to another key, drops it, or summarises it in its place (`TestNativeCompactionWriterGoneSaysWhy`). One of Codex's own models asked on the backend path is decided as a magpie model is when magpie's Codex accounts serve it (`codexAccounts`): the trigger is kept only with the codex provider's native compaction on, else `codexCompact` gives magpie's summary, which any account reads, and no writer is recorded (#2582, ump45nose; before, every such compaction was sealed natively whatever the setting, and the conversation was held to its account; `TestCodexOwnModelCompactionHonoursTheToggle`). Codex's own sign-in alone, relayed as it came, still compacts natively, unrecorded, as does `/responses/compact`; but a request carrying a compaction magpie recorded the writer of goes through routing even then (`codexSealedFor`), so with the writer switched off the one account left isn't sent it. A legacy `/responses/compact` carrying one is judged at its passthrough (`sealedCompact`): the writer on in magpie answers it signed as the writer — its own seal while the sign-in can be asked, a key's seal, a request signing with its own `chatgpt-account-id`, or an unreadable sign-in state relays as it came — and a writer gone is refused with `checkpointError`'s reason, the same words as on `/responses` (`TestNativeCompactionCompactJudgesItsWriter`, #2727). The writer matched is the account user the record holds, so two Team seats of one email read as one and such a compact relays as it came. A gateway key held to some accounts (#905) is asked of the account the compact goes to (`compactSigninHeld`): the writer when it answers it, so a writer the key may not use is refused 403 (`sealedCompactHeldError`) with nothing asked, and one it may use answers though the sign-in isn't the key's (`TestNativeCompactionCompactKeepsTheKeysAccounts`). With the writer held at its usage cap or for credits it may not spend, the answer is 429 `checkpointCappedError` (record `checkpointCappedRecord`, "native compaction's account at its usage cap"), naming that account, that the others serving the model weren't tried for the compaction, and that neither the routing mode nor how conversations stay changes that: wait for the window, raise or lift its cap, or start a new conversation (#2517, lm0711; before, it read "every account at its usage cap" though another had room; `TestNativeCompactionWriterAtItsCap`). `withoutCompaction` (the drop after a refused reasoning item) isn't used for such a request. A route carrying a compaction names its sealer (`Route.Compaction`), and Routing says that is why no other account was asked, not that it is "the only account on" for the model (#2639; `TestNativeCompactionRouteNamesItsWriter`, `routing-compaction.test.cjs`). A refusal of the trigger reaches Codex as the upstream sent it. Not tried against a real upstream; Copilot may refuse a history that holds Codex's `web_search` items. [`native_compaction.go`](../../internal/gateway/native_compaction.go), `TestNativeCompaction*`.
- A compaction magpie answers itself (`codexCompact`: a magpie model, Codex's own models through magpie's Codex accounts, a CC Switch table) goes to Codex as a `compaction` item whose `encrypted_content` is `magpie1:` and the summary in base64 (`codexsummary.Encrypted`), and `codexInputAs` sends Codex's own summary message in its place (`codexsummary.Message`: Codex's summary prefix, a newline and the summary, as a user message). Codex keeps the item in its rollout and replays it, and OpenAI refuses it once Codex talks to OpenAI itself (`invalid_encrypted_content`, LukeMa on X). So `sessions.PortCodexRollouts` writes that same message into the rollouts in its place ([`codex_portable.go`](../../internal/sessions/codex_portable.go)). A reasoning item carrying Claude's block (`magpie-claude1:`, above) is written as its summary, as the gateway sends it to OpenAI (`TestPortLineDropsClaudeBlocks`). It changes only magpie's items, keeps every other line's bytes and the file's line count, mode and time, and drops an unreadable item together with its `replacement_history_metadata` entry. Every rewritten line keeps its length (`fitLine`: a shorter one padded with spaces after its JSON, a longer one in a paginated rollout not written), as Codex keeps places in a rollout by byte: a fork's `history_base.end_byte_offset` and the state database's turn and projection offsets. A shorter parent left a fork's place past its end, and Codex refused to open the fork ("cutoff byte offset is past the source rollout", xybio on Discord); once per Codex home a pass sets each such fork's place again from the ordinal it names (`repairLineages`). `MoveCodexProvider` keeps the session_meta line's length the same way and refuses a longer provider id for a paginated rollout. It and `MoveCodexThreads` (Codex stepping off magpie) hold the thread's writer lock as Codex's own archive does, from before a file is copied until the thread's row has moved: a Codex that has the thread open writes on to the file it opened, and what it wrote after a move was lost (#3751). Such a thread is refused as one written to in the last minute is (`ErrActive`), and a Codex home without writer locks moves as before. A pass publishes as Codex publishes a compressed rollout: under `thread-writer-locks/.coordination.lock`, only with the thread's lock free, through a temp file and a rename. A thread a Codex has open is left for a later pass, and a Codex home without writer locks is left alone. It runs 2 minutes after the GUI or `magpie serve` starts and every 10 minutes after, on Codex Disconnect (3s), and as the GUI quits (3s). Each pass reads what changed since the last whole pass, plus what was held (`codex-portable.json`). A ported thread was resumed by Codex 0.162.1 against an upstream that refuses `magpie1:` as OpenAI does. Not tried: a Codex in a WSL distro, whose locks this machine can't take, so it is skipped. `TestPortCodexRollouts*`, `TestPortedCompactionReadsTheSame`.
- A Responses request Codex sends a Responses provider is fitted for a backend that isn't OpenAI's before it goes (`passthrough`): Responses Lite's shape taken off (`fromLite`): its `additional_tools` item lifted into `tools` (`liftAdditionalTools`, #350), its `reasoning.context` left out, its Lite marker removed from `client_metadata` (`withoutLiteMetadata`), and its `X-OpenAI-Internal-Codex-Responses-Lite` header not sent on (`liteKept`). A Codex websocket turn repeats the marker in `client_metadata`; leaving it beside a removed `reasoning.context` made a downstream Responses relay forward an inconsistent Lite request, which its ChatGPT backend refused with "requires `reasoning.context` to be `all_turns`". A subagent a Lite lead (gpt-6.1-sol) spawns on another model comes in that shape, its model name alone changed, and OpenCode Go's DeepSeek answered it 503 "Endpoint is unavailable" (TangForX on X, `TestLiteSubagentLeavesLite`). Also the tool search as a function, OpenAI-only fields left out. A relay that checks a request is Codex's own turns that shape away (#1758: 400 "Codex 客户端请求特征无效", 10014). A provider set `codexAsSent` (`magpie provider set <id> codex=as-sent`) gets a request from Codex (`fromCodex`) as Codex sent it, its model name alone swapped, with Codex's own headers (`codexClientHeader`). That holds when Codex offers `web_search` too: the request is relayed with it, not translated for magpie's search (`searchedForIt`), which merged the developer messages into `instructions`, sent `apply_patch` as a function, left `web_search` out and added `max_output_tokens` (`codexAsSent` in `route`; maralica on Discord). `spawn_agent`'s description is still widened with the catalog's models (`spawnAgentModels`). Off is the default, and a subscription account is never sent so. `TestCodexAsSent`, `TestCodexAsSentWebSearch`, `TestFromLiteRemovesOnlyTheWebsocketLiteMarker`, `TestCodexTitleRelayKeepsLiteShapeConsistent`.
- A tool a Chat request's message carries joins the request's `tools` (`liftMessageTools` in [`message_tools.go`](../../internal/gateway/message_tools.go), #2322). Pi with `supportsMidConvoToolAdditions` gives a tool it adds mid-conversation as `{"role":"system","tools":[…]}` with no content, and keeps top-level `tools` as they were at the start; no Chat API reads tools there, and `parseChat` read only the top-level ones, so the tool could be called on no route. `handle` lifts them for every Chat body before `serveAgent`, so the Chat relay, the schema fixers, redaction (the tool is a tool definition, not message text), the prompt shown and routing all see them; `parseChat` lifts too, for a body that never came through `handle` (the Claude subscription bridge's direct callers). Each tool joins once by name and the first definition wins: a top-level tool keeps its own, then the earliest message's, as `liftAdditionalTools` and a `tool_addition` (`withoutDeferredTools`) do. A system or developer message that carried only tools is left out, so it is neither relayed as an empty system message nor joined into the system text as an empty part; one with content keeps its place without `tools`. Messages stay where they are: the in-place system message the issue also asks for is not this change. `TestPiAddedToolReachesEveryRoute` sends deephbz's Pi 1.2.0 bytes through the Chat relay, Messages, Responses, Gemini and Codex, and `TestPiAddedToolOfferedToClaudeCode` through the Claude subscription bridge.
- A Chat request's tool in another API's shape is put in the Chat shape, `{"type":"function","function":{name, description, parameters, strict}}` (`shapeChatTools` in [`chat_tool_shape.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat_tool_shape.go), #2622, jinyue806): MCP's `{"name","description","inputSchema"}` (ZCode), Anthropic's `input_schema`, a bare `{"name","parameters"}` and Responses' flat `{"type":"function","name",…}`. No Chat API takes those (SenseNova: "field Tools[0].Type invalid, should be set" and "function cannot be empty"), and `parseChat` read no name from them, so a translated request offered a tool named "". It runs right after `liftMessageTools`, in `handle` for every Chat body and in `parseChat`, so it holds for every Chat upstream, not SenseNova's alone. A tool with its `function`, a hosted tool (any other `type`) and one with no name are left as they came, and a request with none to shape is relayed byte for byte. `TestChatToolInAnotherShapeIsShaped` sends the report's request with each of its four tools through the Chat relay, Messages and Responses; `TestShapeChatToolsLeavesChatShape`.
- Every request to a key+URL provider goes with magpie's User-Agent (`magpie/<Version>`, `userAgentFor` in [`user_agent.go`](../../internal/gateway/user_agent.go)), except a provider set `passUserAgent` (#1851, `magpie provider set <id> user-agent=agent`, the editor's "Send the agent's own User-Agent"): it gets the User-Agent the inbound request carried, unchanged, for a coding plan that serves only the agents it names. One the agent didn't send, and magpie's own (`magpiesOwn`: another magpie's `magpie/…`, `magpie-router/1`, `magpie-vision/1`, `magpie-search/1`, and the image tool's `<client> magpie-image/1`, built from MCP's clientInfo, or behind the MCP router from the first word of the agent's User-Agent), go as magpie; magpie never makes up another client's. `forwardOnce` sets it for chat, Anthropic, Responses, Gemini and count_tokens; embeddings, images and videos (`retrieveFrom`, `sendWith`, `remoteVideoContent`) set it with `stampUA` from the caller the request came with (`caller.ua`, `withCaller`), and before #1851 went with Go's default. Claude Code's and Codex's own headers are copied after, as before, and the user's `header.User-Agent` is put on when the request is signed, so it wins. `provider.PassesUserAgent` refuses it for a signed-in account, a plugin's, and a vendor whose own client magpie goes as (`OwnClient`: OpenCode, Cline, Kilo, another magpie); their signing sets their own. `TestUserAgentPassedOn`, `TestUserAgentAccountUntouched`, `TestUserAgentFor`, `TestPassesUserAgent`, `provider-user-agent.test.cjs`.
- A Chat upstream that wants a turn's reasoning back (`chatReplaysReasoning` in [`chat.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat.go): a host or a model, as magpie or the provider names it, with `deepseek` in it, such as OpenCode Go's `deepseek-v4.1-flash`, or Command Code's plugin) gets every assistant tool call with `reasoning_content`, relayed (`withReasoningOnToolTurns` in `passthrough`) or translated (`buildChat`). DeepSeek turns the whole request away ("The `reasoning_content` in the thinking mode must be passed back to the API") when a tool call made since the user's last message has none, which a client sends for a turn it saw no thinking on: another model's, a translated one (#1462). The turn's own thinking goes back as it came; one sent in `reasoning` or `reasoning_details` is copied in, and `reasoning` is then left out (`asReasoningContent`): a self-hosted DeepSeek server reads the two as one field and refused pi's tool turn, which carried its thinking in `reasoning`, as "duplicate field reasoning" (ArvinCharl on X, v0.1.1560), while `reasoning_details`, a list of typed entries that can hold an encrypted one, stays; otherwise it goes with a space (`noReasoning`: DeepSeek's Chat API takes an empty one, but a relay that leaves empty fields out would drop it). An assistant message right after a tool result, a reply with no call of its own, gets one too: DeepSeek refuses it the same way (checked 2026-10-12, #1822). A reply to the user, any other model and Mistral (#494) are sent as before.
- A message content that is a list of plain text parts (`[{"type":"text","text":…}]`, nothing else in a part) is relayed to a Chat upstream as the string it says, the parts joined by a newline (`textPartsAsString` in [`chat_text_parts.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat_text_parts.go), in `passthrough` before `silentAssistantsOut`). OpenAI takes either, but a vendor's own server may take a string alone: the Intern discovery API (a custom provider on OpenAI compatible, Atria-Dawn, Discord) answered DeepSeek Harness, whose pi-ai sends every user message as such a list, 422 with an "Input should be a valid string" per message. A list with an image, a file, a thinking part or a `cache_control` mark stays a list, and every other byte of the body stays as sent; `buildChat` already writes text as a string. `TestChatRelayTextPartsAsString`, `TestTextPartsAsStringOnlyPlainText`.
- An assistant turn the user cut off before it said anything (only its thinking, an empty text, nothing; an isolated Codex reasoning item; a Gemini thought alone) stays in the agent's history and is sent on every later turn. DeepSeek and Kimi turn the whole request away over an assistant message with neither content nor tool calls, `reasoning_content` alone included ("Invalid assistant message: content or tool_calls must be set", #2599). Every Chat upstream gets such a turn left out, translated (`buildChat`, before `pairToolMessages`) or relayed (`silentAssistantsOut` in `passthrough`), by one rule (`withoutSilentAssistants` in [`chat.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat.go)): the two user messages it stood between join into one, and after a tool result the user's message follows the tool message. A prefill to go on from (`prefix`, `partial`, a Resume), a legacy `function_call`, a refusal and an audio reply stay, and a relayed body without such a turn goes byte for byte. `TestChatEmptyAssistantLeftOutFromAnthropic`, `…FromResponses`, `…FromGemini`, `TestChatRelayLeavesOutEmptyAssistant`, `TestChatRelaySilentAssistantsOnly`.
- Kimi's Chat API (`isKimi`: the Kimi platform's and a Kimi Code membership's hosts or presets) turns `reasoning_details` in a message away in any shape: an entry with a stream's `index` ("the reasoning_details at position 1 entry 0 must not contain streaming index") and any other entry ("… has an invalid type"), checked against api.kimi.com/coding on 2026-10-12. pi keeps another vendor's thinking there (MiMo's, through Command Code, with `index: 0`), so a conversation routed on to Kimi was refused on every turn (#1765). `passthrough` sends it to Kimi as `reasoning_content` (`reasoningDetailsAsContent`, with the message's `reasoning` left out once its text is there), which Kimi takes; a message with `reasoning_content` of its own keeps that. Another upstream that refuses a thinking field "at position N", as Kimi words it (a relay in front of it), is asked once more the same way and marked (`refusedInMessages`, `withoutMessageReasoning`).
- Vertex AI takes a function declaration's parameters as its own Schema object, whose `type` is required, and turns the whole request away over one schema without it, though the tool is never called: 400 "`mcp__cua-driver__drag` functionDeclaration `parameters.target` schema didn't specify the schema type field." (kthink on Discord: dsh with cua-driver's MCP tools, from before cua's own fix in its #4888, on Command Code's `google/gemini-3.8-flash`, which Command Code's gateway serves on Vertex; `target` was an anyOf of an untyped oneOf and null, `scope` a const). A body whose `model` names Gemini (`geminiNamed`: the id's last `/` segment starts with `gemini`, whoever serves it) has each untyped schema under its tools' `function.parameters`, `parameters` or `input_schema` given its type in place (`geminiToolTypes` in [`gemini_schema.go`](../../internal/gateway/gemini_schema.go), in `forwardOnce`, so a plugin's request too): from its enum or const values, its keywords or its branches, null left out (an object's or array's keywords, `properties` or `items`, before its values: a string enum beside `items` typed `"string"` sent items on a string, and Antigravity turned the turn away with "properties[display].items: field predicate failed: $type == Type.ARRAY", ferock on Discord; `TestGeminiToolTypesItemsBeforeEnum`), `{}` or a description alone a string, a tool's root an object. A node whose branches have more than one type, an unresolved `$ref` or union, and anything under `not` or `if` are left as they came, so such a schema can still be refused; every other model's tools go byte for byte. Google's own Gemini API and Code Assist are sent `parametersJsonSchema` and Antigravity `plainSchema`, so a Gemini-shaped body isn't walked (`TestGeminiToolTypesCuaDriverDrag`, with cua-driver's drag schema as its server listed it, `TestGeminiToolTypes`). `plainSchema` itself keeps an array's keywords (`items`, `minItems`, `maxItems`) on an array only and an object's (`properties`, `required` …) on an object only, and of a type list takes the type its `items` or `properties` are for, so a body a magpie from before sends is taken too (`TestAntigravityItemsOnlyOnArrays`). Google's Schema is an object with a fixed set of fields, so a boolean schema (`items: true`, a property's `true` or `false`) or an `x-` keyword such as MCP's `x-mcp-header` (2026-07-28 spec, SEP-2243) turns the whole request away ("Invalid value at '…items.items' (…aiplatform.master.Schema), true" and 'Unknown name "x-mcp-header" … Cannot find field', han_tiv on Discord). `plainSchema` is an allow-list, so `x-` keys never pass it; a boolean is `{}` for true and left out for false (a false property leaves `required` too, a false `items` is `items {}` with `maxItems 0`). Before typing, `geminiToolTypes` runs `geminiSchemaShapes`, which does the same for the relay's case: true is `{}`, false is left out, `x-` keywords go (a property named `x-…` stays), and `additionalProperties` and the `unevaluated…` keywords keep their booleans; a schema with neither goes byte for byte (`TestAntigravityBooleanSchemaAndXKeys`, `TestPlainSchemaBooleansAndXKeysEverywhere`, `TestGeminiToolTypesDropsBooleansAndXKeys`). What is left is still JSON Schema, so `parametersJsonSchema` (Gemini API, Code Assist, Vertex), which runs after it on a Gemini-named body, takes it as before.
- A tool whose schema has a union (`anyOf`, `oneOf`, `allOf`) at its root is folded to an object root (`provider.ObjectRoot`; `foldsRoots` and `objectRootsBody` in [`object_root.go`](../../internal/gateway/object_root.go), #1271): Anthropic's API turns every request offering one away ("input_schema does not support oneOf, allOf, or anyOf at the top level"). A request translated to Messages always went folded (`objectSchema`); a Chat or Responses body relayed as it came is folded for xAI from the first ask and for any other upstream once it refused one (`rootUnionRefused`, retried folded at once). A Messages body relayed as it came, a client that speaks Messages itself (ZCode, Claude Code) to an Anthropic-protocol provider or plugin, went unfolded until 11-04: ziko on X, ZCode with Zed's subscription, Claude refused every request because six of an MCP server's tools (GoAnyAPI's `goanyapi_adsense` among them) had a root `oneOf`. It is now folded for every such upstream from the first ask; a server tool (no `input_schema`) and a schema with no root union go as they came (`TestMessagesRootUnionFolded`, `TestRootUnionFoldedWhereRefused`, `TestRootUnionFoldedForXAI`).
- A Responses request's top-level `access_programs`, the Cyber program Codex asks the model under (`{"cyber":"standard"}` on a subagent it inherits, OpenAI's Daybreak programs, magpie-releases#18), goes only where Codex itself would send it: codex-rs sends it with a ChatGPT sign-in, or an API key on its provider named `openai`, and to no other provider (`cyber_access_program.rs`, `for_auth`, `for_provider`). In `forwardOnce`, relayed or built (from Chat too), it goes as it came to a ChatGPT (Codex) account, a host ending in `openai.com`, another magpie (`IsRemoteMagpie`, whose own upstream decides) and a relay set `codexAsSent` when Codex sent the request; every other Responses upstream is sent the request without it (`keepsAccessPrograms`, `withoutTopMember`, the rest byte for byte). OpenCode Go answered it, for DeepSeek and GLM, with 503 "Upstream request failed: Endpoint is unavailable." and 200 without it or with null; its Haiku took it (#3172, three reporters' real A/B runs). It is taken out before the user's extraBody is merged, so an `access_programs` written there still goes. Chat and Messages builds never carry it; a Chat request relayed as it came keeps whatever its client wrote. `TestAccessProgramsGoToOpenAIAlone`, `TestWithoutTopMember`, `TestCodexAccessProgramsReachBackend`.
- A client that took the gateway for an Ollama server (its `/api/show` is answered in Ollama's shape) sends Ollama's per-request `options` beside its Chat fields. Gemini's OpenAI-compatible API (`geminiCompat`: AI Studio's, or a local proxy in front of it) reads proto JSON and turns the whole request away over a name it doesn't know (#3097), so a body relayed to one goes without `options`, whose runner settings have no place on a Chat API, as the Ollama route's own body builder leaves them out (`ollamaChatBody`). Another vendor's Chat upstream gets `options` as the client sent it. `TestGeminiCompatRelayLeavesOutOllamaOptions`.
- Claude Code's deferred tools (ToolSearch, MCP tools that come and go) put blocks of their own in a message: a `tool_addition` (`tool` a `tool_reference` naming the tool, or a `tool_definition`), a `tool_removal`, and a `tool_reference`. Kimi's Anthropic endpoint refuses any of them with a whole-request 400 "Invalid request Error" that names nothing, so once a session had loaded a tool every turn failed (#2042); DeepSeek's takes a `tool_addition` naming a tool but refuses one defining it, a `tool_removal` and a message's `tool_reference` with 422 "unknown variant `tool_removal`" (both checked against the real endpoints on 2026-10-14). OpenCode Go also rejects DeepSeek inline definitions with a generic 422 "Upstream request failed: Endpoint is unavailable." (checked on 2026-10-15). Kimi and DeepSeek models on OpenCode are never sent these blocks (`forwardOnce`); a provider that refuses one in those words is asked again at once without them and not sent them again while magpie runs (`refuseShapes`, `shapeDeferred`; the retry loop reads a 422 as well as a 400). `withoutDeferredTools` drops the blocks and a message they leave empty; definitions present only in a `tool_addition.tool.definition` join the request's `tools` once by name (an existing top-level definition wins), and a `tool_reference` inside a `tool_result` (ToolSearch's answer, which both take) stays. Any other provider is sent them as Claude Code wrote them ([`anthropic_shapes.go`](../../internal/gateway/anthropic_shapes.go), `TestKimiIsSentNoDeferredToolBlocks`, `TestDeferredToolBlocksRefusedOnceLeftOut`, `TestOpenCodeDeepSeekInlineTools`, `TestDeferredInlineDefinitionPreserved`).
- ToolSearch's answer is a `tool_result` whose content is `tool_reference` blocks alone. OpenDesign's Anthropic endpoint can't read one: 422 "Failed to deserialize the JSON body into the target type: messages[4]: missing field `content`" (#2124, the reporter's direct request without magpie fails the same way; the same result with a text block beside the references is taken). A provider that refuses in those words while the request holds such a result is asked again at once with a text block first in each, "Tool <name> is loaded and can be called now." (`toolLoadedText`, the words a translated route already gives a model in `stringOrText`), the references and the `tool_use`/`tool_result` pairing kept, and is sent it that way while magpie runs (`refuseShapes`, `shapeRefsOnly`, `withRefsSaid`). This is in `forward`, so `/v1/messages`, `count_tokens` and a group's member all get it; a request translated to another API already carries the references as that text. Any other provider is sent the bare references (`TestRefsOnlyToolResultRefusedGetsText`, `TestRefsSaidLeavesOtherResults`).
- A tool Claude Code's ToolSearch loads, translated to a Responses API, used to join the top-level `tools` on the next request, which changed the prompt from its first bytes and missed the cache on every load (#2660). A deferred tool (`defer_loading`, `Tool.Deferred`) that a `tool_result`'s `tool_reference` names (`toolRefs`, `Part.Loads`) now goes, where `Request.LoadedInPlace` is set, in an `{"type":"additional_tools","role":"developer","tools":[…]}` item right after the run of tool outputs that loaded it, once, and not in `tools` (`buildResponses`): tools loaded by parallel results or one result naming several share one item, a tool named again isn't repeated, and the tool output's text is as before. OpenAI's tool search guide documents the item and that its tools load at the end of the context so the cache is kept. A compacted or edited history whose loading result is gone puts the tool back in `tools`, a new prefix, as is expected. `LoadedInPlace` is set (`loadsInPlace`, in `forwardTranslated`) for OpenAI's own API with a key (`api.openai.com`, `*.api.openai.com`), for a ChatGPT (Codex) account, and for a provider set `additionalTools` (`magpie provider set <id> additional-tools=on`); `additional-tools=off` (`additionalToolsOff`) turns it off even where it is the default, and `default` goes back to magpie's. The ChatGPT backend was tried with a real account (#4738, gpt-5.6-terra, gpt-6-luna, gpt-6-sol): it takes the item where it was loaded, the model calls the tool in it, and the next request read 7680–7936 tokens from cache where, with the tool moved into `tools`, it read none. Nothing else is sent it by default, since xAI answers the item 422 (#350), and no relay, other vendor's sign-in (Copilot) or plugin's vendor has been tried. An upstream that refuses a request holding the item with a 400 or 422 naming `additional_tools` is asked again with every tool in `tools`, logged ("turned away additional_tools … loaded tools go in tools from now on") and not sent the item again while magpie runs. Not handled: a tool a `tool_addition` block loads (an MCP server's late tool, the advisor) stays in `tools`. `TestLoadedToolsKeepThePrefix`, `TestLoadedToolsInOneItem`, `TestLoadedToolsWhereNotTaken`, `TestLoadsInPlaceScope`, `TestLoadedToolsThroughTheGateway`, `TestCodexAccountLoadsInPlace`.
- A Responses upstream that wants a turn's reasoning back (`replaysReasoning` in [`responses.go`](../../internal/gateway/responses.go): a model with `deepseek` in its name, on any host but OpenAI's, xAI's, Copilot's and Azure's) gets every reasoning item with a `reasoning_text` and every tool call after one, relayed (`withReasoningText` in `passthrough`) or translated (`buildResponses`). DeepSeek's `/v1/responses` turns the whole request away ("The `reasoning_text` in the thinking mode must be passed back to the API") over a reasoning item without text, one with an empty text, and a `function_call` whose step (since the last user message or tool output) has no reasoning item. Codex sends all three in a routing group that falls to DeepSeek after ChatGPT: the backend's sealed items (`encrypted_content` only), magpie's summary-only items, and another member's calls with none (#1104, #1592). DeepSeek reads a step (a run of reasoning, assistant messages and calls) as one assistant message whose thinking is the item it starts with: in the current turn, a step with a call or after a tool output must lead with a reasoning item, so [message, reasoning, call] and [output, message] are refused while [reasoning, message, call] and [output, reasoning, message] are taken (checked against api.deepseek.com on 2026-10-12). Codex sends a step that said something before its call with no reasoning for another model's turn, which magpie used to send [message, reasoning " ", call] and DeepSeek refused intermittently, only while the turn lasted (#1822, through OpenCode Go). An item's own text is kept; one without gets its summary, else a space (`noReasoning`); each such step leads with its reasoning (`reasonFirst`): its own first reasoning item moved ahead, else one holding a space. A sealed item keeps its id and `encrypted_content`; a body already in that shape goes byte for byte.
- A Messages upstream that wants a turn's thinking back (`anthropicReplaysThinking` in [`anthropic.go`](../../internal/gateway/anthropic.go): a host or a model with `deepseek` in it, never `api.anthropic.com` or a Claude model) gets a thinking block on every assistant message with a `tool_use` or right after tool results alone (refused the same way, checked 2026-10-12, #1822), relayed (`withThinkingOnToolTurns` in `passthrough`) or translated (`build`, which also keeps unsigned thinking as its text there). DeepSeek's `/anthropic/v1/messages`, thinking (its default, or asked for), turns the whole request away with 400 "The `content[].thinking` in the thinking mode must be passed back to the API" when such a message has none, and answers any `redacted_thinking` with 422, a block it can't read. Claude Code sends both in a routing group that falls to DeepSeek after Claude (#1611). A message with no thinking gets one holding a space (`noReasoning`) first; Claude's `redacted_thinking` becomes that block, or is left out beside thinking of the message's own. Signed thinking, DeepSeek's own and every other message go as they came. Checked against the real endpoint on 2026-10-11: it takes Claude's signed thinking, unsigned thinking and a space, anywhere in the message. A thinking block magpie sends unsigned (the space, or a turn's reasoning kept as its text) says `"signature": ""`, as `aBlock` writes `thinking` even when empty: OpenRouter's `/api/v1/messages` holds a block's signature to a string and turns a DeepSeek request with none away, 400 "Invalid Anthropic Messages API request" (#2883, #3696), and DeepSeek's own endpoint takes the empty one (checked 2026-10-19). `TestOpenRouterDeepSeekThinkingHasASignature`.
- A Messages or Chat upstream whose host and model don't say DeepSeek but whose model answers 400 "The `content[].thinking` (or `reasoning_content`) in the thinking mode must be passed back to the API" (`thinkingBackRefusal` in [`thinking_back.go`](../../internal/gateway/thinking_back.go)) is asked once more with its tool turns filled as above (`withThinkingOnToolTurns`, `withReasoningOnToolTurns`), and that provider's model is sent them at once from then on (`thinkingBackNeeded` in `unfit`, filled in `forwardOnce`), on every route to it. A relay serving DeepSeek under another name, or a provider the user named "My Claude", got a routing group's fall from a rate-limited Codex subscription mid tool loop refused, Codex's sealed reasoning having no text to give (qisishiqi on Discord, 2026-11-02). Checked against api.deepseek.com on 2026-11-02: only the tool loop being continued needs it; finished turns and plain replies are taken without. A provider that never asked is sent the turns as before. `TestThinkingBackLearnedBehindAnotherName`, `TestReasoningBackLearnedBehindAnotherName`.
- Every thinking block a Messages client gets says a `signature`, a string, as Anthropic's own do (its stream starts each block with `"signature": ""`): Grok Build reads `ContentBlock::Thinking` as `{thinking, signature}`, both required, and turned a whole reply away, "missing field `signature`" (uk.jent on Discord, on the MiniMax Code plugin). The encoder starts a thinking block signed empty (`anthropicEncoder.thinking`), `renderAnthropic` and `whole_stream.go` write the field too, and a reply relayed from a Messages endpoint that wrote none, streamed or whole, gets an empty one (`sigFieldTidy` in [`thinking_signature.go`](../../internal/gateway/thinking_signature.go), in `passthrough`); a block already signed goes byte for byte, and `redacted_thinking` is untouched. magpie never makes a signature up: an empty one is none to every path that sends history on (`anthropicBody` leaves it out for Anthropic, keeps its text where reasoning is replayed), and a relay sends it back to the vendor that wrote the block. `TestMessagesThinkingIsSigned`, `TestSignedThinkingRelayedAsItCame`.
- A model writes a whole number in a call's arguments with a fraction of zeros (`2500.0`, `17277.0`, tool_search's `limit: 8.0`): Grok's on every turn, whoever serves it (#1431), and muse-spark through the gateway (#3152). Codex reads the integer fields it parses as u64, usize or i32 even though their schemas say `number`, so serde turns away the whole call ("invalid number" for tool_search's arguments). In every reply, every number in a call's arguments written with a fraction of zeros and no exponent loses the fraction: `2500.0` becomes `2500`, `-3.00` becomes `-3`. Strings, keys, other fractions (`2.5`, `2.50`) and exponents (`1.0e3`) stay as written. No tool schema is consulted, since JSON has one number type and one that took `2500.0` takes `2500`. The rule runs over the arguments as a stream of text (`wholeNums`), holding back only a fraction of zeros until the next byte. This gives the same arguments whether they come whole or in deltas cut anywhere, so what a call's deltas add up to is what its done events say. Relayed replies are rewritten in `passthrough` by `intTidy`: Responses' function_call items, argument deltas and done events and the completed response; Chat's `tool_calls` deltas and messages; Messages' `tool_use` input and `input_json_delta`, streamed or whole; a whole reply with no call in it is said as it came, undecoded. A glm-5.3-flash* route's stitched CR (`crTidy`, #4256) is dropped before `intTidy` sees the reply, since `intTidy` holds a whole one to its flush (`TestStitchedCRAndWholeFloatsBothGo`). Translated replies are rewritten at the encoder (`wholeEncoder`, outside `usageEncoder`) and at `render` (`wholeResult`). Grok's schema quirks stay `grokModel`'s ([`grok_integral.go`](../../internal/gateway/grok_integral.go)).
- A tool call a model writes into its reply's text, when the request offered tools, is made the call it is ([`textcalls.go`](../../internal/gateway/textcalls.go), #823): Hermes' `<tool_call>{…}</tool_call>`, DeepSeek's DSML (`<｜DSML｜invoke name=…>` with its parameters; its mark found as the parse regexes read it, by `dsmlMark`, not from a list of spellings: any run of pipes, ASCII or full-width or mixed, spaces after `<` and around `/`, `<｜｜DSML｜｜` too, #2623), DeepSeek's half-Hermes `<parameter>` form (#917), GLM's `<arg_key>`/`<arg_value>` (#906), and the plain `<invoke name=…><parameter name=…>…</parameter></invoke>` of Claude's and MiniMax's calls, bare or in `<function_calls>` or `<minimax:tool_call>` (packinggoodsllc_07668 on Discord: Claude Code through a routing group was shown the invokes as text), read by `parseTextCalls`. Text after a call read out, other than tags, ends the calls, and it and all after it go: the model went on as if its call had run ("*Result: Tool loaded.*", then the next call, then "done"), and the agent runs the call and gives it the real result. Each block must name an offered tool and have arguments that can be read; else the text goes on as it came. The text before the first block is kept, what is left of the tags goes, and the reply ends as one that called tools (`finish_reason` `tool_calls`, `stop_reason` `tool_use`). A DSML close with no block before it (`</｜｜DSML｜｜ calls>`) is held as a block is, and dropped when it is tags and nothing else. A reply whose model made a call itself has no call read out of its text, but tags and nothing else left in it after the call go, with the spaces before them (喜欢一个人 on Discord: a relay made the calls of DeepSeek's invokes and left the wrapper's close as text). Every route reads it the same way: a relayed Chat stream (`textCallTidy`), a relayed Chat reply that came whole, JSON or under `text/plain` or no type (`chatWhole` with `wholeTextCalls`, #1845: a `stream:false` request kept the markup and `finish_reason` `stop`), which also covers a vendor that answered a streamed request whole (`wholeAsStream`), a relayed Messages reply, streamed or whole (`anthropicTextCalls`: the held text's block start and stop are held with it, the calls become `tool_use` blocks after it, or in its place when none of its text went), and every translation, which magpie always asks streamed, a plugin's included, whatever the client asked (`textCallSee`). A relayed Responses reply, streamed or whole, is read too (`responsesTextCalls` in [`textcalls_relay.go`](../../internal/gateway/textcalls_relay.go), #4743, from v0.1.2652): the held text's `output_text.done`, `content_part.done` and `output_item.done` wait with it, and the calls become `function_call` items after it, in the events and in `response.completed`'s output. Before it, Alma (whose provider of type openai speaks Responses) through a group to DeepSeek, whose preset speaks Responses too, had the reply relayed as it came and got DeepSeek's `<｜｜DSML｜｜ calls>` as text with nothing run (wishSeikouudoku on X). A relayed Gemini reply the same (`geminiTextCalls`). Covered by `TestTextWritten*`, `TestInvokeReported*`, `TestDSMLAlma*` (a group of Responses members and a plugin's DeepSeek, from a Responses client, one character to a delta), `TestDSMLClose*`, `TestDSMLBlockClosedWithCalls`, `TestGLMTextCall*`, `TestPluginTextWrittenCall` and `TestQuietWholeReplyStreamed`. Relayed Messages streams buffer complete SSE events with `tidyEvents` ([`phase.go`](../../internal/gateway/phase.go)), so splitting an event header, data and blank delimiter across reads cannot detach headers or join distinct JSON payloads (#4868). Held text-block starts and stops own their bytes so buffer reuse cannot overwrite them. `TestAnthropicTextCallsSSEChunksKeepFraming` and `TestAnthropicMessagesSSEChunksKeepFraming` cover native and text-written calls, held text, whole/event/line/byte reads, and CRLF.
- Structured output is carried across every translation (`Format`, [`format.go`](../../internal/gateway/format.go)). It is read from Responses' `text.format`, Chat's `response_format`, Anthropic's `output_config.format` and Gemini's `responseMimeType` with `responseJsonSchema`/`responseSchema`: `json_schema` with its name, description, schema and strict, or `json_object`. Each upstream is asked in its own field: `text.format` on Responses, `response_format` on Chat, `output_config.format` on Messages, and `generationConfig.responseMimeType`/`responseJsonSchema` on Gemini and Code Assist. An API with no name for it gets `response`. Messages and the Claude subscription's `--json-schema` take a schema only, so `json_object` is told in the system prompt there (`inSystem`). A 400 or 422 that names the field (`refusesFormat`: DeepSeek's "This response_format type is unavailable now", a schema Anthropic can't hold) marks the provider and model (`formatRefused`), and the request is asked again with the format told in the system prompt, as are later requests to that provider and model. A relayed request keeps its format as sent. A Gemini client's format is also still told in its system prompt, as before. A model may still wrap its JSON answer in a Markdown code fence; a client that asked for structured output, in a field or told in the system prompt (`wantsJSON`), gets the JSON inside a single unlabelled or json-labelled fence when that content is a complete valid JSON value, streamed (`fenceEncoder`) or whole (`unfencedResult`), in [`format_fence.go`](../../internal/gateway/format_fence.go). Invalid or truncated JSON, other fence languages, text outside the fence, and answers to plain-text clients reach the client as they came. This normalization applies to translated replies; native protocol passthrough stays byte-preserving.
- A tool's images reach the model on every translation (`toolOutput` in [`ir.go`](../../internal/gateway/ir.go) reads them from Anthropic's `tool_result`, Responses' `function_call_output`, such as Codex's `view_image` output `[{"type":"input_image","image_url":"data:…"}]`, and a Chat tool message). Anthropic's Messages keeps them in the `tool_result`. Chat puts them in a user message right after the results, and the result says they follow; Gemini and Code Assist after the function's response.
