Kiro's results hold text alone, so Kiro's plugin (from 0.1.9 on) sends them with the message that answers the call, and only the latest images go again (#369, #1861). `TestCodexToolImageReachesAnthropicPlugin` and the Kiro plugin's `tool-images.test.mjs`.
- An image's `detail` (`Part.Detail`, read from a Responses `input_image` and a Chat `image_url`, in a message or a tool's output) is carried on a translation to Chat and Responses (`chatImage`, `chatDetail`, `responsesDetail` in [`chat.go`](../../internal/gateway/chat.go)). Chat takes `low`, `high` and `auto` as they came, and Responses' `original`, which Chat lacks, as `high`; an image without one goes without. Responses takes the four as they came, and anything else, none included, as `auto` (#1474). Messages and Gemini have no such field, so it isn't sent there (mysterious_dove on Discord, Codex: `high` and `original` were dropped on the way to Chat; `TestResponsesImageDetailReachesChat`, `TestChatImageDetailReachesResponses`).
- DeepSeek's own API (`api.deepseek.com`, never a relay or another base) is sent an agent's inline images by file id, not their bytes on every turn (#5407, faithleysath; [`deepseek_files.go`](../../internal/gateway/deepseek_files.go)). `withDeepSeekFiles`, in `forwardOnce` after the thinking is put back and before the byte cap, finds each base64 `data:` image a Chat, Responses or Anthropic body holds (`walkImages`: messages and tool outputs) for a model with `flash` in its name (deepseek-flash sees images; deepseek-v4-pro reads each as "[Unsupported Image]"), with the provider's key, not on count_tokens, and not where the provider is set `imageFilesOff` (`magpie provider set <id> image-files=off`, or the editor's *Images* tick under Advanced, offered only where `provider.TakesImageFiles` holds: the one host rule, `ImageFilesAt`, that `dsFilesOn` asks; matrix0487 on Discord; `TestProviderSaveImageFiles`, `provider-image-files.test.cjs`). An image asked at `detail: low` stays inline, as a file is read at full size. Each image not held yet goes up once through `POST /files` on the provider's host (multipart, `purpose=user_data`, `expires_after` a day from creation), signed as a Chat request with the candidate's own key; the swap is per key (`dsKeyHash` beside the image's sha256), so an id never goes with another key. The block becomes Chat's flat `{"type":"file","file_id":…}`, a Responses `input_image` with `file_id` in place of `image_url`, or an Anthropic image whose `source` is `{"type":"file","file_id":…}` with `anthropic-beta: files-api-2025-04-14` added, as DeepSeek's files_api, vision, responses_api and anthropic_api guides give them (the Anthropic guide names the `file` source type and the beta; its field name `file_id` is Anthropic's Files API's, which DeepSeek says it follows). The ids are kept in `deepseek-files.json` beside providers.json (temp file and rename; an unparseable one moved to `.bad`), an id with under an hour left uploaded again, at most 4000 kept. One upload runs per image and key however many requests want it (`flights`), at most four at once for a request, outliving a cancelled request with its own deadline (20s, plus a second per 64KiB). A failed upload leaves the image inline and rests the key's uploads for ten minutes (`dsFilesBreak`); a request waits on an upload for `dsWaitMax` at most and sends what isn't up yet inline. A 4xx whose body names a `file-api-` id or says a file is gone (`dsFileGone`) lets the ids go and asks once more with new uploads (`dsRetryGone` in `forward`); refused so again, it goes inline. Uploaded bytes count in the request's upstream bytes (#4456). Not covered: a `custom_tool_call_output`'s images (walkImages doesn't read it), and no file is deleted (`DELETE /files`): each expires in a day. `TestDeepSeekImagesByFile`, `TestDeepSeekImagesInlineElsewhere`, `TestDeepSeekImageFilesOff`, `TestDeepSeekUploadFailsInline`, `TestDeepSeekUploadSlowGoesInline`, `TestDeepSeekFileGoneUploadsAgain`, `TestDeepSeekFilesPerKey`, `TestDeepSeekFilesOneUpload`, `TestDeepSeekFilesKeptOnDisk`.
- A Gemini client's `generationConfig.imageConfig`, the size and shape it asks an image model to draw at (`imageSize`, `aspectRatio`, and Vertex AI's own fields beside them), is carried as it was sent on a translation to an upstream that speaks Gemini: a custom provider's Gemini URL, Vertex AI, and Code Assist and Antigravity inside their envelope (`Request.ImageConfig`, `buildCodeAssistSent` in [`codeassist.go`](../../internal/gateway/codeassist.go)). A relayed request keeps it as sent. Chat, Responses and Messages have no field for it that magpie knows, so it isn't sent there. A request asked for one body that carries it is translated on the Gemini URL even where the provider has a Chat URL too (One try above); one that doesn't goes to Chat there, unless the user set the model's API to Gemini (#3820, GuoJinguang: `imageSize` 4K asked through magpie drew 1024x1024; `TestGeminiImageConfigReachesTheGeminiAPI`, `TestGeminiImageConfigReachesCodeAssist`).
- Group members are `provider/model[:effort]`. A member with a fixed effort is asked for that effort, bounded by the group's levels (#671).
- A member the user unchecked in its provider's model list is skipped (`unpicked` in [`group.go`](../../internal/provider/group.go), in `membersOff`; #3911, timonwong): the provider has picks and its served models lack it, as the Routing page strikes it, and the Routing card says it is not ready (`provider.ResolveRoute`). A provider with no picks still routes a model its list lacks (#2642), a decision model is still a decision group's, and a request naming the model itself still reaches it (`resolveIn` takes any `provider/model` of a provider that is on). `group_model_pick_test.go`.
- A member's suffix may join several levels with `+` (`provider/model:low+xhigh`, Garydesu on X): the agent picks among them. `provider.MemberLevels` splits it into `Member.Levels` and leaves `Effort` empty, so it isn't fixed; one level is still a fixed effort. The suffix is kept lowest first, each level once, and the whole stays the member's id, so its rules, fast, off and weight are keyed by it as for any member. The gateway fits the agent's ask to the member's own levels (`groupLevels` returns the candidate's `levels` first), and the group lists those for it to agents (`buildGroupEntries`), intersected with the other members' as usual. In the GUI the member's reasoning menu has "Several levels…". Tests: `TestMemberSeveralLevels` (provider), `TestMemberSeveralLevelsFollowAgent` (gateway), `group-member-levels.test.cjs`.
- A group offers agents the reasoning levels its members have in common (`groupEntries` in [`group.go`](../../internal/provider/group.go)), or the ones it names (`Group.Levels`, #295). A member that doesn't follow the agent's effort is left out of that: one fixed at an effort, one whose levels nothing knows (#597), one that thinks with no levels, one the user made reasonless, and one known not to think (`Entry.Quiet`), which the gateway sends no reasoning ask (`withoutReasoningAsk`, [`quiet.go`](../../internal/gateway/quiet.go)). A quiet member used to empty the group's levels, so Codex got its entry with no `supported_reasoning_levels`, showed no effort control and ran at its own medium (#2127, `TestGroupQuietMemberKeepsCodexEffortControl`).
- An agent whose config can't carry an effort has one picked in magpie (`provider.AgentEffort`, settings' `agentEfforts`; Cursor Private Inference's row, #1003, and Antigravity's, #4838). On an Antigravity account the level picks the family's variant, as a request's own effort does (`AntigravitySentID`, `TestAntigravityAgentEffort`). It is asked for on that agent's requests to a model that reasons (`Provider.Thinks`), in place of the level the request sent, fitted to the model's levels and bounded by the group's. Order: a member's fixed effort, then the effort the rule that put the member first sets (`Rule.UseEffort`), then a model's `:effort` suffix, then the turn's pick (a routing rule's), then the agent's pick, then the request's own. A model that doesn't reason is sent as asked; the default (none picked) leaves every request as it came. The pick beats the request's own because Cursor sends its own default as often as a real choice. Picking Default hands the choice back to Cursor.
- In models.dev, entries from `deepseek`, `kimi-code-plan-cn` and `kimi-code-plan-global` gain `none` only when they have both a `toggle` and effort levels; the DeepSeek and Kimi APIs accepted that value in [#1098](https://github.com/yetone/magpie/issues/1098). Other providers keep their listed levels because their APIs may reject `none`; toggle-only entries remain level-less ([`mdModel.efforts`](../../internal/catalog/catalog.go)).
- Gemini's OpenAI-compatible API (AI Studio's, or a proxy in front of Gemini on this computer or the LAN: `geminiCompat`) is asked for the model's thoughts in `extra_body.google.thinking_config`. A 400 or 422 whose error names `extra_body`, `include_thoughts` or thinking at all (`refusesThinkingConfig`) marks the provider unfit for the field (`thinking_config`), and it is asked again with `reasoning_effort` alone. Gemini 3 can't stop thinking, so reasoning turned off goes at `minimal`, its least, or at the model's lowest level where it has no `minimal` (`geminiLevels`: those of the model's levels it hasn't turned away, or of Gemini 3's where its own aren't known or none is left). Gemini 3 turns away a level the model hasn't with a 400 that names the level (`geminiRefusedLevel`): AI Studio's "Thinking level MINIMAL is not supported for this model. Please retry with other thinking level." for gemini-3.8-flash, Vertex AI's "Thinking level is unsupported: THINKING_LEVEL_MINIMAL". It names thinking too, but isn't the field turned away. The level is marked refused for that model (`levelRefused`), and the model is asked again at the nearest level it has left, still for its thoughts, and at that level from then on. A level turned away again, with none left, is the client's 400. Passed through, a Chat client's own `none` or `minimal` that Gemini turns away this way is asked again at the model's lowest level (`onEffort`), and that level goes at the lowest from then on (`turnedAway`); the other still goes as sent, as AI Studio takes `none` from gemini-3.8-flash. A refusal that names the levels the model takes (`effortLevelsNamed`, Command Code's for Qoder's `none`) marks reasoning off as a whole (`offRefused`): both go at the lowest from then on; one that lists the other among the levels taken (`otherOffNamed`, quoted, OpenAI's for gpt-5.1's `minimal`) marks only the level turned away. Both routes read and write the same marks. Vertex AI's OpenAI-compatible API marks each streamed chunk of thoughts in the delta's `extra_content.google.thought`, and such a chunk is read as thinking.
- A Claude that thinks only adaptively (`adaptiveOnly` in [`anthropic.go`](../../internal/gateway/anthropic.go): Opus, Sonnet and Haiku from 4.6 on, Fable and Mythos 5 and 5.1, read by `claudeVersion` however a relay spells them) is never sent a thinking budget, which they answer with 400. magpie's own request from a Chat or Responses client (`buildAnthropic`) goes as `thinking: {type: "adaptive", display: "summarized"}` with the effort in `output_config.effort`, so the client sees the reasoning Opus 4.7 and later otherwise leave out (display `omitted` by default), and a relay that refuses `display` is asked again without it (`refuseShapes`), as is one that refuses a turn's effort on a `{role: "system"}` message (`withoutTurnEffort`: a relay's "messages.1.output_config: Extra inputs are not permitted", or OpenRouter's "Mid-conversation reasoning effort (configuration_update) is not supported on <model>" on DeepSeek and GLM, #3696; `TestOpenRouterTurnEffortLeftOut`), as is one that refuses auto mode's `safeguards` (Copilot's Messages API: 400 "safeguards: Extra inputs are not permitted" on claude-opus-5.5, #1756; `shapeSafeguards`, `TestSafeguardsRefusedLeftOut`), and neither is sent it again while magpie runs; a client's `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens}` is turned into the same in `forwardOnce` (`adaptiveThinking`), the budget as its nearest effort, above `high` as `max`, and `display: "summarized"` unless the client named its own display, so the thinking that enabled shows stays shown (the refused shapes are taken out after it, and `refuseShapes` reads the display as it is sent); and Pi, dsh and omp are written to ask it adaptively (`AdaptiveThinking`: `forceAdaptiveThinking`, omp's `anthropic-adaptive`; #1470). A client's `xhigh` goes as `xhigh`, except to Opus and Sonnet 4.6, which have no `xhigh` and are asked `max` (`noXhigh`, #1471).
- A vendor's model that takes its level in `output_config.effort` beside thinking on (`effortInOutputConfig` in [`anthropic.go`](../../internal/gateway/anthropic.go): Z.ai's GLM-5.2 and GLM-5.3, MiniMax's MiniMax-M3.1-Flash and its -Preview, and any `gpt-<digit>` id on a Messages endpoint, such as a relay's: it showed every request at medium on the relay's console, zxz8009195489 on Discord, `TestGPTMessagesEffortInOutputConfig`) is sent the effort there next to the budget: built from a Chat or Responses client's effort (`buildAnthropic`, when it sends a budget), or added to an Anthropic client's request from its budget's nearest level, fitted to the model's levels (`withOutputEffort`, in `passthrough`). A budget alone left GLM at its default, and under an 8192 cap gave MiniMax's medium to max one budget, 7168 (shin4 on plugins#316). A client that asks such a model adaptively, with its level in `output_config.effort`, has both kept (Z.ai takes adaptive with the level, tried live on GLM-5.3); dsh is written to ask them so (GLM-5.2 and GLM-5.3: `AdaptiveWithEffort`, `forceAdaptiveThinking` in `dshKindConfig`; MiniMax not yet, #3496), since pi-ai's budget path clamps Max to high's 16384 and its Max reached GLM-5.3 as high (01huadalang on Discord, 10-24). Every other model, Claude and MiniMax-M3 included, gets the budget alone. `TestGLMEffortInOutputConfig`, `TestMiniMaxPluginGetsTheNativeEffort`, `TestNativeEffortLeavesOthersAlone`, `TestAdaptiveOnOutputEffortModelAsksItsLevel`, `TestDshMaxReachesOutputEffortModels`.
- A request to an Anthropic endpoint that turns thinking off is sent as the model takes it (`thinkingOffAsTaken`, in `forwardOnce`, so relayed requests, count_tokens, magpie's own thinking-off and retries all get it; the model is read as the vendor names it, from the body, or from the path for Bedrock and Vertex). Claude Sonnet 5.5 refuses `thinking: {type: "disabled"}` and is sent `{type: "between_tools"}` alone, at effort `high` at most. Opus 5.5, Fable 5 and Mythos 5 refuse it at every effort and are sent no thinking, at effort `low` unless one was asked, with `classifierRoom` added to a reply limit below it, as Claude Code does. Opus 5 and Haiku 5.5 take it only up to `high`, so `xhigh` and `max` are asked as `high`. `between_tools` sent to any other Claude goes as `disabled`, or as none where that is refused too. Other vendors' models, older Claudes and later lines than these go as they came (#1454). A Sonnet 5.5 by a name these rules can't read, answering 400 that asks for `between_tools` (`asksBetweenTools`), is asked again with it, once; a refusal of `disabled` at any effort (`thinkingOffRefused`) is asked again with thinking left to the model, as `alwaysThinks` is. So is a relay that reads `disabled` as reasoning effort `none` and turns that level away naming the ones it takes (Sub2API's gpt-6.1-sol, "does not support reasoning effort \"none\"; use low, medium, high, xhigh or max", #1465); `refusesThinkingOff` is the one list of these refusals for both routes. A relayed request refused so marks the model (`markUnfit(offRefused)`), and its later requests go with thinking left to the model at once until magpie restarts, as auto mode's classifier asks on every action.
- A Chat or Responses client's reasoning turned off (`none`, `ThinkOff`), translated to an Anthropic endpoint (`buildAnthropic`), goes as `thinking: {type: "disabled"}`; it went as thinking at a 4096-token budget before (#1636). A model that refuses it (`refusesThinkingOff`: `alwaysThinks`, `mandatoryReasoning`, `thinkingOffRefused`, or `none` turned away naming the levels taken) is asked again at its least (`thinkingBackOn`), and `markUnfit(offRefused)` sends it so at once until magpie restarts. A translated request's level can differ from the one asked: `none` and `minimal` go as `low` where the model's levels don't say it stops (`effortOf`). The route keeps the level asked; the try, the usage log and Routing's "went" say the one built (`noteWireEffort`, `wireEffort` in [`wire_effort.go`](../../internal/gateway/wire_effort.go)). Gemini's builders say theirs their own way and are logged as asked.
- An upstream that takes a model only with thinking enabled answers a request sent none, or sent it off (`thinkingOffUnlessAsked` for a vendor's model), with a 400 saying `thinking.type must be "enabled"` (`thinkingNeededRefusal`, read off the reply at any depth of a relay's wrapping, never off the provider: cybercaius's own 天翼云 relay turned Claude Desktop's connection check away so, v0.1.1862). It is asked again once thinking at Anthropic's least budget, 1024, with `max_tokens` raised past it by what was asked and `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` left out (`withThinkingEnabled`), and `markUnfit(thinkingNeeded)` keeps the model so until magpie restarts, by magpie's name and the vendor's (`markThinkingNeeded`): relayed, the body is given thinking at once (`passthrough`); translated, a Messages body once built, and a Chat or Responses one at its lowest effort (`thinkingBackOn`). `forwardOnce` doesn't make such a model's `enabled` adaptive (`adaptiveThinking`), `enabled` being the type it said it takes. A non-streaming client's translated request whose refusal came inside a 200 stream is asked again the same way (`translate`); a streaming client's is not ([`thinking_needed.go`](../../internal/gateway/thinking_needed.go)).
- A model the user ticked no levels of (`ModelReasonless` in [settings](../../internal/settings/settings.go), `SetModelReasonless`; the provider editor's Names, prices & levels, or `magpie model efforts <provider/model> off`) is sent no reasoning field on any route, whatever the agent asks: the candidate loop strips the body with `withoutAnyReasoning` ([`quiet.go`](../../internal/gateway/quiet.go): Chat's `reasoning_effort`, `reasoning`, `thinking`, `enable_thinking` and `include`'s `reasoning.encrypted_content`, Responses' `reasoning`, Anthropic's `thinking` of any type and `output_config.effort`, Gemini's `thinkingConfig`), the Anthropic relay doesn't add `thinking:{type:disabled}` for it, and `forwardTranslated` clears `Effort`, `ThinkOff` and `Thinking` for its other callers. It offers no levels (`Efforts`, `entryFor`), is `Quiet`, and as a routing-group member doesn't take the group's levels away. The learned refusals above cover only reasoning *off* and Gemini levels; a vendor that answers a plain `reasoning_effort` with 422 (yuexian7 on Discord) is not learned, so this is the user's switch. `TestReasonlessModelSentNoReasoning`, `TestReasonlessAnthropicRelay`, `TestGroupReasonlessMemberKeepsLevels`, `model-reasonless.test.cjs`.
- Levels the user kept of a model's own (`ModelEfforts`, `SetModelEfforts`; `magpie model efforts`, the editor's Names, prices & levels, `magpie model spec --reasoning`) only narrow what is offered (#92): `provider.Efforts` returns the vendor's list's or models.dev's levels, and `fitEffort` fits a request to those, so an agent asking xhigh of a model kept to low–high still asks xhigh. A model no list knows levels of can be given them (`magpie model spec --reasoning`, Dani Ariyanto on Discord); those are then its levels, a request is fitted to them, and it counts as reasoning (`entryFor`). `AutoVision` honours the user's `ModelImages` over the model's own (`seesAt`). `TestModelSpecReachesTheGateway`, `TestNarrowedVendorLevelsAskAsBefore`, `TestAutoVisionTakesTheUsersVision`.
- A request to an Anthropic endpoint with `temperature`, `top_p` or `top_k` reaches a model that refuses them without them (`samplingAsTaken`, beside `thinkingOffAsTaken` in `forwardOnce`): Opus 4.7, 4.8, 5 and 5.5, Fable 5, Mythos 5, Sonnet 5 and 5.5, and Haiku 5.5, whose 400 is "`temperature` is deprecated for this model" (Claude Code's auto mode classifier and magpie's own classifiers ask at temperature 0). Older Claudes, Haiku 4.5 among them, other vendors' models and later lines go as they came (#1454). A Claude by a name these rules can't read, answering that a sampling parameter is deprecated (`samplingDeprecated`), is asked again without them, once — a Chat or Responses client's request translated to Messages (`forwardTranslated`) as much as a relayed one. A model whose own list refuses them (`RejectsTemperature`, from the catalog's `temperature`, or a plugin's `temperature: false` carried by [the plugin host](provider-plugins.md#a-plugin-models-sampling), #2492) is asked without `temperature` and `top_p` whatever the client speaks: a translation leaves them out (`build`), and a Chat or Responses request relayed as it came does too (`passthrough`).
- Claude Code's auto mode asks the API to review each action ([`automode.go`](../../internal/gateway/automode.go), Anthropic's [auto-mode-classifier-billing](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/auto-mode-classifier-billing)): the `dangerous-tool-use-…` beta, a `safeguards` field, and `safeguard_results` in the reply (a stream's in its `message_delta`). Only Anthropic runs that classifier, so they reach only a route it answers on (`AutoModeReviewed`): the Claude subscription (the bridge hands them to Claude Code's own binary), a Claude model under any vendor's spelling on a provider with a key (Anthropic's API, a relay, Bedrock, Vertex), and anything on `api.anthropic.com`. Another subscription's or a plugin's Claude model (Copilot, Kiro, Cursor…) is that vendor's service and isn't reviewed by its name: Copilot turns `safeguards` away (#1756), the others translate the request, so neither the beta nor `safeguards` is sent there (`TestAutoModeReviewedRoutes`, `TestAutoModeReviewLeftOffOtherSubscriptions`). There the beta, `safeguards` and the results pass as sent, a whole reply's results moved into a stream's `message_delta` (`anthropicWholeEvents`), tool-use ids unchanged. A relayed reply carrying `safeguard_results` has no call read out of its text (`anthropicTextCalls`): a call made there would have an id Anthropic never judged, which Claude Code can't match to a verdict, so the text goes as text (xybio on Discord, `TestAutoModeReviewedReplyMakesNoCall`). To any other model they are taken off (`serverReview`; a translation never carries them), and magpie never answers a result on Anthropic's behalf. Claude Code then classifies locally and names the gateway in a notice; the wiring turns the review off in its settings while no model it can reach through magpie is reviewed (`claudeReviewEnv`, [agent wiring](agent-wiring.md)), which is the page's remedy for a gateway that can't run the checks (1EFLOPS on X: Codex's model behind magpie). Reviewed there means `AutoModeReviewable`: a reviewed model on a route that reaches the Messages API, since a Claude model asked on a relay's Chat or Responses URL is translated and never carries the review (nexnull on Discord, `TestAutoModeReviewableAsksOnMessages`).
- Locally, Claude Code's classifier is a request of its own on the session's model: no tools, a system prompt asking for `<block>yes</block>` or `<block>no</block>`, the transcript in `<transcript>`, a small `max_tokens`, and no stream; Claude Code 2.1.310 waits 60 seconds an attempt for it. To a model that isn't Claude it goes with `classifierRoom` (2048) added to a limit below it and asked to reason least (`fitAutoModeClassifier`): none, or the lowest of the model's known levels. A model whose levels magpie doesn't know (a relay's own name, gpt6.1-sol) is asked for none too, as magpie's own ask (`Request.OwnEffort`): left to the vendor, a reasoning model reasoned at its default past the 60 seconds and every classifier request ended 499 (CyberCaius on Discord). Its 400 to that effort (`ownEffortRefused`) is asked again at low when it named the level, and without the effort when it named the field ("Unrecognized request argument supplied: reasoning_effort"), remembered in `unfit` until magpie restarts (`TestAutoModeClassifierReasonsLeastOnAnUnknownModel`, `TestAutoModeClassifierEffortRefusedGoesWithout`).
- Claude Code's billing line (`x-anthropic-billing-header: cc_version=…; cc_entrypoint=…; cch=…;`, its own first system block, `cch` changing every turn) goes on only to a Claude model asked on Anthropic's Messages API (`keepsBillingLine` in [`billing_line.go`](../../internal/gateway/billing_line.go): Anthropic's, a relay's, Bedrock's), as Claude Code sent it. Anywhere else it is left out, the rest of the system as sent (`withoutBillingLine`): a vendor's model relayed on Messages (`passthrough`), every translation to Chat, Responses or Gemini (`forwardTranslated`, Claude models too). Kept, a vendor read it as the prompt's first words and its prefix cache missed from byte 0 every turn (#1600, justrun1983: 4% read from DeepSeek's cache behind Command Code's plan). The Claude subscription's run sends its own (`withoutBillingHeader`), another magpie decides for the provider it picks, and Code Assist (Antigravity, Gemini CLI) is sent it as before, which `antigravityTurnsAway` reads (#666). Once Antigravity has turned such a request away, for `agForeseeing` (6 hours) after, a candidate loop with someone left after an Antigravity account skips it for a request that carries the line, without asking it (`antigravityForeseen`, #2156: each Claude Desktop turn waited about a second on the same refusal before the next member). The skip goes in `Fallback` with the hint, nothing rests, and the agent is told the refusal if nobody after it answers but with a quota. The last one left is still asked, and an old refusal foresees nothing, so a block Google lifts is noticed (`TestAntigravityTurnedAwayIsForeseen`). Codex's own identity line for its GPT-5 models ("You are Codex, an agent based on GPT-5", "…a coding agent based on GPT-5", any case) is turned away the same way (`antigravityTurnsCodexAway`): Codex sends its model's prompt as the thread's first developer message, which goes in as the system instruction, and a thread begun on a GPT model keeps it after `/model`, `resume -m` or a fork as an agent's thread, so such a thread on an Antigravity model 429'd until Codex's retry limit while dsh on the same account answered (Jodiezzzzzz on X, 10-27). It gets its own hint (`antigravityCodexHint`: start a new chat on the model, which is sent magpie's own Codex prompt "You are Codex, based on GPT-5.", which Antigravity answers), chosen by `antigravityHint`. Codex sends its `web_search` tool every turn, so an Antigravity model's Codex turn goes through magpie's own search (`searchReply`); its rounds are wrapped in `antigravityRound`, which tells the 429 (or the reply's error event) the same way and marks the attempt, where before the search path returned the bare 429 that Codex retried to its limit.
- A request translated to a Chat or Responses upstream that carries no `prompt_cache_key` of the client's (every Anthropic client's) gets one for its conversation when the provider is OpenAI's API with a key or one the user added by its address (`ownCacheKey`, `cacheKeyOf` in [`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go)): a hash, in a UUID's shape, of the session the client names, else of its first user message, so every turn of it has the same key and nothing in it names the client or magpie. A relay can turn a request away without one ("prompt_cache_key is required", CyberCaius's AnyRouter). A vendor's preset, a signed-in account, a plugin, Bedrock and Vertex get none, nor does a provider whose extraBody names `prompt_cache_key` (the user's value is sent). An upstream whose 400 names the field is asked again without it and not sent it again; another 400 is not asked again for it, so an unrelated refusal is tried as often as before.
- Claude Desktop's check that a gateway model answers (`{"max_tokens":1,"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"."}]}`, sent as it starts and by its settings' Test connection on the shortest id it is listed with haiku in it, else sonnet, else opus, else the first model listed; `desktopProbe` in [`desktop.go`](../../internal/gateway/desktop.go)) goes to the model the user chose in one of Desktop's own model menus that magpie still serves (`desktopChosen`: `__model_selector_state` in Claude-3p's `local-agent-mode-sessions/<account>/<org>/cowork_account_settings.json`, in the order Code, Cowork, Chat; cybercaius's DeepSeek in the Code tab, v0.1.2033), unless a session's turn was sent after that file was last written: then the model that turn was for (`desktopPicked`), while magpie serves it, the newer of the two being what the user is on now (before, the last session's model came first whenever any menu still held it, so the Code tab switched off DeepSeek with Cowork left on it kept the check on DeepSeek, cybercaius on Discord, v0.1.2212), else to Desktop's default model, the first one listed to it (`desktopDefault`; Desktop's own settings call the first listed model the default), never to the haiku it names, which is another provider's quota, nor, with no menu chosen, to the model the last session ran on (`desktopPicked`), which can be days old: v0.1.1821 sent it there, a relay's GPT model CyberCaius had left for his routing group, and the relay turned it away (Discord, v0.1.1838). It is asked for 16 tokens upstream (`desktopProbeTokens`): OpenAI's models refuse fewer, and a relay in front of one (AnyRouter's "bad response status code 400") passes on no floor for `tokenFloor` to ask again at. A relay that needs `prompt_cache_key` gets one (above). It is recorded with kind `connection_check` (`KindDesktopCheck`), which the Routing page tags "Connection check" with why its model is the id Desktop named and not the one chosen in the conversation: a list with no Claude-named id made the row read "requested deepseek-flash" while the menu's gpt-6.1-sol answered (cybercaius on Discord, v0.1.2212). `TestClaudeDesktopProbeToDefaultModel`, `TestClaudeDesktopProbeToChosenModel`. `TestClaudeDesktopProbeAsksNoClassifier` checks the kind.
- Claude Desktop's `/v1/models` tags a model with `anthropic_family_tier` only when the user picked it for that tier (`DesktopTiers`, the Agents page's opus, sonnet, haiku and fable). Before, a Claude model magpie served was tagged with its own tier unpicked, so Desktop's Code tab told Claude Code to run its haiku calls (its small, fast model) on whichever provider served a `claude-haiku-…`: a chat on AgentRouter's gpt-6.1-sol sent them to Leee's `claude-haiku-5-5` (cybercaius on Discord). Untagged, Claude Code asks for its own id of the tier. A background call (no tools) or a subagent's turn asking for a Claude model by Claude's own id that is no catalog id (`claudeOwnID`) stays on the chat's provider (`desktopPicked`), even when another provider serves a model of that name: `servedBeside` gives that provider's model of that name, else its newest model of the tier, beside the chat's model first, else the call goes to the chat's model. The provider's Claude models stand in only when the chat itself is on one of them: a chat on Leee's DeepSeek had Claude Code's sonnet calls sent to Leee's `claude-sonnet-5-5`, which its key may not use (403, CyberCaius on Discord), so Claude Code's own calls in a chat on another vendor's model go to the chat's model, and a tier the user picked takes Claude Code's own id of it even where a provider lists a model by that name (`TestClaudeDesktopNonClaudeChatKeepsTierCalls`). A lead's turn and an id the user picked for a tier (a catalog id) go as asked. `TestClaudeDesktopBackgroundStaysOnChatProvider`.
- While a model is picked for one of Claude Desktop's tiers, magpie's Desktop profile also lists Desktop's models in `inferenceModels` (`DesktopInferenceModels` in [`desktop_list.go`](../../internal/gateway/desktop_list.go)), so a pick that isn't a Claude model runs in the Code tab with the prompt of its tier's newest Claude model (`behavesAs`: `claude-opus-5-5`, `claude-sonnet-5-5`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, `claude-fable-5-1`, Desktop's catalog v3739), not Claude Code's generic one (moer2831 on Discord). Desktop 4.29 and later hand Claude Code `behavesAs` only from that list (z3t in its app.asar, 4.4212: `modelPicker.options` with each entry's `behavesAs`); the gateway's `behaves_as` row field feeds Desktop's own effort profile only. A non-empty list replaces `/v1/models` in Desktop's menus (pzn: a discovered row gives only its name and description, by id), so the list is every row `/v1/models` gives Desktop, by its id and in its order, each with what Desktop would have read off the row: `supports1m` for a window of 1M and up, `prefer1m` (and the row's `display_name` as `labelOverride`, where Desktop names the row by display_name) for a model listed by its `[1m]` id, and a pick's `anthropicFamilyTier` with `isFamilyDefault`. A Claude model picked runs as itself. Nothing picked (or a pick of a model no longer listed): no list, and Desktop finds the models as before. Picks that can't be read are unknown, and what is written stays. Older builds take the entries' names and drop the rest (2.7032's entry is a zod object, which strips unknown keys). Desktop reads its profile only as it starts, so the tiers' menu says to restart it, and the Agents row's Restart shows (`AppBehind` reads the profile). `TestDesktopInferenceModels`.
- Antigravity answers a system instruction holding one of a few lines with a 429 "Resource has been exhausted" whatever quota is left, on every model (`agTurnedAway` in [`codeassist.go`](../../internal/gateway/codeassist.go)): Claude Code's billing line, the Claude Agent SDK's identity line, Codex's identity lines for its GPT-5 models (which a Codex thread begun on a GPT model keeps after a switch), and Hermes Agent's earlier default persona. An Antigravity account's system instruction goes with a zero-width space after each such line's first letter (`antigravityCloak`, in `codeAssistBody`), which Google lets through and the model reads the same; every other provider, Gemini CLI's sign-in on the same envelope included, is sent the prompt as written. `magpie provider set antigravity prompt=as-written` (`Provider.PromptAsWritten`) turns it off. The refusal is still told against the system prompt as the agent wrote it (`antigravityTurnedAway`): a 400 with the hint for that prompt, the account not rested, and a group skipping Antigravity for that prompt for 6 hours after a refusal (`antigravityForeseen`).
- Claude Code's advisor (`/advisor`, `--advisor`) is Anthropic's server tool `advisor_20260301`, its `model` named as Claude Code names its own: on magpie, by magpie's id (the tier an alias like `opus` is on), which no vendor knows, so the request was turned away whole (JackChou on Discord). Relayed on Messages (`passthrough`), the advisor's model goes as the upstream's own name for it when it is the same provider's Claude model, or a Claude id naming no provider (`advisorTools` in [`advisor.go`](../../internal/gateway/advisor.go)); one it can't run there (another provider's, a group, not Claude's) is left out, as is every advisor when the request's own model isn't a Claude model (a vendor's Messages API knows no such tool), and the turn is answered without one. A translation drops it with every other server tool. Claude Code (2.1.300) also puts the advisor in the conversation: a `{role: "system"}` message whose text ends in its instructions ("# Advisor Tool … call advisor BEFORE substantive work") and a `tool_addition` naming it. Where the advisor is left out, that `tool_addition` goes too (and a message it leaves empty), and the instructions become Claude Code's own "The advisor tool is no longer available; disregard the earlier advisor instructions." (`withoutAdvisor` on Messages, `advisorGoneIn` in `parseAnthropic` for a translation): left there, the model called `advisor()` as one of the agent's tools and Claude Code answered "No such tool available: advisor" (JackChou on Discord). The Claude subscription's run declares only magpie's tools, so it never has one. `TestAdvisorModelAsTheUpstreamNamesIt`, `TestAdvisorLeftOutOnTranslation`.
- Kiro is told where to cache the prompt (plugins#110: a Kiro subscription read 0 from its cache). Its API takes Bedrock's `cachePoint: {"type":"default"}` on a `userInputMessage`, an `assistantResponseMessage` or as a tool entry. The built-in (`buildKiro` in [`kiro.go`](../../internal/gateway/kiro.go)) marks the first message when there is a system prompt, which it folds into that message, and the current message, as `buildAnthropic` marks Anthropic's. A moved Kiro's plugin (opencode-kiro-auth 0.1.10, the mover's `min`) turns each `cache_control` in the Anthropic body it is handed into one, at most 4, the last kept.
- The cache marks `buildAnthropic` adds for a Responses client (Codex) to a Claude model last an hour (`addedMark`, `Request.hourMarks`; 7eleven on Discord): Codex's turns are often more than five minutes apart, and Codex's own vendor keeps a thread cached an hour (`chatGPTCold`). A 1h write costs 2x the input price where Anthropic's default 5m costs 1.25x and a read 0.1x or less, so one turn read after a 5 to 60 minute pause, which a 5m mark would have written again, pays for the dearer writes of dozens of turns in quick succession. Every mark it adds has the same ttl, since Anthropic refuses a 1h mark after a 5m one. The client's `prompt_cache_retention: "in_memory"` keeps them at 5m. A Chat client's marks, a model that isn't Claude's, and marks a client placed itself (an Anthropic client's, relayed as sent) are unchanged. An upstream that answers 400 naming `cache_control` and `ttl` (a relay that knows no ttl) is asked again at 5m and remembered in `unfit` (`hourMarksRefused`) until magpie restarts. A Claude subscription is served by Claude Code, which already asks 1h ([Claude subscription bridge](claude-subscription-bridge.md)). `TestResponsesToClaudeMarksLastAnHour`, `TestHourMarksRefused`.
- A model that refuses a conversation ending with the assistant's message (`refusesPrefill`: OpenCode Go's Claude models over Messages, "This model does not support assistant message prefill"; Volcengine's Agent Plan, "…for a model that does not support prefill"; Gemini's, "Requests ending with a model turn are not supported") is marked for that provider, model and API (`prefillRefused`, #1447); the provider's other models keep their prefill ([`prefill.go`](../../internal/gateway/prefill.go)). A Chat or Responses client's conversation ending with the assistant's message, which those APIs read as a turn already said (`Request.LastIsTurn`), is asked again with a user turn after it (`continueText`), translated (`forwardTranslated`) or relayed (`passthrough`, `userLastBody`), and so from then on. An Anthropic client's prefill is its own ask and gets the refusal as sent. magpie's own continuation of a cut reply (`continuation.go`) isn't asked of that model again; the try the model refused ends the reply with what cut it, not with the refusal. A request magpie builds in Gemini's own shape (`buildCodeAssistSent`: Vertex AI, Gemini CLI's and Antigravity's Code Assist, Factory) never ends with the model's turn, which all of them turn away: a user turn goes after it, `continueText`, or `compactLastText` when an agent is compacting with its ask in the system prompt (`modelLastAsk`), as one goes before a history that opens with the model's. Nor does it send a user turn that mixes a tool's `functionResponse` with text, which Vertex AI answers with the same "ending with a model turn" (#1533: Codex's compaction prompt after a tool's output): what the user said goes in a user turn of its own after the tools' responses (`hasResponse`), except for a Claude model on Antigravity, whose tool_result turn holds both.
- A tool's `strict` that an upstream turns away (`toolStrictRefusal` in [`strict_refused.go`](../../internal/gateway/strict_refused.go): "Unsupported Chat Completions API field: tools[0].function.strict", OpenAI-style unknown_parameter, "Extra inputs are not permitted" and pydantic's extra_forbidden on it) is asked once more without it, in Chat's `tools[].function` and Responses' `tools[]` alike, and marked for that provider and API (`toolStrictRefused`): its next requests go without it at once. OpenCode 2 sends `"strict": false` on every tool through `@ai-sdk/openai-compatible`, and a custom provider that refused it ended the session (#1980). An upstream that hasn't refused it still gets it, as the ChatGPT backend needs it (#383). `TestToolStrictLeftOutWhereRefused` (real OpenCode 2.0.25 requests), `TestToolStrictRefusalWordings`.
- A history call to a tool the request doesn't declare, which an agent that loads its tools on demand sends (pi's and T3 Code's `tool_search`: the tools a search found are called in the history but not declared in the next request's `tools`, and pi sends `"tools": []` with no tools left; Gearclickist on Discord), goes as sent where it is taken: OpenAI's Chat and Responses, the ChatGPT backend and Gemini take it, and Anthropic takes a call to a tool it isn't given ([`undeclared_tools.go`](../../internal/gateway/undeclared_tools.go)). Anthropic refuses calls with no tools at all ("Requests which include `tool_use` or `tool_result` blocks must define tools"), so `anthropicBody` declares a stub for each tool the history called when the request has none (description "Called earlier in this conversation; not available now.", any object as its input) with `tool_choice` `none`, as the Kiro plugin declares its history's tools for Kiro. An upstream that turns the request away naming such a tool as not found, unknown or not defined, or that says tools must be defined beside the calls (`refusesHistoryTools`: a relay in front of Kiro or Bedrock, a vendor checking each name), is asked once more with a stub declared for each tool the history called and `tools` lacks (`withHistoryTools`, Chat, Responses and Messages bodies, relayed or translated), `tool_choice` `none` only where the client offered no tools, and marked for that provider and API (`historyToolsRefused`): its next requests carry the stubs at once. A Responses call under a namespace is the namespace's, and a request whose calls are all declared goes as it was. `TestUndeclaredHistoryToolRefusedThenStubbed`, `TestToollessHistoryToAnthropicDeclaresStubs`, `TestToollessHistoryRefusedOnChat`, `TestUndeclaredHistoryToolTakenAsSent`, `TestRefusesHistoryToolsMatch`.
- A Codex custom tool (exec, apply_patch) that a Responses upstream turns away (`customToolRefusal` in [`custom_tool.go`](../../internal/gateway/custom_tool.go): DeepSeek's 400 "Unsupported custom tool: 'exec'. Only 'apply_patch' is supported.", #2656; xAI's 422 "tools[N].type: unknown variant `custom`, expected one of …", Rust's own wording of the type, #3392) ends the relay: `passthrough` marks the model (`customToolRefused`) and returns not done, and `attempt` translates the request on the same API (`ownAPI`), where each custom tool is offered as a function taking its `input` and a call comes back as the `custom_tool_call` Codex runs, its name kept. A marked model's next requests with a custom tool are translated at once; one without stays relayed, and an upstream that never refused them still gets them as sent. A compaction (`keepsCompaction`) isn't translated, and gets the refusal. Where the refusal reaches a group untranslated it is a shape refusal (`shapeRefused`): the next member is asked. `TestCustomToolTranslatedWhereRefused`, `TestCustomToolRefusalInGroup`, `TestCustomToolRefusalWordings`.
- xAI's own API (api.x.ai, wherever a Grok model is served — the Grok subscription's plugin shapes its own bodies, provider's `grokShaped`) takes none of three things a client's wire puts in wrong (`xaiBody` in [`xai_body.go`](../../internal/gateway/xai_body.go), shaped in `passthrough` and on the built body in `forwardTranslated` alike, for a custom-tool refusal puts a Codex request on the translated path from the next turn on; there is nothing to learn, xAI takes none of them at all, #3392's follow-up): Codex hands a sealed reasoning item back with `"content": null` beside its `encrypted_content`, which the backend answers "Could not decode the compaction blob. Ensure it is unmodified from the compact response." (the first turn goes through, every one after it 400s) — the null goes, the item otherwise as it came; and Claude Code, its messages carrying a system turn mid-conversation since Anthropic made them GA, sends one that xAI's Messages answers "Invalid request content: Invalid message role." — each folds into the top-level `system`, its text and blocks (a `cache_control` kept) as they came, the only place that API takes the role; and a tool's schema can say `"required": null` where none are (Claude Code's own tools, an MCP server's among them — Anthropic's API takes the null), which xAI's validation of each schema answers "Invalid request content: Schema validation failed: [standard_violation] /required: null is not of type \"array\"" (the claude calls 400ed once the system turns folded) — the key goes from every shape a body leaves magpie in, `input_schema`, `parameters` and `function`'s, at the schema's root or nested, a real array of names kept as it came. `TestXAITakesCodexsSealedReasoningBack`, `TestXAILearnsCustomToolsThenTakesTheReplay`, `TestXAIFoldsClaudesSystemTurn`, `TestXAITakesANullRequiredToolSchema`, `TestXaiBodyDropsNullRequiredInEveryShape`.
- A tool call's arguments reach the client as the vendor writes them, translated too (echo_ts on Discord: Codex on Copilot about 10x slower, an apply_patch shown only once whole). The Responses decoder hands each `function_call_arguments.delta` and `custom_tool_call_input.delta` on as a `KToolArgs` delta (`argsDelta`), holding back only the one that would make the JSON whole until `.done`, which `endCall` checks against the final arguments; the Responses encoder streams a custom tool's input as `custom_tool_call_input.delta` (`customStream` in [`custom_tool.go`](../../internal/gateway/custom_tool.go)) before its `.done`. A Copilot account's Responses request is relayed with a `web_search` tool in it (`searchesItself`; checked on a real account 2026-10-17), and Copilot is never picked to search for another provider's model (`searchRank`). It is translated instead when a tool marks a parameter `"encrypted": true` (`asksSealing`, MultiAgentV2's `spawn_agent`): Copilot seals such an argument and can't read its own seal on the subagent's turn. `TestCallArgsStreamWhenTranslated`, `TestCustomInputStreams`, `TestResponsesCallArgsStream`, `TestCopilotRelayedUnlessAskedToSeal`, `TestCopilotUltraSubagentWithWebSearch`.
- A relayed Responses stream gives each output item the one id its `response.output_item.added` gave it, in every event after that carries an `item_id` or an `item`, and in the `output` of `response.completed`, `response.incomplete` or `response.failed` (`idTidy` in [`itemids.go`](../../internal/gateway/itemids.go), applied in `passthrough` before `phaseTidy`). GitHub Copilot's `/responses` gives an item another id in every event (checked 2026-10-19 on a real account, gpt-5.5 with web_search), and Codex starts an item by the added's id and ends one by the done's, so a commentary message was shown twice and a web search never ended (echo_ts on Discord, `TestCopilotItemKeepsItsID`). An upstream whose ids agree goes through byte for byte (`TestItemIDsThatAgreeGoAsTheyCame`); a translated reply's ids are magpie's own.
- A model's reply limit is published within the window beside it (#1438, `provider.OutputWithin`, `Entry.PublishedOutput`): `/v1/models`' `max_output_tokens` (`modelObject`), Cursor Private Inference's `capabilities.max_output_tokens` (`cursorLocalModel`), Muse Code's `limit.output` (`museModels`), the GUI's model and group rows (`providerInfo`, `providersState`), `magpie model output`, and every agent's file (`agent.maxTokens`, #338). The window and the output can come from different sources: Grok's backend lists grok-4.7's 256000 window, and models.dev fills its 500000 output (`catalog.Decorate` and `Provider.replyLimit` fill an output only where the vendor gives none). An unknown window or output is published as it is. The catalog keeps the unclamped `Entry.Output`, and a group's member is still asked for no more than that (`outputLimit`, `withMaxOutput`), so a request isn't lowered by the clamp. A limit the user set stays in their settings as they set it (`TestModelListsKeepOutputWithinTheWindow`). Outside a group, only a Max output the user set for the model (`Entry.OutputSet`) lowers a request (`userOutput`): Claude Code asks a claude-* model for its own full limit (128000 on Opus 5.5) and is told no figure of magpie's for it (`claudeOutput`), and a key whose quota is held per request as max_tokens times the price turned every turn away (YaCo on Discord, `TestUserMaxOutputLowersAPlainProvidersRequest`). A vendor's or models.dev's figure doesn't lower it, since it may be stale.
- `/v1/models` and `/v1/models/{id}` give each model magpie knows a price of its `pricing` in OpenRouter's shape, USD a token as decimal strings: `prompt` and `completion` always (a price set at 0 included), `input_cache_read` and `input_cache_write` only when that part has a price (`catalog.OpenRouterPricing`, `catalog.PerToken` in [`openrouter.go`](../../internal/catalog/openrouter.go), which an agent plugin's `connect` is given too; cleary5779 on Discord, an agent showing what a call costs). The price is the one the Usage page counts the model at (`provider.EntryPricer`, as `EntryPriceIn`: the user's own, else the provider's list price or its maker's at its price rate; a subscription's model at its list price; a group only when every member costs the same), the base price only (no `Tiers`, no 1-hour write). A model no price is known for has no `pricing` key, never a 0. It is told to the callers `magpie_price` is (`local` or `sharedWith`), whether they ask or not. The list reads the settings, groups and providers once (`EntryPricer`), and `catalog.PricedBy` and `AtEndpoint` look a model up in indexes built once a catalog load (`priceIndexOf`, `endpoints`), not by walking every models.dev model a look-up: a 755-model list went from about 38ms to 18ms with pricing on, against about 10ms without, at load 65 (`TestModelsListPricing`, `TestPerToken`). Claude Desktop's, Claude Science's, Muse Code's, Codex's and Ollama's lists, the decision, image and video models carry no `pricing`: their clients read a shape of their own, and an image's price isn't a token's.
- `/v1/models` and `/v1/models/{id}` say each chat model takes tools, a routing group included (`toolsTold`): models.dev's `tool_call: true`, beside its `reasoning`, and OpenRouter's `supported_parameters` (`tools`, `tool_choice`, and `reasoning` when it thinks), which Kilo's and Cline's lists are read by. A client that judges a model by what its list says found nothing for a group, whose id is the user's own name (psikent on Discord: an SSH client), and gave it no tools. magpie has no model that takes none (a call written as text is read back as one, `textcalls.go`), so every member of a group takes them and the group says so. No `capabilities` key is added: Cursor Private Inference reads it as an object (`cursorLocalModel`), Ollama and LM Studio as arrays of their own words, and no reader of an OpenAI-shaped list agrees on one (`TestGroupListedTakesTools`). A group's window and output stay its largest member's, its images any member's (#712, #756): the gateway sends a longer conversation, a longer reply or an image only to the members that hold it (`roomy`, `withRoom`, `withMaxOutput`), and the editor's Context hint names the member a longer conversation skips when the windows differ (windy on Discord).
- A request that names no reply limit, translated to Anthropic's Messages API, which needs `max_tokens` (`forwardTranslated`), is sent the model's published limit (`askedOutput` in [`maxoutput.go`](../../internal/gateway/maxoutput.go)): the user's Max output, else the vendor's list or models.dev, within the window. `anthropicBody`'s `defaultMaxTokens` (16384) is left only for a model whose limit isn't known. Every Codex turn on a Claude went at 16384 before, and a long thinking and tool turn ran out of it: `stop_reason: max_tokens` reaches a Responses client as `incomplete_details.reason: "max_output_tokens"` (#1792, tensornull). A limit the client names goes as it is. The thinking budget fits under the filled limit as under one the client named (`TestAnthropicMaxTokensIsTheModels`).
- A Chat Completions request's reply length goes under the name the vendor reads (`chatTokenField`, `retryTokenField` in [`token_field.go`](../../internal/gateway/token_field.go), on every request through `forwardOnce`): `max_completion_tokens` on OpenAI's own API (`provider.OpenAIHost`), whose newer models answer max_tokens with 400 "Use 'max_completion_tokens' instead" (GoxxKintBlade on X), and on Azure and Bedrock (`asCompletionTokens`); `max_tokens` on DeepSeek (`deepseekTokenField`); as the client sent it elsewhere, since Zhipu and most relays read `max_tokens` alone. A 400 whose words name the other field as the one to use (`provider.TokenFieldWanted`; a limit on the value is not one) is asked once more with that name, the key renamed in place (`provider.WithTokenField`), and the provider remembered in `unfit`, so its next request goes so at once (`TestChatTokenFieldRetriedWhenNamed`). A model's test does the same (`tinyBody`, `probe`; `TestProbeRetriesTokenFieldNamed`).
- A restart to update on a Mac or Linux hands the listening socket to the
  new version ([GUI shell](gui-shell.md#a-restart-to-update-hands-the-gateway-over),
  [`handover.go`](../../internal/gateway/handover.go)). `Inherit` gives the
  new process's `ListenAndServe` a socket to serve when the address is the
  same (`sameAddr`), and calls back once it serves; `HandOff` has the old
  one stop accepting at once (`stopAccepting`: its copy of the socket goes,
  the new one's keeps it open) and its shutdown wait up to `handedOffDrain`
  (15 minutes) for its requests in flight instead of `shortDrain` (2s).
  Before that shutdown, each connection it took sends its first request
  (`unreadConns`, `unreadWait` 5s at most): `http.Server.Shutdown` closes
  one whose request it reads after it began unanswered (#3568). Requests
  per minute, concurrency and sticky routing's in-memory state are each
  process's own: sticky routing is flushed to disk before the socket goes
  (`FlushSticks`), but while the old one drains, a provider's limits can be
  passed by what each has in flight.
  `TestHandedOverGatewayFinishesItsStream`,
  `TestHandedOffGatewayTakesNoNewConnection`,
  `TestHandedOffGatewayAnswersAConnectionItTook`,
  `TestHandedOffGatewayAnswersAConnectionNotedLate`,
  `TestInheritedForAnotherAddressIsLetGo`, `TestToolCallOfTheMagpieBeforeGoesToIt` in
  [`handover_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/handover_test.go).
- An agent's turn (Chat, Responses, Messages, Gemini's generateContent and
  Codex's backend `/responses`) that magpie holds before any route is begun
  for it is watched in `counted` ([`unrouted.go`](../../internal/gateway/unrouted.go);
  heyusaku6 on X: Codex thought for over ten minutes with the Routing page
  empty, until Codex and magpie were restarted; what held it wasn't
  found). After `unroutedShow` (15s) a route of provider `magpie` shows it
  on the Routing page as routing, and gives way to the request's own route
  once one is begun (`noteRoute`, `trace.drop`). While the request is
  masked its phase is `redact` (`phaseRedact`, `noteRedacting` in
  [`redact_phase.go`](../../internal/gateway/redact_phase.go), around
  every `redactedHits` and `maskTextHits`), on `/v1/magpie/concurrency`
  and on the route that shows it (`Route.Phase`), so the row says
  masking, not routing (fremontdango on Discord: a Claude Code request
  sat as "routing…" while it was masked). What masking took is kept
  as a part of magpie's own time, never more than it (`maskingMs`):
  `Route.Masking` and the usage record's `masking_ms`, shown as
  *Masking* in the Routing page's time and the Usage page's detail. It
  holds no text. `TestMaskingIsAPhaseWithItsTime`,
  `TestNoMaskingNoMaskingTime` in
  [`redact_phase_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/redact_phase_test.go),
  `routing-masking.test.cjs`. It is answered 504 after
  `unroutedUnknown` (30s) held in no wait magpie knows of, or after
  `unroutedMost` (2m) excluding masking and earned upload time
  (sharwowwow on X: two minutes of spinning before a 504). An upload
  earns one second per 32 KiB actually read from the wire, capped at the
  time spent in body reads and eight additional minutes
  (`unmaskedLocked`, `unroutedUploadRate`, `unroutedUploadGrace`). Thus a
  non-masking hold ends within ten minutes even for compressed input
  with negligible decoded output. Wire bytes are clamped before duration
  arithmetic so large encoded streams cannot overflow the allowance. A
  large conversation on a slow link can finish past two minutes (#4775);
  a trickle still exhausts the base budget. Declared length and decoded
  bytes earn nothing, and a fast upload cannot bank time for a later
  internal hold. The 60s wire-idle deadline and decoded size cap (512 MiB, more on
  Codex's own path: `codexBodyLimit`) still apply ([`request_bounds.go`](../../internal/gateway/request_bounds.go)).
  An upload that exhausts its allowance is answered 408 with its received
  byte count and advice to check the link or proxy, compact the conversation
  or reduce inline images. An internal hold remains a 504; waiting for
  body room is internal, including before the first read (#4511).
  Between two reads a body is still arriving while less has come than
  its Content-Length says or, chunked, until a read has met its end
  (`bodyArriving`, `bodyEnded`): a decoder spends its time between reads
  too, and a chunked gzip body ended there was answered 504 as magpie's
  own hold (`TestUnroutedChunkedBodyReadWholeHeld504` holds the other
  side). While an upload earns as fast as it is read, its charged time
  stands still, so `checkMost` looks again no sooner than
  `unroutedRecheck` (1s) rather than every sliver left
  (`TestUnroutedMostRecheckIsNoSpin`).
  `TestUnroutedProgressingUploadOutlastsMost` covers TLS through `Front`
  with declared, chunked, gzip and zstd bodies;
  `TestUnroutedUploadCreditKeepsIdleDeadline`, `TestUnroutedUploadTimeIsBounded`,
  `TestUnroutedUploadAllowanceHasCeiling`, `TestUnroutedFastUploadBanksNothing`,
  `TestUnroutedEmptyEncodedStreamIsBounded` (empty gzip members, zstd
  skippable frames)
  and `TestUnroutedTrickledBodyAnswered408` cover the remaining bounds.
  Masking's time, its wait for the local model's
  turn with it, counts toward neither (`masked`, `checkMost`): the model
  is bounded per piece it reads (`classifyTimeout`) and per load, and a
  480 KB request it took 90s over on an RX 470/580, behind the requests
  ahead of it, was answered 504 at 2m every time it was sent (#3682). The known waits are counted as they happen and their
  time added up (`noteWait`, `waited`): the request's body arriving
  (`waitedBody`, every read of `r.Body`), masking (`noteMasking`, from
  `noteRedacting`), System
  One's or the decision model's classifier (`askWaited`), an image's
  description (`seenBody`), Codex's auto reset (`autoReset`), plugins'
  gateway hooks (`GatewayBegin`) and JS gateway middleware, and every
  `notePhase` (slot, rpm, upstream, redact), and the body room's queue
  (`bodyRoom.take`, at most `bodyRoomWait`, 100s). Each is named (its
  `kind`), and the time spent in each kind is kept (`waitKind`) for the
  report below. What is left is magpie's own work
  (the catalog, parsing, planning, reading the usage log), ~8.5s at its
  slowest (a cold catalog sync), so 30s gives it room on a loaded machine.
  With no route and nothing written, the error is sent in its client's
  protocol, whole (`Content-Length`, `Connection: close`) so the client
  has it while the handler may still be held; its context ends with
  `errUnrouted`, its route ends with the error, and the log has every
  goroutine's stack (at most once in 10 minutes). The handler's writes go
  through `heldWriter`, and nowhere once magpie has answered for it.
  The desktop app's log goes only to stderr, which its user can't reach
  (Jason on Discord, a Codex request held 2m0s, told to send "magpie's
  log"), so what held it is also saved as a report
  ([`held_report.go`](../../internal/gateway/held_report.go)): taken while
  the request is still held, before its context ends, in `HeldDir`
  (`<cache>/held`, mode 0700), written through a temp file and renamed,
  the newest `heldKept` (5) kept. It has the time, version and system,
  method and path, the model and agent, how long it was held, each kind
  of known wait and its time ("(under way)" for the one it was in), the
  body room's state, the requests in flight and every goroutine's stack
  (function names and lines, no values; 128 KB at most, one dump for a
  burst within 10s). Never the body or headers. Its file name is
  `Route.Held`; the Routing page's story of that request has Copy Report
  (`GET /api/gateway/held/{name}`, only a report's own name is read) and,
  in the app, Open Folder (`POST /api/gateway/held/reveal`). The 504 and
  the log line say where it is. `TestUnroutedHeldReportSaved`,
  `TestHeldReportsKeptAndNamed`, `routing-held-report.test.cjs`.
  `TestUnroutedRequestShownThenAnswered`, `TestUnroutedShownGivesWayToItsRoute`,
  `TestUnroutedUnknownHoldAnsweredSooner`, `TestUnroutedSlowBodyNotCutOff`,
  `TestUnroutedMaskingNotCutOff`, `TestUnroutedSlotWaitNotCutOff`,
  `TestUnroutedMostLeavesOutMasking`, `TestMaskingModelWaitNotCutOffAtMost`.

## A route group held to chosen accounts

A routing group may be held to some accounts of a provider (`Group.Accounts`,
entries `<provider>/<stable id or KeyID fingerprint>` in a gateway key's
form; duanckham on X: a personal and a company GPT account, one tool and one
gateway, and a group's models must not spend the other's). The rule is the
gateway key's (#905, `Identity.AllowsAccount`): a provider the group names is
used only on the accounts it names, one it doesn't name on every account.

- The asked group narrows the request's identity (`heldByGroup` in
  [`key_models.go`](../../internal/gateway/key_models.go), by
  `access.Identity.HeldToGroup`): the strict intersection with the key's own
  list, `<provider>/\x00` (`heldToNone`) when the two share none of a
  provider, and `Identity.Group` set to the group's name. Every gate that holds
  a request to its key's accounts (`accountAllowed`, `allowedCandidates`,
  `onKeys`, `allowedKey`) then holds it to the group's, on every route: chat,
  responses and messages (`serve`), streamed or not, `count_tokens`
  (`heldByGroupAt`), embeddings, images and video.
- A group reached through another one, as a member or as a fallback, is held
  by `planLevel`'s `hold` and `Member.Via` (`groupHeld`): its candidates on an
  account it leaves out are Held rows with `Weighed.HeldBy`, which the Routing
  page's trace tells ("not an account Work may use").
- Fallback stays within the group's own entries: an allowed account that is
  resting, out or refused falls to the next member, never to an account the
  group leaves out. With every allowed account gone the request gets the
  group's usual error; when a key and the group share no account it is 403
  naming the group and the Routing page (`keyAccountsError`,
  `groupsHeldError`), and nothing upstream is asked.
- `provider.SaveGroup` resolves each entry (`cleanGroupAccounts`,
  `provider.ResolveAccount`): an account named by the name it is shown by is
  kept as its stable id, duplicates collapse, and a new unknown provider or
  account is refused. One the group already held that is gone (signed out, a
  key rotated) is kept, matching nothing, so the group stays held rather than
  let off to every account. A shared or
  imported group carries no accounts (`ShareGroup`, `ImportGroup`): they are
  this computer's. An import whose text carries the id of a group of the
  user's own (not a found one) replaces that group when the page asks
  (`ImportGroupOver`, `replace` in `groups/import`; the Import box reads the
  id itself and says "Replace <name>" before it is pressed): its accounts,
  folder rules, switch and place stay. `TestImportGroupOverReplacesSameID`,
  `group-import-replace.test.cjs`.
- The group editor's Accounts section (`drawAccounts` in the routing plugin's
  `client.js`) has a row for each member's provider with more than one account
  (`/api/groups`' `accounts`, `groupAccounts` in
  [`groups.go`](../../internal/gui/groups.go)): "Any of its accounts" until
  some are ticked in its menu (`openPicker`, `multi`). Every save sends the
  group's accounts, since a save replaces the group.

`TestGroupAccountsHoldEveryRoute`, `TestGroupAccountsFallBackWithinTheGroup`,
`TestGroupAccountsAndAKeysAccounts`, `TestGroupAccountsSaved`,
`TestHeldToGroup`, `group-accounts.test.cjs`.

## Remote magpie APIs

`GET /v1/magpie/trace` answers the routing this gateway has decided, for a
magpie on the same computer that does not serve it, whose console plays it as
its own Routing page (#2606). A trace names accounts and their emails, what
they served, session ids, a folder's last two parts and why accounts rest; it
holds no prompt text and no key. So it answers `local(r)` alone: a request
from another address is refused 403, and so is one from loopback that carries
a proxy's forwarding header (a tunnel or Tailscale Serve here), or a LAN
request while the gateway is shared, whatever key it holds. Rebinding,
`Origin: null` and `*.localhost` pages are stopped before it by `lanGuard` and
`corsGuard`, as on every route. A gateway key held to a budget, models or
accounts is refused with `KeyHeldCode`, as `/v1/magpie/concurrency` and
`/v1/magpie/quotas` refuse it. It answers what `Server.Trace` holds, plus
this process's `pid` and `serves` (the command that started it, `Serves`), so
a caller long-polls it with `after` and `wait` the way the page's own
`/api/gateway/trace` does. `/` adds `pid` and `serves` too, to a loopback
request alone. `Server.gatewayTrace` in
[`trace.go`](../../internal/gateway/trace.go), and
`TestGatewayTraceIsLoopbackOnly` in
[`trace_peer_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/trace_peer_test.go) and
`TestHeldKeyToldNoAccounts` in
[`quotas_held_key_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/quotas_held_key_test.go).

A read-only gateway key (`access.Key.ReadOnly`, set by `add-key` or
`access-key`; `magpie gateway-key add <name> --read-only`, `gateway-key
access <id> read-only|full`, or the key's menu in Gateway → Gateway keys)
reads this magpie's stats and uses nothing: a desk display polling
`/v1/magpie/quotas` and `/v1/magpie/usage` keeps such a key in its flash.
`readOnlyGuard` in [`readonly.go`](../../internal/gateway/readonly.go) sits
after `callerGuard`, so the key is known from loopback and from elsewhere,
and before `withCaller` and `keyLimited`, so nothing is read, reserved or
sent first. It judges the pattern `ServeMux.Handler` routes the request to
against `readOnlyRoutes` (`GET /`, `/v1`, the quotas, their history and
cards, the usage summary and its requests, the key's limit, the key
page and its data, the concurrency, the route events' snapshot and
stream, and the status line's `/v1/magpie/statusline`) and refuses every other with 403 `gateway_key_read_only`:
every model route, `count_tokens`, the Responses WebSocket's upgrade, every
MCP server, Codex's and Gemini's backends, the catch-all and every write
(`POST /v1/magpie/usage/requests`, the quotas' refresh, a Codex reset). A route added later
is refused it until it is put in that list. A read-only key is held to
nothing else, so `heldKey` leaves it alone and it reads the usage summary
and every account's quotas as a key held to nothing does; none of those
routes tells a key's secret. The sharing key can't be read-only: magpie
gives it to its own agents (`LANSecret`). A magpie from before this field
reads such a key as a full one. `TestReadOnlyKeyEveryRoute` in
[`readonly_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/readonly_test.go) fails when a
route the router has is in neither `readOnlyRoutes` nor its refused list,
and sends each refused pattern, by each method it takes, from loopback and
from the network.

`POST /v1/magpie/quotas/codex-reset` (`Server.codexReset` in
[`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go), #3850) spends one of a
Codex account's resets, which can't be undone, so its admission is
stricter than the quotas': a key is required from loopback too. Every
origin exemption `corsGuard` makes without a key (app schemes, `null`,
loopback page names, a request with no Origin) reaches the handler with
an empty `access.Caller(ctx).KeyID`, and is refused 401 there. A
read-only key is refused by `readOnlyGuard` (and again in the handler),
and a held key (`heldKey`) 403 `gateway_key_held` even when it is told
quotas. The account is named in `?user=`, and `codexResetsAsked` lets one
ask per account run at a time (409). It calls `provider.SpendCodexReset`,
the Usage page's own code, then `RefreshUsage` of that card.
`TestCodexResetNeedsAFullKey` in
[`codex_reset_api_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_reset_api_test.go)
sends it without a key from loopback, `Origin: null`, an app scheme, a
`*.localhost` page, a rebound Host and a foreign page, then with a wrong,
read-only and held key, and expects nothing spent until the full key
asks.

A remote provider serves the conversation, token-counting, System One,
embeddings, rerank, speech-to-text, image generation/edit and video
creation/status/content APIs. Gemini clients enter through the local Gemini adapter and follow the
same model routing as other conversation clients. Responses is served
over HTTP/SSE and over a WebSocket ([A Responses
WebSocket](#a-responses-websocket)); magpie's own leg to the ChatGPT
backend is a WebSocket for a streamed turn (see
[Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md)). Discovery and older-peer behavior
are described in [Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md#remote-magpie).
`GET /v1/magpie/limit` (`keyLimit` in
[`key_limit.go`](../../internal/gateway/key_limit.go)) answers the limit of
the gateway key the request carries (`access.Caller`) and of no other: it
reads nothing from the request but its key. For a held key (`heldKey`) it
adds what holds it (`heldTo`), its `models`, `quotas` (`access.Key.Quotas`)
and, with that on, `quotaProviders`, the providers `newQuotaScope` tells it,
so it agrees with the quotas routes and the status line (steven_4422: a key
held to Claude's models read `limited: false` there and 403 on the line).
It names no account. `TestStatuslineHeldKeyScope`. A key may hold up to five
limits at once, each in its own window, and a request needs every one
(#2227, `budget.Reserve`: one reservation per window, and a refusal names
the spent limit that resets last). The saved key keeps the first limit in
`limit`, which older magpie reads, and the rest in `moreLimits`. The answer's
`limit` is the one holding the key most (`budget.Binding`), what older
clients read, and `limits` is each of them (`budget.All`). Holding a
key to its limits is the Key limits plugin's, on the gateway's budget
point ([Plugin runtime](plugin-runtime.md#gateway-extension-points-p3-budget)):
with it off, a key held to a limit gets 503 and nothing is sent. A local magpie
asks it with each of its remote-magpie keys for that key's card on its
Usage page (#1547), a window per limit's cap.

`GET /me` is the key page: a gateway key's holder opens it in a browser at
the address they reach the gateway at, types their key, and sees that key's
own name, limits, usage (today, 7 and 30 days, its most used models) and
the models it may use, with the setup for Claude Code, Codex and an
OpenAI-compatible client whose base URL is the address the page was opened
at (zytszone on X: a department shares one magpie, whose app its people
can't be given). The page itself (`keypage.html`, embedded) is the same
bytes for everyone and holds no data, so `lanGuard` serves exactly `GET`
or `HEAD /me` (`keyPageOpen`: no escaped path) to another machine without a
key, after the allow list, the rebinding check and the not-shared refusal,
which still apply. Its data is `GET /v1/magpie/me` (`keyInfo`), which takes
the key as every route does (`Authorization` or `x-api-key`) and answers
only `access.Caller`'s own: no key, a wrong one, one turned off and
magpie's own token are 401 alike, and `?key=` is refused 400, so a key is
never written into an address or a log. It names no account, no other key,
no provider and no secret; its models are `/v1/models`' for that key
(`keyAllowed`), none for a read-only key. The page loads nothing from
elsewhere, posts no form and can't be framed (its CSP), keeps the key in
the tab's `sessionStorage` only, and sends it to its own origin with
`credentials: "omit"`. Gateway → Gateway keys shows the page's address
(`MAGPIE_PUBLIC_URL`, else the LAN address offered) with a Copy, while
shared and with a key (`gatewayKeyPage` in `keys.js`). `key_page.go`, and
`TestKeyPageTellsAKeyItsOwn`, `TestKeyPageRefusesWithoutItsKey` and
`TestKeyPageOpensWithoutAKey` in
[`key_page_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/key_page_test.go);
`internal/gui/tests/key-page.test.cjs` drives the page itself.

System One uses `RouteDecider` and `postDecide` in
[`decide.go`](../../internal/gateway/decide.go), taking one provider prefix
off at each hop and preserving the state, questions and vendor errors.
A model naming a group of decision models (`provider.Group.Decides`: every
member a decision model; `cleanDecisionGroup` in
[`decide_group.go`](../../internal/provider/decide_group.go) refuses a mixed
one, one inside a chat group, and patterns, rules, effort or fast mode on
it) goes to `serveDecisionGroup` in
[`decide_group.go`](../../internal/gateway/decide_group.go) instead
(`decisionGroupOf`: `group/<id>`, or a group's id or name by `GroupFor`). It
plans the members' keys and accounts with `planGroup` (`perKeyBarred` keeps
a decision-only provider's keys: `DecidesModel`), applies a gateway key's
model and account limits and pause rules as for a chat, and tries each
candidate in turn: a `retryable` failure that is not the request's own
(`shapeRefused`, `promptRefused`, `protectionRefused`, `zdrRefused`) rests it
(`decideFailed` → `restAfter`) and the next is asked; the last one's answer
is passed on. Every try is a `Try` of one route; one usage row is written
for the answer. A group classifier of decision models fails over the same
way (`askDecisionGroup`, `decideRefused` carrying the vendor's status). A
decision group is kept out of the agents' catalog (`groupEntries`), and a
conversation asked of it is refused 400 pointing at `/v1/systemone`.
A single decision provider of several keys or accounts (TypeSafe Jev with
a key out of quota, fflake33 on Discord) is asked the same way, at
`/v1/systemone` (`serveSystemOne`) and as a classifier (`askClassifier` →
`askDecider`): `decideKeys` plans its keys in its routing's order (Smart,
In order), `probeOne` leaves out all but the resting one back soonest, and
`serveDecideTries` / `askDecideTries` ask each in turn as for a group, a
refused one resting. Before, every call went to its first key, whatever the
others' state. As a classifier, every try shares the one classifier's wait
(Settings' Classifier timeout, "its provider's failover included"): once a
try spends it, the rest are neither asked nor rested. Each try was given
the whole wait before, so a request was held a wait per key or member
before its route began (`TestDecisionTriesShareTheClassifierWait`). A gateway key held to some accounts is asked only on those
(`allowedCandidates`), and refused 403 when none of them serves the model.
`TestDecisionProviderKeysFailOver`.
[`embeddings.go`](../../internal/gateway/embeddings.go) relays retrieval
bodies and options (`serveRetrieval`, under `Provider.OpenAIBase`: a
plugin's fetch whatever API its chat models are on), and
[`transcribe.go`](../../internal/gateway/transcribe.go) speech-to-text's
multipart forms through it as sent, rewriting only the model and a masked
prompt under the form's own boundary (#2053), and
[`speech.go`](../../internal/gateway/speech.go) text-to-speech's JSON with
the model rewritten and `input` and `instructions` masked; its answer,
audio or SSE, is copied to the client as it comes (`retrieveOpen`,
`copyAnswer`), never read whole (`TestSpeechStreamsAsItComes`), [`draw.go`](../../internal/gateway/draw.go) sends images
to the remote's images API, and [`video.go`](../../internal/gateway/video.go)
wraps remote task ids for later polls and downloads. `passOnCaller` in
[`remote_magpie.go`](../../internal/gateway/remote_magpie.go) supplies
caller/session headers without replacing the outgoing User-Agent.
Conversation forwarding preserves Claude Code and Codex client headers
(including Codex's `originator`) so relays behind the remote can recognize
them. Decision, retrieval and media requests set magpie's User-Agent at
their own call sites, so the remote accepts their attribution. Vendors
receive no caller labels. The remote names the passed-on agent by one rule
(`callerFrom`, `callerOf`) wherever it shows who sent a request: usage and
sessions, recent calls and the Routing trace, the masking log and the
requests in flight (`counted` runs before `withCaller`, so `active.add` reads
`callerFrom` itself). The label never changes access, limits or behaviour,
which go by the key and the real sender (`agentOf`).

### Images and video in OpenAI's shape

A caller speaks OpenAI's images and videos APIs to the gateway, whichever
vendor draws or films, and gets OpenAI's answers back
([`draw.go`](../../internal/gateway/draw.go),
[`video.go`](../../internal/gateway/video.go)). The `generate_image`,
`generate_video` and `generation_result` tools of Magpie Image call these
same handlers in process (`s.images`, `s.videosCreate`, `s.videosGet`), so
the MCP and an HTTP caller go through one translation and one set of holds.

| Route | Takes | Answers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `POST /v1/images/generations` | JSON: `model`, `prompt`, `n`, `size`, `quality`, `background`, `output_format`, `response_format` | `{"created", "data": [{"b64_json"} or {"url"}]}` |
| `POST /v1/images/edits` | OpenAI's multipart (`image` or `image[]` files, `mask`), or JSON with `image`/`images` (URL, data URL or `{"image_url": …}`) and `mask` | as generations |
| `POST /v1/videos` | JSON or OpenAI's multipart: `model`, `prompt`, `seconds`, `size`, `input_reference` (first frame), plus `last_frame` and `reference_images` | OpenAI's video object, `status: "queued"`, an id that names the provider, the vendor's task and the key or account |
| `GET /v1/videos/{id}` | | the video object: `queued`, `in_progress` (`progress`), `completed` or `failed` (`error`) |
| `GET /v1/videos/{id}/content` | | the video (`video/mp4`, or `video/quicktime` for a MOV: `videoType`), or 409 while it isn't made |
