| `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE /v1/magpie/native/{provider}/{path...}` | the vendor's own request | the vendor's own answer, a stream as it comes (`native.go`, below) |

Each vendor is sent its own request:

| Vendor | Images | Edits | Videos |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| OpenAI-compatible images API (gpt-image, dall-e, a relay) | JSON `/images/generations` | multipart `/images/edits`, mask included; a URL image is downloaded first | at `<base>/videos` only when its list or the user marks a video model (below) |
| ChatGPT account (Codex backend), SenseNova U1 | JSON | JSON `images: [{"image_url"}]`, `mask: {"image_url"}` | none |
| Grok subscription | JSON with `aspect_ratio` | JSON `/images/edits`, `images: [{"url"}]` | `/videos/generations`: `duration`, `aspect_ratio`, `resolution`, `image`, `reference_images` |
| Volcengine Ark (`isArk`) | `/images/generations`, `watermark: false` unless sent, one request per image, ratio as the doc's 2K size, the caller's other fields as sent (`stream: true` is a 400 pointing to the native route) | the same endpoint, images as JSON `image` (URL or data URL); a mask is a 400 that says to describe it in the prompt | Seedance at `/contents/generations/tasks` (below) |
| Gemini at Google, a Google sign-in | `generateContent` asking for images | the images as inline parts | a plugin's Veo through its fetch |
| OpenRouter | `/images` for image-only models, chat with modalities for the others | `input_references` | none |
| DashScope, ModelScope | their own asynchronous image APIs (`draw_dashscope.go`, `draw_modelscope.go`) | their image fields | none |
| MiniMax API key | | | H3 at `/v2/video_generation` (below) |
| Another magpie | its images API | its images API | its videos API, ids wrapped |

Every one of these routes goes through `requestBody` (the body limit,
key and origin checks every `/v1` route has), a gateway key's model and
account holds (`keyHolds`, `accountHolds`, `videoAsker`,
`videoModelHeld`), the user's switched-off models, routing groups in their
order, prompt masking (fails closed), and usage records.

A custom provider (no account, no preset plan's videos) makes videos the
same OpenAI way (#1446, `customVideomakers`), but only with the models its
own fetched list marks `"kind": "video"`, or, with no `kind`, `domain`
`VideoGeneration` or video out without text in `output_modalities`, as
Volcengine Ark's list does (`catalog.LiveVideomakers`), or the
user marks a video model (settings' `ModelKinds`, `provider.SetModelKind`,
a model chip's right-click *Makes…* in the editor, #1399); a name alone
never makes a model a video maker. A plugin's provider makes videos with the
models it says give video, through its fetch on the account that started
each (`keyMark`, `markedKey`; see [provider plugins](provider-plugins.md)). A model marked an image model is drawn
with (`Drawers`) and leaves the video makers, and one marked a video model
leaves the drawers; only a provider set up with a base URL can be marked
(`MarksKinds`). Start, poll and content go to
`videoBase` (its Chat base, else its Responses base) `+ /videos`, with
magpie's User-Agent (or the agent's, `passUserAgent`) and no caller headers. Its ids end with the key's
fingerprint (`customVideoID`, `markedKey`), so a poll and its download go
on the key that started the video. A gateway key's account and model holds
apply to all three routes (`videoAsker`, `videoModelHeld`), and to Grok's
and Volcengine's polls and downloads as well.

Volcengine Ark has no OpenAI-shaped videos API, so no Ark key is asked at
`<base>/videos` (`filmsOpenAI` is false for `isArk`). Its Seedance models go
to Ark's asynchronous content-generation tasks (`arkFilms`, `arkTasks`:
`POST <base>/contents/generations/tasks`, polled at `GET …/tasks/<id>`,
https://www.volcengine.com/docs/82379/1520757): the preset's Agent Plan's
(`PlanVideos`), and any other Ark key's (pay-as-you-go `/api/v3`, or an Ark
endpoint added by hand) whose list or picked models name `seedance`
(`arkVideoModel`, added in `customVideomakers`; axelburks on Discord). Such a
key's video id ends with the key's fingerprint, as a plan key's does, and
its poll and download go on that key (`videoAsker` checks the calling key
may use it). `volcengineVideoBody` turns OpenAI's request into Ark's: the
prompt as a `text` item in `content`, `input_reference` as an `image_url`
item with role `first_frame`, `last_frame` as `last_frame`,
`reference_images` as `reference_image`, `seconds` as `duration` (`-1`,
the model picks, kept), and `size` as the nearest of Ark's tiers by pixels
and ratios (`arkVideoSize`: 3840x2160 is `4k` 16:9, 1470x630 is `720p`
21:9). A `ratio`, `resolution` or `duration` the caller sends itself is
never overridden by `size` or `seconds`, so `ratio: "adaptive"` holds. A
`content` the caller sends is Ark's own and kept whole, its
`reference_video`, `reference_audio` and `draft_task` items included: the
prompt is put first only when it has no `text` item, and OpenAI's image
fields after it. The prompt is optional with an image or a content (Ark's
image-to-video takes none), and an `asset://` image of Ark's asset
library is kept as it is. Those three (no prompt, `asset://`, seconds
`-1`) are Ark's alone: `arkOnly` turns them away with a 400 before any
other vendor is asked. The request's other fields
OpenAI's API doesn't have (Ark's `generate_audio`, `seed`, `camera_fixed`,
`watermark`, `tools`, `return_last_frame`…) go to Ark as sent, masked as
the prompt is. A JSON body's values keep their JSON types; only a
multipart form's are read as JSON (`formValue`), so its `"false"`, `"42"`
or `[{"type":"web_search"}]` goes typed, except Ark's text fields
(`arkTextFields`: `safety_identifier` "12345" stays a string)
(`TestArkVideoTakesLastFrameAndItsOwnFields`, `TestArkVideoFieldTypes`).
Ark's `succeeded`/`running`/`failed`/`expired` and its
`content.video_url` are read back into OpenAI's video object
(`videoObject`), with every other field of Ark's task passed on as it came
(`videoState.Ark`, all but `arkOwnFields`): `content.last_frame_url`,
`usage`, `resolution`, `ratio`, `framespersecond`, `seed`, `tools`,
`output_format` and the rest. Ark's `expired` is a task that ran past its
`execution_expires_after` unfinished, and is said so. `/content` fetches
that URL (`vendorClient`) and serves a MOV as `video/quicktime`
(`videoType` reads the `ftyp` brand). `TestArkKeyMakesSeedanceVideos`,
`TestArkVideoKeepsCallersContent`, `TestArkVideoFromImageNeedsNoPrompt`,
`TestOnlyArkTakesItsOwnVideoAsks`, `TestArkVideoSize`,
`TestArkVideoAnswersItsOwnFields`, `TestVideoType`.

Ark's images get the same: the request's fields magpie doesn't read
(`drawingRead`) are `drawing.Extra`, masked as the prompt is and sent to
Ark as they came (`sequential_image_generation` and its options for a
group of images, `optimize_prompt_options`, `tools`,
`layer_decomposition`, which needs no prompt, and `watermark` over
magpie's `false`). `n` stays OpenAI's: that many requests, each one image
or one group. `response_format: "url"` asks Ark for its URL and answers
it; anything else asks for `b64_json`. `TestArkImagesPassOnTheirOwnFields`.

#### The native route

`/v1/magpie/native/{provider}/{path...}` ([`native.go`](../../internal/gateway/native.go))
passes a request to the provider's own API as it is, and its answer back
as it came, an event stream flushed as it arrives and a large answer
copied through, never held whole (axelburks on Discord): for what the
OpenAI shape can't carry. GET, POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE
(`nativeMethods`) are routed.

- **Which providers.** One reached with an API key (`nativeKeyed`: no
  `Account`, a `Key`, not a remote magpie, not Vertex AI): a vendor's or
  the user's own. A subscription's, a sign-in's or a plugin's account is
  a 403 that says so; an unknown, off or decide-only provider a 404.
- **Where.** The path after the provider, as the caller escaped it, is
  joined to the host of the provider's first base (`nativeProto`: Chat,
  Responses, Anthropic, Gemini), not to the base's path, so a vendor's v2
  paths are reached too (`nativeTarget`). It refuses (400) an empty path,
  a `.` or `..` segment (`%2e%2e` and across `%2F` too), a backslash, a
  leading or encoded leading slash, `://`, a first segment that reads as
  a scheme, and any URL whose host or scheme, parsed back, isn't the
  base's. The mux redirects an unencoded `..` before the route sees it.
- **Headers.** The caller's auth (`Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`,
  `x-api-key`, `api-key`, `x-goog-api-key`, `Cookie`), the browser's and
  a proxy's (`Origin`, `Referer`, `Sec-*`, `X-Forwarded-*`, `Forwarded`,
  `Cf-*`), the hop's own and `X-Magpie-*` are dropped (`nativeDropped`),
  the gateway key's `?key=` too (`nativeQuery`); the rest go as they came.
  `Sign` puts in the provider's key as it takes one, and magpie's
  User-Agent goes in (`stampUA`). The answer's hop headers, `Set-Cookie`,
  `Alt-Svc` and HSTS don't come back (`nativeAnswerDropped`).
- **Keys.** It is behind the gateway's guards as every route is (a key
  beyond loopback, lanGuard's Host check against a rebound page,
  corsGuard: a foreign Origin, `null` and `*.localhost` without a key,
  the key's rate), refused to a read-only key (`readOnlyRefused`), and
  refused (403, `nativeHeld`) to a key held to models, accounts or a
  spend limit, or issued by one: magpie can't tie a path it doesn't read
  to a model, and prices no answer it doesn't read, so the OpenAI routes
  serve such a key. A model the user switched off, named in a JSON body's
  `model`, is refused.
- **Masking.** With Settings › Privacy masking and a provider not set to
  go unmasked, the body is decoded as JSON (numbers exact, `UseNumber`)
  and its strings masked (fails closed); a body that isn't JSON is a 415,
  as nothing unmasked may go. Only a request something was masked in has
  its answer read through `restoring`, which holds a JSON answer whole to
  put the values back; every other answer streams through.
- **Record.** Each request is a Requests row and a usage record
  (`Operation: "native"`), with the tokens a JSON or event-stream answer
  reports (`usageSniffer`, read as the path's protocol, `nativeSniffed`),
  none otherwise.

Ark's images and video tasks at their short paths under the key's base
(`nativeArkPath`: `images/generations` POST, `contents/generations/tasks`
POST and GET, `contents/generations/tasks/<id>` GET and DELETE) are served
as they were first (`nativeArk`): the body must be a JSON object, it is
re-encoded after masking, and only `Content-Type` and `Accept` go on.
`TestNativeArkRoute`, `TestNativeArkStreamsAsItComes`,
`TestNativeAnyForwardsAtTheHost`, `TestNativeAnyRecordsUsage`,
`TestNativeAnyRefusals`, `TestNativeAnyStaysOnTheHost`,
`TestNativeAnyRefusesPagesWithoutAKey`, `TestNativeAnyMasks`,
`TestNativeAnyStreamsAsItComes`, `TestNativeAnyArkHost`.

A MiniMax API key (no account, a Chat, Anthropic or Responses base on
api.minimax.io, api.minimaxi.com or api.minimax.cn: `filmsMiniMax`,
`minimaxHost`; a relay serving MiniMax's models is not one) makes videos
with the H3 models, MiniMax-H3, -Highspeed, -Lite and -Max
([`video_minimax.go`](../../internal/gateway/video_minimax.go),
`minimaxVideoModels`). They are video models only (`Films`), listed by
`Videomakers` and never added to a chat list. A video starts at
`<host>/v2/video_generation` on that key's own host, so international and
Chinese keys each reach their platform, and is polled at
`<host>/v2/query/video_generation/<task_id>`. Its MP4 is `task.content.url`,
fetched as other vendors' video URLs are (`vendorClient`). `minimaxVideoBody`
sends the prompt and images in `content` (`first_frame` from
`input_reference`, `last_frame` from the request's `last_frame`, references
as `reference_image`). It turns away frames and references together, or more
than 9 references, with a 400 before MiniMax is asked. The duration is 5
unless set, raised to the model's shortest (4, or 5 for Max) and cut at 15.
The resolution is the request's own `resolution`, else derived from `size`'s
shorter side among the model's resolutions (H3 768P/2K, Highspeed 768P,
Lite and Max 768P/480P). The ratio is the nearest of MiniMax's to `size`.
None is sent with a frame, since the video takes the image's shape. Text
alone, and Lite's references, default to 16:9. No `callback_url` is ever
sent. Ids carry the key's mark (`markedKey`), as a custom provider's do,
and a key whose plan doesn't make H3 (codes 2061/2068/2057: a Token Plan
key) hands the video to the provider's next key. Failures are said for what
they are before MiniMax's own words (`minimaxWhy`, `minimaxError`): a bad
key (1004, 2049, 401), an empty pay-as-you-go balance (1008, 402), refused
content (1026, 1027), a plan without H3, and the rate limit (1002, 1045,
429). A failed task's `error.code` is explained the same way.
`AutoVideomaker` puts a MiniMax key after every other video maker: H3 is
paid by the second, so a subscription that made the videos before keeps
making them, and generate_video, which names no model, uses MiniMax-H3 only
when nothing else makes videos. Covered by `TestMiniMaxKeysMakeH3Videos`,
`TestMiniMaxVideoIsStartedPolledAndFetched`, `TestMiniMaxVideoBody`,
`TestMiniMaxVideoFailuresSayWhy`, `TestImageMCPMakesMiniMaxVideos`,
`TestMiniMaxComesAfterOtherVideomakers` and
`TestMiniMaxVideoMovesPastATokenPlanKey`.

## Short-lived gateway keys

A gateway key may expire (`access.Key.Expires`), and one issued under another
key keeps that key's id (`Key.Issuer`). They are for an orchestrator that
gives each sandboxed agent a key of its own that stops working by itself
(xazaj on X, moving off an enterprise LLM gateway). `access.IssueKey` in
[`issue.go`](../../internal/access/issue.go) makes one, living 1 minute to
30 days (`MinTTL`, `MaxTTL`); `--tokens`/`--cost` cap it over its whole life,
as one `hours` limit as long as its life from the moment it is issued.

Where it is issued:

- `POST /v1/magpie/keys` with `{"ttl": "1h" | 3600, "name", "models",
  "accounts", "tokens", "cost", "readOnly"}` answers 201 with the key's
  `id`, `name`, `key` (the secret, told once, `Cache-Control: no-store`),
  `expires` and what it is held to. `GET /v1/magpie/keys` lists the keys
  the caller issued, masked; `DELETE /v1/magpie/keys/{id}` revokes one.
  See [`issue_keys.go`](../../internal/gateway/issue_keys.go).
- `magpie gateway-key issue --ttl 1h [--name n] [--models a,b] [--accounts
  a,b] [--tokens N] [--cost USD] [--read-only] [--from <id>] [--json]`
  prints the secret alone; `gateway-key issuer <id> on|off` lets a key
  issue; `gateway-key list` shows `until <time>`, `expired`, `issued by
  <id>` and `issues keys`.

Who may call the endpoint (`keyIssuer`): a key the user let issue
(`Key.Issues`, `issuer-key` in `access.Update`), from wherever that key is
let in, so an orchestrator in a container or on another computer can; or a
request with no key that `profileRefused` passes: loopback, no proxy's
forwarding header, no browser's marks (`Origin`, `Sec-Fetch-*`, a
`Mozilla/` agent) and a loopback `Host`, so neither a page nor a rebound
name can. That request may already use every model with no key and could
run `magpie gateway-key` itself, so it gains nothing. A key not let issue,
a read-only key (`readOnlyGuard` refuses the route), an issued key and an
expiring key never may; the sharing key may only if the user lets it.

An issued key never outlives or exceeds its issuer. `IssueKey` clamps its
expiry to the issuer's and refuses a model or account the issuer may not
use. Then at every authentication (`access.Check`, which `Authenticate`,
`access.On` and so every route reach) `identityOf` folds it into its issuer
as the issuer is now: its models are those both allow (`foldModels`; none
in common is a refusal), its accounts are held to the issuer's
(`foldAccounts`), it is read-only if either is, told quotas only if both
are, and it expires when the first of them does. An issuer removed, turned
off or expired refuses it. What it spends counts against the issuer's
limits too: its usage rows carry `callerIssuerId` (`usage.Record.
CallerIssuerID`), `budget.read` and `budget.Append` count a row for both
keys, and `budget.Reserve` reserves on both (`Identity.Issuer`). `GET
/v1/magpie/limit` answers its `expires` and `issuerLimits`.

An expired key, or one its issuer no longer lets in, is answered 401 with
why (`access.Refused`, "the gateway key "sandbox-1" expired at …: ask for a
new one") on every route, from loopback too (`identifyCaller`): a sandbox on
this computer whose restricted key ran out would otherwise be let on as a
request with no key, which may use everything. Whether a connection already open
when the key expires (a Responses WebSocket) is refused at its next turn is
untested. An expired key is kept an hour for
that message, then `access.Prune` removes it (every 10 minutes while the
gateway serves, and on each issue), with the keys of an issuer that is
gone. A turned-off key on loopback is unchanged: it reads as no key.

`TestIssuedKeyExpiresOnEveryRoute`, `TestKeysEndpointWhoMayIssue` and
`TestIssuedKeysSpendTheirIssuersLimit` in
[`issue_keys_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/issue_keys_test.go), and
`TestIssuedKeyBoundedByIssuer` in
[`issue_test.go`](../../internal/access/issue_test.go).

### Trading an OIDC token for a key

A CI job need keep no gateway key in its secrets (#4531, xazaj on X): it
trades its OIDC token for a short-lived key. The user writes a rule
([`internal/oidc`](../../internal/oidc/rules.go), kept in
`oidc-rules.json`) with `magpie gateway-key oidc add --key <id> --repo
owner/name [--ref refs/heads/main] [--environment e] [--claim k=v] [--ttl
1h] [--issuer URL] [--audience a]`; `oidc list` and `oidc remove <id>`
manage them. No rule, no exchange. A rule names:

- the issuer, GitHub Actions' (`token.actions.githubusercontent.com`)
  by default, https only;
- the audience, `magpie-<rule id>` by default, so a token asked for one
  rule opens no other;
- the claims the token must have, each exact or a `path.Match` glob. A
  GitHub rule names `repository`, `repository_owner` or their ids with no
  wildcard, or any repository's workflow could get a token GitHub signs;
- the gateway key the keys are issued under, which must be let issue
  (`Key.Issues`) and not be issued itself, and how long they live (1h by
  default, `MinTTL` to `MaxTTL`).

`POST /v1/magpie/keys/oidc` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and an
optional `{"ttl", "models"}` ([`oidc_keys.go`](../../internal/gateway/oidc_keys.go))
verifies the token (`oidc.Match`, [`verify.go`](../../internal/oidc/verify.go)):
RS256 alone, signed by a key from the issuer's discovery document's https
`jwks_uri`, `exp` required, `nbf`/`iat` with a minute's leeway, the rule's
audience and claims, and a `jti` traded once until it expires. A token is
judged only against rules naming its `iss`, so it can't make magpie fetch
from a URL of its choosing. Keys are cached an hour per issuer, read by
one request at a time, asked again for an unknown `kid` at most every 10s,
and the last good keys keep serving while the issuer is down; with none
ever read nothing is let in. A matching token gets `access.IssueKey` under
the rule's key, named `repo@ref (run N)`, living no longer than the rule,
so everything above about issued keys holds: bounded by its issuer, its
spend counts against it, turning the issuer off stops both.

The route is the one exemption from `remoteKeyed` in `lanGuard`
(`oidcExchange`: that exact path, POST, no gateway key). It is answered
only while magpie is shared and past the allow list, and a refused token
counts against `tooManyAuthFails` as a wrong key does. A read-only key is
refused it. Covered by `TestOIDCTokenTradedForKey`
([`oidc_keys_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/oidc_keys_test.go)),
`TestMatchEachCheck`, `TestKeysCachedAndKept`, `TestUnreadKeysLetNothingIn`
and `TestAddRefuses` ([`oidc_test.go`](../../internal/oidc/oidc_test.go)).
Not tried: a real GitHub Actions run against a shared magpie.

### The stats and management API

Every route under `/v1/magpie/` answers a program on this computer, and
another computer only with an enabled gateway key while magpie is shared
(`sharedWith`). A key held to a budget, models or accounts (`heldKey`) is
told its own calls and limit alone:

| Route | What it answers |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /v1/magpie/usage?period=today\|7d\|30d\|all` | the usage summary: totals, by model, agent, provider, account and gateway key |
| `GET /v1/magpie/usage/requests?period=&offset=&limit=` | the request log, filtered by `day`, `model`, `agent`, `provider`, `account`, `callerKey` (a gateway key's id, an issued one's too), `failed=1`, `retried=1`, `q`, `computer`, `via` |
| `GET /v1/magpie/quotas[?kept=1]` | each subscription's windows; `kept=1` without asking a vendor |
| `GET /v1/magpie/quotas/history?days=&provider=&user=` | what was left of each window over time (45 days at most) |
| `GET /v1/magpie/limit` | the key's own limits, its `expires`, and `issuerLimits`; a held key's `heldTo`, `models`, `quotas` and `quotaProviders` |
| `GET`/`POST /v1/magpie/statusline[?format=json]` | Claude Code's status line, as text or JSON, of the cards `scopeQuotas` tells the key |
| `GET /v1/magpie/concurrency` | requests in flight per account |
| `POST`/`GET /v1/magpie/keys`, `DELETE /v1/magpie/keys/{id}` | short-lived keys, above |
| `POST /v1/magpie/keys/oidc` | an OIDC token traded for a short-lived key, above |

### Route events for a display

A display on the network (#3387, joe21_eee: an ESP32 screen) shows every
agent's requests as they are routed, live, without knowing their sessions,
which `/v1/magpie/route` asks for, and without the full trace
(`/v1/magpie/trace`), which names accounts, folders and errors and stays
loopback-only. [`route_events.go`](../../internal/gateway/route_events.go)
serves two GETs:

- `GET /v1/magpie/route-events/snapshot`: `cursor`, `active` and `routes`,
  every request the trace has that is still going, read together with the
  cursor under `trace.mu`.
- `GET /v1/magpie/route-events?after=<cursor>` (or `Last-Event-ID`, which
  wins): SSE. Each `route` event has an `id` equal to its JSON `cursor`, a
  `kind`, the `active` count after the change and one `route`, which
  replaces the display's route of that `id`. A display removes it when its
  `phase` is `done` or `dropped`.

A route is `routeActivity`, an allow list, never a `Route` with fields
taken out: its id (the trace's, base 36, two requests of one session are
two ids), start `time`, `agent` (the caller's label), the `provider` of the
seat its last try went to (never the seat's id, which names an account or
a key's fingerprint), `model` (the served one once the reply names it),
`attempt` (from 1), `fallback` (a later attempt, a retry on the same
provider included), `phase` (`started`, `attempt`, `responding`,
`attempt_done`, `done`, `dropped`), `status`, `failed`, `ms` (the finished
try's, or the request's once done) and `ttft_ms`. No session, account, key,
folder, prompt, reply or error text. Kinds: `request_started`,
`attempt_started`, `first_content`, `attempt_finished`, `request_finished`,
`request_dropped`, `route_changed`.

Changes are recorded in `trace.begin` and `trace.update` (a diff of
`activityOf` before and after) into a ring of the last 512, under
`trace.mu`; writes to the socket hold no lock. The first content is told as
it comes: `firstToken.onFirst` (`s.trace.firstContent`, set per try in
`serve` and for the ChatGPT backend's reads in
[`codex_backend.go`](../../internal/gateway/codex_backend.go)) writes the
try's unexported `firstMs`, so the full trace's `TTFT`, told once the try
is done, is unchanged. A route that leaves the trace unfinished (`drop`: an
unrouted request's stand-in once its own route begins; `trim` past twice
`traceKeep`) is told `request_dropped` and marked `offFeed`, so later writes
to it by the request holding it are told no more: without it a display
showed a stand-in going forever.

A cursor that isn't this process's (the ring's `epoch`), or older than the
ring, is answered 409 `{"error":"snapshot_required"}`; a reader that falls
behind mid-stream gets `event: reset` and the stream ends. Take a snapshot
in either case. A heartbeat comes every 15 seconds; a write waits at most
10. The stream ends when the client goes, when the gateway stops serving
(`Server.streamsEnding`, closed first thing in `ListenAndServe`'s drain, so
`Shutdown` doesn't wait its whole drain, 15 minutes on a handover, for a
display), when the key it came with is turned off, removed or runs out
(`access.On`, asked at most once a second when there are changes and at
each heartbeat), and when magpie stops being shared with a reader on
another machine (`remoteKeyed`). A key rotated keeps its id, so a stream
open under the old secret goes on until it reconnects. Nothing is kept on
disk.

Who reads it is who reads the usage log (`routeEventsAllowed`, as
`usageSummary`): this computer, and another machine with an enabled gateway
key while magpie is shared; never a key held to a budget, models or
accounts (`refuseHeld`), which is told no one else's requests. A read-only
key reads it (`readOnlyRoutes`), so a display is given
`magpie gateway-key add Screen --read-only`: it can spend nothing. Every
route's guards come first (`lanGuard`, `corsGuard`): a page sending
`Origin: null`, a `*.localhost` page, a rebound name and a proxy's client
are refused it without a key. A separate key type, as the community patch
had, was left out: the read-only key already reads more than this feed
(every call's usage), and is the key for a display.
`TestRouteEventsTold`, `TestRouteEventsReplayAndRevocation`,
`TestRouteEventsDroppedRouteGoes`, `TestRouteEventsEndWithTheGateway`,
`TestRouteEventsLiveFallback` in
[`route_events_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/route_events_test.go), and
`TestReadOnlyKeyEveryRoute`.

### A reply's progress while it is out

A long or reasoning reply otherwise looks stuck on the Routing page (#4951,
Canda15). [`live.go`](../../internal/gateway/live.go) counts each request's
reply as it reaches the agent: `serve` begins a `liveRow` on the request's
`inflight` (`liveBegin`, none for the gateway's own requests), and
`captureResponseWriter.Write`, which every byte to the agent passes, calls
`observe`. The route (the full trace only, never `routeActivity`) gets
`bytes` (every byte written, SSE framing and keepalives included),
`contentAt` (epoch ms of the last write that was not only SSE comments, so
a keepalive never moves it) and `estTok`. `trace.liveBytes` writes them
under `trace.mu` and bumps the route's `Seq`, so a long poll gathers them
like any change.

`estTok` is the content written so far (text, reasoning, tool names and
arguments, per the agent's protocol,
[`live_content.go`](../../internal/gateway/live_content.go)) at the rate
learned for the provider and model that answer (`routed`, per try), from
streamed replies that did not fail and whose vendor said its output
tokens ([`live_rates.go`](../../internal/gateway/live_rates.go)). Under
three such replies there is no estimate and the page shows none. Rates
are in memory only (256 pairs), learned again after a restart; an
estimate is never learned from.

Cost: `observe` takes the row's lock and the trace's, neither held while
anything blocks. A whole JSON reply (its first byte `{` or `[`) is counted
in bytes only, never copied or decoded; a stream is cut into lines in
place, only a line cut by the end of a write is copied, and a line over
1 MiB is passed over. The ChatGPT backend's chain (`codex_backend.go`)
writes past the capture writer and is not counted.

The page ([`plugins/routing/client.js`](../../internal/gui/assets/plugins/routing/client.js),
`liveParts`, `paintLive`) shows the numbers on a request in flight's row
and beside the account answering it, changed in place each second; after
15 s with no content the account's dot stops pulsing. Nothing stays once
the request is done. `TestLiveCountsARealStream` and the other tests in
[`live_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/live_test.go), and
`routing-live-bytes.test.cjs`.

## Turning provider search off

[Settings](../../internal/settings/settings.go)' `searcher: "off"` disables magpie's provider-assisted search,
including Kimi Code's, a GLM Coding Plan's and WorkBuddy's search services and Google sign-ins'
separate search requests. Configured Search APIs still run in their saved order; failures
never fall back to a provider while this setting is off. Without a ready
Search API, [`canSearchFor`](../../internal/gateway/search_kimi.go) does not offer magpie's replacement search tool.
`searchesOwn` is the one check for a provider searching for its own models (Kimi Code, a GLM Coding Plan, WorkBuddy, a Google sign-in), so
`ownSearcher` and the model list another magpie reads (`webSearchOf`, which then says no search) follow it too.
A provider's native search on the client's protocol remains unchanged,
unless `searchAll` is on (next section).
An empty `searcher` still selects a provider automatically; an unavailable
named provider (gone, off, can't search) still falls back to that selection.
One that is available but fails a search is not replaced by another
provider (below).

[`webSearch`](../../internal/gateway/search.go) enforces the API-only path.
Covered by `TestSearchProviderOff`, `TestSearchProviderOffForwarding`, `TestSearchProviderOffTellsRemoteMagpie`,
`TestSearcherChosen`, `TestSettingsSearchProviderOff`, and `searcher-pick.test.cjs`.

## magpie's search for a model that searches by itself

[Settings](../../internal/settings/settings.go)' `searchAll` (cybercaius on
Discord) gives a model whose provider searches the web by itself
(`searchesFor`) magpie's search too: the searcher and the search APIs, in
their usual order, as a model that can't search has. Settings › Models ›
Web search shows it as "Models that search by themselves", Their own or
magpie's; with nothing magpie can search with the row is dimmed and says where to add a search.
[`magpieSearches`](../../internal/gateway/search.go) is the one check: on
in Settings, magpie can search for this provider (`canSearchFor`), and the
request isn't magpie's own searcher request (`searching`), which needs the
provider's search. Where it holds, `searchedForIt` stops the relay of a
body that asks for web search, and `translate` neither picks an API for
the provider's own search nor keeps it, so the provider is sent magpie's
web_search function tool. With nothing magpie can search with, the
provider keeps its own search and the relay as sent.

Not covered: a Codex sign-in's turns relayed as Codex sent them
(`codexAsSent`) and a sealed agent message keep the provider's search, as
they keep the rest of the body; the model list another magpie reads
(`webSearchOf`) still says native.

Covered by `TestSearchAllTakesTheSearchFromAProviderThatSearches` and
`search-all.test.cjs`.

## WorkBuddy's search service

A WorkBuddy plan's models (the China build's and WorkBuddy AI's, served by
the plugin since the built-in was retired) have no search tool of their own. With nothing
else in magpie that searches, `canSearchFor` was false and Claude Code's
WebSearch came back empty (#1798). `wbSearch`
([`search_workbuddy.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_workbuddy.go)) asks the
plan's own service as WorkBuddy's CLI does: POST
`<site>/agenttool/v1/search` with `{"query","type":"text2text","max_results":5}`,
signed by `Provider.Sign` and sent by `Provider.Do`, so a moved account goes
through the plugin's fetch. `searchesOwn` names it for the plan's own models
only, before magpie's picked searcher, as with Kimi Code. A `{code, msg}`
refusal (15001 is its search quota) is reported as an error, never as no
results. Covered by `TestWorkBuddyPluginSearches` (Claude Code's literal
WebSearch request, the real service's reply bytes, the quota refusal and
search turned off).

## A GLM Coding Plan's search MCP server

A GLM Coding Plan's key (Zhipu's or Z.ai's) is also given for the plan's
Web Search MCP server, which users otherwise add to each agent by hand
(young02036 on Discord). `glmSearch`
([`search_glm.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_glm.go)) asks it with the
provider's own key, nothing to set up: a Streamable HTTP session at
`<host>/api/mcp/web_search_prime/mcp` (`provider.ZhipuCodingSearch`, only
for a provider or key on the plan's `/api/coding/paas/v4`, since the
server serves no pay-as-you-go key), `initialize`, one `tools/call` of
`webSearchPrime` with `{"search_query"}`, then the session's DELETE. The
tool's text is read as a list of results, a list in a JSON string or a list
under `search_result`/`results`/`items`/`data` (`readGLMSearch`); `isError`
or words with no results are reported as an error, the key masked. A
provider with several keys asks each of the plan's keys in turn.
`searchesOwn` names it for the plan's own models first, as with Kimi Code,
but only while Settings names no searcher (`glmSearches`): a searcher the
user named searches for every model. It is not a searcher choice for other
providers' models: a plan with the Responses base already searches by
itself there (#660). Covered by `TestGLMCodingPlanSearchesForItsModels`
(a fake server with an event-stream reply, the refusal, a named searcher,
a pay-as-you-go key), `TestReadGLMSearch` and
`TestZhipuCodingSearchAddress`. Not tried against the real server: the
docs give neither the tool's arguments nor its reply's shape.

## Search results in translated Responses replies

When a search is done for a model (`searchReply`), or a Responses provider's
search is decoded for translation, its `KSearch` event and collected
`Search` part hold the query and pages found. [`responses.go`](../../internal/gateway/responses.go)'s
`responsesEncoder.event` and `renderResponses` return these as completed
`web_search_call` output items through the shared `responsesSearchItem`.
The action says `search`, carries both `query` (older clients) and `queries`,
and lists the nonempty source URLs in `action.sources`. An actual search
with no sources has an empty list; a text answer alone creates no search.

The stream adds and completes each search item, emits
`response.web_search_call.completed`, and keeps the same item in the final
response's `output`. Search events arrive after the search has run, so no
earlier progress is fabricated. Search items keep their order among text,
reasoning and client tool calls. They carry sources, not invented
`url_citation` annotations: the shared search result has no citation offsets
in the model's answer. Native Responses passthrough is unchanged. A client
sends the item back in its next input (Codex replays its output).
`parseResponses` leaves it out, as before, unless Claude's signed thinking
is in the same assistant run: then the search was magpie's own round for a
Claude reply, which Claude wrote as two messages (thinking and magpie's
`web_search` call; then, after the result, more thinking and the answer).
`searchRoundsBack` splits the run there into the call and a result naming
the query and the pages found (what the search said was not kept), so the
thinking blocks of the latest assistant message are where Claude wrote
them. Told as one message, Claude on Bedrock refused the next turn 400
"thinking or redacted_thinking blocks in the latest assistant message
cannot be modified" and Codex retried until the stream gave up (oreoft on
Discord; `TestClaudeThinkingAroundMagpieSearchGoesBackAsWritten`,
`TestSearchWithoutClaudeThinkingStaysOut` in
[`claude_search_round_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/claude_search_round_test.go)).

This lets a Responses client verify search execution and display source
links without sending a separate Anthropic Messages request. Covered by
the `TestResponsesSearch` tests in
[`responses_search_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/responses_search_test.go)
and the Responses cases of `TestWebSearchForAModelThatCannot` in
[`search_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_test.go).

## Citations in translated Anthropic replies

After a `KSearch` event (or a `Search` part), the Anthropic encoder and
`renderAnthropic` cut the text that follows into lines (`citer` in
[`search_cite.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_cite.go)). magpie's
`web_search` tool asks the model to cite the URLs it uses, so a line that
names the URL of a page the reply's searches found, whole (`urlIn`: not a
longer path, a query or another host), is a text block of its own whose
`citations` are `web_search_result_location` entries for those pages
(`citesOf`), as Anthropic's own search cites them; the lines between run
together in one block without citations, and a whitespace-only line is
held for the next, so no block is whitespace alone. Streamed, each cited
block's `citations_delta` events come before its `text_delta`, and the
stream buffers only to the end of a line, only after a search. The text
itself is the model's, unchanged. `encrypted_index` is `""`, like the
results' `encrypted_content`: it is Anthropic's sealed pointer, which
magpie can't make. `cited_text` is what the search said of the page,
cut to 150 characters (`Hit.Cited`: a search API's snippet, or the text an
Anthropic searcher's own citation quoted), else `""`. A reply with no
search is said as before. Covered by `TestSearchCitedAsAnthropics`
(streamed and whole), `TestNoSearchNoCitations` and
`TestCitesOnlyTheWholeURL`. Responses and Chat replies carry no
`url_citation` annotations yet.

While a provider asked by `searchReply` is queued or thinking, sending only
keepalives or `response.in_progress`, `askTranslated` reads it with
`readSSEAlive` and hands on a `KAlive` event after `keepaliveGap` with
nothing else sent. It is nothing of the reply: `searchRounds` passes it on
uncollected, and `relay` takes it as the provider heard from, begins the
client's stream and sends the client's own keepalive (`enc.keepalive`).
Before, `relay` waited for the first content to make the client's encoder,
so Codex heard only the held stream's comments, which its idle timeout
doesn't count, and hung up after ~315s (#2316). An error before any
`KAlive` is still a status another account may answer. Covered by
`TestSearchQueueKeepsCodexAlive` in
[`search_queue_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_queue_test.go).
A provider that says nothing at all, not even a keepalive, is handed on a
`KAlive` every `keepaliveEvery` by the same ticker `streamTranslated` uses
(`keepThroughSilence`), for up to `translatedQuietLongest` of its silence.

A Claude subscription run is heard from the same way (`keepBridgeHeard`,
in `claude_subscription.go`). Its run says nothing before its reply: in
the interactive mode a message is told whole from the transcript, and a
long conversation replayed to a new Claude Code is prefilled uncached
first. The held stream's comments stop at `keepaliveLongest`, and Claude
Code 2.1.316's byte watchdog hung up 300s later, at 599s (#5043). Comments
forever would not do: its event watchdog counts no comment and hangs up
600s after the headers. So after `bridgeHeardAfter` (1 minute) of a quiet
run, the run's events gain an empty `KStart`, which begins the reply
(`message_start`), and a `KAlive` every `keepaliveEvery` while it stays
quiet, for up to `translatedQuietLongest`. The client gets the protocol's
own pings, held like any frames-only stream (`holdLead`). The run's own
start, later, adds its usage. A failure in the first minute is still a
status. The gain is bounded: Claude Code 2.1.316 turns every 10s with
bytes into a ping of its own and counts 30 pings in a row, so a stream of
pings and comments alone ends 600s after its last other event. The
reply's start goes out when the hold lets it go, about 4 minutes in, and
the run has until about 14 minutes where it had 599s. A run quiet for
longer still ends: only a real event of the reply would keep it. Covered by `TestClaudeBridgeQuietRunKeepsClientAlive` in
[`claude_bridge_keepalive_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/claude_bridge_keepalive_test.go).

## Which providers search for another model

`Searchers()` (Settings › Web search's picker) and `autoSearcher` (magpie's
own pick) take a provider by `searchRank`, and its models by
`searchableModel`, from the same rules that send a provider's own search
tool on (`searchesItself`, `modelSearches`, `remoteSearch`). A provider
that searches for some of its models only (`searchModels`: Command Code's
Provider API, `partSearcher`) is offered and picked with those models
alone, never under `x-cmd-zdr`; before, its searching models were left out
of both (01huadalang on Discord). So is a plugin's provider with models
its list marks `searches` (`pluginSearches`: a model that searches the web
by itself when asked in plain words, catalog `WebSearch` "native"): it is
asked with no search tool, and `searchWith` takes a reply that names a
link. Before, every plugin's provider was left out whatever its models
did (01huadalang, 10-20, of Trae's search model). A client's own
`web_search` to such a model still gets magpie's searcher. A provider none
of whose models searches by itself, such as a plugin that marks none and
whose `config` hook doesn't set `searches`, is not a searcher: its models
are given magpie's `web_search`, and the row's `searchLeftOut` names it.
The models looked at are `searcherModels`: what the provider lists
(`Available()`) and the ones typed in by hand (`Exposed`). A custom
Responses relay with no model list, marked as searching by itself, has
only the hand-typed ones. Before, it was left out of the picker while
the page listed it among the relays to pick by hand (axelburks on
Discord). Covered by `TestSearcherRelayWithoutModelList` and
`TestSearcherPickerOffersResponsesRelay`.
A key at AI Studio's Gemini API (`geminiKeySearches`) ranks with a Google
sign-in (5), by googleSearch, and like one is `Own`: its own models search
first whoever is named. Before, it searched for its own models only and
the picker never listed it (cocoasoda on Discord,
`TestAIStudioKeySearchesForOthers`).
A ChatGPT account (Codex's sign-in, `codexSearches`) searches for another
model by the ChatGPT backend's own search, `POST <CodexBase>/alpha/search`,
the request Codex's web.run sends by itself with no model turn
(`codexSearch`, codex-rs ext/web-search): one `search_query`, the
account's small model in `model` (the backend answered the same for any),
`allowed_callers: ["direct"]`, and `external_web_access` from the
client's `web_search` tool (`Request.WebAccess`, `withWebAccess`: false
for Codex's "cached", #5018), else true. The output's pages, split on its
80-dash rule, keep their titles and links by the results' `ref_id`, with
the private-use citation marks dropped (`codexPages`). It is magpie's own
pick for the account (`autoSearcherReady`, `searchInTurn` via
`turnModel`) and Settings' `<id>/@search` (`codexSearchModel`, shown as
"its web search"); `<id>` and `<id>/<model>` still search by that model,
which is also what `codexSearchBy` falls back on when the backend's
search fails. lucy_85908 on Discord: on a real Plus account 151 such
searches left the 5-hour window at the same percent, about 2s each, while
51 by gpt-6-luna through /responses took 1% of it, 7-15s each and about
9k tokens in. The fallback model search is still made live: the cached
mode reaches only the backend's search. Covered by
`TestChatGPTAccountSearchesByItsWebSearch`, `TestResponsesWebSearchModeKept`
and `TestCodexPagesRead`.
Covered by `TestCommandCodeSearchesForOtherModels`,
`TestPluginSearchModelIsASearcher` and `TestSearcherChosen`.

## A searcher that fails or finds nothing

`modelSearch` asks the searcher picked in Settings (or the one magpie picks)
first. `searchWith` tells three outcomes apart: an error, a reply that
searched and named no page (`errFoundNothing`), and a reply with no search
in it at all, no `server_tool_use` or `web_search_tool_result`
(`errNotSearched`, which is found nothing too). The last is a vendor that
ran no search: the tool dropped or left unused, and the model answered
from what it knew.

A searcher magpie picked by itself gives way to the next of `Searchers()`,
up to three in all (#669). That fallback is not silent: `searchingInstead`
puts on the search's `CallFor` the searcher asked first (`instead`), and
why it didn't search (`why`, `nothingFound` or `notSearched`). The Routing
row of the search that answered says which searcher was asked first, for
whom, why it didn't search, and which one searched in its place
(`kindWhy`).

A searcher named in Settings gives way only to the ones the user put
after it, `SearcherThen` (Settings › Web search's "If it fails" row), in
their order. One that can't search now, is the one named, or is the same
model is skipped. The first that searches answers, and the row says why
the named one didn't search (`searchingInstead`). No other provider
searches in their place (axelburks on Discord asked for this list), unless
the named one can't serve at all: every account or key of it out of its
allowance (`searcherSpent`), or its search refused for quota, credit or
rate (`failure`, in `searchWith`), both `errCantServe`. Then, after the
ones named after it, the searchers magpie would pick search in its place
in turn (`searchInTurn`, up to two more providers), and `namedFailed`
names the one that did. pedestrianz (plugins#635) named Codex to search
for Cursor's models; when Codex ran out and Cursor took the turns over,
every search failed with it (`TestPickedSearcherThatCantServeGivesWay`).
When every one fails, their errors go back to the model together, as a
failed tool result, and the turn goes on; `webSearch` then asks the
search APIs, when any are set up. Covered by
`TestPickedSearchersInTheirOrder`; `TestSearcherThenSaved` and
`TestSearcherThenFromSettings` cover how the list is saved. Settings only
takes a provider `Searchers()` offers. A save without the list keeps it,
since the page's `prefsKeep` doesn't send it; `[]` clears it. `namedFailed` keeps the last search it didn't make, with the one that
searched in its place (`Instead`), and clears it when it next searches. Settings › Web search shows that (`SearcherFailed`,
`searchFailed`) at the start of the row's help, which wraps whole.
Gearclickist on Discord picked Grok. Their grok-4.7 rows were about 1.3k
tokens, the size of a request Grok ran no search for (one that searched
took 6k-66k on our account). Every search then went on to Codex's
gpt-6-astra, a pricier model they hadn't picked. Rows recorded
before this, with `picked` set, still read as before.

Covered by `TestPickedSearcherIsNotHandedOn`,
`TestSearchInPlaceOfAutoSaysWhy`, `TestSearcherFindingNothingGivesWay`,
`search-instead.test.cjs` and `searcher-pick.test.cjs`.

## How hard a side model thinks

Settings › Models has a chip beside Image recognition's model and the
searcher's (MOMO on Discord): `visionEffort` and `searcherEffort` in
[settings](../../internal/settings/settings.go), one of `catalog.EffortRank`
or empty. The chip offers only the model's own levels (`ModelEfforts`:
`Efforts`, weakest first; `visionEfforts`, `searchEfforts` in the settings
read), and none for a model whose levels aren't known. A level picked is
fitted to the model's levels (`sideEffort`, `fitEffort`) when the call is
made, so a saved level outlives a change of model. Empty keeps magpie's own:
Image recognition at the least level the model has (`classifyEffort`), the
searcher with no level at all. Only these two calls carry it
([`side_effort.go`](../../internal/gateway/side_effort.go)):

- `askVisionWith` sends Chat's `reasoning_effort`; the image cache key
  (`sightKey`) has the level picked in it, so a new pick reads the image
  again.
- `searchBody` sends the searcher's Anthropic body `thinking` (`disabled` for
  `none`, else enabled with `budgetOf`'s budget, 1024 for `minimal`) and
  `output_config.effort` unless the vendor model is a Claude older than
  adaptive thinking. The gateway's translation then fits it to the vendor:
  adaptive thinking or `between_tools` for new Claude, `reasoning.effort` for
  Responses, `thinkingLevel` for Gemini 3 on Code Assist and Antigravity.
- A picked level raises the call's `max_tokens` by its budget
  (`replyRoom`), so thinking doesn't spend the reply; the model's own limit
  still caps it.
- A provider searching for its own models (`ownSearch`, Kimi Code's search
  service) is sent none. A fallback searcher is sent the level fitted to its
  own model.

Covered by `TestVisionEffortPicked`, `TestVisionEffortOnAnthropic`,
`TestSearcherEffortPicked`, `TestSearcherEffortOnGemini`,
`TestSideEffortChecked` and `side-effort.test.cjs`.

## A search API's base URL

Each search API ([`provider.SearchAPI`](../../internal/provider/search_api.go))
has an optional `url`, its base URL: an endpoint of the user's own that
answers as that API does (#1580). Empty is the vendor's own
(`SearchVendor.Base`); SearXNG has none, so its URL is required. `check`
takes only an `http://` or `https://` URL with a host and no `?query` or
`#fragment`, since `askSearchAPI` puts the vendor's path after it
(`/search`, `/res/v1/web/search`, `/v2/search`, `/v1/tools/search`,
`/search_api/web_search`, `/SearchPro`; `SearchVendor.Paths`) with the slash at its end
trimmed. A URL given ending in that path (`…/search` for Tavily, any
case) is its base: `SetSearchAPI` saves it without the path, and
`SearchAPI.Base` drops it from one saved with it before, so it is asked
once, never `/search/search` (plugins#509,
`TestSearchAPIBaseURLWithItsPath`). A base of the user's own that
itself ends in that path can't be set. The request goes through the gateway's client, so it follows
magpie's proxy settings as the vendor's own address does
(`TestSearchFollowsTheSearchersProxy`).

The key is sent only to that URL. Go keeps Brave's `X-Subscription-Token`
and Exa's `x-api-key` across a redirect to another host, so `askSearchAPI`
doesn't follow one. The 3xx is the error, and it names where the search
was sent. magpie ships and lists no third-party search endpoint; the URL
is only ever the user's.

A value the user set is never replaced by the vendor's default. In
Settings › Models › Web search the field sits under the key for every API,
with the vendor's own address as its placeholder. Picking an API that is
already added fills in its saved URL, so Add with a new key alone keeps
the URL, and Add with no key changes only the URL. On the CLI,
`magpie search add <api> <key>` keeps the URL set before, and `url=` with
nothing after it puts the vendor's own back. Backup and sync carry `url`
with the rest of the entry.

Covered by `TestSearchAPIAtItsOwnBaseURL`, `TestSearchKeyStaysAtItsBaseURL`,
`TestSearchAPIBaseURLFromSettings`, `TestSearchCLI` and
`search-api.test.cjs`.

### A search API a plugin gives

A plugin's `search` hook adds search APIs under the id `<plugin>:<id>`
(`provider.AllSearchVendors`; [plugin-tools](plugin-tools.md#search-apis-from-a-plugin)).
`askSearchAPI` hands one to `askPluginSearch`, so the keys, their rest after a
401/402/403/429 (`SearchRefused`), the listed order, the switch, the masked
query and the request-log row are the built-ins' own, and the plugin is
asked only while it is on. A plugin API has no `Base` of its own: its URL
field shows only when the plugin declares `url: true`, and then is required.
Its pages are cleaned like a built-in's (`searchPlain`, `clipText`, six at
most). It is asked with the time left of a quarter of the search's limit, and
`plugin.RunSearch` returns when that is out, though the plugin's own fetch may
run on until the host's abort reaches it. `TestPluginSearchAPI`.

## The search APIs' order, and one switched off

`provider.SearchAPIs` gives the search APIs in their saved order, the
order `apiSearch` asks them in, leaving out one not ready and one
switched off (`SearchAPI.Off`, #2286). Added, one goes last.
`ArrangeSearchAPIs` puts them in the order of the vendors named; one not
named keeps its place after them, and a vendor not set up or named twice
refuses the whole order, so an order sent from a page drawn before one was
removed changes nothing. `SwitchSearchAPI` sets `off` and keeps the key,
URL and place; `SetSearchAPI` (a new key or URL) leaves `off` as it was.
An API switched off is never asked, by a model's search or by the search
MCP, and with every API off, Settings' Search first with row is gone as
with none at all. Backup and sync carry `off` and the order with the rest
of the entry: a restore (`RestoreSearchAPIs`) puts the backup's APIs in
the backup's order, one only here after them, and a backup made before
`off` turns every API on.

In Settings › Models › Web search each API's row and its keys' row move as
one (`.search-unit`, `searchArrange`): dragged by any part of it off its
buttons and fields (`dragRows`, as routing's groups are, #779) or moved a
place with Alt+↑/↓, the rows renumbered at once; an order that can't be
saved is drawn again as saved. A switch on each row sends `{vendor, on}`;
one off is dimmed and says Off. On the CLI, `magpie search off <api>`,
`on <api>` and `move <api> <n>` do the same, and `magpie search` lists one
off as off. `POST /api/settings/search-api` takes `{arrange: [vendors]}`
and `{vendor, on}` beside the add and remove it took before.

Covered by `TestSearchAPIOrderAndOff`, `TestSearchCLIOffAndMove`,
`TestBackupSearchAPIsOrderAndOff` and `search-order.test.cjs`.

## A search API's keys

A search API's `key` may hold several keys, separated by commas (#1477).
`askSearchAPIKeys` asks them in the order `searchKeyOrder` gives
([`search_keys.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_keys.go)), each with a
quarter of the search's time. By default they take turns (SlouchyQuill on
Discord, after dsh-tavily-pool): a counter per vendor moves the first key
asked on by one each search. `pick` `order` (`provider.SearchPickOrder`,
`magpie search add … pick=order`, In order in Settings) always starts from
the first. Only a refusal (`searchRefused`: 401, 402, 403, 429, 432, 433)
goes on to the next key; any other error ends the search there.

A refused key rests (`searchRestFor`): 401 and 403 ten minutes ("turned
away"), 402 an hour ("out of credit"), a rate limit its `Retry-After` held
to 30 seconds through 10 minutes (a minute without one). 432 and 433 are
Tavily's plan and pay-as-you-go limits, out of credit from Tavily only; from
any other vendor they rest as a rate limit. Doubao Search (Volcengine)
says its error in `ResponseMetadata.Error`, possibly with a 200, so
`volcSearchError` reads it by its code first: 10401, 10403 and 10409 as
403, 10406, 10408, 10410 and 10412 (no free searches left, none paid
for, a plan missing or spent) as 402, 700429 as 429; any other code is a
plain error (`TestVolcengineSearchAPI`). Yuanbao Search (Tencent Cloud,
`wsa`) answers 200 to every request it handled, its error in
`Response.Error.Code`, so `wsaSearchError` reads the code: `AuthFailure.*`,
`UnauthorizedOperation` (a wrong key) and `ResourceNotFound` (the service
not opened) as 403, `ResourceUnavailable` (an account in arrears),
`LimitExceeded` and `ResourceInsufficient` as 402,
`RequestLimitExceeded.*` as 429; any other code is a plain error
(`TestWSASearchAPI`). A resting key is not dropped:
it is asked after every key that isn't, the one back soonest first, so a
search still has every key to fall back on. A key that answers wakes.
Rests are held by vendor and a hash of the key (`searchKeyID`), never the
key itself, and the vendor's error has the key masked
(`provider.MaskSearchKey`, `tvly-…abcd`) before it is logged or returned.

The query a search API, a Kimi Code plan's or GLM Coding Plan's search service or WorkBuddy's
search is asked is masked as Settings → Privacy says (`maskedQuery` in
[`redact.go`](../../internal/gateway/redact.go); a plan set to go unmasked,
`provider.SkipsRedaction`, gets it as it is). A searcher model is asked
through `serve`, which masks its request the usual way. An agent's call to
the search MCP carries its query with the values put back, so before this
it went out as the agent wrote it (lc on Discord); `searchMCPRun` puts the
values back in what it returns (`redact.Restore`). What a model's own
web_search finds goes back to its vendor as found, placeholders and all.

What each Tavily key has used is read from `GET /usage` with that key
([`search_quota.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_quota.go),
`readTavilyUsage`): the key's own `limit` when it has one, else its account
plan's `plan_limit` and `plan_usage`, shared by the account's keys (a free
account's keys have `limit` null); both null is no limit. A read that fails
or a reply missing those fields is unknown, with why, never 0 used. A read
is kept two minutes (Tavily allows /usage 10 times in 10 minutes) unless
asked fresh. A key at its limit with no pay-as-you-go credit left rests an
hour as "used up"; a later read that finds credit wakes a key resting for
that or for being out of credit. Settings › Models › Web search shows a row
of the keys under an API with several or with Tavily's (In turn / In order,
each key masked with what it has used, Check again), and `magpie search`
lists the same, read fresh.

Covered by `TestReadTavilyUsage`, `TestSearchKeysTakeTurnsRoundRobin`,
`TestSearchKeyRefusedRests`, `TestSearchRestFor`, `TestSearchQuotas`,
`TestSearchKeyNeverInFull`, `TestSearchAPIKeysTakeTurns` and
`search-keys.test.cjs`.

## A search API's asks in the request log

Each ask of a search API with one key is a row of the request log
(cybercaius on Discord: which search API a search used couldn't be seen).
`logSearchAPI` in [`search_api.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_api.go)
writes it after every `askSearchAPI` in `askSearchAPIKeys`, a refused key
and the next one each a row: `Source` `search` (`usage.SourceSearch`),
`Kind` `web_search`, the agent the search was for (`searchFor`'s
`CallFor.Agent`, else `magpie`), the API's name as `Requested`, its host,
the key as `searchKeyID` and masked (`provider.MaskSearchKey`), how long
it took, and how it ended: 200, the API's status when it refused the key,
the error, or "found nothing". `Provider` stays empty: `deepseek` is a
provider's id as well as a search API's. The query is never kept: the
error is cut to the transport's own error and host when Go's says the
URL (an API asked by GET carries the query there), and the query, plain
or escaped, is taken out of what the API said back.

The rows ride the probes' lane (`IsProbe`): listed under the Web search
purpose and found by the API's name in the search box, counted in no
total, budget or provider share, and never exported (`observeLedger`), so
telemetry, which reads no usage row, has nothing new to send. The Usage
page's Requests tab badges one "Search API" with the host and masked key
where the provider goes. A searcher model's request is a model row of its
own, as before. A plan's own search service (Kimi Code's, a GLM Coding Plan's, WorkBuddy's:
`ownSearch`, through `logOwnSearch`) is a row the same way, under the
plan's provider id and name, badged "Web search".

Each such ask is on the Routing page and in Recent calls too, with its
exchange in the request archive when that is on (axelburks on Discord:
the Routing page didn't say which API answered, and the row had no
archive). `logSearch` calls `keepSearch` in
[`search_archive.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_archive.go) before
the row is written: a done route, agent `magpie` with `For` the agent
the search was for, `Kind` `web_search`, the API's or service's name as
the model and its host (a plan's provider id) as the provider, its seat
the masked key or the account. The row gets the route's id (`RouteID`)
and the archive's name (`Archive`), which the Requests tab opens. Each
ask runs with a `searchWire` in its context (`withSearchWire`, one per
key or account); the place that sends it fills it (`tapSearch`: the
request as signed, its body, and the answer as it is read, the last
exchange standing, so GLM's MCP search keeps its `tools/call`). A
plugin's search API is noted as magpie handed it to the plugin
(`notePluginSearch`, method `PLUGIN`, the key masked) and what came
back. The key an ask went with is replaced with its masked form in the
path, query, headers and both bodies before anything is kept (`taken`),
and the archive's own scrub takes Authorization and the like out as for
every call. Bodies are kept up to `RequestArchiveMaxMB` (a longer answer
is marked cut) and Recent calls keep their own 256 KiB; with the
archive off nothing is archived, but the route and Recent call are
still there. Covered by `TestSearchAPIAskIsRoutedAndArchived`,
`TestSearchAPIKeyInQueryNotArchived`, `TestPluginSearchAPIRoutedAndArchived`,
`TestKimiPlanSearchRoutedAndArchived`, `TestGLMPlanSearchArchivesItsToolsCall`,
`TestSearchRowKeepsRouteAndArchive` (internal/gui),
`usage-ledger-search.test.cjs` and `routing-side-calls.test.cjs`. Not
covered: `web_fetch` (`search_fetch.go`) and a vendor's own search
(`search_native.go`), which magpie doesn't send.

A search a provider's model runs with its provider's own tool is a row
the same way when its reply shows it (cybercaius on Discord: DeepSeek
searched and Usage showed nothing). `searchTally` in
[`search_native.go`](../../internal/gateway/search_native.go) counts
Anthropic's `server_tool_use` blocks named `web_search` (DeepSeek's
Anthropic API answers them too) and `usage.server_tool_use.web_search_requests`,
and the Responses API's `web_search_call` items: what a stream named as
it went (a block's start, an item done, by id), what a whole reply or the
stream's end listed, and what the usage said are counted apart, and the
most of them is the count. The usage sniffer of a relayed reply and both
translated readers feed it (`Usage.searches`), and after the request's own
row `logNativeSearches` writes one row a search, at most 20: `Source`
`search`, `Kind` `web_search`, the request's agent, provider id, host and
model, status 200, no query and no time of its own. An event is parsed for
it only when it holds `server_tool_use` or `web_search_call`. A provider
whose reply doesn't show its searches (a Chat API's model that searches
inside, with nothing in the reply but text) has none to show, and
DeepSeek's Chat API has no search tool at all.

Covered by `TestSearchAPIAsksAreLogged`, `TestSearchAPIFailureKeepsNoURL`,
`TestOwnSearchIsLogged`, `TestWorkBuddyPluginSearches`,
`TestSearchAPIRowsListedNotCounted`, `TestOwnProviderSearchesAreLogged`,
`TestSearchRowsListTheirPurpose` and `usage-ledger-search.test.cjs`.

## Web search as an MCP server

`SearchMCPPath`, `/mcp/magpie/web-search`, serves the same `webSearch` a
model that can't search is given (the searcher's model, a Kimi Code plan's
service, the search APIs, as Settings › Models › Web search orders them)
as an MCP server of its own, so Cursor, Claude Desktop or any agent can
mount it as a `web_search` tool (Kayphoon on Discord). It speaks Streamable
HTTP only: each POST of a JSON-RPC message or batch is answered with JSON
(`initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`), notifications get 202,
and GET and DELETE get 405, since the server has no stream or session of
its own. Legacy SSE isn't served; a client that only speaks stdio mounts it
through `mcp-remote`. The path has two segments under `/mcp/`, so it can't
hide a signed-in server's `/mcp/{name}`.

It is a gateway route like the others. `lanGuard` takes a gateway key from
another machine (only while magpie is shared) and refuses a rebound browser
host, and the handler checks `local`/`sharedWith` again as a backstop when
`Handler` is served without `lanGuard`. Unlike other routes, a web page on
this computer (a `localhost` Origin, which `corsGuard` lets through) is
refused too: only an origin listed in Settings, which `corsGuard` already
held to a gateway key, may call it, as the MCP spec has a server check
`Origin`. `keyLimited` passes `/mcp/` by, so each `tools/call` reserves the
calling key's limit itself (`budget.Reserve`), and a spent key's call is a
tool error with no search made. The search is marked `CallFor{Agent, MCP:
true}`, so Routing says the agent called magpie's web_search MCP tool rather
than that magpie searched for one of its models. With nothing that can
search, the tool error says where to add a search API. Settings › Models ›
Web search shows the address (`#searchMcpRow`, from the Settings page's own
`gateway` address).

Its second tool, `web_fetch` (`search_fetch.go`, #2286), reads a page by
its URL for an agent with no fetch tool of its own. The URL is the agent's,
so it is downloaded as a caller's image is (`webFetchClient`, which is
`publicClient`): public addresses only, every resolved address checked and
pinned, through at most five redirects each checked the same, wherever
the request came from (a name a fake-IP resolver answers in 198.18.0.0/15
is read through its TUN or a proxy on this computer, as an image is: see
the guard below, #2593). It reads for at most 30 seconds and 5 MiB. An
HTML page is decoded from its charset and given as Markdown (`htmlText`:
headings, lists, links made absolute, code; scripts, styles and hidden
elements dropped), other text as it is, anything else is a tool error, and
so is a status of 400 or more. It answers `max_chars` characters (20000 by
default, at most 100000) from `offset`, and says the offset the rest starts
at. Each call reserves the calling key's limit as a search does.
`TestWebFetchReadsAPage` and `TestWebFetchOnlyPublic`.

A POST's body is read by `readMCPBody`, up to 1 MiB here and 32 MiB for
vision and for a signed-in server's `/mcp/{name}`. A longer body is refused
whole with 413, before any message in it runs or is passed on: the length is
told from the bytes read, one past the limit, not from Content-Length, so a
valid message padded past the limit is never run as its prefix (#1766).
`TestOwnMCPRejectsOversizedBody` and `TestMCPProxyRejectsOversizedBody`
cover it.

Covered by `TestSearchMCPServesWebSearch`, `TestSearchMCPWithNoSearcher`,
`TestSearchMCPGuarded`, `TestSearchMCPHeldToKeyLimit`,
`TestSearchMCPRoutedAsTheAgentsCall`, `TestSearchQueryMasked` and `search-mcp.test.cjs`.

## A model that turns an image away

Whether a model sees is its provider's list's word (`ImageInput`), else
the served entry's (`blindTo`), and a list that says nothing takes
models.dev's modalities for the model (`catalog.SeesImages`). A list's
modalities are read as models.dev words them (`catalog.Kinds`): OpenCode
Go's `/models` names media types (`image/jpeg`, `video/mp4`,
`application/pdf`), which read word for word held no `image`, so every
Go model was text-only (zeo9901 on Discord); a list saved that way is
mended when read (`mendKinds`). A relay's
copy of a model can take text only where models.dev says it sees: xyenon
(Discord) picked provider-1/DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash in pi with Image
recognition set, magpie sent it the raw image, DeepSeek answered 400
"unknown variant `image_url`", and since that reads as a shape refusal
(`shapeWords`) the request went on to the fallback, the same model at
provider-2, which sees. The image was never described.

A 400, 415 or 422 whose words say images aren't taken (`imageRefused`:
unknown variant image_url/input_image/image, OpenAI's "only supported by
certain models", OpenRouter's "no endpoints found that support image",
vLLM's "not a multimodal model", 不支持图片 and the like) now marks the
model blind for the server's run (`refuseSight`, `Server.blind`;
`blindTo` checks it first). With a describer (`seer`), the same candidate
is asked again at once with the images described (`seenBody`, or the
group's `seenGroup`; the try's failure is `failSight`), before any
fallback or other member, and later requests are described up front, for
a direct pick and a group alike. The pick and the describer the user set
win over another provider's copy of the model.

With no describer (Image recognition off, or nothing that sees), going on
to a candidate whose copy sees is the only way to answer, so that is kept
on purpose (`TestRefusedImageWithNoDescriberGoesToOneThatSees`).

Covered by `TestPickedModelThatRefusesAnImageIsGivenItDescribed` (direct,
user group and auto group, over Chat, Responses and Messages) and
`TestImageRefused`
([`vision_refused_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/vision_refused_test.go)).

## An image the describer can't describe

`seer` names one describer, and before kelvinjason11 on X ("图片识别好像只支持一个模型识别 没有fallback") that was the only one asked: when it
failed (a 5xx, out of its allowance, a timeout, an image it turned away, an
empty answer) the latest turn's image failed the request, though other
models that see were enabled. `describe` now asks through `describeWith`:
the describer first, always, and when it fails, the next model that sees,
as `AutoVision` ranks them (`seersRanked`, the ranking `autoVision` takes
its first from), passing over the models already asked and any that
turned an image away (`sightRefused`). Up to `visionTries` (3) models are
asked, within `visionWaitMost` all told (each try is given what is left,
`askVisionWith`'s `most`). A model out of its allowance is not asked in
another's place: `seersRanked` puts those last, so the first being out
means every one is. A request whose context ended (the agent canceled it)
asks no other.

- The description's header names the model that described it (`seenBody`,
  `sight.by`).
- The Routing row of a description asked in another's place carries
  `CallFor.Instead` (the one asked before it), `Why` (its error) and
  `Picked` (it was Settings' pick), and says so (`kindWhy`), as a search
  in another searcher's place does. Each try is a row of its own.
- When none answers, the error is the first's reason as before, then
  "nor could X, asked in its place: why" for each other one
  (`visionFailed`).
- `sights` keeps a description under the image (`sightKey`): a base64
  data URL's decoded bytes, else the URL, with the level picked in
  Settings. Not under a model: the image sent again next turn is asked of
  no one, whichever model is picked now (AutoVision's pick changing
  between turns had a long conversation's every image described again,
  Pkh on Discord), and a new image is asked of the pick first again, so a
  pick that failed once is not given up for good. The same bytes sent on
  another protocol, or under another media type, are described once
  (`seenBody` dedupes by the key too). A failure is still not kept.
- Completed descriptions of inline images are also appended to
  `vision-cache.jsonl` in magpie's config directory before their waiters
  are released (`saveSight`), and restored by `New`. A restart or update
  therefore doesn't describe a conversation's history again. Only inline
  images are persisted: their keys come from their bytes, while a URL can
  later name different bytes and stays process-local. The private (0600)
  file keeps the same 2048-entry LRU on reload; a JSON-malformed or
  crash-truncated record is skipped without hiding the other records, and
  is copied to a private `.broken-*` file before a later compaction drops
  it. An inline description is counted from its creation until it has saved
  or failed; gateway shutdown waits for that count to reach zero, so a
  canceled request's completed description isn't lost on exit. Appends and compaction
  are serialized across gateway processes by `vision-cache.jsonl.lock`;
  compaction rereads the latest file under that lock before atomically
  replacing it, so a handover can't overwrite another gateway's new record.
  A lock or disk-write failure only loses the persistence optimization,
  never a description that already succeeded.

This differs from a searcher named in Settings, which gives way to no other
provider: a failed search still leaves the search APIs and the model's own
answer, while an image that isn't described fails the turn.

Covered by `TestPickedDescriberThatFailsGivesWayToTheNextThatSees`,
`TestAutoDescriberThatFailsGivesWayToTheNext`,
`TestNoDescriberAnswersNamesEach`,
`TestPickedDescriberThatAnswersIsTheOnlyOneAsked`,
`TestCanceledDescriptionAsksNoOther`
([`vision_fallback_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/vision_fallback_test.go))
and `vision-instead.test.cjs`.

## A description is no one request's

A description is asked once (`describe`) on a context of its own
(`context.WithoutCancel` of the first asker's, with its own cancel):
every request that sends the image while it is being described waits on
the same one (`sight.waiting`). One whose agent cancels it stops
waiting; the description goes on for the others, and only the last to
go stops it and takes it out of `sights`, so the next to send the image
asks again. Before, the description ran on the first asker's context:
its cancel failed the description for every other request waiting on it
with "context canceled", and took it out of the cache, so the next turn
read the image again. A reply that came whole is kept though its asker
ended as it came (`askVisionWith` reads the reply before the context).
Once the model has answered, `sight.answered` also keeps cancellation from
stopping the save: a completed inline description is written before its
waiters are released, even if the last waiter left during that write.

In `seenBody`, an image waiting for one of the `visionParallel` slots
when its request ends is asked of no one: before, each queued image went
on to `serve` with a dead context, and one canceled request put a row for each
image on the Routing page in a few ms (Pkh: 63 in ~60ms).

A description's `serve` runs on its request's context, so its route is
noted on that request's `inflight` (`noteRoute`) and stands in for it on
the Routing page while it waits; when the images can't be described,
the request is given a route of its own, ended 499 when its agent
canceled it and 502 else (`notDescribed`), and its usage record names
it. One magpie answered for, held too long (`giveUp`), keeps the route
that showed it. A routing group's request, routed already, ends 499 too
when its agent canceled it.

Covered by `TestSameImageDescribedOnceWhateverDescribesIt`,
`TestOneRequestGoneLeavesTheDescriptionToAnother`,
`TestCanceledRequestAsksNoMoreDescriptions` and
`TestCanceledWhileDescribingKeepsItsRoute`
([`vision_dedupe_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/vision_dedupe_test.go)).
Restart reuse, cancellation during a save, failed writes, truncated records,
cross-Server compaction, duplicate records, the LRU and URL behavior are
covered by `TestDescriptionSurvivesGatewayRestart` and
[`vision_cache_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/vision_cache_test.go).

## Image recognition as an MCP server

`VisionMCPPath`, `/mcp/magpie/vision`, serves the model that describes
images to a model that can't see them (`seer`: Settings › Models › Image
recognition, else `AutoVision`'s) as an MCP server with one `analyze_image`
tool (JonahFu on X). `serveMCP` is shared with the web search server, so it
is answered, guarded and Origin-checked the same way, with a 32 MB body so
an image fits as a data URL, and each call reserves the calling key's limit
(`budget.Reserve`). The tool takes `image` and an optional `prompt`. With no
prompt it is described as a model's image is (`describe`, the same cache and
`visionSystem`); with one, `describeWith` asks the prompt beside it under
`visionAskSystem`, with the same fallback as a description (see "An image
the describer can't describe"). `image` is an http(s) URL or a `data:image/…;base64,` URL,
passed on as they are, or an absolute file path (`~/` and `file://` taken),
read into a data URL up to 20 MB when `http.DetectContentType` says it is an
image. A path is read only for a `local` caller: another machine's path
names nothing here, a shared magpie's files aren't its callers', and a
request through a proxy or tunnel isn't `local`. The description's route is
marked `CallFor{Agent, MCP: true}`, so Routing says the agent called
analyze_image. Image recognition off or no model that sees is a tool error
that says where to pick one. Settings › Models shows the address
(`#visionMcpRow`, below Image generation).

Covered by `TestVisionMCPAnalyzesImage`, `TestVisionMCPWithNoSeer`,
`TestVisionMCPGuarded`, `TestVisionMCPHeldToKeyLimit` and
`vision-mcp.test.cjs`.

## Image generation as an MCP server

`ImageMCPPath`, `/mcp/magpie/image` ([`image_mcp.go`](../../internal/gateway/image_mcp.go)),
serves Settings › Images › Image generation as a Streamable HTTP MCP server
with the tools the stdio Magpie Image server has (`internal/imagemcp`):
`generate_image`, `generate_video` and `generation_result` (JonahFu on X:
magpie in Docker in gateway mode has no Library, so no stdio server). Their
schemas are the gateway's `ImageToolProps`, `VideoToolProps` and
`ResultToolProps`, which the stdio server takes too, adding only its `path`
argument. It is served by `serveMCP`, so it is guarded as the vision server
is: `local` or a shared magpie's caller, a refused Origin 403, POST only, a
32 MB body, and each call reserves the calling key's limit. An input image
goes through `mcpImageSrc`, the vision server's reader: a URL or data URL as
it is, a file path only for a `local` caller.

A drawing runs `images` in-process on a clone of the request
(`inProcess`, the caller and key kept), so it is routed, counted and held to
the key's models as `/v1/images/generations` and `/v1/images/edits` are. It
runs as a job under `context.WithoutCancel` for at most 15 minutes; the tool
answers within 20 seconds, else with an id that `generation_result` waits on.
Each image comes back as MCP image content (up to 5 MB each) and at a URL
under `/mcp/magpie/image/files/` (a vendor's URL is fetched first). The
file store is in memory, kept an hour and 256 MB at most, oldest first out.
A file and a job belong to the gateway key that made them: another key gets
404 (`KeyID`). The URL handed out carries `?t=<exp>.<sig>`
(`imageFileToken`: HMAC-SHA256 of the file's name, its expiry and the
making key's id, under a secret made at the first file and kept only in
memory), so it opens with no header, in a browser too (dhanyqaz on
Discord: a model relays the link to a person). `lanGuard` lets a keyless
GET or HEAD of a file with a `t` through (`imageTokenAsked`) after the
allow list and sharing are judged, and `imageMCPFile` serves it only when
the token is that file's, unexpired, and its key still on (`access.On`);
anything else is the 401 a keyless request gets. A wrong key with a token
is still 401, a page's Origin still 403, and the token names neither the
key nor its id. Rotating the key keeps its id, so its links stay
good for the rest of their hour (they never held the secret); turning it
off or removing it ends them at once. The URL's base is `MAGPIE_PUBLIC_URL` when set, else the
request's scheme and `Host`. A video is started through `videosCreate` and
polled through `videosGet` in-process; when done the tool gives
`/v1/videos/<id>/content`, which needs the same key. Image generation Off
(`settings.ImageGen == "off"`) is a tool error that says where to pick a
model, checked before the arguments. Nothing is written into the agent's
project. Settings › Models shows the address (`#imageMcpRow`, below
`#visionMcpRow`).

`generate_image` and `generate_video` take an optional `model` (XYenon on
Discord), in [`image_mcp_models.go`](../../internal/gateway/image_mcp_models.go).
`mediaModels` lists what may be asked, as provider/model: Settings' default
first (`Drawer`, `videomaker`), then each provider's `OfferedDrawers` (a
model the user unticked is left out) or `OfferedVideomakers` (likewise),
from providers that
are on and not decide-only. `mediaModelsFor` keeps only those the calling
