key may use (`refAllowed`: `allows`, or `groupAllowed` for a group ref). The
schema's `model` is an `enum` of them, or, past 40 (`mediaEnumMost`), a
description naming three. `pickMediaModel` takes a listed ref (a ChatGPT
account's other GPT Image name through `ListedDrawer`), a bare id only one
provider has, or a newer model of a family a provider serves (`drawsFamily`,
`grokVideoFamily`). Anything else is a tool error that names the models
the key may use, and nothing is drawn. Left out, Settings' default is used,
as before. The model chosen is put in the request's `model`, so the images
and videos APIs' own key check (`keyHolds`, `keyModelError`) refuses one
the key may not use, the same check `/v1/images` makes. The stdio Magpie
Image server can't read providers (WSL, a gateway elsewhere), so it copies
the gateway's `model` property from this server's `tools/list` and asks the
JSON-RPC method `magpie/media_model` (`MediaModelMethod`; not a tool) which
model one asked for stands for. An older gateway's -32601 passes the model
through as named. Covered by `TestImageMCPDrawsWithTheModelAsked`,
`TestImageMCPModelHeldToTheKey`, `TestImageMCPVideoModelAsked` and
imagemcp's `TestGenerateTakesTheModel`.

Each answer also carries `_meta` for a client that acts on it rather than
reads it: `usemagpie.ai/pending` is the id to wait on (`PendingMeta`, beside
the text that says to call `generation_result`), and `usemagpie.ai/files`
lists each file made (`FilesMeta`, `MadeFile`: its URL, and `inline` when the
image is in the content too). `magpie tool` ([`tool_cli.go`](../../internal/cli/tool_cli.go))
is such a client: it lists and calls the tools of every server in
`OwnMCPServers` (image, vision, web search, plugin tools) over HTTP with the
local token and the `magpie-tool/1` User-Agent, waits on a pending id, and
saves the files (`imagemcp.Target` names them, never over a file). It starts
nothing of its own (#3318) and refuses an unknown or missing parameter before
calling. Covered by `TestToolDrawsThroughTheGateway` (the real gateway, a fake
vendor) and the other `TestTool*` tests.

Covered by `TestImageMCPDrawsAndServesTheImage`, `TestImageMCPSaysWhenOff`,
`TestImageMCPGuarded`, `TestImageMCPHeldToKeyLimit`,
`TestImageMCPSlowDrawingIsWaitedOn`, `TestImageMCPMakesVideos`,
`TestImageMCPMakesMiniMaxVideos`,
`TestImageMCPIsServed` and `image-mcp.test.cjs`.

## Which of magpie's own MCP servers are on

`GET /v1/magpie/mcp` (`OwnMCPPath`, [`own_mcp.go`](../../internal/gateway/own_mcp.go))
lists the MCP servers above that are on now, as
`{"servers":[{"name","title","path"}]}`, each named as its `initialize`
names it, with its path on this gateway. It is for an app that gives a
model the Library's servers and wants magpie's own beside them: the
community chat page's "use all of magpie's MCP servers" read only
library.json, so web search, image recognition and image generation never
reached its chats (ferock on Discord). A server is listed while an agent
mounting it would get working tools: `magpie-web-search` while
plugins/search serves and something searches (`canSearch`, so Off in
Settings › Models › Web search with no search API leaves it out),
`magpie-vision` while a model sees (`seer`, not Off), `magpie-image` while
a model draws (`drawer`, not Off), and `magpie-tools` while plugins/tools
serves and a plugin that is on gives a tool (`plugin.ToolsOn`; a note that
can't be read lists it, and its `tools/list` tells). The list holds no
secret, so a read-only gateway key may read it (`readOnlyRoutes`); the route is otherwise guarded as every `/v1/magpie/*` route is, and a caller
mounts each path with the same key it called with. `OwnMCPServers` is the
fixed list `magpie tool` walks; this one follows the switches. Covered by
`TestOwnMCPNoneWhenNothingIsOn` and `TestOwnMCPLists*WhileOn`.

## Side models magpie picks: allowance first

When magpie picks the model itself (`AutoVision` for image recognition,
`autoSearcher` and the turn in `modelSearch` for web search), it picks
before routing runs, so routing has one provider to try and would try it
"all the same" while every account of it rests. Each request would then be
refused upstream again (#1801). `spentModel` (fallback.go) says a model is
out of its allowance when every candidate routing would try for it is
barred at a usage cap, resting for quota or credit (not for rate), or has
a full window (`candidate.full`), and when it is back. `smallModel`
(`smallReady`) prefers models that aren't spent, and the pickers rank a
provider with allowance above any tier or searchRank. With every one spent
they still name a pick, so `seer`/`Described` and `canSearch`, which tell
agents what magpie offers, don't flip with a window. Such a pick isn't
asked: `askVisionWith` (`autoSpent`) answers with the reason and when it is
back, and `modelSearch` (`searcherSpent`) skips it, puts spent searchers
after the others and joins the reason into its error. A model named in
Settings is asked as before (an image it can't describe goes on to the next
model that sees, see "An image the describer can't describe"), and a
searcher named there is not handed on
to another provider when it fails (see "A searcher that fails or finds
nothing"). Image and video generation (`AutoDrawer`,
`AutoVideomaker`) are not held this way.

Covered by `TestAutoVisionPassesOverOneOut`,
`TestAutoVisionTierAmongThoseWithAllowance`, `TestSpentModelAcrossAccounts`,
`TestVisionDoesNotAskSpentDescriber` and `TestSearcherPassesOverOneOut`.

## Hidden image models

A provider's editor lists its image models (`gateway.Drawers`, the
`drawIds` of `/api/providers`) apart from its chat models, each ticked.
Unticking one saves its id in `provider.HiddenDrawers` (providers.json,
and an account's picks entry through `withPicks`). It is a deny list
beside `Models`, not an entry in it: an image id in `Models` would be
offered to agents to chat with, and the allow list would make hiding one
picking every chat model by hand. A Save that leaves `hiddenDrawers` out
keeps the old list; `[]` ticks them all again.

`gateway.OfferedDrawers` is `Drawers` less the hidden ones. It is what
lists image models to pick from or picks among: `AutoDrawer`,
`drawerObjects` (another magpie's `X-Magpie-Drawers` list) and Aside's
image options. Settings › Images (`settingsState`) leaves them out too,
except the one picked there. A hidden model still draws: `resolveDrawing`
and `drawingModel` read `Drawers`, so a request that names one, or a
Settings › Images pick made before it was hidden, draws as before. That
pick stays listed as picked, and `imageGenHidden` puts a line under the
row that it is unticked and leaves the list once another is picked.
Refusing a model outright is Disabled models, below.

The editor shows each image model by its name where it has one
(`drawNames`), with the id as the chip's tooltip: a ChatGPT account's
`gpt-image-2`, the id Codex CLI asks for, is its one GPT Image, since its
backend draws with the model it picks whichever is named (#1865; StarBits
on X read the id as an old model). Unticking it still saves the id.

`AutoDrawer` never picks a GPT Image that a newer one set up supersedes
(`gptImageGenOf`: by version, gpt-image-2.6 over 2.5 over 2, unversioned
dall-e and chatgpt-image-latest under them all; then the base or Sunburst
over Flare over mini). An OpenAI key's list names 2.5 and 2.6 with no
price while models.dev prices gpt-image-2, so cost alone took the old one.
A GPT Image with no price is costed as the newest priced one, as OpenAI
prices them alike. A ChatGPT account's GPT Image is never superseded, as
its name says nothing of what draws, so the plan's images stay Automatic's
choice. Another kind of model that costs less is still the cheaper choice.
`TestAutoDrawerTakesNewestGPTImage`, `TestAutoDrawerKeepsChatGPTsOwnPick`,
`chatgpt-image-chip.test.cjs`.

Video models are listed the same way, under Video models (axelburks on
Discord: an Ark key's Seedance had nowhere to be ticked). The editor's
`filmIds` are `gateway.VideomakersOf` the listing it already has, and an
unticked one is saved in `provider.HiddenVideomakers`, kept and cleared as
`hiddenDrawers` is. `gateway.OfferedVideomakers` is `Videomakers` less the
hidden ones, read by `AutoVideomaker`, `videomakerObjects` and
`mediaModels(true)`. A hidden one still makes its video when named:
`bareVideomaker`, `videosCreate` and `arkFilms` read `Videomakers`.

An Ark key's list marks its video and image models by `domain`
(`VideoGeneration`, `ImageGeneration`) and
`modalities.output_modalities`, with no `kind`; `catalog`'s live parse
reads both, so Seedance is a video model there and no longer a chat
model, and 3D, embedding and other models that give no text are left out
(`TestFetchArkMarksVideoModels`, on a captured list). An Ark list fetched
before has Seedance among its chat models until the next fetch;
`customVideomakers` still counts any Seedance id an Ark key lists or
picks as a video model.

Covered by `TestHiddenDrawerLeavesListsButStillDraws`,
`TestImageModelsHidden`, `internal/gui/tests/image-models-hide.test.cjs`,
`TestHiddenVideomakerLeavesListsButStillFilms`, `TestVideoModelsHidden`
and `internal/gui/tests/video-models-hide.test.cjs`.

## Disabled models

Settings › Models › Disabled models, `magpie model disable|enable|disabled`
and `POST /api/settings/disabled-models` keep `settings.DisabledModels`
(#1867): patterns, each an exact id or a glob (`*` any run of characters,
`/` among them; `?` one), matched ignoring case against a model's
`<provider>/<model>`, its bare id and the bare id's last segment
([`internal/provider/disabled.go`](../../internal/provider/disabled.go)).
Nothing is disabled by default.

- Lists: `buildEntries` (`entriesOff`) leaves a disabled model out of the
  catalog, so `/v1/models`, the agents' model files and pickers, Codex's
  list, Muse and every routing group go without it; `membersIn`
  (`membersOff`) skips it as a member and `Deciders` as a decision model.
  `codexModels` drops a disabled one of Codex's own, and `codexListTag`
  changes with the list so Codex fetches it again.
- Requests: the gateway refuses it with a 403 in each API's shape
  ([`internal/gateway/disabled.go`](../../internal/gateway/disabled.go)),
  `model <id> is disabled in magpie …`, at the choke points #882 holds keys
  at: `serve` (every chat, Responses, Messages and Gemini request, magpie's
  own side calls, a session that was on it, a Codex sub-agent put on it),
  `perKeyBarred` (no candidate is planned for it: a group's next member, a
  provider's `Fallback`), `countTokens` and Gemini's `:countTokens`
  (`countOff`), `serveSystemOne`, `serveDecisionGroup` and `postDecide`
  (every decision call), draw, video and embeddings, and `codexBackend` for
  one of Codex's own bare models. A bare id the catalog no longer has is
  refused as disabled, not 404 (`provider.BareDisabled`). A group with a
  member left goes on without the rest; one with none left is refused,
  naming its disabled members (`provider.GroupDisabledOut`). A Codex
  sub-agent pick onto a disabled model is kept on its lead's model
  (`codexSubagentPick`, "it is disabled in magpie").
- A settings.json that can't be read is never "none disabled": the list read
  last stays in force, and with none read since start every request is
  refused until it is fixed (`DisabledUnknown`); `SetDisabledModels` writes
  nothing over a file it couldn't read.
- Only requests through magpie are covered: an agent signed in to the vendor
  itself is not, which the Settings row says.
- Tests: `TestDisabled*` in `internal/gateway`, `internal/provider`,
  `TestModelDisableCmd`, `TestDisabledModelsSetting`, and
  `internal/gui/tests/disabled-models.test.cjs`.

## Model ids without the provider

A client moved from another proxy (love1sbug on X, from CLIProxyAPI) keeps
asking for `gpt-5.6-sol`, and picks from `/v1/models`, where magpie lists
only `codex/gpt-5.6-sol`. A bare request already routes (Model resolution,
above); the list didn't offer it. "Provider in model names" (`PlainNames`)
changes labels only, never ids.

Settings › Network and sharing › Model ids without the provider,
`POST /api/settings/bare-ids` (`settings.BareModelIDs`,
`provider.SetBareModelIDs`), off by default. On, `models` lists every row
it listed before, unchanged and first, then one row per bare model id
(`provider.BareEntries`, [`internal/provider/bare.go`](../../internal/provider/bare.go)):
the row of the entry that id reaches, with its label, under the bare id.
`GET /v1/models/{id}` answers a bare id the same way (`provider.BareEntry`).

- Which entry: the gateway's own resolution, so the list never offers what
  a request would not reach. A found or user group the id names (`groupForIn`,
  as `GroupFor`) gives the group's row; else `resolveIn` (as `Resolve`)
  gives the first provider in the Providers order that serves it. So a model
  several providers serve is listed once, and answered by its group with
  found groups on, else by the first provider, as before.
- An id already in the catalog as is (a group's or a provider's own id) is
  not listed twice. Group rows themselves add nothing.
- The target must be in the list shown: a gateway key held to some models
  sees only the bare ids that reach one of its models, so a key held to
  `b/*` doesn't see `glm-5.3` that would go to `a`.
- Never for another magpie (`DrawersHeader`), whose remote provider would
  list each model twice, nor for Claude Desktop's list, which has its own
  shape.
- No agent file changes: magpie still writes `provider/model` ids
  (`magpieModels`), so turning it on or off rewrites nothing.
- Tests: `TestBareModelIDsReporterCase` (Codex `gpt-5.6-sol` listed bare and
  answered on Chat, Responses and Messages, the list unchanged when off),
  `TestBareModelIDsShared` (several providers, found groups on and off, a
  held key, another magpie) in
  [`bare_ids_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/bare_ids_test.go),
  `TestBareModelIDsSetting` in `internal/gui`, and
  `internal/gui/tests/bare-ids.test.cjs`.
- A URL magpie downloads but didn't choose itself is downloaded from public addresses only, wherever the request came from ([`image_fetch_guard.go`](../../internal/gateway/image_fetch_guard.go)). That is an image a caller names (an `/v1/images/edits` or `/v1/images/generations` reference image, for an images API that takes files, Gemini's own API or Code Assist: `fetchPicture` in [`draw.go`](../../internal/gateway/draw.go), `publicClient`), a page `web_fetch` reads (`webFetchClient`), and an image or video a vendor answers by URL, which what the model was asked to draw or do can steer (the inline download for a `b64_json` caller in `draw.go` and `/v1/videos/{id}/content` in [`video.go`](../../internal/gateway/video.go), `vendorClient`; generate_image's own download in [`internal/imagemcp`](../../internal/imagemcp/imagemcp.go), `PublicClient`). Every address the host resolves to must be public (`publicAddr`, which is [`internal/netguard`](../../internal/netguard/netguard.go)'s `Public`, the rule a provider's icon uses too: not loopback, RFC 1918, fc00::/7, link-local with 169.254.169.254, CGNAT 100.64/10, unspecified, multicast, documentation, reserved, benchmarking, Teredo, nor an IPv4-mapped, NAT64 or 6to4 form of one; an AAAA record Loon synthesizes for an A answer in the same reply, `fd27:712::/96` with that IPv4 address inside, is left out and the A answer judged in its place, `dropLoon`), and the request goes to the address checked, so a name that resolves elsewhere the second time changes nothing; a direct connection is checked again on the address dialled (`guardDialer`). Through a proxy the proxy is sent that address, never the name (an `https://` CONNECT or SOCKS keeps the name for TLS and Host; a plain `http://` URL through an HTTP proxy is asked for by the address in Host too). A fake-IP resolver's answer is the one shape let past that (#2593; ggbdpq, with h4rk8s's and TomXPRIME's reports): Clash's TUN with fake-ip, Clash Verge, Surge's enhanced mode and Loon answer every name in 198.18.0.0/15 (`netguard.FakeIP`), so with one on every download was refused as a private address. A name (never an address literal) whose every answer is in that range is fetched: with no proxy (pure TUN) its fake address is pinned and dialled, the dial guard letting that one address in, and the TUN carries it; through a proxy on this computer (a loopback address or `localhost`, `onThisComputer`) the proxy is given the name, never the fake address, which a real proxy can't route (h4rk8s: a CONNECT by the name went through where one by its 198.18 address failed), and a plain `http://` request's Host is the checked name, never the caller's own; through a proxy elsewhere it is refused. An answer with any other address beside the fake ones (private or public) is judged as any, so a fake one beside 192.168.x is refused. The trust this asks for is the fake-IP resolver's: which host the name stands for, and so where the TUN or the proxy connects, is decided where magpie can't see, and a name that really points at the user's network is reached if that resolver's rules send it there. A provider's icon was already fetched this way (`publicIP` in [`internal/provider/icon.go`](../../internal/provider/icon.go), #252), and shares the rule. `TestFakeIPNameGoesToALocalProxyByName`, `TestFakeIPNameDialledThroughTheTUN`, `TestFakeIPRefusesTheRest` in [`fakeip_test.go`](../../internal/gateway/fakeip_test.go). Each redirect, up to 5, is checked the same, and a caller's image must sniff as an image. A request from this computer gets nothing more: a reverse proxy or relay in front of magpie on the same host makes every request it carries arrive from loopback, so where a request came from is never a reason to reach a private address. Two things are: settings.json's `fetchPrivate` (`settings.FetchPrivate`, none by default, no Settings page, this computer's own and never brought by a sync or restore), a list of names, `name:port`s, IPs and CIDR ranges the user lets magpie download from (`allowFor`; a settings.json that can't be read lets nothing extra in), and for a vendor's URL the `host:port` of that provider's own endpoints, which the user named themselves (a provider on the LAN that serves its images from itself). A vendor's image that can't be downloaded is answered by its URL, never dropped; its video is a 502 naming why. A URL left for the vendor to fetch (a chat model's message) is the vendor's to fetch.

### Instruction roles on translated Responses turns

A Responses request translated back to Responses keeps its `system` and
`developer` input messages in place, including messages added after an
assistant reply (`parseRequest` in
[`gateway.go`](../../internal/gateway/gateway.go), `readResponsesFor` in
[`responses.go`](../../internal/gateway/responses.go)). Its top-level
`instructions` stays separate. Offering `web_search` to a custom provider
that is not known to search natively can select this path even when no
search is called. Previously it lifted initial instruction items into
`instructions` and changed later ones into user reminders, so a Codex
Plan-to-Default transition lost its developer authority.

This does not change the Claude subscription or other APIs' existing
cache-preserving mapping. If the upstream falls back from Responses to
another API, that API's builder applies the existing mapping through
`responseInstructions`; building it leaves the native request intact.
`TestResponsesInstructionRolesThroughSearch` checks the upstream-bound
roles and positions, `TestCodexSearchAnsweredOnResponses` checks both sides
of a search round, and `TestResponsesInstructionsOtherAPIsUnchanged`
compares the other builders with their previous output.

## A Responses WebSocket

Codex speaks Responses over a WebSocket to its own provider, which magpie's
wiring is (`openai_base_url` on the Codex backend path), and only there
names the reply a turn follows (`previous_response_id`) and sends what came
after it; over HTTP it sends the whole conversation, images and all, each
turn (#4058, kevinzhow and neighbads on X). magpie answered the upgrade 426
(a87dc1ff2) until #4058. [`responses_ws.go`](../../internal/gateway/responses_ws.go)
serves it on `/v1/responses`, `/responses` and the Codex backend's
`/responses`, with Codex's protocol (codex-rs
`responses_websocket.rs`):

- Each `response.create` text frame is one turn, served as the POST it
  stands for: the upgrade's own request (URL, Host, address, headers but
  the socket's own and `OpenAI-Beta: responses_websockets`) with the frame
  as its body, `type`, `previous_response_id` and `generate` taken out, and
  `client_metadata`'s turn state and responses-lite flag as the
  `X-Codex-Turn-State` and `X-OpenAI-Internal-Codex-Responses-Lite`
  headers Codex sends over HTTP. It goes through the whole handler the
  upgrade came in on (`socketEntry`, outside `Front` and `Handler`), so
  the allow list, `rebound`, the key, `corsGuard`, `keyLimited`, routing,
  failover, usage and the log judge every turn as an HTTP one: a key
  turned off or past its limit refuses the next turn
  (`TestResponsesSocketGuards`). The upgrade itself passed those too, and
  coder/websocket's `Accept` refuses a browser Origin whose host isn't the
  `Host`. `holdCatalog` holds each turn, not the socket.
- The reply's events go back a text frame each; the reply's headers as
  Codex reads them on a socket (`response.metadata` with
  `x-codex-turn-state`, `openai-model` and `x-reasoning-included`;
  `codex.response.metadata` with `x-models-etag`, the backend's own event
  given magpie's tag as `modelsEtag` gives it; `codex.rate_limits` from the
  `x-codex-*` windows). A whole JSON reply is told as its stream would be. A
  refused turn is an `error` frame with the status, the error object and
  the headers Codex acts on (`Retry-After`, `x-codex-*`, `x-ratelimit-*`).
- The conversation a turn continues is the socket's own: each completed
  reply's input and output (in the order of `output_item.done`, as Codex
  adds them), the last two kept (`socketKeep`; a warmup's and its first
  turn's). A turn naming one is sent the whole conversation, so a provider
  or account other than the last one's sees what it would over HTTP. An id
  the socket doesn't hold, from another socket, or a failed reply's, is
  answered `previous_response_not_found`, on which Codex opens a new socket
  and sends everything. `prevResponses` isn't used: Codex says `store:
  false`.
- `generate: false` (Codex's warmup) is answered locally with a
  `response.created` and `response.completed` of its own id; nothing is
  sent upstream.
- `response.interrupt` naming the reply in flight cancels its request and
  is answered `response.interrupt.accepted`, then an interrupted
  `response.incomplete`; one naming another reply, or none in flight,
  `response.interrupt.failed`. Other frames during a turn wait for it.
- An upstream cut the gateway tells as `response.failed` goes back as
  that frame, and the reply isn't kept; a stream that still ends with no
  terminal event closes the socket 1011, as an HTTP stream would be cut
  (`TestResponsesSocketCutReplyFails`). Binary frames close it 1003.
- `Run`'s shutdown ends each socket going away (1001) once its turn is
  answered, through `clientSockets.drain` beside `srv.Shutdown` (a hijacked
  connection isn't the server's), and refuses new ones 503.
- Not covered: the leg to the vendor is the whole conversation each turn,
  as before (for the ChatGPT backend, a socket per turn: see
  [Providers and accounts](providers-accounts.md)); what is saved is
  Codex's upload to magpie, and, when the provider is another magpie, the
  leg between the two magpies (below). A socket's conversation doesn't
  outlive the socket or the gateway.

## A Responses request that names the response it follows

A Responses client may send only its new input with `previous_response_id`
(kevinzhow on X: every turn sent the whole conversation again, images
included). `previousResponse` in
[`prev_response.go`](../../internal/gateway/prev_response.go) runs in
`serveAgent` before anything else reads the body.

- Every Responses reply magpie passes with status 200, not failed or
  cancelled, is kept by its id with the conversation it answered (input,
  then output) in `prevResponses`: in memory only, least recently used
  first out past 256 MB, each kept 6 hours (`prevTTL`, `prevCap`). A
  request with `store: false` keeps nothing. Codex over HTTP sends
  `store: false` with the whole input and never names a previous response
  (only its websocket request has the field), so nothing changes for it.
  On a WebSocket it names one, which the socket keeps itself (below).
- A turn is kept for the caller that made it (`prevScope`): its gateway
  key, the remote magpie it came through, and the address of a keyless
  remote caller. Another caller naming the id is told it isn't there.
- A request naming a kept id is given the conversation whole
  (`previous_response_id` taken out, the kept items before its own input),
  so every route, translation and masking below sees what it would have
  seen had the client sent it all. The stored items are what the client
  sent, and masking runs on each request as it goes, so a stored secret
  is masked the same way on every later turn.
- An id magpie doesn't keep (never seen, another caller's, expired, or
  lost to a restart) gets OpenAI's 400 `previous_response_not_found`
  (`writePrevNotFound`) on a route that translates, before anything is
  sent. A Responses relay is sent the request as it came, and its own
  answer reaches the client.
- Where the provider keeps responses itself (`prevKeeps`: an API key, no
  plugin, on api.openai.com, Azure, or Ark's `/api/v3` pay-as-you-go
  Responses) and it answered the previous turn on the same key
  (`prevRoute`), with `store` not off, the request goes as the client's
  new input with the provider's own id (`prevDelta`), so images aren't
  uploaded again. A provider that says it doesn't know that id
  (`prevRefused`) is sent the whole conversation at once.
- Not covered: the kept turns don't outlive the gateway, and a kept-id
  request is still read and masked whole on this computer before the
  delta is cut.

### Turns to another magpie

Two magpies that share their gateways (LotusDecoder on X: one in China,
one in the US, each the other's provider) sent the leg between them the
whole conversation every turn. Codex says `store: false`, so
`prevResponses` and `prevDelta` don't apply.
[`remote_delta.go`](../../internal/gateway/remote_delta.go) handles it in
`passthrough`, after `prevDelta` and only on Responses.

- A Responses request to a remote magpie (`IsRemoteMagpie`) whose body
  names no previous response and whose input is a list of items asks the
  other magpie to keep the turn: `X-Magpie-Keep-Turn: magpie/<version>`,
  put on in `passOnCaller`.
- The other magpie honours the header only from a request shaped as a
  magpie's hop (`keepTurnAsks`): the value starts `magpie/`, and the
  User-Agent is magpie's, or Codex's (forwardOnce passes Codex's headers
  on as Codex sent them). It then keeps the turn in `prevResponses`
  whatever `store` says, under the caller's `prevScope` and the same caps,
  and answers `X-Magpie-Kept-Turn: 1`. An id such a request names that it
  doesn't hold gets `previous_response_not_found` before anything is sent,
  never relayed to a vendor that might ignore it (`keptTurnLost` in
  `serveAgent`).
- The header comes off every request as it arrives (`withCaller`), and
  `X-Magpie-` is never a passable request header, so no vendor is sent it.
  A client that copies a hop's headers is kept to its own scope and the
  caps, as `store: true` would have it.
- When the reply says it was kept, this magpie records the turn per route
  and scope (`remoteTurns`): a SHA-256 of each input item as sent (after
  masking and translation), each output item's type, id and call_id, and
  the other magpie's response id. At most 16 per route and scope, 32 MB in
  all, each for `prevTTL`.
- The next request whose input starts with exactly those items, then as
  many items as the reply had with the same type and call_id (and id where
  both have one; Codex sends its items back without ids), and then at least
  one more, goes as the items after them with `previous_response_id`.
  Anything else goes whole: an edited or shortened history, another
  answer in place of the reply, a request naming its own previous
  response, or a magpie that never said it keeps turns (one from before
  this).
- A delta the other magpie refuses (`prevRefused`: it restarted, or let
  the turn go under its cap) is sent again whole at once, the record is
  dropped, and that route goes whole for 30 minutes (`remoteRefusedWait`).
- Not covered: the reply's output is matched by type and ids only, so a
  client that edits the text of the last answer (no ids) is sent a delta
  over the answer the other magpie kept. Tools and instructions still go
  every turn. The other magpie's kept turns are in memory only.

The gateway's `TestMain` gives the package a home of its own, so these tests
never read real providers or accounts.
